# Financial Modeler Pro, Change Record

**Dated session entries, newest first.** Moved verbatim out of CLAUDE.md on 2026-08-30 when it exceeded the 150k session limit; each entry below is the exact bullet CLAUDE.md used to carry (the fund-layer entry was a blockquote line there; only its `> ` prefix was stripped). New dated entries are appended HERE at the top of their date, never to CLAUDE.md (see the End-of-session rule in CLAUDE.md).

Entries are grouped by their most recent date. Fuller per-day narrative for REFM platform work lives in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md); per-route and migration detail in [CLAUDE-ROUTES.md](CLAUDE-ROUTES.md) and [CLAUDE-DB.md](CLAUDE-DB.md); the general lessons in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md); fund-layer standing rules in [docs/FUND_LAYER_GUIDELINE.md](docs/FUND_LAYER_GUIDELINE.md). This file is loaded on demand only and has no size limit.

## 2026-09-26

- **THE INSTALLED WINDOW IS THE MODELING HUB, NOT THE WEBSITE; A BROWSER TAB IS UNCHANGED** (founder). ONE detection rule in `lib/pwa/appManifest.ts`: CSS `display-mode` is standalone, minimal-ui, window-controls-overlay or fullscreen (what an installed app window reports, never a tab, and applied before any script, so nothing flashes), OR `navigator.standalone === true` on an iPhone home-screen app, which the client turns into `data-installed-app` on <html>. Under it, and only under it: the website header (`nav[data-fmp-nav]`) and anything marked `data-pwa-hide` are hidden (the sign-in page's Training Hub link and its "separate from training hub" line, "Back to Home" on sign-in and register), and the window is titled exactly "Modeling Hub" (app name now "Modeling Hub"; Chrome titles an installed window "<name> - <page title>" unless the title already starts with the name, so the client sets it and re-asserts it on every Next retitle). The hiding CSS is rendered with the page. **The Modeling Hub confirmation email** now says the Modeling Hub can be installed as an app from the browser (Chrome on a computer, Android, iPhone, in one line each; never "download") and links the sign-in page; the Training Hub confirmation has none of it. `verify-pwa` 83 (H: every hiding rule is inside the media query or behind the attribute, the detector fires on a stripped copy; J: both emails RENDERED). `scripts/probe-installed-window.ts` 10/10 in real Chromium: in a tab the header, Training link and Back to Home are visible and the title is the page's own; on the installed path all three are hidden, the form stays, the title is "Modeling Hub". Headless Chromium cannot emulate display-mode (measured), so the probe drives the iPhone half of the same rule; the media-query half is pinned by H1/H2. Not yet seen in a real installed window on a device.

- **THE INSTALLED APP IS THE MODELING HUB, NOT THE WEBSITE, AND ITS ICON IS THE SITE FAVICON** (founder). Measured first: the manifest said start `/dashboard`, scope `/` on the app host, so the main site and Training Hub were already other origins, but (1) the manifest was linked from the app host's marketing landing (`/`, served from `/modeling`) and the platform pages, all inside scope; (2) the sign-in page carries the site navbar (Home, Articles, Training, About, Contact, Pricing, all absolute www / learn links, none `target=_blank`), and an out-of-scope link in a standalone window navigates IN that window on Chrome desktop, so one click put the whole website in the app; (3) the icon was a separately drawn bar mark, not the favicon. Fixed: `lib/pwa/appManifest.ts` states the app (name "FMP Modeling Hub" / "Modeling Hub", start the dashboard, which sends a signed-out user to /signin with Sign In and Create Account, id kept so an installed copy updates) and the ONE in-window rule `linkTarget` / `isMarketingPath` (another origin or a hub marketing page opens a browser tab; the app-page list is proved equal to the folders under `app/modeling`); `PwaClient` applies it ONLY in the installed window (capture-phase click, before a Next Link; landing on a marketing page goes to the dashboard), also mounted on `/settings`. `/app.webmanifest` is now a ROUTE and `/app-icon/<file>` draws every icon from the favicon through `readSiteFaviconUrl` (`src/shared/cms/siteFavicon.ts`, the ONE favicon rule, now also the root layout's), versioned by the favicon URL so a changed favicon reaches installed apps; the static `/pwa/` copies are the fallback, drawn by the same renderer. The maskable share is 56% (the largest square the 80% safe circle holds): 64% failed the new F6 on a square mark. `verify-pwa` 68 (E and F new). Forgot-password lives on the main site, so from the app it opens in a browser tab. Not yet exercised on a device.

## 2026-09-23

- **MODULE 10 IS FINISHED: A REVIEWER CAN REVIEW WITHOUT LEAVING THE SCREEN, AND AN EDITOR CAN ACT WITHOUT GOING LOOKING.** Comments existed and lived on ONE screen, so a reviewer had to leave the number they were questioning in order to ask about it. Six steps, each committed on its own.
  - **THE MAP IS THE EVIDENCE FOR WHERE A COMMENT BELONGS** (`db6fca5f`, `lib/collab/pathScreen.ts`): every screen name in it is WRITE-VERIFIED by `scripts/audit-path-screen-map.ts`, which checked all 26 rules against what the screens actually WRITE, and changed four answers. `project.tax` is edited on the P&L and NOT Project & Phases; `project.costEscalationPct` on Asset Types & Standards and NOT Capex; `landAllocationMode` and `retailAreaPerSlotSqm` are RETIRED so they are declared editable NOWHERE rather than pointed at a plausible tab; and `displayScale`, `displayDecimals`, `resultsViewMode` are WRITTEN by real tabs, so they are mapped there (they were to be declared unmapped as view settings until the write evidence said otherwise, founder's reversal). FIVE FAMILIES HAVE TWO HOMES and the map names both. **The audit itself had to be corrected four times before it could be believed**: a bare-key join reported a tranche rename as a phase edit, a component span ended at its own destructured PARAMETER brace so the whole branch contributed nothing silently, an expression-bodied helper swallowed the rest of its file, and a deepest-segment walk matched a bare `enabled` and named the fund tab as editor of the dividend policy. A fifth heuristic that would have resolved two more rules was written and REVERTED for inventing one home to find another. Final: 0 wrong claims, 14 of 26 rules with positive write evidence, 12 settled by direct inspection.
  - **COMMENT WHERE YOU ARE** (`2364dcf3` core, `b3157c86` screens): `FieldCommentsProvider` mounts ONCE in the shell on the SAME `useProjectComments` hook the Collaborate screen uses (per-field fetching would be hundreds of requests on the Capex screen alone); the in-place popover renders `CommentThread` out of CollabPanels, the very component the Collaborate tab uses, EXPORTED rather than copied so reply, resolve, reopen, edit and delete cannot drift. Nine screens carry a tab banner and six carry row markers, anchored to the unit the audit trail already names (`costLines[id=...]` is what the change log logs and what an override keys on). **A COMMENT ON A TAB IS NOT A COMMENT ON A FIELD**: `screen:<tabKey>`, explicitly not a path, resolved in pathScreen because that file already knows what a screen is; the tempting lie (`project.financing`) would file a remark against a real object it is not about. **A MODULE WITH NO SUB-TABS IS STILL A SCREEN**: wiring the banner surfaced that `screen:m6` resolved to NOTHING, so a comment raised on Scenario Analysis could never have been counted or shown again; found by checking what the anchor resolved to rather than trusting it would.
  - **THE WHOLE PICTURE, AND WHAT IS FOR YOU** (`c70232b0`): the Collaborate tab filters by SCREEN as well as person and version, through the same filing rule, so nothing exists in only one place; a comment the map CANNOT place is LISTED rather than hidden, because one nobody can find is worse than one filed oddly. **THERE IS NO NOTIFICATION LAYER ON THIS PLATFORM AND THIS DOES NOT INVENT ONE**: no email for a comment, nothing pushed, no per-item unread state. What exists is the last-seen stamp (mig 246), so "For you" answers, from rows already loaded and against the SAME held marker the unread badge uses, who replied to your threads and who resolved them. **Mentions are deliberately absent because there is no way to WRITE one**: a mention filter would match nothing and imply a feature that is not there.
  - **PROVED END TO END ON THE LIVE PROJECT, AS TWO REAL PEOPLE** (`9dadf018`, `scripts/probe-collab-loop-live.ts`, 29/0): the SERVER gate refuses the viewer who tries to comment and allows the same person once they are a reviewer; the reviewer raises it on a real asset; the map files it; the editor sees it in the field marker, the tab banner AND the sidebar count; replies (one thread, not two); resolves (leaves every open count, stays readable as resolved); the reviewer is told and the editor is not told about their own reply; it reads the same in the Collaborate tab with the reply not a separate entry. Comments on the project 2 to 0 afterwards: the probe leaves nothing behind and the elevated role is restored. **MY FIRST RUN PASSED 25/0 AND WAS NOT AN HONEST PROOF**: the only other member of Marina Gate is a VIEWER, the script called them "the reviewer" and inserted rows straight into the table, demonstrating the data path while bypassing the very gate that says a viewer may not comment.
  - **I BROKE THE PRODUCTION BUILD AGAIN, THE SAME WAY AS 03b7db27 TEN DAYS EARLIER** (`9dadf018` fixes `f8b9eba`/`c70232b`): the probe's row mapper omitted `edited` from `ProjectCommentDTO`. I ran the new script with `tsx`, which TRANSPILES and does not type check, saw 29/0 and committed; the `tsc --noEmit` I did run was BEFORE the file existed. A green script says the code ran, never that it compiles. [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 10.22.
  - **THE ACTIVITY LOG READS, AND THE PROOF IS ON STORED ROWS** (`5a7878e7`, `46a61608`): scalar leaves now carry a LABEL (they were the common case and the one kind the earlier pass never labelled, so most rows still showed a raw path); `stableStringify` stopped 49 rows on the live project reading "[15 items, unchanged] -> [15 items, unchanged]" (`JSON.stringify` follows INSERTION order, and jsonb normalises key order on storage, so a row existed because something changed while saying nothing had). Confirmed by running the save route's own chain against Marina Gate's real snapshot and reading the rows back OUT of the table, then deleting them: 810 to 808. **The first probe run PASSED its no-noise check having tested nothing**, looking for an object to re-order only under `fundTerms`, which this project has none of.
  - **THE SURFACE IS UNIFORM, NOT PARTIAL** (`d949dd36`): the banner went on the nine screens with inputs first, then on ALL 21 tabs the shell renders, because a reviewer reads the outputs too and "why is this number what it is" is asked on the Cash Flow and the Balance Sheet more often than on the inputs that drive them. The list comes from the SHELL'S OWN render conditions in the wiring AND in the check, so a tab added tomorrow is covered on the day it is added. Per-FIELD markers landed on Financials and Returns at the twelve `OverrideBadge path="..."` call sites, which already state the exact snapshot path of the field beside them, so the anchor costs nothing to derive and cannot be mistyped; the two components stay SEPARATE, since the badge means "differs from Management" and the marker means "there is something to discuss here".
  - **THE SESSION-END SUITE EARNED ITS KEEP, TWICE IN ONE RUN** (`d2b9913a`). First reading **177 pass / 4 fail of 181**; both extra failures were chased rather than waved through. `verify-project-membership` B1 was REAL: the unread-marker route added the day before POSTs and gates on the READ resolver. **The CODE was right and the CHECK needed re-aiming**: what it writes is the CALLER'S OWN marker and not one byte of the project, and gating it on `getProjectForWrite` would require a write permission, so a VIEWER could never mark a project as seen, which is precisely the person the marker is for. It is now a NAMED ALLOWANCE WITH AN ENFORCED OBLIGATION (B1b proves the route takes its user id from the session and not the body, writes only through `markSeen` for that id, and touches no project table; B1c keeps the list short), never a bare exemption. `verify-retail-companion` passed **61/0 on its own**, so it was an artefact of the run, reported rather than quietly dropped. Final: **179 pass / 2 fail of 181, 10,776 individual checks**, the standing pair only.
  - **THE BUILD CHECK IS MECHANICAL NOW** (founder's direction at session close, after the same production break twice in ten days): `.githooks/pre-commit` runs `npx tsc --noEmit` and REFUSES the commit on any type error, with `package.json`'s `prepare` script pointing `core.hooksPath` at it so a fresh clone installs it. **`tsconfig.json` already included `scripts/`**, so the config was never the problem: the missing part was WHEN the check ran, and both breaks came from a `tsc` that had passed BEFORE the new file existed, which is evidence about a tree that no longer exists. **Proved by committing a deliberate type error and watching the commit be refused**, then confirming no commit had been created. [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 10.24.
  - **AN APPLIER MAY NEVER COMMIT ITS OWN PROBE ROWS** (`cdf9cf3b`, `scripts/lib/migrationProbe.ts`, `verify-migration-appliers` 13/0): appliers 245 and 246 wrote proof rows and never undid them, so `--apply` committed THREE FICTIONAL ROWS into a real project's append-only audit log plus a future-dated `last_seen` that broke the unread marker for one person until the clock caught up. The dry run hid it completely, because a dry run rolls back everything including the mistake. `withProbes` rolls back in a `finally` and never RELEASES. **The verifier's first version asserted the MECHANISM and failed two appliers that are perfectly safe**, one day after the standing rule about that was written.

## 2026-09-22

- **THE FOUNDER'S EXPORT REVIEW, THIRTEEN ITEMS, PLUS THREE CORRECTIONS THE REVIEW ITSELF PRODUCED.** The PDF and the workbook were read against each other on the live project ("the figures agree, the statements tie and the balance sheet balances") and the gaps listed. Items 1 to 10 were presentation and are in their own commits (`0367414f` one terminal-value name from the screen's own list, `96c603ff` one meaning each for Equity Multiple and Debt / Equity, `415dbace` the fund fee base named for what it is, `fd2bcc1d` net leads gross everywhere, `2291d4ae` the RE Metrics tiles derived from the covenant series beneath them, `14009a66` the consolidated preview carrying every stage, `fbf45369` the scenarios report printing every section the workbook does, `79a8e4dc` the PDF front page rebuilt on the Project Dashboard's bands, `0990c9e6` costs filing under the same sections as revenue, `642be825` one project location label). Items 11 to 13 moved numbers and were measured on the live model before each commit, as instructed.
  - **DSCR IS MEASURED OVER OPERATING YEARS** (`cb25248e`): the window was 2027 to 2032, so construction years with no debt service scored on CFADS alone and the headline min came from a year with no debt service at all. Now 2031 to 2032. Min DSCR **-1.27x to 0.27x**, avg 1.07x to 0.78x, the count "1 of 3" to "1 of 2 operating yrs"; `operatingPerPeriod` is derived once on the snapshot so the covenant table, the tiles and the metric cannot disagree about which years are operating. **The covenant status did not change and the breach was always real; the NUMBER was not.** A briefing line calling it "a covenant breach that is not real" was repeated into the commit without checking and is corrected here.
  - **CAPITALISATION STOPS AT COMPLETION** (`6e6841e0`): `computeFacilitySchedule` gated capitalisation on construction SPEND alone, and two trailing marketing lines spent 11.14m and 14.79m in 2031 and 2032, after construction ended in 2030, so interest kept being capitalised and **nothing was expensed in the model's entire life**. Measured: IDC to asset basis 172.50 to 118.13 (**-54.37**), interest expensed **0.00 to 54.33**, cost of sales 1,045.51 to 1,007.87 (**-37.64**, the capitalised IDC inside the base released on sale), D&A 157.62 to 151.50, EBITDA 827.54 to 865.19, PAT 674.95 to **675.26 (+0.31)**. PAT barely moving is the point: capitalise-versus-expense is timing, not value.
  - **FCFE: ONLY A LENDER'S CLAIM AND A REQUIRED BALANCE TRAP CASH** (`70d96a7a`, corrected by `631fc208` and `af7e2e70` the same day). The reported defect was real: cash the sweep held back showed as paid to equity in 2030 and clawed back in 2031, 192.3m each way, worth 2.69 points of equity IRR. **The first fix deducted the whole movement in cash, which turned FCFE into the DIVIDEND stream** (identical to DDM to 0.00 in every period under BOTH repayment methods, 18.39% swept and 25.47% fixed), and it was shipped on the strength of an identity that was the collapse restating itself. See [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.56. The rule now: a sweep's claim (excess over the reserve, capped at the debt) plus the minimum cash reserve, which is a REQUIRED BALANCE and works like working capital, equity funded it and cannot take it out until release (founder's decision, correcting the same day's rule that an operating floor was free cash). Pure timing, so the lifetime FCFE total does not move. Live: equity IRR 21.07% before any of it, **18.39%** after; on a fixed-schedule copy FCFE **28.17%** against DDM 25.47%, differing by 192.31m, which is what proves the two streams are still different questions.
- **THE COVENANT READS SCHEDULED DEBT SERVICE, SWEEP EXCLUDED** (`8af98a93`, founder's direction after reading the DSCR numbers). A cash sweep is the surplus left AFTER debt service, so counting swept principal makes the ratio circular: it collapses toward 1.00x whatever the project earns and the harder a project sweeps the worse its coverage reads. On the live project every penny of principal is swept and nothing is contractually amortised, so 2031 was measured against 507.4m of "debt service" that was 462.9m of voluntary early repayment. **Min DSCR 0.28x to 3.23x, avg 0.82x to 11.31x, BREACH to PASS.** `scheduledDebtServiceCash` is computed ONCE in the financing engine (interest plus contractual principal, floored at the interest); `debtServiceCash` is unchanged and stays the cash statements' number, because total outflow is what a cash flow should show. Every surface already read `dscrPerPeriod`, so the RE Metrics tiles, the covenant table, the workbook and the PDF moved together. The Combined Debt Service table splits "of which repaid by cash sweep" and "of which scheduled amortisation" and prints "Scheduled Debt Service (covenant basis)" beneath the cash total, so early repayment stays visible. The row carries its basis through the existing `basisLabel` the LTV rows use, which leaves a user-edited covenant label alone. **AND WHERE DSCR EQUALS ICR, THE PAGE SAYS SO**: with no contractual amortisation both denominators are the interest, so the two rows print the same figure (3.23x / 19.39x) and `covenantBasisNote` is ONE sentence, read by the screen, the workbook and the PDF, whose second half appears only when that is true. `verify-covenants` 41/0.
- **MODULE 7 DESCRIBED THE WRONG SCHEME WHILE EVERY HEADLINE FIGURE WAS RIGHT** (`a242a8c3`, `c320117f`; [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.54b): the deck read STORED asset fields instead of the platform's own rules, so BUA read 2,970 against 89,380, land 11,000 against 37,000, and every asset was "1 unit". It now resolves through `resolveAssetAreaMetrics`, `computeAssetLandBreakdown` and `resolveAssetKeys` like every other surface, which makes `icReport.ts` a KEYS DOOR (below).
- **TWO VERIFIERS RE-AIMED AT THE RULE AFTER THE DAY'S SURFACE CHANGES** (`3b93f274`). The session-end suite read 175/4 of 179; two of the four were NOT the expected pair and both went red on the day's own work, which is the suite doing exactly the job the per-commit rule leaves it. `verify-capex-structure` P4q-e pinned the screen calling `assetCapexCategory`, but Table 6 now files by `assetCapexSection`, which IS `revenueSection` re-exported, because the founder's item was that capex disagreed with the P&L sections; re-aimed at the rule and not relaxed ([docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.55), with a new P4q-f asserting capex files by the revenue rule so the two cannot drift again (202/0). `verify-asset-type-standards` A1 caught `icReport.ts` reading `assetTypeId` / `assetTypeValues`, which is the Module 7 fix above; it joins `KEYS_DOORS` with its reason, and A1c then FORCES it to prove it calls the one rule and prices nothing from the values (125/0). Neither guard is weaker: one gained a check, the other gained an enforced obligation rather than an exemption. This is the second instance of the pattern the same file already records for `cc093d40`.
- **A COMMITTED SCRIPT REGENERATES THE REVIEW EXPORTS** (`f5c4abf9`, `scripts/regenerate-exports.ts`): the previous set came from an ad-hoc script that was deleted. It mirrors the Export modal's saved-version path rather than assembling the state a second way, and the first run proved why that matters: without the case-comparison bundle the Scenarios tab is an 18-row "no scenarios" stub and the workbook came out a sixth of its previous size. With it, 136 rows and all three cases. Parties come from `refm_parties`, and the IC deck falls back to a SEEDED deck when none is saved, which is this project's case.

- **A PROJECT CAN BE COPIED FROM ITS CARD, AND A COPY COSTS A SLOT (founder: "Add it to the project card on the Portfolio dashboard, so a user can copy a project and vary it").**
  - **THE ROUTE ALREADY EXISTED AND NOTHING CALLED IT**: `POST /api/refm/projects/[id]/duplicate` has been live since M1.6/3, with `duplicateProject` in `client.ts` wrapping it and ZERO callers anywhere in `src/` or `app/`. The API shipped and was unreachable, so a user who wanted a variant rebuilt the project by hand.
  - **WHAT A COPY CARRIES**: the source's current snapshot as version 1, so everything inside it comes across, the scenarios and the active case, the project's own asset type list and its values, the cost lines and overrides, the assets, sub-units, parcels, phases, financing tranches and equity. Plus the card metadata (location, status, `asset_mix`, `schema_version`). It is named `Copy of {original}` and the DUPLICATOR is the author, not the author of the source version.
  - **WHAT IT DOES NOT CARRY**: the version history, deliberately (one row, `version_number` 1), and anything kept outside the snapshot, which is the durable `refm_fund_terms` row, report decks and deck versions, report inputs, members, comments, the change log and delete requests.
  - **THE CAP GAP, FOUND BY WIRING IT**: the route gated lapse and ownership but never the project cap, which the create route has always checked. That did not matter while nothing could reach it; a Duplicate button on the card would have made it the way past a plan's project limit. Same check, same `CAP_REACHED` code, checked BEFORE the project is read, and the client maps it to the existing upgrade prompt.
  - **IT DOES NOT OPEN THE COPY**: the wizard opens what it just built because the user is mid-flow, but a duplicate starts from a card and someone copying a project to vary it may copy more than one, or rename first. The new card appears in the list.
  - `verify-project-duplicate` (new, live, read only: a guard check for a create must not send the payload, so it never duplicates a real project, it reads what a copy WOULD carry).

The roads and parks deduction was retired on 2026-09-08 (it modelled LAND development where this platform models VERTICAL development, and the area chain's Land Utilisation % states the same thing at asset level): `projectRoadsPct` is marked read by nothing, NO engine or resolver file touches `projectNdaEnabled` / `projectParksPct` / `hasNdaDeduction`, and the screens keep only historical markers recording where the card used to live. The assertions are inverted: an input the model does not read must NOT appear in a workbook that claims to be a copy of the model.

It now finds them BY HEADER NAME, because a fixed index turns any column edit into a false failure that is indistinguishable from a real one until somebody looks.
