Both rules predate the Module 1 restructure, so a plot whose area the CHAIN derives read zero, every host kept the gross plot its retail strip had carved from, and the strips themselves read no land at all.
  - **THE LAND TOTAL FOOTED THE WHOLE TIME, WHICH IS WHAT HID IT**: 37,000 sqm either way, while every per-asset row was wrong. This is the failure mode `verify-retail-companion` I2 exists for, reconciling PER ASSET rather than on the total.
  - **WHAT MOVES ON FMP - MARINA GATE**: built area **68,093 to 89,380 sqm**. Land 2 **0 to 11,329** (its area is entirely derived), Land 1 23,153 to 24,923, Land 4 14,470 to 22,658. Land per asset: Land 1 **11,000 to 10,472**, Land 2 5,000 to 4,760, Land 4 10,000 to 9,520, and the two retail strips **0 to 768 and 0 to 480**, the land their hosts gave up. Plot ratio was wrong on five of eight rows and undefined on both strips; it now reads 2.38 where the platform says 2.38. Land 3, Land 5 and Land 6 were already correct and do not move.
  - **ONE HELPER, THE PLATFORM'S OWN RULES**: `pdfAreaOf` calls `resolveAssetAreaMetrics` and `computeAssetLandBreakdown` with the SAME argument list the Assets tab and the workbook's Land & Area sheet use, so the three cannot disagree. The workbook's Land & Area sheet already matched the platform exactly and is untouched.
  - `verify-pdf-export` gains a guard: the report may not hand-roll area or land. Its 58 existing checks are structural and asserted no figure, which is why a 21,287 sqm divergence sat unnoticed.

- **THE INCOME STATEMENT READS THE ENGINE'S OWN PBT AND PAT, IT DOES NOT RE-DERIVE THEM (founder: the exports must be a faithful copy of the platform, and nothing in them computes its own total).**
  - **FOUND WHILE DIAGNOSING THE EXPORTS, AND IT WAS NOT AN EXPORT DEFECT AT ALL.** `buildPLRows` in `lib/reports/m4Reports.ts` re-derived the whole chain below EBITDA (`ebit - interest`, then `max(0, pbt) x rate`), and the `Gain on Disposal of Operating Assets` row added on 2026-09-14 was a DISPLAY row only: it printed immediately above PBT and nothing added it to PBT. Every surface that renders this builder understated PAT by the entire gain, and that is all three of them, the Module 4 screen, the Excel workbook and the PDF. The engine's `snap.pl.patPerPeriod` carries the gain, and the balance sheet's retained earnings, the indirect cash flow and Returns all read it, so the income statement disagreed with the balance sheet printed beside it.
  - **A SECOND TAX RULE WENT WITH IT**: the engine excludes the gain from tax unless `tax.applyToDisposalGain`, and the re-derivation taxed whatever PBT it had built, knowing nothing of that rule.
  - **WHAT MOVES ON THE LIVE MODEL (FMP - MARINA GATE)**: PBT **669,922,229 to 692,132,959** and PAT **652,740,937 to 674,951,668**, each short by exactly the **22,210,730** gain. EBITDA (827,544,797), EBIT (669,922,229), Tax (17,181,291) and every revenue line are unchanged, and the statement now foots against the Gain row it was already printing. The phase-level P&L is untouched: it returns above this point because D&A, interest and tax are project level.
  - `verify-m4-reports` 19 (the builder's own verifier), and `verify-excel-export`'s standing `P&L PAT == snapshot PAT` failure is closed, taking that file from 4 failures to 3.

- **A LOAD NEVER WRITES, AND A MIGRATION THAT RECORDS ITSELF APPLIED MUST NOT RUN AGAIN (founder: persist the migration, "but not as a side effect of opening a project. A load should never write"; and resolve the dirty-on-open cause).**
  - **THE MIGRATION ALREADY PERSISTS ON THE NEXT SAVE, and nothing had to be built for it.** The save door sends `extractPersistSnapshot()` at all four save sites, and that runs the same settle chain the load does, so what a save writes IS the migrated, settled model. The migration therefore settles the first time a user EDITS, never when they look, and no load-time write exists or was added. A save creates a NEW version, so the legacy version it came from stays as it was stored, which is what version history is for.
  - **THE DIRTY-ON-OPEN CAUSE WAS NOT THE STANDARDS SETTLE.** Measured stage by stage on FMP - MARINA GATE 1.0: the stored line reads 0 and the MIGRATION hands back 11,000, before any settle runs. `migrateM20costsPass7PerAsset` gates on SHAPE ("any master line, OR any override entry"), which is the shape Pass 10 restored the day after Pass 7 shipped and every save has written since, so it re-ran on every load of every project: it exploded each master into one replica per asset, folded each override's value into its replica, dropped `costOverrides` wholesale (taking `origin` with it, a field it predates), and Pass 10 rebuilt a master from the FIRST asset's replica, hoisting that asset's standard rate onto a line the user stored at zero.
  - **WORSE, AND FOUND BY THE SAME A/B**: on a phase with no VISIBLE asset Pass 7 drops every master line in it (`phaseAssets.length === 0`), the default seeder then finds the phase empty and re-seeds it from today's catalog. Measured with the fix stashed, that **replaced 36 stored cost lines across 6 of the 9 live versions** with catalog lines, on every open, silently: on `fe6c4b60` it was eight of the project's own (infrastructure, pre-operating, professional fee and commission, both phases), and XYZ, ABC, Jadan Maroom, Musoma and ORA EAST lost the same four on their phase 1.
  - **THE FIX IS THE MECHANISM THIS FILE ALREADY BUILT**: `migrationsApplied` carries `m20costs-pass7` on all nine live versions and `snapshotNeedsPass7Migration` already honoured it; only the migration that WRITES the marker ignored it. Honouring it makes the pair inert on a modern snapshot (no replicas, so Pass 10 finds no work), and the orphan case the gate also caught is covered by `repairProjectIntegrity` on every load.
  - **WHAT MOVES ON THE LIVE MODEL: no money, anywhere.** Marina Gate 1.0 revenue **2,162,708,165.20**, PAT **674,951,667.55** and capex base **1,045,509,080.41** are identical before and after. What moves is the dirt: its moved paths fall **61 to 20** (and all 20 are absent defaults being filled, no value rewrites left), the sum of its cost line values falls **30,460.10 to 400.00** as the hoisted rates go, and its overrides round-trip exactly (**78, of which 69 standard, to 75 of which 75 standard**: the old load was manufacturing nine origin-less overrides while losing six standard ones). `fe6c4b60` falls 145 to 103 moved paths and keeps its eight lines; the only removal left there is the documented country gate retiring two `rett` lines (`country` undefined against `requiresCountry` "Saudi Arabia", both at value 0, hidden and charging nothing).
  - `verify-load-route` 11 (was 9): **E2 fails if any stored cost line is dropped or replaced by a load**, with the country gate as the one allowed removal. Sabotage tested against the unfixed migration, where it reports **36 line(s) across 6 project(s)** and names them, which is how the scope above was measured.

- **THE SCHEMA BANNER REPORTS A VALUE THE USER STATED BEING REINTERPRETED, AND ONCE READ IT STAYS READ (founder: "Project updated to latest schema, please verify your inputs" "shows on every open"; two earlier attempts moved the ROUTE and both changed numbers, so neither shipped).**
  - **MEASURED FIRST, AND IT KILLED THE OBVIOUS FIX.** "Stop showing it when the load is a no-op" finds nothing, because there is no no-op load: every one of the nine stored versions genuinely migrates on open, moving 61 to 145 paths, and even a store built field by field moves 31. The banner was set by the BRANCH (every saved project takes the loose legacy route), but it was also telling the truth. It never cleared because **the migrated result is never written back**: the load migrates, says so, discards it, and the next open repeats it. `migrationsApplied` is the ledger meant to settle that and reads `["m20costs-pass7"]` on all nine versions with ZERO keys added by any load, because only that one pass ever calls `markApplied`.
  - **THE RULE IS WHAT KIND OF THING MOVED.** A field the snapshot never carried is a DEFAULT being supplied and says nothing; a value the user STATED being reinterpreted is worth reading about. Stated is the platform's existing vocabulary, not a new one: `CostLine.rateStated`, `SubUnit.priceStated`, and a cost override whose origin is not a standard. This is what `withoutDerivedOverrides` already does for scenario overrides, applied in the diff grammar instead of an override map (the two cannot share a function, so `isDerivedFill` says so in its own words).
  - **DECIDED AT THE LOAD, NOT IN THE MIGRATION** (`loadChangedTheModel` in `module1-sync.ts`, over the shared `snapshotMovedSomething`): the migration on its own drops the standard-origin cost overrides that the store's load puts straight back, so at that level it "changed something" on every live project while the model came back the same. One rule, asked at two levels.
  - **ONCE READ IT STAYS READ**: the Dismiss button existed but only cleared React state, so the notice returned on the next open. The dismissal is now remembered against the VERSION it was read on (`isSchemaNoticeDismissed` / `dismissSchemaNotice`, `refm_v2_schema_notice_<projectId>` holding that version id), so a different version, or a new save, says its piece again. Per browser, like every other key there: having read a notice is not part of the model.
  - **WHAT MOVES ON THE LIVE MODEL: no number, anywhere.** The route is untouched and the notice is a string. Of the nine stored versions, FMP - MARINA GATE 1.0 goes SILENT (61 moves, every one an unstated line rate the standards settle supplies), and the eight genuinely old-shape versions still speak, for real reasons: line renames (`Landscaping` to `Landscape`), method rewrites (`rate_per_nda` to `rate_x_landscape_area`), repaired wizard windows (`endPeriod` 25 to 4), removed lines, `landAllocationMode` `autoByBua` to `sqm`, and a tranche repaying `straight_line` where the migration says `equal_repayment`.
  - **WHAT A LOAD SETTLES STAYS SETTLED, 9 of 9**: load what the load produced and there is nothing left to say. That is the check that would have caught a banner nobody could clear, and it is why the notice is now self-limiting rather than permanent.
  - `verify-migration-banner` (new, 17, live). `verify-load-route` A1 re-aimed off the banner text onto the ROUTE, since which branch runs and whether it announces itself are now two questions (9, live).

- **THE PROJECT OVERVIEW READS LIKE A PROJECT, NOT A RETURNS STRIP (founder).**
  - **THREE PAIRS, NEVER FOUR LOOSE FIGURES**: Project (FCFF) IRR beside its MOIC, Equity (FCFE) beside its MOIC, Distributed (DDM) beside its MOIC. The distributed pair reads POST-FEE with the pre-fee pair beneath it where a fund layer exists, and as a single figure where there is none (the two streams are identical without a performance fee). On FMP - MARINA GATE: project 16.36% / 2.00x, equity 21.07% / 2.31x, distributed 17.14% / 2.68x after the fee and 18.37% / 2.96x before it.
  - **DSCR LEAVES THE DASHBOARD ENTIRELY**, minimum and average, and ICR with it (founder: "they belong on the financing surfaces, not here").
  - **COST PER SQM HAS ITS OWN BAND** under the hero (founder: "the first number a developer quotes and it is nowhere today"): development cost per GFA **12,617**, construction cost per GFA 10,352, development cost per saleable sqm 19,630 and revenue per saleable sqm 31,761 on the live project.
  - **PROJECT DETAIL**: total land area 37,000 sqm (240.00m), total GFA 105,940, BUA 89,380, saleable 68,093, plot ratio 2.86, 144 keys and 11,720 sqm let; land and build BY THE TYPES IN USE (Branded Villas 66.90%, 4 Star Hotel 20.27%, Standalone Commercial 9.46%, Retail Ground Floor 3.37%); the revenue mix by the Revenue tab's own sections; a phase table (construction years, operations start, lines, land, GFA, capex, revenue, EBITDA); and an exit and cash group (exit year, terminal value, gain on disposal, peak debt, and the cash low point with its year).
  - **ONE BUILDER** (`lib/reports/overviewReport.ts`) so the overview cannot hold its own definition of land, area or cost: areas from `resolveAssetAreaMetrics`, land from `computeAssetLandBreakdown` (a retail strip carries the land its hosts gave up, and files as Retail Ground Floor rather than under its host's type), revenue from `revenueBySection`, cost by phase from the capex report, peak debt and the cash low from the balance sheet. **A type keyed by its label joins the registered entry**, so an asset typed before the registry moved onto the project does not show its type twice.
  - **EVERY FIGURE TIES, proved live**: land by type to the scheme land, the shares to 100%, the revenue mix to P&L revenue, phase capex to total development cost (1,336.68m) and phase revenue to total revenue (2,162.71m).
  - `verify-project-overview` (new, 19, live).

- **STEP 10b: CONSTRUCTION COST ESCALATION (founder: one project rate, base year the project start, all rates base-year money).**
  - **THE RULE** (`src/core/calculations/costEscalation.ts`, pure): a rate typed anywhere (a Standards default, a Capex phase line, a per-asset override) is money of the PROJECT START YEAR, and escalates into the years its line actually spends. `project.costEscalationPct` is one annual whole percent for the project. **What does NOT escalate**: a `fixed` lump sum (a stated amount is already an amount), the two land VALUE lines and anything charged on land (a parcel's price is the deal, not a rate), and a line charged on revenue (revenue carries its own indexation). **A percentage line** (soft costs, contingency, developer fee) charges on the ESCALATED base and is never escalated again.
  - **THE TIMING IS THE LINE'S OWN SPEND PROFILE**, so escalation and phasing interact BY DESIGN (founder, asked and confirmed before the build): moving a line's spend later raises its cost at an unchanged rate, and a phase that starts later escalates further with nobody retyping a rate. The alternative (one factor per phase start) would price a 2033 payment in 2029 money. The engine escalates each period by its own year and the line total by the same profile-weighted factor, so the periods still sum to the total (TRAPS 7.31).
  - **THE SURFACES**: Types and Standards takes the rate beside the two lists, its rate column reads "Rate (<base year> money)" and the caption says the rates are base-year money that escalate into the years each line spends; Module 6 offers `costEscalationPct` as a curated lever.
  - **WHAT MOVES ON THE LIVE MODEL**: with no rate, or a rate of zero, every figure is identical to the stored model (escalation is inert until it is typed). At 4% a year on FMP - MARINA GATE: total development cost **1,336.68m to 1,415.26m**, cost of sales 1,045.51m to 1,104.41m, PAT **674.95m to 582.39m**, equity IRR **21.07% to 17.41%**, project IRR 16.36% to 14.25%, peak debt 593.65m to 629.83m, capitalised interest 172.50m to 186.63m. Phase 1 capex rises 4.5% and Phase 2 6.6%, which is the later phase escalating further at one rate.
  - `verify-cost-escalation` (new, 21): the pure rules and the exemptions, a zero rate byte-identical, phase 1 at the average of 1.00 and 1.04 and phase 2 (three years later) at the average of 1.04^2 and 1.04^3, every line's periods still summing to its total, a soft percentage on the escalated base exactly once, and the lever and the base year on screen.

- **STEP 10a: THE SCHEDULES SHOW THE DISPOSAL THE BALANCE SHEET BOOKED.**
  - **THE DEFECT**: the exit sells the held assets and the balance sheet takes their land, net book value and capitalised interest to zero in the exit year, but every fixed asset and capitalised interest SCHEDULE still showed the engine's own roll-forward. On the live project (its exit is the last axis year) the schedules carried **306.05m of fixed assets in 2038 (219.69m building, 54.36m land, 32.00m capitalised interest) where the balance sheet showed 0.00**, with no row to explain the difference.
  - **THE FIX**: `lib/reports/disposalSchedules.ts` runs the SAME `writeOffAtExit` the composer runs, and returns a schedule that foots: closing = opening + additions - depreciation - disposal. Every surface reads it: the Module 4 fixed asset roll-forwards (land, depreciable, per line and project total), the Financing capitalised interest schedule, the workbook's fixed asset schedules and IDC pool, and the PDF's fixed asset tables and IDC summaries. Each gains a "Disposed at exit" row, stops depreciating what was sold, and closes where the balance sheet closes. With no terminal value nothing is written off and every series is the engine's own.
  - **A DISPOSAL IN THE LAST YEAR IS STILL A DISPOSAL**: the first cut carried over the trading rule's guard (`stopAfterExit` has nothing to stop when the exit is the last year) and so left the live project's own case untouched. Only "nothing was sold" leaves a schedule alone.
  - **WHAT MOVED ON THE LIVE MODEL**: the 2038 closing balances fall to zero with a 306.05m disposal row beside them, and the schedules now tie to the balance sheet in every year, which the engine series did not. No engine figure changes: this is presentation of a booking that was already made.
  - `verify-terminal-disposal` 67 (E19 to E24 new: every schedule foots, the disposal is the exit year balance and only that year, the schedules close where the balance sheet does, nothing depreciates after the sale, a project with no terminal value is untouched, and all four surfaces read the one builder).

- **STEP 9: THE READER RESTRUCTURES.**
  - **WHAT STILL PRESENTED PER PLOT after the per-line pass, measured by surveying every surface after the assets tab**: the revenue basis and missing-downpayment advisories (workbook and PDF checks, and the reconciliation warning), the IC report's strategy mix (it counted plots), and the workbook's recognition profile tables (one per asset). Everything else was already by line or a total that names no plot. All three now read per consolidated line: the advisories pool their amounts (`poolRevenueBasisByLine`, `poolSaleCohortByLine` in `lib/reports/lineRows.ts`), the strategy mix counts lines, the recognition profile follows `lineHosts`.
  - **THE P&L REVENUE ROWS FILE BY CATEGORY, NEVER BY STRATEGY**: `revenueBySection` (`lib/reports/revenueSections.ts`) sums each asset's own revenue under `revenueSection`, the Revenue tab's filing rule, for the P&L headers (screen, PDF, workbook, every phase) and the revenue summaries in the workbook, the PDF and the IC schedule. A hotel still reads by department under its section. The engine's strategy buckets are untouched: Returns reads them as income from held assets (NOI, the terminal value).
  - **WHAT MOVED ON THE LIVE MODEL (no money)**: "Retail Revenue" 103.00m splits into Standalone Commercial Revenue 75.12m and Retail Ground Floor Revenue 27.88m, on the project and each phase P&L and in the three summaries; the IC strategy mix reads "2 Sell, 4 Lease, 1 Operate" (was 3 Sell, Land 1 + Land 2 being one line); the check advisories name "Phase 1, Branded Villas" rather than "Land 1, Branded Villas, Phase 1". Revenue 2,162.71m, PAT and equity IRR identical. **The latent case, measured on a copy**: a standalone commercial Lease line typed Branded Villas filed its 10.73m under Retail Revenue before and under Residential Revenue after, as the Revenue tab files it, with total revenue and returns unchanged.
  - **STILL BY STRATEGY, deliberately**: the P&L operating expense rows (a hotel's costs read from its operating statement).
  - **Module 1's "no further changes before launch" line in CLAUDE.md was stale** (Module 1 was reopened deliberately and rebuilt); corrected at the founder's direction.
  - **STANDING RULE (founder, 2026-09-16): never hold work for a background run.** The full suite reports, it does not gate.

## 2026-09-15

- **STEP 8: THE SCENARIO LEVERS (review gaps: no sales pace lever, occupancy out of the defaults, type prices and cost defaults inactive).**
  - **SALES PACE**: `assets[].revenue.sell.paceFactor`, absent on the base, read by the revenue resolver through `scaleSalesPace` (`core/calculations/revenue/scenarioLevers.ts`, pure): the factor stretches TIME on the line's cumulative sales curve, so 0.8 sells the same total later and 1.25 sooner, pre-sales staying before the first post-sales year. Offered on every asset that owns a revenue row, curated by default. **THE ROW SAYS WHAT IT DOES (founder)**: a slower pace can RAISE revenue and profit while LOWERING IRR and NPV, because the same units sell later at indexed prices with the cash arriving later (`leverNote`, shown as text under the label in Module 6).
  - **OCCUPANCY**: `assets[].revenue.operate|lease.occupancyShiftPts`, absent on the base, read through `shiftOccupancy`: every operating period moves by the stated points, held within 0 to 100%. Curated by default.
  - **TYPE PRICES AND COST DEFAULTS APPLY INSIDE A SCENARIO**: the load-time settle moved verbatim into `lib/state/settleModel.ts` (hydrate calls it), and every scenario model is built by `caseModelOf` (`lib/cases/caseModel.ts`: overrides applied, then settled) on every surface: a case switch, a grid edit, the comparison, the year-on-year report, the exports and the saved-model helper. Until this a type price or a cost standard overridden in a scenario changed nothing unless that scenario was the one the project opened on, and read 0.00 in the comparison column. Both are no longer gated inactive, and the used types' prices and construction standards are in the curated defaults.
  - **A DERIVED VALUE IS NEVER A SCENARIO OVERRIDE** (`withoutDerivedOverrides`): a type-priced row's price and a standard-origin capex override are dropped from every diff-built override map, so they re-derive from the inputs the scenario does override; resetting a type price returns the scenario to the base, and a base edit still reaches it (the TRAPS 7.47 shape).
  - **FOUND BY THE BUILD, ALSO FIXED: A SCENARIO'S COST WINDOW WAS OVERWRITTEN BY THE SETTLE.** A line carrying `windowFollowsConstruction` has its window re-derived, so once every case model settled, 22 window levers on the pipeline fixture moved nothing (61 of 120 fields moving on committed code, 40 of 122 with the settle); reopening a project with such a scenario active already reset them. `applyOverrides` now marks a window a case states as the user's (62 of 122 after, the difference being the two new levers).
  - **WHAT EACH MOVES ON THE LIVE MODEL** (FMP - MARINA GATE, base equity IRR 21.07%, each applied to every eligible asset as a scenario): type prices -10%: equity IRR -6.99 points, PAT -225.36m, revenue -216.27m, the LARGEST (every Table 5 row takes its type's price); cost defaults +10%: -4.78 points, capex +100.57m, PAT -121.61m; sales pace 0.8x: -4.31 points, NPV -46.63m, with revenue +69.56m and PAT +24.60m; occupancy -10 points: -1.09 points, PAT -72.67m. **No stored figure moves**: every live version opened and saved on the base and on each scenario is identical before and after (36 of 36).
  - `verify-scenario-levers` (new, 25, live). `verify-type-cost-standards` G7 and S12 now assert the two are live levers, and G12, S14, T8 and `verify-revenue-lines` I7 read the settle where it now lives.
- **STEP 7: A SOLD ASSET STOPS TRADING AFTER THE EXIT, AND THE FUND STOPS CHARGING (founder: an exit set before the last year kept trading).**
  - **THE DEFECT**: the exit sold the held assets (proceeds, gain, write-off, debt repaid) but their results ran on to the end of the axis. On a copy of FMP - MARINA GATE with the exit moved to 2035, the sold assets booked 195.99m of revenue, 112.24m of opex and 70.92m of profit in 2036 to 2038; per-asset rows carried 59.16m of depreciation after the sale; and on the verifier fixture the debt repaid at the exit still charged 3.0m and 1.98m of interest after it.
  - **THE FIX**: `stopAfterExit` (`core/calculations/returns/disposal.ts`, pure) zeroes an engine result's axis series after the exit year. The composer applies it to the Operate and Lease revenue and opex results where they are made, so AP, the P&L, both cash flows, the balance sheet, the Module 2 and 3 screens and Returns read one stopped series; per-asset depreciation and interest (charged and paid) stop too, and the funding gap reads cash interest from the direct cash flow so its waterfall still ties. The exit year trades in full; with the exit in the last year or no terminal value nothing is cut.
  - **FUND FEES STOP AT THE EXIT (founder: "the fund has sold what it managed")**: the frozen schedule's amounts, line totals and total are cut after the exit year before the solver, the fee bases left as measured. With trading stopped and fees still charged, the 2035 copy ended at -11.01m of closing cash.
  - **EXIT-YEAR CANDIDATES RUN ONLY TO THE CHOSEN EXIT (founder)**: later candidates were valued from a snapshot that has sold and stopped trading (2037 and 2038 read EV 0.00m); valuing them properly would need a second engine run for a number nobody can act on.
  - **WHAT MOVED, measured on the 2035 copy**: after the exit, revenue 195.99m, opex 112.24m, tax 1.82m and PAT 70.92m all to 0.00m; lifetime PAT 607.79m to 536.87m; dividends 932.24m to 881.32m; closing cash in the last year 20.00m to 0.00m. Terminal value 262.58m, project IRR 16.3708%, equity IRR 21.6389% and NPV unchanged, the balance sheet closing at 0.00 and every movement bridged. **Latent on every live model**: Marina Gate exits in its last year (2038, nothing moved) and no other live project has assets.
  - `verify-terminal-disposal` 61 (E6 to E18 new).
- **THE MANAGEMENT MODEL IS ONE MODEL: THE IC DECK, THE COMPARISON AND THE SCREEN READ THE SAME FIGURES (founder: "an investor document showing 22.36% equity IRR against 21.07% on screen is the worst failure on the list").**
  - **THE DEFECT**: the store kept its base copy as the base AS STORED, before the load-time settles, while the screen showed it after them. The IC deck read that copy as its Management case whenever the project was open (Marina Gate: equity IRR 22.3566%, PAT 705.29m, capex 1,312.2m against 21.0727%, 674.95m, 1,336.7m on screen), and did not follow base edits; opened with a scenario active, the comparison showed it as the Management and Upside columns; a save with a scenario active wrote every settle difference into the scenario as an override (69 on Marina Gate) and the unsettled base to the top level, pinning the scenario against later Types and Standards edits (base 12000, Downside stayed 11000).
  - **THE FIX**: `hydrate` settles the base first and keeps that as the copy, then applies a scenario over it and settles again; the deck takes its Management case through `managementModelOf` (`lib/cases/managementModel.ts`: the live model while the base is active, the copy while a scenario is).
  - **WHAT MOVED ON THE LIVE MODEL, measured before and after on every live version**: 24 figures identical (every base open, base save and scenario screen) and 12 moved, all of them what a save writes with a scenario active (Marina Gate 69 spurious overrides to 0, ABC and Jadan Maroom 1 to 0, and the top level written equal to a save on the base). The deck on Marina Gate moves from 22.3566% to 21.0727%. No stored data was lost: every live scenario is empty.
  - **FOUND BY THE FIX, AND ALSO FIXED: MODULE 6 PRICE LEVERS NEVER WORKED ON A LIVE PROJECT** (TRAPS 7.47). A scenario override on a Table 5 unit price was reset on every load and save: `unitPrice` is only the active one of a row's two stated prices, and a row priced from its type (`priceStated: false`) is re-priced from the type by the settle (Marina Gate Villa override 44,000 read back 40,000). The one check covering the lever, `verify-module6-scenarios`, passed only because its old-shape fixture had no stated prices AND the unsettled base copy let the settle backfill them from the override; fixing the copy turned it red. `applyOverrides` now marks a price-overridden row stated and writes a `unitPrice`-only override to the price its basis reads.
  - `verify-management-model` (new, 16, live): deck = screen = comparison Management on the base; the copy = the base with a scenario active; an empty scenario's save writes nothing; a Types and Standards edit reaches the scenario and prices it; a unit price lever survives a load and a save.
- **EVERY SURFACE AFTER THE ASSETS TAB PRESENTS BY LINE, NOT BY PLOT (founder: "Land 2 appears as its own row with BUA 0 taking a 13.31% IDC share, when it is part of the same Branded Villas line as Land 1").**
  - **THE RULE**: `lib/reports/lineRows.ts` groups every asset-keyed engine result by `planCapexSummaryLines`, the capex tables' own planner, so every surface names the same rows. Presentation only: the engine stays per asset, and an input typed on a line card (escrow, opex, fixed assets) is written to every plot on the line, as the capex and revenue line cards already do.
  - **POOLING**: amounts, series and matrices add; rates are struck on the pooled figures (hotel occupancy and ADR on room nights, lease occupancy and rent on area, per-line margin and yield on cost); a day count, a held percentage and a useful life read from the line's first plot. A capex input whose plots carry different rates prints the rate that reproduces the pooled amount and says "varies by plot".
  - **SURFACES**: IDC allocation (Marina Gate Phase 1 Branded Villas: Land 1 29.29% + Land 2 13.31% = one row at 42.60%), escrow, AP, fixed assets, opex inputs, the statement members and feeder rows, the disposal working, the IC report asset schedule, phasing and mix, the one-pager, and both exports (capex inputs and the capex sheet with pooled Land & Area bases, revenue configuration, revenue output with the cohort grid and vintage matrices, AR and unearned schedules, escrow, AP, fixed assets, per-line economics).
  - **LEFT PER PLOT, BY DECISION**: land funding (debt and equity are raised against a parcel) and the Module 1 plot and asset tables. Measured on the live Marina Gate workbook: the only cells still naming Land 2 alone are the plot table and the two assets-tab mirrors.
  - The Per-Tranche Debt title lost its em dash ("Per-Tranche Debt: Sweep & Outstanding").
- **STEP 6: A SAVED SCENARIO SURVIVES A SAVE AND REOPEN (founder: "Module 6 does not survive a save and reopen today").**
  - **THE DEFECT**: every saved project loads through the loose legacy route (saves carry no version marker, and the v7 fingerprint matches exact base line ids while saved ids carry a phase suffix), and that route's normaliser, `migrateLegacyToV8`, rebuilt the snapshot from a fixed field list that left out `cases` and `activeCaseId`. The store then seeded fresh default cases, so a saved scenario came back empty on every open. Measured on a copy of Marina Gate with a Downside override saved active: it reopened as Management active with zero overrides.
  - **THE FIX**: the normaliser carries the saved cases and active case through, verbatim.
  - **DECISION (founder, 2026-09-15): SAVED CASES COME BACK EXACTLY AS STORED.** This changes what loads for any project that ever saved scenario overrides: they now reappear. No live project holds an override today (every live case is the default, empty).
  - **THE PROOF**: a load census recorded every live project version (9 across 6 projects) and every fixture file carrying a model (3) BEFORE the change, raw snapshots included, then re-measured the SAME bytes after: **12 of 12 load identically**, full store load, engine money and cases alike. The old fixtures on the older route were compared one by one: `module1-v5` (the "Costs UI refined" route), `fmpReHubSnapshot` (the v7 route, with a Downside scenario of its own) and `marinaGateSnapshot` (legacy), each identical.
  - **MEASURED AND WITHHELD, the rest of the proposed step**: (1) making Pass 7 honour its own marker loaded only 1 of 12 identically: it zeroed Marina Gate's phase line values (11,000 superstructure to 0), dropped its 3 user marketing overrides, changed an override's window, restored 3 overrides on the RE HUB fixture and changed every empty project's lines; (2) sending a current snapshot to the v8 route loaded 11 of 12 identically: it added a zeroed `percentagesByPhase` to two Marina Gate assets and swapped the "latest schema" notice for another stale one ("Cost lines now carry Category + Driver"). Neither is shipped.
  - **THE FIVE EMPTY PROJECTS, reported, not decided**: XYZ, ORA East, ABC (two versions), Jadan Maroom (two versions) and Musoma store the retired catalog lines (infrastructure, pre-operating, professional fee, commission). On every load, Pass 7 drops a phase's lines when the phase has no assets and the seed re-fills it with the Types and Standards list (rett, engineering supervision, design consultancy, permits). The shipped fix leaves that exactly as it was; the withheld Pass 7 change would have kept their stored old lines instead. No money moves either way (no assets).
  - **FOUND ALONG THE WAY, separate**: saving while a SCENARIO is active rebuilds that case's overrides as a diff of the settled live model against the unsettled base snapshot the store kept on hydrate, so every load-time settle difference is written into the scenario as an override. The fixture above picked up entries for the default cost overrides and the standards list that way.
  - `verify-load-route` (new): the saved project takes the legacy route, migration and the full load return the stored cases exactly, a second save and reopen changes nothing, the reopened scenario applies its override, and every live version's stored cases come back exactly.
- **REVIEW FIXES, STEPS 1 TO 5 (founder: "a client's exported PDF not matching the screen is the worst defect in this list").**
  - **5. A SAVED VERSION READS THROUGH THE STORE.** `loadStoredModel` (`lib/state/loadStoredModel.ts`) migrates, hydrates a fresh store with the base case active and extracts, restoring the stored active case id. The Portfolio dashboard route and the saved-version branch of the export dialog now call it; both ran on migration output alone, which stops before the store's load steps and dropped the 69 Types and Standards default overrides on the live project (Portfolio: development cost 1,313.13m against the screen's 1,337.57m, equity IRR 20% against 19%; a saved-version export: profit after tax 710.9m against 680.6m). `verify-stored-model-load` (new): the helper keeps every saved override, the base case is top level, no reader outside the load path calls migration alone, and every live project's portfolio figures equal the screen's.
  - **4. A SCENARIO WITH NO OVERRIDES SAYS SO**: `caseOverridesNote` reads "no overrides: same as Management" on the Module 5 and Module 6 case columns, the workbook Scenarios tab and the PDF, so identical columns are not read as three views.
  - **2. EXCEL AND PDF RETURNS**: the PDF's Returns Assumptions labelled a cap rate method "Exit multiple" with the exit multiple "applied" (it tested perpetuity or not); it now names the method and prints only its input. The Assumptions and Returns sheets print only the input the terminal value method uses (cap rate with its derived or typed source under the cap rate method; the exit multiple and perpetuity growth only under theirs). The dividend-basis row reads "(+) Terminal Equity Value (not already paid as a dividend)", since it is zero whenever the exit dividend distributed the equity, on every surface that shares the builder.
  - **3. DOCS**: two stale type comments in `financials-resolvers` (the disposal gain was described as "zeros today"; a cost of sales comment sat on the gain), the CLAUDE.md balance sheet claim re-measured (0.00), and the em dashes removed from CLAUDE-ROUTES.
  - **1. VERIFIERS**: `verify-tab3-default-seed` reads the seed from the one phase builder; `verify-land-chain` U66 normalises line endings (the tab source is CRLF); `verify-ai-grounding` asserts the external claim is caught without requiring the figure to escape the rounding match.
- **THE PORTFOLIO CARD LISTS THE PROJECT'S ASSET TYPES ONCE EACH** (founder: Marina Gate read "Branded Villas, Branded Villas, Branded Villas - Retail, Retail, ... +2 more"). The card and the project list read `refm_projects.asset_mix`, saved on every save, and it was built in TWO places (`computeAssetMix` in `module1-sync.ts` for the three save paths, an inline copy in `RealEstatePlatform.tsx` for create), both one entry per visible asset from `Asset.name`, which was retired on 2026-09-10 and is read by nothing else. Both are deleted; `projectAssetTypeMix` (`lib/state/assetTypeMix.ts`) is the ONE computation: the types a visible asset uses, once each, in Types and Standards order, through `standardTypeIdFor` (a strip is ground-floor retail); a listed type with no asset is left out, and an asset whose type is not on the list keeps its own type text after the listed ones. **Marina Gate's stored list was rewritten once** from its current version, so the card corrected without waiting for a save. `verify-asset-type-mix` (new: the rule, one computation, every live project's saved list against the rule) and `verify-asset-label` C0/C0b (the rename proof now covers the saved list, which it missed).
- **A SOFT COST'S BASIS IS PICKED BY LINE ON TYPES AND STANDARDS** (founder: "the basis is one rule, chosen once in Standards, rather than a basket here and a line list there"): the two-basket dropdown (hard cost, hard and soft costs) is replaced by the line selection Capex uses. `CostStandardRow.baseIds` stores the picked lines as IDENTITIES, so one pick reaches every phase: each phase selects its own lines of those identities above the percentage (`derivedSelection`, the positional rule the engine enforces). The tab offers the construction list (type rows as one construction line), the soft rows above and any line a phase carries above it (`baseOptionsFor`), never land, selling or anything below; the basis reads as the lines picked (`rowBasisLabel`), beside the rate and in Capex's "From Types and Standards" note. A phase that picks its own lines in Capex still keeps them. A row nobody has picked for keeps its old basket, ticked as it selects, so loading moves nothing. `verify-type-cost-standards` with new section V.
- **TWO PRICE COLUMNS, NOT FOUR** (founder: "four columns where most cells are dimmed is not clean ... the rate column carries its own unit per row"): the construction list's type rows carry a **price per unit** and a **price per sqm** (`AssetTypeValues.pricePerUnit` / `pricePerSqm`), and `typePriceColumns` reads them by the type's strategy: a Sell type as sale prices, an Operate type's per-unit price as the ADR per key night, a Lease type's per-sqm price as the rent per sqm per year. The unit label sits under each cell; a column the strategy does not use shows a dash. **The Table 5 default follows the same rule** (`typePricesFor`), so a figure the tab does not show can never price a row; a type with no strategy prices each row by its category. The four first-cut fields fold into the two on load and save (`settleTypePriceFields`, settles). `verify-type-cost-standards` with new section T.
- **"USE TYPE PRICE" ON TABLE 5** (founder: "once a row is typed there is no way back, so a mistyped price detaches that row from its type permanently"): both Table 5 price cells (the table and the drawer row) show the control on a row whose price was typed, where its type states a price for that row's category; it clears `SubUnit.priceStated`, the settle re-prices the row from the type, and later type edits reach it again. The title names the type and its prices. `typePriceDefaultFor` (`subUnitPriceDefaults.ts`) decides both whether it shows and what it offers, from the same category rule as the settle. Locked in view mode. `verify-type-cost-standards` 107/0 with new section U. **LOGGED SEPARATELY** (CLAUDE-TODO 0h): a scenario cannot vary a price at type level, only row by row.
- **A TYPE STATES ITS SALE PRICES, ADR AND LEASE RATE, AND TABLE 5 TAKES THEM (founder: "if our asset doesn't have multiple sub-units table 5 will get these prices and the user can override there").**
  - **Types and Standards**: the construction list is now "Construction cost, sale price and ADR", and each type row carries a sale price per unit, a sale price per sqm, an ADR per key night and a lease rate per sqm per year (`AssetTypeValues.salePricePerUnit` / `salePricePerSqm` / `adrPerKeyNight` / `leaseRatePerSqmYear`). A column that does not fit the type's strategy is dimmed, never hidden.
  - **`SubUnit.priceStated`** says whether a row's price is the user's: every Table 5 price cell (the rate, the other basis, the drawer's rate) sets it; on load a row with no marker counts as stated when it carries any positive price.
  - **`settleSubUnitPriceDefaults`** (`lib/state/subUnitPriceDefaults.ts`) prices every UNSTATED row from its type by category (Sellable both sale prices, Operable the ADR, Leasable the lease rate; a blank type price is 0), resolving the type through `standardTypeIdFor` so a retail strip prices as ground-floor retail. Support rows and companion mirror rows are left alone. It runs before `settleSubUnitPrices` on load, on save and after every edit (the sub-unit actions now go through `setAndSettle`), and settles. A metric switch is not a statement.
  - **Scope, stated**: the default applies to every row with no price of its own, a single row or several; a line with several rows starts every row at the type's price and each is overridden on Table 5.
  - **Module 6**: the four type prices are shown inactive (a scenario does not re-run the store step; the sub-unit price is the dial); the marker is structural.
  - **MEASURED on the live model, before and after on the same probe**: all 7 Marina Gate rows load as stated, no price moves, revenue 1,667,766,027.74 and profit after tax 260,398,748.69 identical. No live type states a price yet.
  - `verify-type-cost-standards` 102/0 with new section S; `verify-revenue-lines` 114, `verify-strategy-switch` 84, `verify-retail-companion` 61, `verify-asset-type-standards` 124, `verify-land-chain` 229, `verify-capex-structure` 201, `verify-new-project-defaults` 97, `verify-view-lock` 59, `verify-snapshot-field-survival` 27, `verify-project-integrity` 20, all green.
- **CAPEX INPUTS OPEN BY DEFAULT** (founder): each line's cost section on the Capex inputs starts expanded (it started collapsed from 2026-05-13); the header still collapses it.
- **ONE WAY TO BUILD A PHASE'S COST LINES, AND PERCENTAGES AT TWO DECIMALS (founder: "two ways to build a phase's lines is how they drift apart"; "marketing showing rounded at 4% in capex input").**
  - **`buildPhaseCostLines`** (`lib/state/costStandards.ts`) is now the only builder: the first asset of a new phase, the new project wizard, the store's empty default, the load-time seed for a phase with no lines, the legacy snapshot seed and the reset all call it. It produces the standards list in order (land, the transfer tax, the Construction Cost list, the Soft Costs list); a project with no stored list takes the shipped one. `makeBlankCostLines` survives only as the source of the two locked land lines.
  - **THE STRATEGY REVIEW IGNORES DEFAULTS**: `assetHasStrategyAssumptions` counted every override on an asset as the user's assumption, so a new asset carrying Types and Standards defaults raised the review banner on its first strategy pick. Standard-sourced overrides are now excluded.
  - **PERCENTAGES SHOW TWO DECIMALS**: a percentage method's value on the Capex inputs, and the soft cost rates, phase rates, utilisation, coverage and service percentages on Types and Standards.
  - **FOUND, NOT CHANGED**: a loose legacy snapshot with NO assets loses its project cost lines in the legacy conversion and is re-seeded. It predates today; `verify-cost-catalog`'s end-to-end phasing-source check was passing only because the old catalog seed carried a commission line of its own, and it now hydrates a snapshot with an asset, where the line and its restored source survive.
  - **MEASURED on the live model**: load unchanged (development cost 824,512,770.28, 22 lines); a new phase added there gets exactly the twelve standard lines in order and its villa takes every default.
  - Verifiers re-aimed from the old seed to the builder: `verify-new-project-defaults` A19 to A21 (97/0), `verify-capex-structure` wizard check (201/0); `verify-strategy-switch` 84/0; `verify-type-cost-standards` 88/0 with new section O.
- **FOUR FIXES TO THE STANDARDS WORK (founder).**
  - **ONE TYPE LOOKUP**: the cost standards defaults (`standardTypeIdFor`), the plot fill-back (`planTypeMassingWriteBack`) and the store's edit path now resolve an asset's type with `resolveAssetTypeKey`, the lookup table 4's merge uses (the type id, else the type label). Land 2 carried the label "Branded Villas" and no type id, so it merged into the villas line while those two read the id alone and skipped it. A retail strip, which carries its hosts' label, still prices as ground-floor retail.
  - **NO POINTERS TO NOTHING**: `repairProjectIntegrity` now drops a selection's reference to a line that no longer exists (`dangling-selection`), on load and save like every other dangling reference; the Phase 1 developer fee and contingency no longer name the deleted marketing and commission lines.
  - **RESET PRODUCES EXACTLY THE LIST**: `planCapexReset` builds each phase from the standards lists, not the catalog seed: the land lines, then the transfer tax, then the Construction Cost list in its order (one superstructure line, parking, one landscape line), then the Soft Costs list in its order (engineering, design, permits, developer fee, contingency, marketing). The catalog extras (infrastructure, pre-operating, professional fee, commission) are gone, and each soft line charges on its stated basis.
  - **THE TEXT**: Types and Standards no longer says type names wait for Save; both halves have saved as you type since 2026-09-10, and the one explicit save is "Save this list to the firm's template".
  - **MEASURED on the live model**: load unchanged (development cost 824,512,770.28); Land 2 prices as Branded Villas; no line names a deleted line; the villas type's filled-back values are unchanged (utilisation 100, coverage 60, service 20). A reset now lists the twelve standard items in both phases and prices Land 2 at 11,000, so the reset's development cost is 1,015,904,339.67 (it was 892,536,841.42 while Land 2 took no default); Undo restores 824,512,770.28.
- **A SOFT COST'S BASIS IS STATED WITH ITS PERCENTAGE ON TYPES AND STANDARDS (founder: "stated together, not selected line by line in Capex; keep the Capex selection as an override for a phase that genuinely differs").**
  - **THE ROW STATES IT** (`CostStandardRow.chargesOn`: '% of hard cost' or '% of hard and soft costs'; shipped as hard for engineering, design and permits, hard and soft for developer fee and contingency; a selector beside the percentage, and on an added soft row). **The settle derives every matching phase line's selection from it** (`derivedSelection`: the lines above it in the phase, never land or a selling cost, hard only or hard and soft), so a line created later is picked up on its own and the same selection is never made twice.
  - **A PHASE CAN STILL PICK ITS OWN** (`CostLine.selectionStated`, set when lines are picked in Capex, cleared by "Use Types and Standards" in the picker, which now says where its basis comes from). On load, a selection that already matches the basis takes the basis and one that differs stays the phase's own, ignoring references to lines that no longer exist, so opening a project moves no number.
  - **MEASURED on the live model**: development cost 824,512,770.28 unchanged on load. The Phase 1 developer fee and contingency named a deleted marketing and commission line; those dead references charge nothing and are not treated as a difference.
  - Verifiers: `verify-type-cost-standards` rewritten section B.
- **RESET TO TYPES AND STANDARDS, THE EXIT WORKING ON RETURNS, AND FOUR TABLE FIXES (founder: "refresh the capex table so it gets all the capex lines and input numbers as per the standard, so I can test what a new project gets"; "a working in the Returns where we calculate the terminal value and then the gain or loss").**
  - **CAPEX: RESET TO TYPES AND STANDARDS** (`planCapexReset` in `lib/state/costStandards.ts`, store `resetCapexToStandards` and `setCapexState`, both under the view lock): a card on the Capex inputs rebuilds every phase's lines the way a new phase seeds them (the standard catalog at no rate of its own), removing rates typed on those phase lines, per-asset overrides and added lines there; land lines and asset-targeted lines stay. The settle then sees unpriced phases, creates the rest of the list and gives every asset its default. Two clicks to run, and Undo puts the previous lines and overrides back exactly.
  - **A CREATED LINE JOINS THE PERCENTAGES BELOW IT** (found on the live reset before commit): the seeded developer fee and contingency select the lines that existed at seeding, so the engineering, design and permits lines the list creates were charged on by nothing. A created hard line now joins every soft percentage below it, a created soft line the developer fee and contingency.
  - **TYPES AND STANDARDS**: the Construction Cost list shows only the asset types this project lists, in its order (a shipped default for an unlisted type stays stored and applies when the type is added); developer fee and contingency read "% of hard and soft costs"; every header on the tab is centred both ways.
  - **RETURNS: THE EXIT, WORKED THROUGH** (`lib/reports/disposalReport.ts`, rendered at the foot of the Returns tab before the sensitivity): the terminal value from the basis year's hospitality EBITDA and retail NOI and the method, the net book value disposed (building, land, capitalised interest, and per held asset), the gain or loss, zakat on it or its exclusion, debt repaid, and where each figure is booked, with a check row. Every number is read from `snap.disposal`, so the working moves with the basis, method and cap rate.
  - **MEASURED on the live model**: the working reads 2037 hotel EBITDA 15,234,671.27 plus retail NOI 24,622,364.40 = 39,857,035.67 at 8.5% = 468,906,302.05, less 245,372,349.50 = gain 223,533,952.55, check 0.00. The reset puts every standard line in both phases (development cost 892,536,841.42 with the selection fix, 887,422,219.93 without it) and Undo restores 824,512,770.28 with identical lines and overrides. On that reset, Land 2 gets no superstructure default because it has no asset type, and the hotel none because its row is blank.
  - Verifiers: `verify-type-cost-standards` 64/0, `verify-terminal-disposal` 48/0.
- **COST STANDARDS REBUILT AS THE REFERENCE MODEL'S TWO LISTS (founder: "a short list of asset types with their construction rate, and the other rates and percentages entered once for the project").** Replaces the per-type grid of 2026-09-14, which became unreadable at ten or fifteen types.
  - **CONSTRUCTION COST**: one row per asset type (the superstructure rate), then Parking, Landscape and Villas Landscape as rows of the same list; Villas Landscape is a row SCOPED to the villa types, so the reference's villa landscape rate survives with no per-type column. **SOFT COSTS**: Engineering Supervision, Design Consultancy, Permits and Approvals, Developer Fee, Contingency, RETT and Marketing, one percentage each. Shipped with the reference figures (`seedCostStandardRows`, stored on first load as `project.costStandardRows`, change-logged by row id; an emptied list is never reseeded). A project that carried the morning's per-type rates keeps them as type rows and type-scoped rows.
  - **THEY ARE DEFAULTS, ON ONE PRECEDENCE RULE** (`lib/state/costStandards.ts`): the user's own Capex override, then a rate typed on the PHASE LINE (`CostLine.rateStated`, set by typing on the line, a typed 0 included, and on load for older lines when the value is not zero), then the row's rate for that phase (a phase exception on the tab), then the most specific row (type row, then a type-scoped row, then a row for all assets). A line with none stays blank. The engine is unchanged: defaults reach it as `origin: 'standard'` overrides. Capex shows "From Types and Standards" and a "Use the Types and Standards default" link that clears a phase line's own rate.
  - **ROWS CREATE LINES**: a phase nobody has priced takes the whole list; a phase someone has priced gets a new line only from a row the user adds or edits, so seeding the list onto an existing project adds no cost line. A row added on the tab (name plus basis) creates its Capex line in every phase at once, placed by stage, a soft percentage charging the construction lines above it.
  - **A RETAIL STRIP'S SEED FOLLOWS ITS LINE** (`followStripSeeds`, found by the live probe before commit): the strip's construction override is seeded at the line rate, and it did not move when the line changed, so clearing Phase 2's line left the strip on 5,800 and the settle read the stale copy as the user's own. It now follows the line, and a cleared line hands it the ground-floor default on its retail basis.
  - **MEASURED on the live model**: load seeds 20 rows, all 18 unlocked lines state their own rate, no line is added, and development cost 824,512,770.28, lifetime PAT 440,424,344.50 and FCFE IRR 11.6885% are unchanged to the cent. Clearing Phase 2's superstructure line puts the villas on 11,000, Standalone Commercial on 3,000 and the strip on 2,200; the 4 Star Hotel row is blank in the list, so the hotel falls to zero (development cost 774,400,215.18); typing 5,800 back restores every figure exactly.
  - **NOT IN THIS CHANGE, logged as its own item**: construction cost escalation (the reference escalates construction 5% a year from a 2027 base year; the platform escalates only sale prices, ADR and rents).
  - Verifiers: `verify-type-cost-standards` 51/0 (rewritten), `verify-asset-type-standards` 124/0, `verify-capex-structure` 201/0, `verify-retail-companion` 61/0, `verify-module6-pipeline` 34/0.
## 2026-09-14

- **THE EXIT IS A DISPOSAL (founder: "build it, in your order"), matching the reference model.** Diagnosed first the same day: the terminal value existed only inside the Returns streams, nothing reached the P&L, the cash flow or the balance sheet, and the metric was the higher of the exit year's NOI and the best year before it.
  - **ONE TERMINAL VALUE, COMPUTED ONCE** (`src/core/calculations/returns/disposal.ts`, pure): `valueAtExit` capitalises the income of a stated BASIS year, `project.returns.terminalValueBasis` = 'prior_year' (default, the reference) or 'exit_year'; the max-to-date behaviour is RETIRED. The financials composer books it as proceeds and the stream builder values every candidate exit with the same call, so the proceeds and the Returns terminal value are one number (verified to the cent). Stabilised NOI is now the income capitalised, so the cap rate at exit reads back the cap rate used.
  - **THE LAND SPLIT, REPORTED BEFORE RELYING ON IT**: the fixed asset balance holds only visible Operate and Lease assets (`isDepreciableAsset`), each with its OWN land lines (cash and in-kind, the retail carve applied) plus its building and capitalised interest. On the live model the five held assets (the hotel, two Standalone Commercial plots, two retail strips) sum to the balance sheet to the cent, and no Sell asset carries any, so the disposal is the whole fixed asset balance.
  - **THE STATEMENTS**: P&L "Gain on Disposal of Operating Assets" (proceeds less building, land and capitalised interest at the exit) below interest and above zakat; zakat EXCLUDES the gain by default (`project.tax.applyToDisposalGain`, a toggle beside the rate on the P&L tab); investing cash carries "Proceeds from Disposal of Operating Assets"; the indirect method backs the gain out of operations; debt outstanding at the exit is repaid from the proceeds; fixed assets are zero from the exit year and no depreciation arises on them after it; the exit dividend pays the proceeds out. A terminal method of 'none' books no disposal.
  - **RETURNS COUNTS IT ONCE**: the streams read investing cash BEFORE the proceeds and add the terminal value themselves; the distributed-equity stream, its build-up row and the partner split add no terminal equity when the exit dividend already paid it.
  - **SURFACES**: the P&L, both cash flow builders and the balance sheet rows (which now read the written-off balances), the PDF summary P&L and the IC report income statement; the Returns assumptions carry the basis.
  - **MEASURED on the live model (exit 2038, cap rate 8.5%)**: terminal value 487,662,554.13 to 468,906,302.05 (2037 hotel EBITDA plus retail NOI, 39,857,035.67); gain 223,533,952.55; profit before tax 24,360,138.44 to 247,894,091.00 with zakat unchanged at 609,003.46; profit after tax 23,751,134.98 to 247,285,087.54; fixed assets at exit 245,372,349.50 to 0.00; exit dividend 57,724,829.35 to 526,631,131.40; balance sheet difference and unexplained both 0.00; direct and indirect cash close together. FCFF IRR 11.0874% to 10.8924%, FCFE IRR 11.9861% to 11.6885%, distributed-equity IRR 11.2635% to 10.9768%, yield on cost 5.0274% to 4.8340%, cap rate at exit 8.5000% both. The IRRs fall only because the basis moved from the 2038 income to the 2037 income.
  - **NOT DONE, stated**: after an exit set BEFORE the last axis year, the held assets' revenue, opex and the engine's interest still run in the statements (Returns stops at the exit, and the live exit is the last year, so nothing live is affected); the fixed asset and capitalised interest roll-forward schedules (Fixed Assets tab, Financing IDC table and their Excel and PDF copies) still show the engine's roll-forward without a disposal row; the Excel live-formula twin (`liveModel.ts`) keeps its own terminal value.
  - Verifiers: `verify-terminal-disposal` 40/0 (new); `verify-returns-snapshot` re-aimed (CFI before proceeds, prior-year perpetuity metric, cap rate on capitalised income, the dividend build-up's own terminal row); `verify-returns-engine` development spread re-aimed; `verify-report-arithmetic` counts the gain row; `verify-module6-field-census` declares the basis domain.
- **ROUND TWO ON THE STANDARDS, REVENUE, OPEX AND THE WORKSPACE (founder: "as 1 table ... construction costs, parking, commercial, landscape costs and all list of soft costs ... by the category and strategy").**
  - **ONE COST TABLE, ROWS ARE ITEMS, COLUMNS ARE TYPES** (`standardCostRows`): hard costs (superstructure, parking, infrastructure, landscape), soft costs (design, engineering, permits, project management, professional fee, pre-operating, developer fee, contingency), land charges (transfer tax) and selling costs (commission, marketing), from the built-in catalog plus any identity the project's own lines carry. A selling cost reads n/a for a type whose strategy does not sell.
  - **THE TABLE FILLS THE CAPEX TAB**: a rate for an item a phase has no line for MINTS the line from the catalog at its catalog position with a zero master rate (a percentage charging the construction lines above it, developer fee and contingency the soft lines too), then the standard override carries the type's rate. **Two defects found by the live probe and fixed before commit**: the edit path stored the overrides but not the minted lines, and a live phase 2 line (`commission__phase_2`, named Marketing, stage marketing) read as commission, so a marketing standard minted a second marketing line beside it. `standardIdentity` now reads a marketing-stage line as marketing, and the Revenue tab names selling costs by the same rule.
  - **ONE PHASING CURVE PER PHASE ON THE STANDARDS TAB**, through the Capex tab's own control, written to every visible asset of the phase; a line can still take its own curve on Capex.

## 2026-09-12

- **THE LINE IS WHAT A USER SEES, A PLOT THAT CARRIES ASSETS REFUSES TO BE DELETED, AND EVERY LOAD CHECKS ITS OWN REFERENCES. `verify-capex-report` 22 -> 28/0, `verify-asset-label` 35/6 -> 43/0, `verify-consolidated-view` 19/2 -> 27/0, `verify-project-integrity` 13/0 (NEW), `verify-capex-structure` 156 -> 161/0, all with credentials; sixteen sabotages caught across the day.**
  - **TABLE 1 IS THE LINE'S SCHEDULE, WITH ITS PLOTS INSIDE IT.** It was one block per plot in ASSET ORDER, so a line's plots sat apart with a retail companion and another phase's plots between them. It runs through `planCapexSummaryLines` now, the planner tables 2 to 4 and both exports already shared. The first cut headed only the blocks whose line held more than one plot, which on the live projects is ONE LINE IN THIRTEEN, so the tab still read plot by plot; the founder's call was that the line heads EVERY block, and it does. What stays conditional is the plot subtotal, because two adjacent rows carrying the same number say nothing. **THE NESTED HEADING IS THE PLOT, NOT THE WHOLE LABEL**: the line already states the phase and the type, so the full label under it prints "Phase 2: Branded Residences" over "Phase 2, Branded Residences", the same words twice with a different separator, on EVERY block of FMP RE HUB, whose assets all draw from a sentinel. `assetPlotLabel` is the plot half of the label, beside `assetLabel` and reading the same sentinel rule. **147 numeric rows before and after, an identical multiset; only headings changed, 16 to 21.**
  - **THE COSTS CAPTION WAS A FOURTH SURFACE INVERTING THE TWO OUTER TIERS.** 06aded4c corrected the picker labels, the tooltips and the export basis column; `costLineCaption` in core was not touched, so the screen printed "sqm Total GFA" for `rate_per_gfa` while the picker one cell away called the same method "Rate x Total BUA" and charged the outermost tier. **16 captions change, both directions, and not one figure moves.** Proved by arithmetic on the assets rather than the field name: Marina Gate's Land 1 is nsa 22,440.50 + support 1,400.00 = bua 23,840.50, + parking 2,800.00 = gfa 26,640.50, and the caption printed 26,641 under the name the tab gives the tier without parking. It kept pointing at a plot that no longer existed; `assetLabel` fell back to the phase, and the row stopped reading "Land 7, High End Apartments" and started reading "Phase 1, High End Apartments", which is a row the founder had deleted appearing under a new name on a tab they had not touched. **The answer is a refusal, not a cascade**: deleting the assets with the plot destroys sub-units, costs and revenue nobody asked to lose, and clearing their plot silently unmoors them from their land. `removeParcel` returns a `ParcelRemoval` rather than void, because a refusal a caller cannot see is a silent lock. The button also declares `data-view-mutates="true"`, which it never did.

Proved end to end, not on the pure function: open a live project and save it with no edit, then diff. RE HUB 0 changes before and after; Marina Gate 2 before and 3 after, the one addition being the repair itself. (The 2 already there are `cases[case_downside]` and `cases[case_upside]` auto-seeded by `normaliseCases`, a separate dirty-on-open, reported not fixed.)
