Registered in the five places, P4 widened, `AssetAreaMetrics.retailGfa` added and pooled. **THE PICKER NOW FOLLOWS TABLE 4** (`COST_METHODS` reordered: land and footprint, floor area outermost in, units and parking, the total; P4j reads the tab's own header row and fails on a step out of order). **A strip on a chain-only method says so** (caption: '0 on a retail strip, which has no chain of its own; its floor area is its Retail GFA, priced by Rate x Retail GFA', P4k-d) instead of 'this plot states no chain inputs', which sent its reader to a plot row it does not have. **The capex report stated the MASTER line's basis beside the OVERRIDE's amount** ('4,200.00 x 0.00 main asset GFA sqm = 7,667,914.90' on the strip): it read the window from the override and the method and rate from the line; it reads all three from the override now. **Seven tab tooltips said 'Read by no cost method' about columns that are priced** (footprint, landscape, retail GFA, main asset GFA, both parking areas, net developable), and `verify-land-chain` U18b counted the sentence (>= 10) so it could not see the sentence go false; re-aimed to check each tooltip against the picker's own labels (U18b: only an unpriced column says so; U18c: a priced column names its method), sabotage-tested both ways. **MEASURED, Marina Gate, latest saved version (the founder was editing live during this work: the Phase 1 line had moved to Main Asset GFA and the Phase 1 PARKING line to Retail Parking Area, which zeroes the hosts' parking, the trap this closes):** as saved 808,421,015.04; with hosts on Main Asset GFA + Parking Area and the strips on Retail GFA + Retail Parking Area at the saved rates, 809,332,790.57 (Land 1 79,827,845.49 = 4,200 x 19,006.63; Land 4 104,910,400.00 = 5,800 x 18,088.00 down from 131,414,080.00 on Total GFA; Phase 1 strip 7,667,914.90 = 4,200 x 1,825.69 retail GFA plus 8,760,000.00 = 3,000 x 2,920 retail parking). Nothing written to the database: the phase-1 Parking line back to Rate x Parking Area and the two strips' overrides are the founder's dropdowns. `verify-capex-structure` 168/0, `verify-land-chain` 220/0, `verify-capex-report` 28/0.
  - **THE CAPEX INPUT IS THE MERGED LINE, ONE PER LINE, AND AN ASSET WITH NOTHING TO PRICE IS NO ROW FROM TABLE 4 ONWARDS (founder's correction of the first cut, same day).** The first cut kept one input section per plot and added a plot pill row, which is exactly the per-plot structure the restructure retires. Now: one `AssetCostSection` per consolidated line, its breakdown the sum of its plots' breakdowns (`mergeBreakdowns`, generic over the shape so a new field is summed rather than dropped), its metrics the plots' pooled areas, and a rate or override typed on the line written to EVERY plot on it. Storage is unchanged (`CostOverride.assetId`, no migration); the fan-out is at the one write site, so the engine (per plot) and the input (per line) agree by construction. **One navigation row per phase with every line of that phase beside it**, one click, instead of a phase pill and then an asset pill. **`assetHasSubstance` is the one rule** for what the model prices: a plot draw, a sub-unit, a chain input, a typed area or a companion; table 4 and the capex tab both read it, so the High End Apartments husk (no plot since its plot was deleted, no sub-units, no chain, no areas) is a row on table 2 only, where it is deleted, and nowhere downstream. RE HUB's plot-less assets all have sub-units or typed areas and are unaffected. Verified on live data: Phase 1 of Marina Gate is ONE Branded Villas input line merging two plots and no High End Apartments line; the ten capex verifiers unchanged at green.
  - **CAPEX FOLLOWS THE ASSETS TAB, AND THE ASSETS TAB IS THE ONE SOURCE OF AREA (founder's requirement, all eleven points).** Measured first with the exact chain call the tab makes: capex did not read Table 4 at all. It read sub-unit areas, typed asset fields and a five-field bag, and only five of Table 4's sixteen figures ever crossed, two of those behind `useDerivedAreas`, which was off on both live projects. On one plot Table 4 showed 25,279 sqm of Total GFA against capex's 23,841 and 3,560 sqm of parking against a hand-typed 2,800; on the next plot Table 4 showed 11,900 sqm of GFA and capex priced ZERO, because that plot had no sub-units and no typed areas. **THE BRIDGE IS WIDENED TO THE FULL TABLE 4 SET AND FILLED ON LOAD AND SAVE**: the chain call the row builder ran inline moved to `computeAssetChain` in the shared model, the store calls the same function in `hydrate` and `extractPersistSnapshot`, and the applier returns its input when nothing moved. **DERIVED WINS, ABSENT FALLS BACK**, so RE HUB, which has no chain inputs on any asset, is byte-identical on every figure. **NSA stays the sub-units'** (table 5) because the store keeps them equal to the line's NSA by share. **EACH FIGURE IS CHARGED ONCE ACROSS A HOST AND ITS STRIP**: a host's Total GFA excludes the retail its companion carries; `rate_x_parking_area` is main parking, 0 on a strip; the new `rate_x_retail_parking_area` is the strip's, 0 on a host; `rate_x_main_asset_gfa` is new. **THE MOVE, Marina Gate, through the store's own hydrate: 753,585,717.75 to 903,521,562.37.** Land 2 from land and RETT only (36,771,000.00) to 106,825,222.39; Land 4 83,928,320.00 to 131,414,080.00 on the built-area line; the hotel gains 5,760 sqm of parking it had never charged; Land 1's parking 2,800 to 3,560. The two strips LOSE their parking lines (8,760,000.00 and 5,520,000.00) until a Retail Parking line exists, which is the price of never charging a square metre twice, and it is stated on the row. **THE INPUTS READ BY LINE, WITH THE PLOTS UNDERNEATH**: one pill per consolidated line through the same planner the tables use; a second row of plot pills only where a line holds more than one, because the override is per asset. **Sell + Manage untouched**, pinned. `verify-land-chain` 219/0 (U78 to U80 re-aimed to the full set and the settle), `verify-capex-structure` 161/0 (P4 registers the two methods; P4e and P6 re-aimed), `verify-retail-companion` 61/0, `verify-project-integrity` 13/0, `verify-asset-type-standards` 123/0, `verify-capex-report` 28/0, `verify-consolidated-view` 27/0. Five checks that pinned "the tab calls `computeLandChain` once" now pin the one shared site and zero in the tab, and fail when either grows a second. An end-to-end flow through the store (67 checks: every area base per plot against Table 4, add an asset with no tab open and watch capex follow, remove it and watch capex forget, hydrate-then-persist a fixed point) passes.
  - **AND THE WHOLE DAY SAT LOCAL. EIGHT COMMITS, SEVENTEEN HOURS, NO PUSH** ([docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 9.6). Vercel builds on push, so it never attempted anything: no failed deployment, no alert, nothing red anywhere. The founder was clicking through a build from 2026-09-11 17:21 while reasoning about today's tabs, and found the parking double count in code that had already been corrected locally. He noticed only by opening the Vercel dashboard himself. **A failed deploy is loud; a deploy nobody asked for produces no signal at either end**, and the assistant's "seven commits today" reports were all TRUE, which is what made the two pictures look consistent while being of different code. Three one-line checks were available and unread throughout: `git status -sb` prints `[ahead 8]`, `git push` prints a ref-update line whose ABSENCE is the evidence, and `GET /api/health` returns the deployed commit for comparison with `git rev-parse HEAD`, which CLAUDE.md documents for exactly this. Pushed as `40221720..6eb0a6f1`; the rule now adds that the health SHA is checked BEFORE reasoning about anything the user reports seeing on the live site.
  - **HELD, WITH A REASON.** Moving the four assets-tab reconciliations into the store is the real answer to "a change must propagate everywhere", and it is not done: `syncLineSubUnits` moves money, so it deserves its own before and after.

## 2026-09-11

- **SURVEY ITEM 7: MASSING STAYS ON THE TAB, AND THE CARVE STOPS IGNORING THE TYPE. `verify-retail-companion` 55 -> 61/0, `verify-asset-type-standards` 122/0, `verify-land-chain` 211/0, `verify-module6-pipeline` 30 -> 34/0, `verify-module6-field-census` 18/0, `verify-capex-report` 22/0, `verify-capex-structure` 148/0, all with credentials; six sabotages caught.** Diagnosed first, built second, in the order the diagnosis proposed.
  - **THE DEFECT, WHICH BELONGED TO NEITHER DIRECTION.** Coverage, FAR and the service share default from the asset TYPE since 2026-09-10, and the engine's one chain call passed the PLOT's inputs alone. So a plot INHERITING its FAR produced an undefined total GFA, no derivable carve share, and its host silently kept land its companion should have taken. Measured by moving one live plot's FAR onto its type and changing nothing else: host 10,454.12 -> 11,000.00 sqm and 78,405,876.51 -> 82,500,000.00, companion 785.88 -> 240.00 sqm and 5,894,123.49 -> 1,800,000.00, **project land total unchanged at 37,000 sqm**. That is precisely the failure the per-asset reconciliation exists to catch, arriving through a door it did not watch: every check in section I calls the engine directly, and the fix lives in the composer.
  - **A FRONT DOOR, NOT A PARAMETER, AND THE TRADE IS WRITTEN DOWN.** `withInheritedMassingAll` merges the type's massing over COPIES, the read-time view `withResolvedAssetNames` established, so nothing is stamped and a type edit still moves the model on the next compute. Threading a required resolver was tried first and abandoned: it reached `computeAssetLandSqm`, `computeAssetLandBreakdown`, `computeAssetLandCost`, `computeLandReconciliation`, `resolveAssetAreaMetrics`, `computeAssetCost` and both allocation-factor resolvers. The front door's cost is that a surface skipping it fails silently, so **FIVE DOORS ARE NAMED** and J5 fails on a sixth: the composer (which covers every engine consumer, each called with the state it already resolved), the shared capex report (entered by the PDF and the workbook), and the three screens that price assets themselves.
  - **TWO NARROW INVARIANTS WIDENED BY ONE VERB, both with the narrowness re-asserted.** `verify-land-chain` C1 admits two more files to the chain module and new **C1d** proves neither runs a chain, so `computeLandChain` is still called in exactly ONE place for the carve share. `verify-asset-type-standards` A1 admits the same two to name the values **BY LINE**, and new **A1b** fails if either reads a value for anything but `chainMassingFor`. No engine file computes a chain of its own, and none prices from the standards.
  - **THE DIRECTION: MASSING STAYS ON THE TAB.** Wiring the chain into the engine was rejected on a measured premise, not a preference: the chain produces ROWS THE USER THEN PRICES rather than numbers, so a synthesised sub-unit has nowhere for a rate to live; every saved snapshot already holds the derived rows, which would double-count unless a migration stripped them, destroying the prices typed on them; 183 call sites across 18 files read `subUnits` as given; and the chain would become a second opinion on area beside `resolveAssetAreaMetrics`. On RE HUB, 0 of 8 assets carry chain inputs, so both paths would stay alive permanently, which is the argument that kept the chain out of a cost method.
  - **THE GATE SAYS WHAT IS TRUE, NOT "MOVES NOTHING".** Measured one input at a time on the live project: coverage, FAR and the retail share each move the retail land CARVE and nothing else (no built area, no capex, no revenue), while utilisation and the service share move NOTHING, because the engine reads the chain for one figure and halving utilisation halves both halves of it. All 37 `landChain` levers on MARINA GATE are inactive with the differentiated reason; the retired `retailAreaPerSlotSqm` (3) and the unread `maxFloors` (4) get their own sentences. **`verify-module6-pipeline` asserts the text differentiates and `verify-retail-companion` J6 asserts the claim against the engine**, so the sentence cannot go stale.
  - **THE DOOR LEFT OPEN, recorded rather than built.** If scenario massing later matters, the honest version is a case carrying its OWN derived rows by re-running the tab's planner: it reuses the planner unchanged, keeps one definition of every area, and keeps prices where a user can type them. Moving the chain into the engine is not that.
  - **NO NUMBER MOVES**: a same-snapshot before/after over every live project is byte-identical across capex, peak debt, equity invested, both IRRs and every per-asset capex series. **A FIGURE QUOTED EARLIER TODAY IS STALE**: FMP - MARINA GATE was edited in the browser at 10:43 and its capex is now 708,807,135.75, not the 557,843,883.95 quoted in the morning's commits. Those comparisons were same-snapshot before/after and stand; the absolute figure moved for reasons unrelated to them.

## 2026-09-10

- **THE ASSET TYPE LIST BELONGS TO THE PROJECT, AND THAT CLOSES TRAPS 7.35. `verify-asset-type-standards` 99 -> 121/0, `verify-land-chain` 204/0, `verify-accounts` 125/0, `verify-change-log` 59/0, `verify-module6-field-census` 18/0, all with credentials; four sabotages caught, and the new checks caught two defects in my own work before commit.** Reported before building. The names were the ACCOUNT'S while the values beside them were the project's, and that split cost two things: a firm's projects come from different land owners and developers, so each names its types as its own scheme requires, and an edit made inside one project reached every other one, adding rows to their tables or orphaning their values under a banner that blamed a deletion nobody had made.
  - **THE LIST JOINED THE VALUES IN THE SNAPSHOT** (`project.assetTypes`), so the id that keys `assetTypeValues` is minted by a list in the same object as the values. That dissolves the question the fix was waiting on (whose list does a type added inside a client's project join?) rather than answering it: it joins THAT PROJECT's, and no other. No migration, no new table, additive and schema-tolerant.
  - **CONSEQUENCES, all deliberate.** The whole tab locks with the project now (it used to lock only the values half, which was right while the other half was account data and is wrong now), and there is NO Save button on either half: a name in the snapshot is a model input like the number beside it. A duplicate takes its own copy for free. The change log names WHICH type moved (`PER_ELEMENT_ARRAYS.assetTypes`). A NAME is gated OUT of the Module 6 picker as non-economic, while the values stay INACTIVE (economic but unread), which is a different gate. `TypeChoice.fromFirm` is `fromList`.
  - **`refm_asset_types` SURVIVES AS A TEMPLATE**, read by nothing that computes. Seeded from ON DEMAND, never at project creation: the create route takes a client-built snapshot, so seeding there would put a network dependency on creation for an editing convenience and would freeze the choice before anyone knows what the project is. Pushed back explicitly, ONE WAY, by a button, adding rather than replacing. **Both ends are scoped to the account that OWNS the project** (`?projectId=`), through ONE resolver all four verbs share so a read and a write cannot disagree about which firm is meant, and it REFUSES rather than falling back to the caller's account when the project cannot be reached: a fallback is how an admin seeds their own vocabulary into a client's project with nothing on screen saying so.
  - **A PROJECT THAT PREDATES THE MOVE IS BACKFILLED FROM WHAT IT ALREADY REFERENCES**, at hydrate, never from an account, so the repair cannot reintroduce the bug it repairs: its assets' types (reference plus the label the user was shown when they picked it), plus any type its own `assetTypeValues` hold numbers for, which is the retail companion's `retail-combined` on the live project. A label is recovered, never invented; no order is invented either, because stamping 0..N would claim the user had arranged a list they have never seen. **Measured: FMP RE HUB 5 types, FMP - MARINA GATE 5 types, every value key attached, and an engine fingerprint over both projects BYTE-IDENTICAL before and after** (capex incl/excl land, per-asset series, consolidated lines, peak debt, equity invested, both IRRs).
  - **WHAT THE TEN TYPES ON THE ACCOUNT WERE.** Exactly the platform catalog, ten for ten, same labels and categories, reordered once. Nothing bespoke had ever been typed; there are eleven vocabulary rows in the entire database across ten accounts, and every one is reproducible from the catalog. The migration cost of the move was nil, which is why it was worth doing now rather than later.
  - **TWO DEFECTS THE NEW CHECKS FOUND IN MY OWN WORK**, both fixed before commit. The backfill's "first REAL label wins" rule did not do that: the recovered fallback is never blank, so "is it still empty" could not tell a recovered label from a typed one, and an asset carrying an id with no type pinned the fallback and locked out the real name on the next asset. And the Module 6 gate for the list landed in `inactiveLeverReason` when a name must be REMOVED from the picker rather than badged as inert, so renaming an asset type was still an offered scenario lever.

- **AN ASSET IS CALLED BY WHERE IT IS AND WHAT IT IS, and the manual name is retired. `verify-asset-label` 41/0 (was `verify-asset-display-name` 27), `verify-land-chain` 204/0, `verify-capex-structure` 145/0, `verify-retail-companion` 55/0, `verify-report-consistency` 60/0, all with credentials; eight sabotages caught.** Reported before removing anything. The name was optional and fell back to the type, which was already an admission that five plots of Branded Villas with five invented names is a user typing five identities for one thing, and the schedules then MERGE those plots by type, so the names vanish from the output with nothing saying why. Measured first: "plot plus type" alone does not identify an asset (FMP RE HUB has 1 of 8 assets with a plot; 6,860 of 9,399 historical rows carry a sentinel parcel id), so the label is the plot WHERE THERE IS ONE, else the phase, then the type always, then the phase again where the project has more than one and the plot alone would not separate two assets, then a companion marker.
  - **THE COMPILER ENUMERATED THE READERS.** `assetName.ts` was rewritten to export one function taking a REQUIRED context (`assetLabel(asset, { parcels, phases })`), which broke every reader on purpose: `assetDisplayName`, `assetNameIsDerived` and `assetTypeSuffix` are gone, forty call sites across nine report builders, six resolvers, both exports, the deck and nine module screens were re-pointed, and `Module2Escrow` was found reading no plots at all. `buildICReportModel` and `buildOnePagerReportModel` gained `parcels` in their input, so eleven verifier fixtures had to say what they meant by a plot.
  - **THE FIELD IS RETIRED, NOT DELETED.** `Asset.name` is still declared and still stored, sixteen live assets still carry the name someone typed, and nothing reads it. No migration rewrites or strips it. The assets table's name column is gone (the input table drops from 14 columns to 13).
  - **CAPEX OUTPUT NOW CALLS AN ASSET WHAT CAPEX INPUT CALLS IT.** The input table read `assetLabel(a, state)` and the output rows read `a.name`, the raw stored field, so one screen could call one asset two things. It reads the same label now, and `verify-asset-label` C4 fails if the two halves ever diverge again.
  - **THE CAPEX SUMMARY CONSOLIDATES BY PHASE AND TYPE, through the consolidation's OWN grouping function** rather than a second statement of what a line is, so tables 2 to 4 and the assets tab's table 4 agree by construction. Table 1 stays per asset: a cost line belongs to a plot. **AN ASSET IN NO GROUP KEEPS ITS OWN ROW**, because `groupAssetsForConsolidation` excludes companions by design and the retail companion has carried its own cost lines since consolidation step 6; without that it would vanish from the summary while still sitting in the total. Measured on FMP - MARINA GATE: the two Branded Villas plots in phase 1 merge into one row of 127,712,203, seven rows where there were eight, and the project total is unchanged at 557,843,884. FMP RE HUB is 8 assets into 7 lines, total unchanged at 4,912,201,582.
  - **THE EXCEL MODEL LOOKS AN ASSET UP BY ID.** `refs.capex.find((a) => a.name === m.name)` and three name-keyed series maps were matching a DISPLAY STRING; the capex report carries `assetSeries`, keyed by `assetId`, and the workbook reads that. A label that can repeat was never an identity, and the summary tables it used to dig them out of are no longer per asset.
  - **THE LAST PLACE THE RETIRED NAME REACHED A SENTENCE** was the sale-cohort advisory in `computeFinancialsSnapshot`, which took the raw list and printed `"<name>: no downpayment stated"` into the financing reconciliation. Found by the verifier, not by reading: it renames every asset in the fixture and requires nothing built from a name to move.

- **PARKING: THE BASIS IS HONOURED, THE DIVISOR IS A TYPE STANDARD, AND A TYPED ZERO IS AN ANSWER. `verify-land-chain` 159 -> 169/0, `verify-retail-companion` 52/0, `verify-asset-type-standards` 98/0, all with credentials; four sabotages caught (the typed zero, the divide, the stamping migration, the resolver's preference).** Reported before changing anything, and the report inverted the premise: the chain ALREADY divided for `sqm_per_slot`, and it is the REFERENCE that does not. Read out of the workbook itself rather than inferred: its parking-slots column is `units x ratio` on all 117 plot rows (one shared formula), its retail-parking column divides retail GFA by ONE FIXED CELL (the Retail row of the parking-ratio table, 25) and never by the plot's own basis, and its areas are both `slots x 40`. So the `m2/slot` unit its standards table prints against its two retail types is a LABEL THE FORMULA IGNORES, and all 14 Standalone Commercial plots in the workbook derive 0 units, 0 slots, 0 parking area and a BUA equal to their GFA. The symptom was real, the mechanism was exactly as described, and the source has it too.
  - **THE DIVIDE STAYS, AND THE FILE NOW SAYS WHY** (founder's call). The unit means what it says; the workbook not honouring it is its shortcut, not a rule about parking. Step 8's comment stated the branch as if it mirrored the source, which would have sent the next reader to reconcile a difference that is deliberate. `verify-land-chain` A13/A13b run the divide (no unit count anywhere in it) and A14 requires the divergence to be stated AT the branch, scoped to the step 8 slice so a note elsewhere in a long file cannot satisfy it.
  - **A TYPED ZERO RATIO DERIVES ZERO SLOTS, not a blank.** `parkingRatio > 0` guarded the divide and pushed no gap, so a type stating "this needs no parking" (which the reference company table does state, for villas and for standalone commercial) produced an empty cell with nothing to explain it. A12 pins it against reference row 209 (land 2265.48, FAR 1.6): GFA 3624.768, 0 slots, BUA 3624.768, the workbook's own figures.
  - **THE RETAIL DIVISOR MOVED ONTO THE RETAIL TYPE, and the project field is retired one day after it arrived.** It is a retail type's parking ratio on the sqm-per-slot basis, which is the cell every other type states its ratio in, and the reference reads it from exactly there. A project field was a second home for one number, the same defect the move off the plot rows was correcting, one level up. `resolveRetailSlotArea` is the ONE reader: the ground-floor retail type wins, a lone sqm-per-slot type serves a firm that renamed the entry, a tie resolves to NEITHER and the chain names the gap. A typed zero and a count-based ratio are not candidates. **THE LIFTED VALUE IS DELIBERATELY NOT CARRIED**: Marina Gate held 40, which is the area a slot OCCUPIES, so its retail slots were understated by 37.5% (measured: 31/14/13 where 25 gives 50/23/21). It now shows the named gap until someone states 25 on the retail type, which is the honest answer, and `liftRetailAreaPerSlot` is deleted with the field it fed.
  - **THE DRAWER WAS PASSING NO DIVISOR AT ALL**, found while moving it: the panel reported a missing retail ratio on a plot whose own row three tables up derived retail parking fine. Both surfaces resolve it now, through the same call.
  - **A MISSING UNIT SIZE IS NO LONGER A GAP WHEN PARKING COMES OFF AREA.** The reference leaves the unit size of its two sqm-per-slot types blank ON PURPOSE (nobody sizes a shop in units), so "the unit count cannot be derived" read as a problem to fix when it was the standard working.
  - **THE PARKING BASIS IS STORED, NOT DISPLAYED.** The dropdown showed "slots per unit" for any unset type and only WROTE one if the user changed it, so a type could hold a ratio with no basis and the chain read it as count-based: screen and model agreed by coincidence, not by record, and a ratio meant as sqm per slot would silently multiply a unit count. Hydrate stamps the basis the chain was already assuming (so it MOVES NO NUMBER, proven by running both) and the ratio cell writes it with the ratio, so no new type can reach that state. Live: `branded-villas` was the one type in that condition; its slots are unchanged at 112/28/25.

- **THE CHAIN'S QUANTITIES CAN BE CHARGED AGAINST: parking wired, three methods added, and the platform stops writing user fields. `verify-capex-structure` 139 -> 145/0, `verify-land-chain` 204/0, `verify-retail-companion` 55/0, `verify-capex-phasing` 91/0, `verify-cost-of-sales` 53/0, `verify-fixed-assets` 99/0, `verify-capex-report` 14/0, `verify-capex-stage-buckets` 17/0, `verify-capex-picker-stacking` 17/0, `verify-asset-cost-allocation` 19/0, `verify-consolidated-line` 38/0, with credentials; two sabotages caught.**
  - **THE ONE-WAY DOOR IS CLOSED WITH A SEPARATE BAG (option 2, founder's call).** `Asset.derivedAreas` holds what the chain derived; `parkingArea` and `parkingBaysRequired` stay the user's. Precedence is a READ rule (`resolveAssetParkingArea`, typed wins) rather than an invariant every write path must remember, so clearing a derived figure is always safe and nothing the platform wrote can masquerade as something a person typed. **A SEEDED ZERO IS NOT TYPED**: the factory writes `parkingBaysRequired: 0` and a migration wrote `parkingArea: 0`, so typed means GREATER THAN ZERO, the same reading the seeded `landAllocation.sqm` taught.
  - **BOTH PARKING METHODS NOW HAVE A QUANTITY.** `rate_per_parking_bay` read a field the factory seeds at 0 and only a retail companion updates, so on a host it silently multiplied zero; `rate_x_parking_area` the same. Both read the derived bag now. **MEASURED on FMP - MARINA GATE with the toggle on: 557,843,883.95 -> 570,534,099.95 (+12,690,216.00)**, all of it on the two plots whose parking area was never typed (1,120 and 2,240 sqm at the phase's 3,000 plus soft percentages); the plot that HAS a typed 2,800 charges 2,800 and does not move, which is the precedence proven on live data rather than in a fixture. Debt outstanding 2.80bn -> 2.92bn. FMP RE HUB derives nothing.
  - **THREE NEW METHODS**, each multiplying a chain figure nothing could price: `rate_x_net_developable_area`, `rate_x_footprint_area`, `rate_x_landscape_area`. Landscape was the largest gap: hard landscaping per sqm is a real budget line and the chain derives the area on every plot.
  - **`rate_per_nda` IS NOT REVIVED, and that is the decision.** It is retired and now multiplies GROSS land; re-pointing it at the utilised area would change what every stored line means without anybody editing it, which is the one thing a retirement is supposed to prevent. The new method is a NEW method, and P5 fails if the retired one is ever quietly re-aimed.
  - **A METHOD IS REGISTERED IN FIVE PLACES OR IT IS HALF ADDED, and P4 caught exactly that**: all three were in the union, the labels, the engine and both report surfaces, and MISSING FROM THE PICKER ORDER, so they existed and could not be selected. The check was written for that failure and found it on the first run.
- **COVERAGE, FAR AND SERVICE DEFAULT FROM THE ASSET TYPE, AND THE STANDARDS TAB NOW SAYS IT MOVES THE MODEL. `verify-land-chain` 199 -> 204/0, `verify-asset-type-standards` 99/0, `verify-retail-companion` 55/0, `verify-capex-structure` 139/0, with credentials; two sabotages caught.** Three percentages were typed per plot and five plots of one type usually share them: the reference workbook's fourteen Standalone Commercial rows all read FAR 1.6 and coverage 60%, which is fifteen typed numbers where three would do. They join the unit size and the parking ratio on the PROJECT VALUES side of the standards tab, in the same inherit-and-override shape: the type carries the default, the PLOT overrides where it differs, absent inherits and **a typed 0 on the plot is a real override** (0% coverage is a plot left open, not a blank to be filled).
  - **FAR IS OFFERED, NOT ASSUMED, and the plot row SHOWS it inherited.** Coverage and the service share are conventions of a building type and inherit silently; FAR is a planning constraint of a piece of GROUND, and the reference's own FAR runs 1.2 to 5 WITHIN one type. So a plot inheriting FAR renders it as a placeholder reading `1.6 (type)` with a tooltip saying where it came from, using the cell's existing placeholder mechanism, whose whole property is that it cannot be mistaken for a stored value. **The retail share does not default at all**: no field, no resolver, no column, because it is ground-floor retail on THIS plot, it decides which companion exists and how much land is carved.
  - **THE TAB NOW WARNS THAT AN EDIT HERE MOVES THE MODEL**, which it did not until today. Every value on that tab was an input nothing read; coverage, FAR and service feed the chain, the chain drives a share-stated sub-unit's area, and that area is what construction charges on and revenue prices. A user who thinks they are editing a reference table is entitled to be told otherwise, so the banner says one standards edit changes every inheriting plot at once.
  - **NOTHING MOVED ON ANY LIVE PROJECT**: no asset type states a coverage, FAR or service value yet, so every plot still uses exactly what it typed. The feature is inert until somebody fills a cell, which is the same property the parking ratio had on the day it shipped.
- **THE CHAIN REACHES CAPEX, AS A ROW AND BY INVITATION. `verify-land-chain` 194 -> 199/0, `verify-retail-companion` 55/0, `verify-capex-structure` 139/0, `verify-consolidated-line` 38/0, `verify-cost-of-sales` 53/0, `verify-fixed-assets` 99/0, with credentials; one sabotage caught.** Reported before building, and the report set the shape: **the sub-units are the priced area and the chain never reaches a cost method.** Capex charges on the BUILT area, which the engine reads as the sub-unit rows plus the asset's own support and parking; the chain also knows the lobby and the service share, so where nobody typed a support area the engine was short by exactly that (a Marina line charging on 23,841 sqm of BUA that the chain puts at 28,051 of main GFA). The gap is closed the way everything else in this tab closes one: a derived Support ROW, which the engine has always read and a user can see, edit and delete.
  - **OPT-IN PER PROJECT (`project.useDerivedAreas`), off by default, founder's call and the right one.** The seeded NSA row fired automatically and moved 45m because it corrected assets charging NOTHING; this ADDS cost to assets already charging on a live model, so the user says when. A project that never turns it on is byte-identical for ever.
  - **WHAT IT WOULD DO ON FMP - MARINA GATE, per asset, if turned on:** Branded Villas BUA 0.00 -> 4,093.60 and cost 36,771,000.00 -> 58,416,278.42; Retail 1,250.00 -> 1,700.00 and 10,471,987.50 -> 12,851,403.00; Branded Villas P2 14,470.40 -> 22,657.60 and 179,203,558.46 -> 238,985,756.02; Hospitality 18,750.00 -> 23,250.00 and 156,223,312.50 -> 189,081,907.50; Retail P2 7,500.00 -> 10,200.00 and 54,764,325.00 -> 74,479,482.00. **Project 682,208,496.95 -> 818,589,140.42 (+136,380,643.48)**, debt outstanding 4.03bn -> 5.26bn, and **revenue 0.00 -> 0.00 because Support is not part of NSA**, which is the property that lets this move cost without touching what is sold.
  - **FMP RE HUB DERIVES NOTHING EVEN WITH THE TOGGLE ON**, which is the safety property proven rather than asserted: six of its assets state their own support area and are left alone, and the rest have no chain. A project where the chain is silent cannot be changed by this, because nothing here reads the chain unless it produces a row.
  - **DERIVE ONLY WHILE NOBODY HAS SAID OTHERWISE**, the same rule the retail companion's own sub-unit follows: a typed support area, in the asset field or in a row of the user's own, wins; an existing derived row then STANDS DOWN rather than double counting; and a chain that stops running removes it, because a stale derived figure is worse than none.
  - **PARKING IS NOT IN THIS COMMIT, deliberately.** The chain derives a parking area too, and GFA is BUA plus parking, but parking lives in an asset FIELD rather than a row, and a field the platform writes cannot be told apart from one a user typed. That is a one-way door, so it needs its own decision (a provenance flag, or its own opt-in) rather than being smuggled in beside a reversible row.
- **THE METRIC IS THE ASSET'S, IN THE ENGINE TOO, AND IT WAS WORTH 10.09bn ON ONE ASSET. `verify-capex-structure` 134 -> 139/0, `verify-retail-companion` 55/0, `verify-land-chain` 194/0, `verify-asset-cost-allocation` 19/0, `verify-cost-of-sales` 53/0, `verify-fixed-assets` 99/0, `verify-idc-depreciation` 133/0, `verify-consolidated-line` 38/0, `verify-portfolio-dashboard` 48/0, with credentials; one sabotage caught in both directions.** `computeSubUnitArea` asked the ROW's metric while every surface asked the ASSET's (docs/TRAPS.md 7.32), so where a user had switched an asset from counting units to stating areas and the rows kept their old metric, the engine read an AREA as a COUNT and multiplied it by the unit size.
  - **WHAT MOVED, MEASURED ON ALL SEVEN LIVE PROJECTS.** Exactly ONE row disagrees anywhere: "2 BR" on FMP - MARINA GATE, 10,098.23 sqm under an asset whose metric is 'area', read as 10,098 units at 190 sqm. That asset's BUA **1,932,405.36 -> 23,840.50 sqm**, its development cost **10,308,708,465.45 -> 217,000,013.46**, the project **10,773,916,948.94 -> 682,208,496.95 (-10,091,708,451.99)** and debt outstanding **94,895,226,587.30 -> 4,025,415,269.91**. Revenue is unaffected (0.00 either side, nothing priced). **Every other live project has zero disagreeing rows and does not move**, FMP RE HUB included at 6,262,882,342.18 unchanged.
  - **THE ASSET IS A REQUIRED PARAMETER, which is how all 37 call sites were found.** An optional one is a defect waiting for a caller to forget (TRAPS 7.7); making it required put every site in front of the COMPILER instead of a reviewer, the same technique the role matrix used to enumerate its fifteen. Two call sites deserved their own answer rather than a mechanical one: the M2.0g parking migration passes `undefined` explicitly, because it reads a RETIRED category on snapshots that predate asset-level metrics and the row is the only statement there is; and the portfolio metrics resolved the owner ONCE for both the area and the unit count beside it, which had the same defect and would otherwise have been two readings of one statement.
  - **ONE HOME FOR THE RULE.** `resolveSubUnitMetric` lives in core and both the engine and the platform's table rules read it; the private copy those rules gained yesterday is gone. A local copy of a rule the engine also holds is exactly how these two came to disagree.
  - **THE LIVE FIGURE IS NOW SANE**, which is the real check: 1.93m sqm of BUA on one plot was never a number anybody could have meant, and the corrected 23,840.50 is what every screen has been showing all along.

THIS MOVES MONEY: +45,731,506.02 on FMP - MARINA GATE.** A SECOND ASSET BEHAVING NORMALLY, which is what the founder asked for and what the diff shows: five narrowed predicates and two fields, no new machinery, no retail-specific cost path.

The host stays Sell with its cost going to cost of sales, untouched.

Every hand-made Lease asset has one, created lazily by the Revenue tab; a derived asset nobody has visited never got it. Seeded with the tab's OWN defaults, nothing invented: rate 0, occupancy zeros, arDays 30, no indexation. Proven end to end: **0.00 at rate 0, and 25,644,332.37 at a probe 1,200/sqm/yr and 90% occupancy** (15,773,996.37 + 9,870,336.00), on the per sqm per year basis, from the sub-unit step 4b gave it.
