It predates every commit today and is the capex half of the open TODO item 5. Do not read the project total above as sound until it is fixed.
- **THE CONVERSION RAN, THE AREAS DO FOLLOW, AND WHAT IS WRONG IS THE VALUE IT STAMPED. Answered from `refm_project_changes`, the append-only log, not from reasoning.** Reported as a defect that the areas were still holding; the log says otherwise, in three rows: **09:48:08Z** `landChain.servicePct` changed together with 1 BR's `metricValue` AND `nsaSharePct` (a hand-typed share, on the build before conversion existed); **11:58:43Z** 2 BR's `nsaSharePct` appeared (the conversion, twenty minutes after that build deployed); **12:02:04Z** `servicePct` changed again and BOTH rows' `metricValue` moved with it. So the mechanism works: a stored share re-derives its area on the next NSA move, and the last save proves it on real rows.
  - **WHAT IS ACTUALLY WRONG IS 107.62%.** 1 BR holds 55.00% and 2 BR holds 52.62%, and the two were captured at DIFFERENT moments: 1 BR when the line still footed, 2 BR after it had already drifted through a service-percent change it did not follow. Conversion stamps the ratio a row is showing AT THE MOMENT IT RUNS, so a model that drifted first has its drift frozen. That risk was named in the options report and judged not to bite because the line measured 100.00% at 09:00; it had stopped footing by the time the conversion reached that machine. Both areas are consistent with one NSA of about 20,540, so nothing is internally inconsistent: the line is simply over-allocated by 7.62% and says so on screen ("22,105.55 of 20,539.84 sqm allocated (107.6%)"). The fix is the user's to make and is one keystroke, 45 on the 2 BR row; rewriting their 52.62 would be the platform moving a number nobody asked it to move, which is the whole reason conversion was built to write shares and never areas.
  - **THE PICKER'S COUNT NOW SAYS WHAT IT COUNTS.** "+ Add asset (2)" read as "two assets are about to be added"; it is how many already draw from that plot, so the label says so in words. A number in a control's label is a claim about that control unless it is told otherwise. `verify-land-chain` 192 -> 193/0.
- **EVERY OLD ROW IS CONVERTED TO THE SHARE IT ALREADY SHOWED, AND THE TYPE PICKER MOVES TO WHERE PEOPLE LOOK. `verify-land-chain` 184 -> 192/0, `verify-retail-companion` 53/0, `verify-consolidated-line` 38/0, `verify-asset-type-standards` 98/0, with credentials; two sabotages caught (Support converted, the row's metric read instead of the asset's).** Options reported first, founder chose convert-everything.
  - **THE CONVERSION MOVES NO NUMBER, which is why it was the option to choose.** Every row DISPLAYED a share long before one could be stated (the column divides its area by the line's NSA), so the share was always on screen and never in the file, and the two agreed only until the NSA moved. A conversion writes what the row already shows and touches no area: MEASURED on all six live projects, Marina Gate converts exactly two rows, "1 BR" to **55.00%** and "2 BR" to **45.00%**, areas unchanged, and the financial snapshot is **BYTE-IDENTICAL** either side. Those round numbers are the evidence the split was always the intent. The rejected alternatives are in the report: normalising to 100% moves areas nobody asked to move, and inverting the default records nothing (the TRAPS 7.34 shape).
  - **SCOPE, ALL THREE PARTS DELIBERATE.** Only a line with a RESOLVABLE NSA (chain or entered): one live project's eight lines all report source 'none', and all twelve of its rows are left exactly as they are, because a share of nothing is not a statement. Only AREA rows: a count row's count is its statement. **NOT Support** (founder's call): NSA is Sellable + Operable + Leasable, so a back-of-house row is not part of the whole the share is a share of, and it stays area-stated with the same escape hatch in the other direction.
  - **THE METRIC IS THE ASSET'S, WHICH IS TRAPS 7.32 INSIDE THIS FILE'S OWN RULES.** The seed and reallocation rules shipped yesterday asked the ROW (`u.metric`), and one live row carries metric 'units' under an asset whose metric is 'area': the exact row the founder was looking at, which would have been silently left behind by every rule here. One `effectiveMetric` helper now, asked in one place, pinned by U62 in both directions.
  - **IT RUNS IN THE TAB'S SYNC, NOT HYDRATE**, because hydrate has no chain and therefore no line NSA. Conversion lands the first time someone opens the assets tab, settles immediately (a converted row has a stored share, so the next pass says nothing) and writes the SHARE only, never an area.
  - **THE PICKER WAS THE DEFECT, NOT ITS ABSENCE.** It rendered, it was live, and it sat at the far end of a fourteen-column scrolling row trailing the plot-check sentence, which is a control nobody finds. It now sits beside the plot NAME and count on the band, and the LAND table carries the same one on every plot row (`parcel-<id>-add-asset`), because a user who has just typed a plot looks there next. Both write through the SAME `onAddAsset` and the SAME `assetTypePatch`, and the choices are resolved ONCE at the root and passed to all three surfaces, so "which type can I pick" cannot depend on where you picked it.
- **A LINE ALWAYS HAS SOMETHING TO PRICE, A TYPED SHARE FOLLOWS THE NSA, AND THE TYPE IS OFFERED WHEN THE ASSET IS ADDED. `verify-land-chain` 175 -> 184/0, `verify-retail-companion` 53/0, `verify-consolidated-line` 38/0, `verify-asset-type-standards` 98/0, with credentials; two sabotages caught. THIS ONE MOVES MONEY ON A LIVE PROJECT, measured below.** Four items, reported before fixing where a diagnosis was asked for.
  - **WHY THE STANDALONE COMMERCIAL LINES WERE NOT IN TABLE 5, and it was nothing to do with the type.** Table 5 lists SUB-UNITS: `build()` returns undefined for a line whose assets have none (`Module1Assets.tsx:2668`), so the line has no row, cannot be priced and earns nothing however complete its areas are. FIVE live assets were in that state across FOUR types (Branded Villas phase 2, Hospitality, and the two Standalone Commercial plots carrying exactly the 1,250 and 7,500 sqm of NSA reported). Table 4 shows them because it is built from ASSETS; table 5 does not because it is built from sub-units. Same asymmetry, no filter, no type rule.
  - **A LINE WITH NO SUB-UNIT NOW GETS ONE**, the same answer the retail companion already gets: derived rather than typed, because its area is a figure the model already knows. One row per line that has none, on the line's first plot, named for that asset, category from its strategy, priced at ZERO, stating a 100% share so it follows the NSA for ever after. NOT seeded when the line has no NSA (a row of zero area is not something to price) or when the asset counts its parts in keys (there "the whole NSA" is a number of units and inventing one would be the platform deciding how many apartments a tower has).
  - **IT IS NOT FREE THE WAY THE RETAIL COMPANION IS, and that is the headline.** A companion is short-circuited before any cost line resolves; these sit on ordinary assets. MEASURED on FMP - MARINA GATE, the only project that seeds (four lines): Retail 0 -> 1,250.00 sqm, Hospitality 0 -> 18,750.00, Branded Villas P2 0 -> 14,470.40, Retail 0 -> 7,500.00, each moving that asset's NSA, BUA and GFA from ZERO, so every rate_per_nsa / rate_per_bua / rate_per_gfa line starts charging for it: debt outstanding +8.02bn, total drawdown +956m, closing cash +3.68bn over the model. That is the point (a line nobody can price also charges nothing) and it fires when the assets tab is opened, exactly like the companion reconcile. The other five projects seed nothing.
  - **A TYPED SHARE IS NOW THE STATEMENT.** The area was the only stored quantity and the share was derived, so typing a share wrote an area ONCE and the two drifted the moment land, coverage, FAR or the retail share moved the line's NSA: the share column kept reading what the user typed while the areas under it added up to something else. `SubUnit.nsaSharePct` stores it, and the store re-derives the area; ABSENT means the area is the statement, so every stored row keeps its exact behaviour and a row only starts following the NSA once somebody says it should. Proven live: FAR doubled on a seeded plot, NSA 1,250 -> 2,500, the 100% row followed to 2,500 and the line stayed exactly allocated. The area stays the ONE quantity the engine, the reports and the exports read; deriving at read time would be a second answer to "how big is this row".
  - **THE PLAN SETTLES, which is what makes it safe to run on every render**: re-planning a seeded line asks for nothing, a share already within a hundredth of a sqm produces no write, and the store returns the same state when nothing moved. Pinned by U57 rather than assumed, because a plan that never settles marks a project dirty for ever.
  - **THE TYPE IS OFFERED WHEN THE ASSET IS ADDED.** "+ Add asset here" seeded a blank-typed row and left the type to a second action on another table, though type is what the line, the schedules, the cost methods and the standards all key off. It is a picker now, offering the SAME choices as the row's own dropdown through the SAME `assetTypePatch`, so a type set at creation and one set a second later are one write. "Type not decided yet" stays on the list: an undecided type is a real state, not a gap to fill with a default. The type is on the ASSET, so one plot can hold as many differently typed assets as someone adds to it.
- **THE CARVE WAS INVISIBLE, AND BOTH TABLES NOW TOTAL. `verify-land-chain` 169 -> 175/0, `verify-retail-companion` 52 -> 53/0, `verify-consolidated-line` 38/0, with credentials; three sabotages caught (the companion land dropped from the total, the table 4 dash restored, and one the verifier caught on my own first cut).** Reported before fixing. The land was dropped in THREE places, none of them the engine: (1) `groupAssetsByPlot` filters every companion out, with a comment that was true until step 5 made the retail companion the one companion WITH land, so tables 2 and 3 never gave it a row; (2) table 4 DID have a row, and its Land cell was a hardcoded dash titled "A companion takes no land. The land carve-out is a later step", written during step 4 and never revisited when step 5 shipped the carve the next day; (3) the section caption above it still said "No land and no cost lines yet". Measured on Marina Gate: the Land column showed 23,454.12 under a 24,000 plot, 4,760.00 under 5,000 and 4,284.00 under 4,500, so **545.88 + 240.00 + 216.00 sqm had left the hosts and arrived nowhere on screen** while the engine held all three on the companions (785.88 across two plots, 216.00 on a third).
  - **THE PLOT CHECK STILL RECONCILED, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** It reads `resolveAssetPlotDraw`, which is the GROSS draw and returns nothing for a companion, so it compared 24,000 against 24,000 and printed "Assets draw exactly this plot's area" over a column that was 545.88 short. Not broken: measuring a DIFFERENT QUANTITY from the column beneath it, which is why nothing caught this. Both agree again now, and `verify-retail-companion` I5 pins the visible sum per plot on every carving project, stating in its own comment that it checks the NUMBERS and that the markup is U12i's job.
  - **THE COMPANION'S LAND ARRIVES IN PIECES**, because it pools retail from hosts on DIFFERENT plots (Marina Residences 545.88 on Land 1 plus Branded Villas 240.00 on Land 2), so table 3 files each piece under the plot it came from, at that plot's own rate, from `computeAssetLandBreakdown().splits`. Land only: its floor area IS its hosts' Retail GFA, already inside their rows, and repeating it would double count the very area the carve exists to place.
  - **TOTAL ROWS ON TABLES 3 AND 4, FROM ONE RULE.** `totalsFromRows` lives in `_shared/assetTableModel.ts` (not the tab) so a verifier can RUN it, and both tables render the same twenty cells through one `TotalCells`: agreement is structural, not two careful sums. Areas pool by the line's own `poolLineAreas`; the three ratios are quotients of the SUMS (proven: 240 sqm, not the mean of 200 and 400); an underivable column is ABSENT, never zero. Marina Gate: both totals read 33,500.00 sqm of land against 33,500.00 of plots, and agree cell for cell on all sixteen pooled figures.
  - **THE VERIFIER CAUGHT MY OWN FIRST CUT, twice.** U29h4: I banded the two total rows on the `<tr>` alone, which is exactly TRAPS 6.4, the platform-wide sticky first-cell rule that paints white over a row band. U13/U15: the shared cell component sat between two table functions and was counted as part of the input table's columns, and a `.filter()` in the results body broke "the results table orders nothing itself". Fixed by moving the arrangement into the root memo (`RetailLandView`, keyed by asset and by parcel) so the tables only render, which is the rule the rows already followed.
- **THE STANDARDS TAB SHOWS THE CALLER'S FIRM LIST WHILE THE VALUES BELONG TO THE PROJECT. Diagnosed, NOT fixed ([docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.35, [CLAUDE-TODO.md](CLAUDE-TODO.md) item 9).** Found while diagnosing a standards tab showing none of the ten seeded reference types. `GET /api/refm/asset-types` scopes to `resolveAccountId(caller)`, a straight read of the CALLER's `users.account_id`, and never consults the project's owner, while `project.assetTypeValues` is keyed by that list's entry ids. So a platform admin in a client's project types values against their own vocabulary, into the client's project. Entry ids are label-derived, so identical labels on two accounts collide BENIGNLY and nothing looks wrong; only a differing list bites. Two shapes, both verified by reading: a value under an id the owner's list lacks becomes an ORPHAN for the owner (kept, uneditable from any row, and the banner blames a removal that never happened), and a type PICK writes `asset.assetTypeId` from the caller's registry, which OUTRANKS the label in `resolveAssetTypeKey`, pinning a client's asset to a vocabulary that client does not have. **The numbers are not corrupted**: the chain resolves values by the ASSET's own key, which does not depend on who is looking. **The cost catalog shares the scoping call and NOT the consequence**, because selecting an entry STAMPS behaviour onto the line, so a foreign `catalogId` is inert; asset types are deliberately the opposite (mig 244, nothing is stamped), and that choice is exactly what exposes them. Left open because the fix needs a product decision, not a code one: whose list does a type ADDED from inside a client's project join?
- **ONE OF THE FIVE OPEN SUITE FAILURES IS CLOSED: `verify-accounts` 124/1 -> 125/0.** S10 named `app/api/refm/asset-types/route.ts` as reading `users.account_id` outside the allow-list. Read at last rather than assumed: the route queries `refm_asset_types` alone and resolves the caller through `resolveAccountId`, so it reads no `users` row at all. The check is a coarse string match on `account_id` BY DESIGN (a raw query is then caught with no help from the author) and its own comment says each step of the account model amends the list consciously; mig 241 added the cost-catalog route for exactly this reason and mig 242 added this route without doing so. A STALE ALLOW-LIST, admitted by name with the reason. It had been failing since 2026-09-07. Four failures remain.
- **A correction: the trap I filed this morning as 7.32 was a DUPLICATE number** (7.32 is the sub-unit metric, 7.33 the companion classifiers). It is **7.34**; the commit message that says 7.32 is already pushed and stands as written.

## 2026-09-09

- **CONSOLIDATION STEP 5, THE RETAIL LAND CARVE-OUT. THE FIRST STEP THAT MOVES MONEY (`63784cd7`). `verify-retail-companion` 40 -> 52/0; `verify-land-chain` 157 -> 159/0; `verify-asset-type-standards` 97 -> 98/0; 14 sabotages all caught.** The retail companion holds floor area that sits ON its hosts' land, so it takes a share and every host loses EXACTLY what it gains. BOTH HALVES FROM ONE FUNCTION (`carveRetailLand`), which returns them together and measures them SEPARATELY so a caller compares two numbers rather than reading one twice under two names; the engine reaches it through two thin wrappers, one per side, and restates nothing. The share is retail GFA over total GFA taken from the CHAIN RESULTS, so there is one definition of retail GFA; land cancels out of that ratio, so the share is a property of the massing and a plot twice as big gives up twice the land at the same percentage. Per plot at that plot's OWN rate, which is why the companion gets SPLITS: a blended rate would make its value depend on which plot it was written against. **MARINA GATE, PER ASSET:** Marina Residences 11,000.00 -> 10,454.12 sqm (82,500,000 -> 78,405,876.51), Branded Villas P1 5,000.00 -> 4,760.00 (37,500,000 -> 35,700,000), its retail companion 0 -> 785.88 (0 -> 5,894,123.49), Branded Villas P2 4,500.00 -> 4,284.00 (33,750,000 -> 32,130,000), its companion 0 -> 216.00 (0 -> 1,620,000); Podium Retail, Marina Hotel and Waterfront Retail unchanged; TOTAL 33,500.00 -> 33,500.00 sqm and 251,250,000 -> 251,250,000 unchanged. The other five projects do not move. The financial snapshot moves on Marina alone (financing, IDC, P&L, both cash flows, BS, fund fees, sweep, dividends) and the BS reconciliation's unexplained figure stays 0.0000 either side, so every movement is bridged.
  - **THE RECONCILIATION IS PER ASSET, NOT PER TOTAL**, because the carve moves land BETWEEN assets and can only ever leave the total alone: a total-only check would pass however wrong the individual figures were. I2 compares every host against its own before-and-after; I3 says in its own name that the total is necessary and not sufficient.
  - **TWO SABOTAGES WALKED THROUGH AND EXPOSED A REAL GAP**, not a weak test: `computeAssetLandSqm` and `computeAssetLandBreakdown` both answer "how much land does this asset have", the carve doubled the places that answer it, and nothing compared them. I4 now does, on every live asset. A third sabotage CRASHED rather than being caught (a mangled shell replacement wrote `undefined` into the source); a crash is not a catch and it was redone from a file.
  - **STEP 5 RETIRES A STANDING INVARIANT, DELIBERATELY.** "The area chain is one pure function that NOTHING reads" had been true since it was written. Keeping it green by restating the share in the engine would have bought a tick with two definitions of retail GFA. So it NARROWS: one named engine file, for the two figures the share is made of, pinned by C1/C1b/C1c and by a NAMED allowance in `verify-asset-type-standards` A2 whose staleness A2c catches. CLAUDE.md's wording updated the same day.

- **CONSOLIDATION STEP 4b, THE RETAIL COMPANION GETS A ROW IT CAN BE PRICED ON (`0c55cc0b`). `verify-retail-companion` 30 -> 40/0; 11 sabotages caught.** DERIVED, NOT USER-ADDED, and reported before building: its area IS the pooled retail GFA, so asking a user to retype it would put one fact in two places; the platform's existing answer to "a companion needs sub-units" is already a derivation; and without a row the asset cannot earn at all, so it would sit there as a half-built thing with nothing saying so. Rate ZERO keeps the engine byte-identical. **Per sqm per year falls out of the EXISTING rule** (Leasable on an area metric is what `rateUnitLabel` and `rateTimeBasis` already read that way), so table 5 labels and blends it with no new case. **THE RULE THAT KEEPS IT OUT OF THE USER'S WAY:** the derived row is refreshed only while it is the companion's ONLY sub-unit; the moment a second exists the split is theirs and the derivation steps back. The strip is its OWN line in table 5, not the leftover bucket. A sabotage passed and exposed a weak check: G6 asserted the rate and name survive a re-derive, and both have their own explicit fallback, so removing the spread that carries everything ELSE went unnoticed (a rent escalation would have been dropped); it now checks a field only the spread preserves.

- **THE RETAIL GFA PER SLOT IS ONE COMPANY FIGURE, ON THE STANDARDS TAB (`71df58f7`).** Reported before removing, and the stored history answered it: across ALL 1,406 stored versions the per-plot field appears on THREE assets, in one version, every one holding 40. Two cases could in principle differ per plot (two municipalities, two retail formats) but NEITHER IS SAYABLE in this model, so a per-plot figure would answer a question nobody can ask; its sibling `parkingAreaPerSlotSqm` was already a single project value and is the same kind of quantity. The column, the drawer field and the plot-level classification are gone, and `landChainIsEmpty` no longer consults the retired field, so a legacy value cannot make an empty chain look started. **The one historical value is not lost**: hydrate lifts a stored per-plot figure onto the project and ONLY into an absence, proven live (Marina now reads 40 without anyone retyping it). Also fixed: the type-only-key census ran on ALL assets while our grouping excludes companions, so step 4's companions read as flattened pairs our key had never been offered.

- **CONSOLIDATION STEP 4, THE RETAIL COMPANION, COSTED AT ZERO (`6eb007a5`). `verify-retail-companion` 30/0 new; 15 sabotages all caught first time.** Ground-floor retail is an ASSET, not a column: the developer SELLS the apartments and HOLDS the shops, and those are two strategies. ONE POOLED COMPANION PER LINE, not per plot; the areas POOL, which needs no rule of its own since the chain has already run per plot and floor areas add. Live: Marina's Branded Villas line yields one companion of 1,920 sqm (1,320 + 600). Costed at zero is not a stub: `computeAssetCost` short-circuits any companion before a cost line is resolved and Rule 2 gives it no land, and the verifier asserts BOTH rather than trusting them, offering the companion a whole 9,999 sqm plot to prove it draws nothing. Reuses the existing mechanism, discriminated by `companionType`, carrying NO `parentAssetId` (a line has many hosts) which is what makes both Operate sync passes skip it. **"COMPANION" HAD COME TO MEAN "OPERATE" IN SEVEN PREDICATES**, which would have filed a Lease retail companion into hospitality revenue and opex; every classifier reads the STRATEGY now, measured first (every live companion is Operate, so all six snapshots hash identical). **The proof that matters is not the refactor hash**: the verifier builds each project's companions from its OWN chain, injects them and re-runs the engine, byte-identical on all six, with a guard that at least one real line builds retail so the pass cannot be vacuous.

- **A BAND GOES ON THE CELLS, AND THE CAUSE WAS ONE GLOBAL CSS RULE (`ff6cdb5a`). `verify-land-chain` 149 -> 156/0.** Reported three times on three different rows and fixed twice as if row-specific. `app/globals.css` carries an unclassed platform-wide `td:first-child { position: sticky; left: 0; background: var(--color-surface); z-index: 1 }`, so the first cell of EVERY table in the application is a frozen column with its own opaque background painted above the row: a `<tr>` background is invisible there, always, and which half looks wrong depends only on where the colSpans fall. `<th>` is untouched, which is why the navy headers always looked right. Three band objects now, each spread onto every cell; the global rule has no `!important` so an inline background wins and the stickiness survives. The verifier pins the CAUSE (U29h3 asserts the global rule still exists) rather than the instances. Recorded as [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 6.4.

- **THOUSANDS SEPARATORS ON THE CELL INPUTS, AND THE UNIT COUNT ASKS THE ASSET (`f0dc9117`). `verify-land-chain` 145 -> 154/0.** Table 3 showed 12,599 units against 19,999 sqm of NSA at 170 sqm a unit, which is 118: every surface resolves a sub-unit's metric as `asset.subUnitMetric ?? u.metric`, and `computeAssetUnitCount` asked the ROW alone, so a row left at 'units' while its asset counts AREA was an area on screen and a COUNT in the engine. Measured across all six projects before changing: exactly one count moves (to 0, so the chain derives 118) and NO live project carries a `rate_per_unit` line, so no money moved and all six hash identical. Separators REUSE the AccountingNumberInput contract (grouped idle, RAW while focused, its comma-tolerant parser) so a display rounding can never round-trip into storage; opt-in is per field and average unit size KEEPS two decimals on the founder's call, because it divides into the unit count.

- **TABLE 5: ONE BAND, AN EDITABLE RATE, UNIQUE IDS, AND NO NIGHTLY RATE PER SQM (`13eff94c`, `b365b6bf`). `verify-land-chain` 139 -> 145/0.** The Rate cell had been read-only text since the table was built. The "carried over" rate was a SHARED ID: every add button minted `${prefix}_${Date.now()}`, millisecond resolution, so a double-click made two rows one row wearing two coats (an edit patched both, a delete removed both); all four mints go through one monotonic minter now, proven over 500 mints in one millisecond. A hotel line blended to "9 per sqm", which is 140 keys at 850 a night over 13,300 sqm: a rate now carries its TIME BASIS and a per-night blend is refused with the reason named. The grand total lost its blended rate entirely: residential, hospitality and retail have no average between them, and sharing a time basis does not make one meaningful.

- **THE SUB-UNIT TABLE: ONE HEADER ROW, A CHECK THAT FIRES, AND TOTALS PER LINE (`ded6eda2`).** `groupSubUnitsByAsset` was handed the LINE's members but the WHOLE project's sub-units, once per line, so every other line's rows came back as an "Unassigned" orphan group under every line: 4 sub-units showed as 16 rows on one project and 15 as 105 on another, and every header printed the project total. **The check had NEVER FIRED ONCE**: it read the asset's entered NSA and every live asset has 0, while the chain sat one table above with the answer. It is now opt-in like the rest of the chain and SAYS which NSA it used; live, the first check on the platform fires (Branded Villas, chain NSA 27,599.1, parts 26,600.0, under-allocated).

- **THE CHAIN RUNS PER PLOT, AND THE MERGE SUMS RESULTS (`7f4d907d`, `95d39447`).** The merge happened one table too early: massing belongs to a piece of ground, so pooling before the chain ran left a merged row with no single answer and Landscape %, Average Unit Size and Parking Ratio printed dashes. Five tables now, and the merge derives its ratios from its OWN sums. Two defects found while proving it: **the merge was not unconditional** (`consolidationKey` dropped strategy while the grouping loop kept building `phase|type|strategy` beside it, so four green checks asserted the key nothing groups by), and **absence is not disagreement** (the first live two-plot line reported five conflicts, every one "<a value> vs null").

- **ONE IDENTITY FOR A TYPE, A REAL DROPDOWN, AND A HOME FOR RETAIL PARKING (`db6c43cb`).** The Type cell was a `<datalist>`, which FILTERS its options by what is already typed, so a row reading "Branded Villas" offered one option and an unlisted label offered none. It also wrote the LABEL and never the reference, while the values lookup demanded the reference alone: 11 of 12 live typed assets carried a label with no reference, which is why one plot derived its units and its neighbour on the same type showed dashes from Average Unit Size onward. Identity resolves ONCE now (reference, else the label normalised into the same id space), and the cell is a real `<select>` writing both. This CLOSES the type field, reversing the 2026-09-07 "free text with suggestions" decision.

## 2026-09-07

- **THE STANDARDS TAB'S TWO HALVES ARE VISIBLY SEPARATE (2026-09-08e). `verify-asset-type-standards` 79 -> 80/0; all eight tab verifiers green.** The Save button sat at the END of the row, past the project values, so it read as saving the whole row when it only saves the firm's list. Every control that needs Save (name, category, order, Save, Remove) now sits contiguously on the LEFT behind a 3px divider on a tinted ground, and this project's values sit to its right; each half's group header states its own behaviour ("Press Save to apply a change" against "Saves as you type, like every other input"), and the Save tooltip says explicitly that it does not save the values on the right. **S3b pins the arrangement by ORDER**, that Save renders before the values, and a sabotage moving it back to the row end fails it by name.
  - A first version of that check also required Save and Remove within 400 characters of each other and failed on the CORRECT layout, because the tooltips are longer than that: it was asserting the length of a title attribute rather than the arrangement of the row. Replaced with the ordering clause, which is what the invariant actually is.

- **THE BUILD COST AND REVENUE RATE COLUMNS ARE HIDDEN, NOT DROPPED (2026-09-08d). `verify-asset-type-standards` 78 -> 79/0; all eight tab verifiers green.** Nothing reads either value yet, so a rate on screen invites the question of where it applies and today the answer is nowhere. The construction cost, the revenue rate and its unit column are removed from the standards tab, and the build/revenue clause is removed from the asset card caption too, since leaving it would have made that caption the ONLY place showing a number the user can no longer see or edit.
  - **REMOVED FROM THE MARKUP, not gated behind a condition.** A `{false && ...}` would still grep as present, which is the 2026-08-20 lesson; J3b now fails on any `{false &&` in the file, and a sabotage that hid the cost column that way fails it by name.
  - **STORAGE IS UNTOUCHED**: `AssetTypeValues` keeps all three fields, `setAssetTypeValue` still merges them, the route still validates them, and the live project's `{"branded-villas":{"revenueRate":20000,"parkingRatio":1,"avgUnitSizeSqm":260,"constructionCostPerSqm":11000}}` is verified still present after the change. J2 and J3 were re-aimed from asserting the columns exist to asserting the fields and their round trip survive, which is the promise that matters: the columns come back with the wiring, and the values will be waiting.
  - Capex and revenue are untouched and keep taking their rates exactly where they always did.

- **ONE ASSET, ONE PLOT: THE MULTI-PARCEL CASE IS DROPPED (2026-09-08c). All eight tab verifiers green; Marina Gate still 7249 cells, same sha256, and this time WITH chain inputs live on it.** Two plots for one building is modelled by merging the plots or by splitting the asset, which is the same model with one less special case. **Measured before removing anything: 1406 stored versions, 9399 asset rows, ZERO used a multi-parcel split**, so nothing live was lost, including in old versions that can still be opened. By contrast 6860 of those rows sit on a sentinel parcel, which is why the unplotted group stays.
  - **REMOVED**: the parcel-count cell, the split editor and its "+ Add Parcel Allocation" button, the `addSplit` / `removeSplit` / `updateSplit` helpers, `assetParcelCount`, and the splits branches of `assetDrawFromParcel` and `primaryParcelId`. The plot check is now a simple sum of the assets under the plot rather than a scan for slices filed elsewhere.
  - **DELIBERATELY KEPT**: the ENGINE still reads a legacy `multiParcelSplits` (`computeAssetLandSqm`, `computeAssetLandBreakdown`, `resolveAssetAreaMetrics`, the workbook), so a snapshot that somehow carries one computes rather than silently losing its land; there is simply no longer any way to create one. `primaryParcelId` files such an asset in the UNPLOTTED group, so it is visible rather than hidden, which T2 now pins.
  - **TWO CHECKS RE-AIMED, both because the feature they described changed**: T2 stopped testing "files under the plot it draws most from" (no such rule remains) and now tests that a legacy split is still visible; U3 stopped asserting the count cell exists and now asserts nothing in the tab can CREATE a split. And **F2 was stale by success**: it asserted no live project carried chain inputs, which stopped being true the moment the founder started using the tables, so it now asserts what still must hold, that chain inputs round-trip verbatim through hydrate and no area input moves.
  - **HEADERS NO LONGER TRUNCATE.** Table headers wrap onto as many lines as they need (cells stay on one line) and both colgroups are resized: identity columns trimmed where they were wider than their text (input table asset 240 to 196, type 220 to 168; results table asset 200 to 168) so the numeric columns can take the space (input 72 to 90, results 82 to 78 across 17 columns), and the input table's group header shortened to "Chain inputs". Results minimum width drops 1720 to 1596.
  - **A DEFECT THE LIVE DATA EXPOSED**: the founder's project held `revenueRate: 20000` with NO `revenueRateUnit`, because the standards tab wrote the rate and the unit independently and the unit select only writes on change. A rate with no unit names no basis, so `describeValues` correctly showed "Revenue not set" while a rate sat there. Entering a rate now commits the unit shown beside it (defaulting to per sqm), the same rule the account route always enforced.

- **THE ASSETS TABLE SPLITS INTO INPUTS AND RESULTS (2026-09-08b). `verify-land-chain` 59 -> 63/0; all eight tab verifiers green; Marina Gate still 7249 cells, same sha256.** Two tables stacked on the page: **1. what you enter** (plot, asset, type, strategy, phase, land area and the five chain percentages) and **2. what the chain produces** (the WHOLE cascade, read only, land utilised through footprint, landscape, retail and lobby GFA, total and main GFA, net saleable, units, all three slot counts, both parking splits, to Total BUA). With the derived columns out of the input table, neither table has to choose between being complete and being readable: identity columns get 240px and 220px, results columns a uniform 82px. The plot check stays on the input table, since it is about what was entered.
  - **THE TWO CANNOT DRIFT APART, BY CONSTRUCTION.** `buildAssetRows` resolves the ordered, grouped rows ONCE and both tables render that array; the plot header is one shared component with the check switched on for the input table only. So a row in the results table is the row at the same position above it, rather than the output of a second sort that a later edit could separate.
  - **A CHECK THAT DID NOT BITE, CAUGHT BY ITS OWN SABOTAGE.** The first U13 asserted only that both tables were PASSED the same array, and a sabotage that re-sorted the rows INSIDE the results component passed it happily. Re-scoped to the results component's own body, which must contain no `.sort(`, no `.filter(` and no grouping call of its own; the same sabotage now fails it by name. Same family as TRAPS 3.19 (asserting presence where the invariant was exclusivity).

- **THE TABLES ARE MADE READABLE, AND THE SUB-UNIT TABLE'S NUMBERS DIAGNOSED (2026-09-08). `verify-land-chain` 55 -> 59/0; all seven coupled verifiers green; Marina Gate still 7249 cells, same sha256.** Founder feedback on the live tables: names and types truncated to a few characters, too many derived columns to fit, and the sub-unit table showing numbers that had been right on the cards. **Diagnosed against the live snapshot before changing anything**, comparing what the table renders with what the card rendered, row by row:
  - **THE RATE WAS THE REAL DEFECT, and it hit every row.** The table passed the project's number scale to a per-sqm RATE, so at `thousands`/0dp an 18,500 per sqm price rendered as **19**, 1,400 as 1, 850 as 1, 1,600 as 2. A rate is a price per unit of area, not a project total, and the card always rendered rates at full scale. Fixed, and pinned: U7 fails if the project scale is ever applied to a rate again (sabotage-tested).
  - **A COUNT NOBODY CAN DERIVE WAS PRINTED AS ZERO.** With no unit size there is nothing to divide the area by, and the card showed a dash; the table asserted "0 units", a different and false claim. Three of Marina Gate's four sub-units were affected. Now a dash with the reason on hover (U8), and a stored unit size of 0 reads as not set rather than as a real zero (U9). Same blank-versus-zero rule the standards tab already follows.
  - **THE NAMES WERE FAITHFUL, NOT A MAPPING ERROR.** "Marina Residences" really is that sub-unit's stored name and Podium Retail's really is empty; the cards showed the same. Area matched `computeSubUnitArea` on every row. So the mapping was right and only the two presentation rules above were wrong, which is worth stating because the reported symptom (a sub-unit named after its asset) was the data telling the truth.
  - **LAYOUT**: fixed table layout with an explicit colgroup, identity columns given real width (asset 210px, type 190px) and derived ones narrowed to 92px; the derived columns cut from eighteen to the **six a user checks** (land utilised, total GFA, net saleable, units, parking area, total BUA), with the full cascade in the open row where the chain already lists every step (U10).

- **LAND PLANNING STEP 3: THE ASSETS CARD BECOMES TWO TABLES (2026-09-07g, two commits). All seven coupled verifiers green: tab2-fixes 47, tab2-pass2 22, tab2-pass3 30, capex-structure 134, land-chain 42 -> 55, asset-type-standards 78, strategy-switch 84. Marina Gate byte-identical after BOTH commits: 7249 numeric cells of `computeFinancialsSnapshot`, same sha256.** One table where each row is an asset, grouped under the plot it draws from, following the reference workbook's column order (identity, land, the chain's percent inputs, then the derived cascade left to right ending at Total BUA), plus a second table holding every sub-unit with the parent asset picked on the add row. Phase became a column and the plot grouping won, because the chain is per plot.
  - **GROUPING IS A PURE MODULE** (`_shared/assetTableModel.ts`) with three cases, and the third is the one that is easy to miss: an asset on one parcel sits under it; a multi-parcel asset sits under the parcel it draws MOST from with the parcel count in its row opening the editor, so the exception does not reshape the table; and an asset naming no real parcel (a weighted-average or custom-rate sentinel, which the hub project actually uses) falls into an UNPLOTTED group rather than being hidden or silently filed under plot one. The per-plot check counts EVERY draw on that plot including the slice of an asset filed elsewhere, and companions are excluded because they carry no land.
  - **TWO COMMITS ON PURPOSE.** Seven verifiers assert against this file's RAW SOURCE, including a proximity rule (`!asset.isCompanion &&` within 500 characters above five markers) and an ordering rule (a testid after a specific const), so a one-shot rewrite would have broken them by construction. Commit 1 was additive: the card was untouched and became the row's expandable drawer, so every marker survived. Commit 2 removed from the drawer exactly the four fields the row now carries (name, phase, the free-text type, Delete) and re-aimed the ONE check that genuinely pinned a moved element, `verify-asset-type-standards` G5, from the drawer's datalist to the row's, sabotage-tested by stripping the list attribute. The other six needed no re-aim, which is the additive-first sequence paying for itself.
  - **A RED CHECK FROM STEP 2 SURFACED HERE**, and it had been red since that commit because only one verifier was run then: `verify-asset-type-standards` A1 forbids `parkingRatio` anywhere under `src/core/calculations`, and `landChain.ts` names it as its own PARAMETER shape while reading no project and no table. The definition file is now excluded with the reason written down, and a new A2 proves the exclusion is safe by asserting nothing in the engine calls that file. This is exactly the cross-cutting breakage the narrowed pre-commit rule defers, caught one commit later than it should have been.
  - The new surface gained its own coverage (land-chain sections T and U, 13 checks): the grouping rules, the plot sum, the unplotted group, the companion exclusion, the row's inputs and derived cascade, the parcel-count cell, the sub-unit parent picker, no field having two homes, and every one of the nine drawer-only items still having a home. Sabotaging the plot sum to count only assets filed under it fails T4 and T6 by name. The dead `PhaseAssetSection` and its now-unused imports were DELETED rather than left behind a `false &&` condition, which would grep as present (the 2026-08-20 lesson). NOT browser-verified.

- **LAND PLANNING STEP 2: THE TOP-DOWN AREA CHAIN, AS A PURE FUNCTION AND A READ-ONLY PANEL (2026-09-07f). `verify-land-chain` NEW, 42/0 with credentials; type-check and lint clean; two sabotages.** `src/core/calculations/landChain.ts` derives the reference land structure's areas from land, utilisation and FAR: land utilised, footprint, landscape, retail and lobby GFA, total GFA, main asset GFA, net saleable, units, parking slots and areas, and total BUA. It imports NOTHING, and **nothing imports it except one read-only panel** (`components/modules/_shared/LandChainSection.tsx`) on the asset card, which shows derived beside entered and never writes a derived figure back.
  - **CHECKED AGAINST THE SOURCE, NOT AGAINST THE SUMMARY.** Two real rows of "Inputs - Plots Info" are reproduced to the cent, one without retail (Resort 5 Star: BUA 171,020.43) and one with it (High End Apartments: BUA 32,132.4384), every intermediate step included. **Four things the summary compressed, now stated:** (1) total GFA is utilised land x FAR, NOT the footprint and not the gross plot; (2) landscape is a share of the UTILISED area, not of the footprint; (3) **retail parking divides by ONE fixed company figure** (`'Inputs - Consolidated'!$F$213`, the Retail-combined 25 sqm/slot), never by the plot's own asset type, so it is its OWN per-asset input rather than a lookup; (4) the workbook does NOT round units (1221.5745 keys, 108.92352 apartments) and neither does the chain, since rounding here would move every figure after it. Also confirmed: Max Floors is stored and drives nothing, and with retail at zero the main asset keeps the WHOLE GFA, lobby included, because the reference branches rather than subtracting.
  - **THE TWO VOCABULARIES MEET ONLY HERE**, and their outermost tiers have opposite names, so every compared row names both: reference Net Saleable = platform NSA, reference Total GFA = platform BUA (the building, no parking), reference BUA Area = platform GFA (everything, parking in). Getting that backwards is the one mistake that makes a correct chain look wrong.
  - **A BLANK INPUT IS NOT A ZERO**: an absent input stops the chain and is recorded as a named gap with a sentence the panel prints, rather than multiplying by zero and reporting a confident 0; a typed 0 computes (zero coverage gives no footprint, all landscape, and still a GFA). Sub-units still WIN over the derived unit count.
  - **THE ENGINE DOES NOT MOVE**: `computeFinancialsSnapshot` is byte-identical on a two-phase mixed-use fixture with chain inputs on every asset and without, **with a positive control** so the check cannot pass vacuously. The first control chosen (parking area) moved nothing and was WRONG, correctly: that fixture carries no parking area and charges parking per BAY, so the control now bumps BUA, which the fixture's `rate_per_bua` line genuinely reads. No live project carries a chain (all six re-probed).
  - Two sabotages: applying FAR to the footprint instead of the utilised land fails eight workbook checks by name; making a resolver import the chain fails C1. NOT browser-verified.

- **THE NAMES ARE THE FIRM'S, THE VALUES ARE THE PROJECT'S (2026-09-07e, mig 244 APPLIED). Stamping is DELETED. `verify-asset-type-standards` 68 -> 77/0 with credentials; applier 7/0 dry then 7/0 apply; type-check and lint clean; two sabotages.** An asset type has two halves and they belong in different places. The VOCABULARY (name, category, order) stays on the account, so `refm_asset_types` becomes a pure names table, exactly what `refm_cost_catalog` is. The VALUES (unit size, parking ratio and its basis, build cost per sqm, revenue rate and its unit, plus the parking area per slot that `refm_account_standards` used to hold) move into the PROJECT SNAPSHOT as `project.assetTypeValues` keyed by the type's entry id, and `project.parkingAreaPerSlotSqm`.
  - **THAT REMOVES THE NEED FOR STAMPING, which was only ever a workaround.** Values on an account table could not be read by the engine, so they had to be COPIED onto the asset at selection time and could go stale. As project inputs they need no copy: editing one is an ordinary input change, so it recalculates when the chain lands, the change log records it (paths like `project.assetTypeValues.branded-villas.avgUnitSizeSqm` fall out of `diffObject` with no grammar change), versions capture it, and nobody re-picks anything. `Asset.assetTypeStandards` is DELETED; `Asset.assetTypeId` stays and becomes load-bearing as the lookup key. Checked before building rather than assumed: the project normaliser SPREADS (`...makeDefaultProject(), ...legacyProject`) so the new field needs no whitelist edit, and the diff walker recurses into nested objects.
  - **THE VERIFIER CAUGHT A LIVE ARTEFACT OF THE OLD DESIGN.** Marina Gate failed F1 because a type had been picked in the deployed app an hour earlier, writing a stamp (label + category, no numbers, so nothing was lost). Hydrate now STRIPS a legacy stamp on BOTH wrapper paths via `stripLegacyAssetTypeStamp`, outermost so it sees the final asset shape; without it the spread would have carried a dead second home for these values forever and re-saved it on every write.
  - **THE TAB IS NOW TWO-SOURCED**, with the split stated in its header row: the firm's list on the left with an explicit Save per row, this project's values on the right with NO Save button, because nothing else in the model has one. Values write through one store action (`setAssetTypeValue`) that owns the merge, clears a field with `undefined` rather than keeping the key, and drops an entry left holding nothing. Value cells are draft-or-store with no syncing effect, so a half-typed "1." and an empty cell both survive while an external change still shows through. The tab needs an open project for the values half and says so.
  - **REMOVING A TYPE KEEPS THE PROJECT'S NUMBERS** (`orphanedValueTypeIds`), surfaced as "values for types no longer in your firm's list", because an account-level edit must not delete a project's data. **THE MODULE 6 GATE MOVED FROM NON-ECONOMIC TO INACTIVE**: these are real economic levers that no calculation reads YET, so they are annotated rather than reclassified, and the day the chain lands the branch simply stops firing. The type reference stays non-economic. **REORDER IS NOW ONE BATCHED WRITE** (it was ten sequential round trips, about four seconds live, and not atomic: a mid-flight failure left a half-applied order).
  - Migration 244 was found ALREADY APPLIED to the live database when the applier first ran, which its own pre-drop probe reported as a crash ("column does not exist"); the probe is now tolerant of the drop having happened, since an idempotent migration may legitimately meet a database that has had it. What it discarded is recorded in the migration header rather than lost silently: one row (Branded Villas: unit 200, parking 2, build 11000, revenue 21500 per sqm), re-entered on the project.
  - NOT in this step, unchanged: nothing is wired to the engine, capex and revenue still take their rates where they always did, and no existing project's areas moved (section F re-proved across every live snapshot).

- **LAND AND ASSET DATA MANAGEMENT: THE STANDARDS GET THEIR OWN TAB, AND THE LIST BECOMES THE FIRM'S (2026-09-07d, mig 243 APPLIED). `verify-asset-type-standards` 45 -> 68/0 with credentials; applier 8/0 dry then 8/0 apply; type-check clean; two sabotages.** The dialog is gone: `Module1AssetStandards.tsx` is **Module 1 tab 4, "Asset Types & Standards"**, built on the OpEx table pattern (navy header row, one editable row per type, an add row at the foot, remove at the end of each row).
  - **PLACEMENT, and what it displaced**: the tab sits **immediately before Assets & Sub-units**, the same adjacency rule Fund Terms follows after Parties: it is the source the next tab consumes, so it reads in the order the work happens. It renumbered the three tabs after it (Assets 4 -> 5, Capex 5 -> 6, Financing 6 -> 7); **the KEYS are unchanged**, so every e2e test (which clicks `m1-tab-costs` and friends), the `fmp:trace-to` event, the PDF's own `Tab N:` taxonomy and the 16-sheet Excel map are all untouched. `verify-platform-guide` would have FAILED on a tab with no guide entry, so `module1/asset-standards` was written into `guideContent.ts` (intro + 5 steps + review) and the tour picks it up structurally (its step count is derived, not hardcoded).
  - **THE LIST IS A STARTING SET, NOT A FIXTURE.** Every row can be renamed (the entry id survives a rename, so an asset stamped from it keeps pointing at it), re-categorised (category is an editable field with the three reference categories as suggestions), removed and REORDERED. Ordering is `sort_order`, NULLABLE on the mig-229 rule (NULL is "never reordered" and falls back to label order; 0 is a real first position), written by a new `PUT` as a whole-list dense write that refuses an id that is not on the account. A rate with no unit names no basis, so the route REQUIRES the unit with a rate rather than guessing one, and neither is stamped without it.

Capex and revenue take their rates exactly where they did yesterday.

One self-inflicted bug found and fixed on the way: the new live check E10 ran an expected-refusal INSERT without a SAVEPOINT, and since a failed statement aborts the whole Postgres transaction, the section crashed instead of reporting (the appliers already used savepoints for exactly this; the check now does too).

Recorded in `seats.ts` (the counter's header), `inviteSeatState` (the one reservation arithmetic that would re-key), CLAUDE-TODO and here. **THE PAIN ASSESSMENT, measured against the current implementation:**
  - **What makes the split EASY (deliberate choices that pay off)**: the count is COMPUTED LIVE, never stored, so there is no counter data to migrate; the reservation arithmetic lives in ONE function (`inviteSeatState`); the step-6/7 holder surfaces do NO seat math (assignment is seat-free by construction), so `team.ts` and `deleteQueue.ts` are untouched by a re-key; and step 3's decoupling of seats from project membership means per-platform seats will not fight project data.
  - **What makes it REAL WORK**: (1) the person-platform fact DOES NOT EXIST: `users.account_id` says who is on the account, platform-less by design, so the split needs a new fact (a platform column on `account_invites` plus a person-platform table or equivalent); (2) the `seats` entitlement key is ONE plan-level number with no platform dimension (`plan_permissions(plan_key, feature_key)`), and its single spelling is pinned across the plan builder, pricing page and override screen, so the split either adds a platform dimension there or mints per-platform keys, a real schema decision; (3) **the deepest coupling is not in seats at all**: `users.subscription_plan` is ONE global plan per user resolved by `setUserPlan` and the gate, while per-platform seats presuppose per-platform PLANS, so the seat split must ride with (or behind) per-platform plan resolution, which is the larger project; (4) `checkSeatForMember`'s "already seated = on the account" stops being sufficient when being on the account no longer implies a seat on THAT platform (moot today because the boundary only admits account people); (5) small UI: the Team tab's single seats line becomes per-platform display.

  - **ONLY THE HOLDER MANAGES THE TEAM** (`resolveAccountHolder`, the step-4 one-place rule; a member gets `not_holder` and no surface). **THE BOUNDARY IS REUSED, NOT RESTATED, and is STRICTER here than the admin route**: assignment requires `checkAccountBoundary` to answer `same_account` (or `owner_admin` for the operator's own internal account, or `pre_migration`); a `candidate_admin` answer is REFUSED on this route, because FMP staff are attached by FMP through the admin screen, never pulled in by a client. **NO SEAT ARITHMETIC, by construction**: since step 3 a seat is held by being ON the account, and this engine can only name people already on it (verify-seats G4 re-aimed accordingly: the retired claim "no owner-adding route exists" was replaced with the true invariant, that the holder path can never consume a seat).
  - **A foreign or missing project gets ONE answer** (`no_project`), so the holder route confirms nothing about other people's projects; the owner is immutable in both writes; membership rows record `added_by` = the holder.
  - **Live, real engine, probe trio** (firm holder + wired member + outsider + a real `refm_projects` row): member sees no surface (L25); holder sees exactly their project and person (L26); assign writes an authored editor row (L27); owner-role and self both refused (L28); ANOTHER holder gets `no_project` on the same id (L29); an off-account person gets `not_on_account` (L30); remove deletes the row (L31). The admin screen and route are byte-untouched (F8), the operator fallback. Direct verifiers only, per instruction: `verify-accounts` 90/0 and `verify-seats` 43/0 (edited for G4), with credentials; no full suite run or backgrounded. NOT browser-verified; the card's new section is the thing to click on live.

- **THE ACCOUNT MODEL, STEP 5: INVITES (2026-09-04, mig 240 APPLIED). `verify-accounts` grows 55 -> 74; the platform's first client-facing account surface.** A client invites a team member by EMAIL from a "Your team" card on the dashboard; the person signs up through `register?invite=<token>` and lands under the client's account as a MEMBER (attached at birth, so the 239 trigger mints no personal account and the step-4 gate inherits the client's plan the moment they exist). The invite EXPIRES (7 days, one constant) and is SINGLE USE.
  - **THE SEAT IS RESERVED AT CREATE**, per the brief: people-on-the-account + open unexpired invites + 1 vs the limit, reusing the step-3 arithmetic (`countAccountSeats` / `resolveSeatLimit` / `seatsAllow`); redemption RE-CHECKS with the reservation counted, not grown, so only a limit lowered since create can refuse there. Proven live: a pro holder (1 seat, owner in it) was refused at CREATE (L20). Re-inviting REPLACES the open invite (partial unique per account+email never fires in the happy path); revoking frees the seat; an unsendable invite email ROLLS BACK the reservation (an invite nobody received is a seat nobody can use). An existing user cannot be invited: one person, one account.
  - **ATTACH + CONSUME IS ONE TRANSACTION**: `redeem_account_invite` (mig 240) locks the invite FOR UPDATE, verifies the email matches, inserts the attached user and stamps `consumed_by`, all or nothing; the applier CAUSED the mismatch case and proved NOTHING was left behind. Token discipline is mig 213's: SHA-256 only, the raw token lives in the email link alone.
  - **THE ORDINARY SIGNUP PATH IS UNTOUCHED, and the fork sits exactly where the brief put it**: after the shared validation and captcha, BEFORE the pre-launch register gate, so the gate never blocks an invited person (the client pays for them); E8 pins the byte order, E11 pins the ordinary path's markers. The redeemed email also joins `modeling_access_whitelist` (best effort), because the SIGNIN gate shares that list and a paid member must be able to log in even while the hub is gated. The register page resolves `?invite=` server-side (`previewInviteByToken`), renders the form with the email prefilled and LOCKED plus a "Join Your Team" header, and falls through to the ordinary page on any invalid token.
  - **THE CARD DECIDES NOTHING**: `TeamInvitesCard` renders only what `GET /api/account/invites` says (eligible, seat numbers, open invites); a member, a solo one-seat holder, or an error all render no card. All rules live in `src/shared/account/invites.ts` (the engine), the route only maps to HTTP. Members are refused (`not_holder`): only the holder spends seats.
  - Applier `scripts/apply-migration-240.ts` 14/0 dry + 14/0 apply. Direct verifier ONLY, per instruction (no full suite, not even backgrounded): `verify-accounts` 74/0 with credentials, whose live half runs the REAL engine and rpc end to end (refusals return before the email send, so the suite never mails anyone). D1's caller pin became an allow-list (the invite engine legitimately reuses `resolveAccountHolder` to ask "is this caller the holder"); S10 grew by `invites.ts`. NOT browser-verified; the invite email itself (Brevo delivery + the rendered template) is the one leg only a real send can prove.

- **THE ACCOUNT MODEL, STEP 4: THE MEMBER GATE (2026-09-04, no migration). `verify-accounts` grows 42 -> 55.** A member is not a client: `resolveUserGate` now resolves plan, lapse and grace from the ACCOUNT HOLDER for a member, forces `projectLimit 0` and `archiveAllowed false` (no allowance of their own), and keeps the member's own `role`. **THE LOOKUP HAPPENS IN ONE PLACE**: `resolveAccountHolder` (in `accountBoundary.ts`, beside the boundary rule) is called by `resolveUserGate` and by NOTHING else, pinned by D1 which walks src/ and app/ and fails on any second caller, because a call site that resolved the MEMBER instead of the holder would show a paying client's colleague the request-access storefront. `billingUserId` feeds every plan-shaped read (the subscription row, the plan cells, the overrides, the trial anchor; D2 counts the exact call sites); both storefront surfaces (the `/refm` layout redirect and the dashboard access card) read the gate, so inheritance fixes them without either being touched. An unreadable holder DENIES for a member, never guesses; `resolveAccountHolder` itself fails toward SELF (pre-239 behaviour, and a member misread as self has plan 'none', which denies).
  - **MEMBERS DROP OUT OF THE NO-PLAN CHASERS.** The daily access-reminder scan (`runAccessReminderScan`) excludes account members alongside the existing trial-request and subscription disqualifiers, via bulk `accountMemberIds` (fails toward EMPTY: the cost of that direction is one unneeded email, never a lost one). Admin campaigns: a FILTER audience whose plan filter targets `'none'` excludes members and REPORTS the count (`membersExcluded`, the same pattern as `adminsExcluded`); an explicit pick still includes one deliberately.

S10's reader allow-list is UNCHANGED (all three new call sites read through the helpers, no raw column touch). Direct verifier: `verify-accounts` 55/0 with credentials.

- **THE ACCOUNT MODEL, STEP 3: SEATS COUNT THE ACCOUNT'S PEOPLE (2026-09-04, no migration). `verify-seats` 43/0, `verify-accounts` 42/0.** `countAccountSeats` stops walking every project the holder owns and collecting whoever sat in the membership rows (arithmetic that was right over a set with NO BOUNDARY, which is how adding another client's user consumed YOUR seat), and counts the account's people DIRECTLY: the account resolved through `accounts.owner_user_id`, then `users.account_id = account`, holder added unconditionally. **PROJECT membership never creates a seat**: a person occupies one by BEING ON THE ACCOUNT, so the limit will bite where people join the account (signup today, invites in step 5), and with the step-2 boundary in place a member add can only name people already seated (or a platform admin, whose add still costs a would-be seat exactly as before, unchanged on purpose). The interface (`SeatUsage.used/userIds`), `checkSeatForMember`, `resolveSeatLimit`, the per-client override and the plan limits are all UNTOUCHED; `SeatUsage.sources` is gone (no reader existed). A pre-239 database or a holder with no accounts row REFUSES loudly rather than under-counting (the route already turns a thrown count into a refusal). **PARITY MEASURED BEFORE COMMIT, per the brief's stop condition**: old walk vs new count computed live for all 8 accounts, every one 1 = 1, NO ACCOUNT MOVED. `verify-seats` sections B/C re-aimed (B3 is now a permanent tripwire failing if the counter ever reads a project or membership table again; new H10 executes the live count for every real holder and compares it against an independent REST tally, 8/8 agree). `verify-accounts` S10 allow-list grew by `seats.ts`, its third and only new reader. Direct verifiers only, per the narrowed 2026-09-04 practice: `verify-seats` (the change), `verify-accounts` (its own allow-list edit).

- **STEP 2 REFINEMENT: ADMINS ARE NOT OFFERED IN THE PERSON LIST (2026-09-04, same day).** `listAccountCandidates` now returns a client owner's account ONLY; the platform admin no longer appears as a standing dropdown option. **The server exemption STANDS**: `checkAccountBoundary` still accepts an admin candidate (the future FMP-advisor shape), so the list is deliberately NARROWER than the write, never the reverse. A client owner with a NULL account (broken invariant) is offered nobody, matching the check's NULL refusal. `verify-accounts` 42: L12 proves the write still accepts the admin live, new L13b proves the list does not offer them, L13 tightened to "their account ONLY". **Same session, DASHBOARD VISIBILITY DIAGNOSED (read-only, no fix needed)**: the REAL dashboard resolver (`listProjects`, the function `GET /api/refm/projects` calls) was executed for ALL 8 live user ids against production. Every client sees only their own: abuzarsadiq sees "ABC", waleedbakhat sees "Jadan Maroom", the other five clients see NOTHING, the admin sees the two FMP projects, and ZERO rows were reachable without ownership (all membership rows are owner self-seeds). The one leg not exercised is NextAuth session-to-userId resolution, shared by every authenticated route.

- **THE ACCOUNT MODEL, STEP 2: THE BOUNDARY IS ENFORCED (2026-09-04, no migration). `verify-accounts` grows 25 -> 41.** One client's user can no longer be attached to another client's project. The rule is stated ONCE in `src/shared/admin/accountBoundary.ts` and enforced in the ONE place a membership row is written, `POST /api/admin/project-members`: the candidate must be on the SAME ACCOUNT as the project's owner, refused as a 403 that names both people, checked AFTER the user lookup and BEFORE the seat check (a cross-account candidate must hear "wrong account", never "no seats left", because the second message invites buying a seat for a grant that must not happen at any price). A failed boundary read REFUSES the write, same direction as the seat check.
  - **A PLATFORM ADMIN IS EXEMPT IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, each for a stated reason.** An admin CANDIDATE can be added to any project (the future FMP-advisor shape: internal-account person on a client project, no client seat). An admin-OWNED project accepts anyone (the standing "admin is never blocked" rule, applied the way `seats.ts` applies it: the operator's own workspace is not rationed). The ACTOR is deliberately NOT tested, because only admins reach this route and an actor exemption would make the whole check dead, the same reasoning the seat limit recorded.
  - **THE DROPDOWN NOW MATCHES THE WRITE.** `TeamAccessPanel` stops fetching `/api/admin/users` (every user on the platform) and asks the new `?candidatesFor=<projectId>` mode on the same route, which lists people through the SAME `listAccountCandidates` rule: the owner's account plus platform admins, everyone for an admin-owned project, everyone (flagged `scoped:false`) on a pre-239 database. The person picker is disabled until a project is chosen, because eligibility depends on whose project it is. The dropdown is a courtesy; the 403 is the scope.
  - **THE STALE READ-ONLY NOTE IS REMOVED.** The panel still told admins "Everyone except the Owner has read-only access for now... take effect when it lands", which has been false since the edit lock shipped (step 5, mig 233, an Editor can write). Its own comment said the note goes when step 5 lands; it now has. No verifier pinned it (checked before removal); `verify-accounts` C4b now pins its ABSENCE.
  - **NULL is refused, not matched**: a post-239 user with a NULL `account_id` (a broken invariant) is refused rather than letting NULL == NULL grant access. Schema tolerance: a pre-239 database allows with reason `pre_migration`, which is exactly pre-239 behaviour.
  - **Nothing changed for anyone live**: all existing memberships are owner self-seeds (measured in step 1's probe), and the boundary permits every one of them. `verify-accounts` C5-C11 pin the decision offline against the real helper; L11-L14 run it LIVE over the real rows (two real users on different accounts refused; admin candidate allowed; a client owner's candidate list = their account plus admins and provably excludes the other client). S10's reader allow-list grew by exactly one file (`accountBoundary.ts`), and the route is deliberately NOT listed: it reads the column only through the helper, so a raw `account_id` query appearing in the route fails S10. Affected verifiers before commit (full-path grep of the diff): `verify-accounts` 41/0, `verify-seats` 42/0, `verify-dashboard-nav` 20/0, all with credentials. NOT browser-verified; the Team access screen changed (scoped dropdown, note removed) and is the thing to click on live.

- **THE ACCOUNT MODEL, STEP 1: EVERY USER HAS AN ACCOUNT (2026-09-04, mig 239 APPLIED). NEW `verify-accounts` 25; suite 156 verifiers.** `accounts` (`id uuid PK`, `name`, `kind` client|internal, `owner_user_id` UNIQUE FK ON DELETE CASCADE) + `users.account_id`. One personal account per existing user, holder = self; the platform admin's is FMP's own (`kind 'internal'`, exactly one such row); every other account is a client; name from `users.company` where present, else the person's name, else the email. **ZERO projects and ZERO memberships touched** (a project's account comes from its owner), and **NOTHING reads `users.account_id` yet**: `verify-accounts` S10 pins the reader allow-list at exactly one file, so each later step of the account model (membership boundary, seats by account, invites) must amend that list consciously. The platform behaves exactly as before, by measurement: only the deletion engine changed behaviour, and only for a state (a holder with members) that cannot exist yet.
  - **THE ACCOUNT IS ITS OWN ROW, NEVER A POINTER TO A PERSON'S ROW.** The cheap `users.account_owner_id` self-reference was rejected by design (it makes an organisation's identity a person's row, the mistake `refm_projects.user_id` already makes for projects), and S6 fails if that column ever appears anywhere.
  - **THE INVARIANT IS HELD AT THE DATABASE, because users rows are born in more places than the one register route**: probe scripts and the SQL console create them too. `trg_users_personal_account` (AFTER INSERT on `users`) gives every new user a personal account; a row arriving WITH an `account_id` already set is the future invited member and gets no personal account (proven in the applier). The register route is UNTOUCHED. `users.account_id` is deliberately NULLABLE (the FK pair is circular, so NOT NULL would need deferred constraints in every insert path); the trigger plus the live half of `verify-accounts` hold "every user has an account, every holder points back at their own" instead, measured over the real rows on every suite run.
  - **DELETING A HOLDER WHOSE ACCOUNT STILL HAS MEMBERS REFUSES, twice.** The engine (`deleteUserAccount`) refuses first with `account_has_members` (409 on both HTTP routes), placed BEFORE the audit write and BEFORE the Paddle handling so a refusal has zero side effects, and NAMING the account and member count. The DB is the backstop for paths the engine cannot see: `users.account_id` is ON DELETE NO ACTION, so a member's row blocks the holder's cascade inside Postgres no matter what tried the delete; the applier CAUSED that refusal and observed it. Schema-tolerant: on a pre-239 database the accounts read comes back empty and behaviour is exactly pre-239. A holder with NO members deletes exactly as before (their personal account CASCADES away with them, which also keeps every probe-user cleanup working for free).
  - **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE**: no plan columns on `accounts` (an account's plan IS its holder's plan, reached through `owner_user_id`; moving billing off the users row would rewrite `setUserPlan` and everything downstream for zero behaviour change), and no constraint tying a project member's account to the project owner's account (the future FMP-advisor is a member from the internal account; a DB constraint would make that impossible, so the boundary lands in the route in step 2).
  - Applier `scripts/apply-migration-239.ts`, 16 proofs, dry-run 16/0 then apply 16/0. Affected verifiers before commit (selected by full-path grep of the diff): `verify-accounts` 25/0, `verify-account-deletion` 40/0, `verify-collab-roles` 28/0, all with credentials. NOT browser-verified: nothing on screen changed.

## 2026-09-03

- **THERE IS NO ACCOUNT BOUNDARY, AND THE TEAM ACCESS DROPDOWN PROVES IT (2026-09-03, DIAGNOSIS ONLY, no code changed).** Reported from live: the Team access person picker lists every user on the platform, so one client's project can be assigned to another client's user.
  - **NO CONCEPT OF AN ACCOUNT EXISTS ANYWHERE.** A live schema search for `org|account|team|company|tenant|client|workspace|invit|seat` returns exactly one table, `account_deletions`, which is an audit of closed accounts. `users` has 26 columns and NOT ONE is a parent or tenant reference. `users.company` is free text from signup and cannot join: four of eight live users have NULL, and `"SFA"` sits beside `"Synergistic Financial Advisors"` at `@sfaresearch.com`, plausibly one firm, unjoinable. **The platform's real model is one user = one account**, and `refm_project_members` (mig 231) was the first thing to cross that line without a boundary.
  - **THE BOUNDARY IS NOT THE DROPDOWN.** `TeamAccessPanel` renders every row of `/api/admin/users` as an `<option>`, but `POST /api/admin/project-members` validates only that the user EXISTS. A scope enforced in a `<select>` is not a scope, and the mitigation before any client-facing member management is a server-side predicate on that route.
  - **IT COMPROMISES SEATS (step 8) MORE THAN ANYTHING ELSE.** `countAccountSeats` infers the account from `refm_projects.user_id`. That is SOUND for "who pays", since plans live on the user row and the owner is the plan holder. It is UNSOUND for "who is on the team": the set counted has no boundary, so adding another client's user CONSUMES YOUR SEAT and GRANTS THEM ACCESS, while their own account is untouched. The arithmetic is right over the wrong set.
  - **THE DECISION THAT SIZES THE WORK** is whether one person may belong to several accounts. `users.account_id` makes it impossible (clean; a consultant serving two clients needs two logins). An `account_members` join table makes it possible and then every "which account am I acting in" question appears at once. A self-referencing `users.account_owner_id` is the cheap option and is a TRAP: it makes an organisation's identity a person's row, the same mistake `refm_projects.user_id` already makes for projects and that step 9's applier had to work around.
  - **THERE IS NO INVITE PATH.** A `users` row is created in exactly ONE place, `app/api/auth/register/route.ts` (self-signup, scrypt, `email_confirmed:false`, `plan:'none'`); `grep` for `from('users').insert` across `app/` and `src/` returns only that route, and `/api/admin/users` has no POST. So a client's colleague can only be added if they self-registered first, which is precisely WHY the picker is global: it is a list of people who happen to have signed up.
  - **IT BLOCKS NOTHING THAT SHIPPED.** Step 9's paths are admin-mediated or owner-scoped and neither create nor widen membership. It does make the existing leak more consequential, since a wrongly-added Editor can now request a delete where before they could only edit, though the decision stays with an admin. Logged as the NEXT MAJOR ITEM at the top of [CLAUDE-TODO.md](CLAUDE-TODO.md).
- **SESSION CLOSE 2026-09-03. Module 10 Collaboration is FEATURE COMPLETE, steps 0 to 9, migs 230 to 238 all APPLIED.** Today shipped steps 7 (comments, mig 236), 8 (seats, mig 237) and 9 (delete approval, mig 238), plus the reviewer-visibility change, two entitlement label fixes (mig 235) and the Team access dead-button fix. **NEW STANDING PRACTICE, adopted mid-session and recorded in CLAUDE.md**: run the verifiers that READ A CHANGED FILE before each commit, selected mechanically by grepping every `verify-*.ts` for the full repo path of each file in the diff, and run the full suite ONCE at session end. A first pass on bare filenames matched 38 verifiers through prose mentions of `types.ts`; full paths gave the true 24. **Nothing today was browser-verified except the reviewer visibility change**, which the user ran on live; every other entry states its own status.

- **MODULE 10 STEP 9: DELETE REQUIRES ADMIN APPROVAL, FOR AN EDITOR (2026-09-03, mig 238 APPLIED). NEW `verify-delete-approval` 49; suite 155 verifiers.** The OWNER keeps direct delete, still soft and still restorable for 30 days. An EDITOR, who could not delete at all, gains a way to ASK. Reviewers and Viewers get neither. **Nothing was taken away from anyone**: this is a new supervised path for the one role that had none, expressed as a new matrix permission `canRequestDelete` (editor true; **owner FALSE**, which looks inverted and is not, since an owner holds `canDeleteProject` and a request would be a slower road to the same place).
  - **PLATFORM AGNOSTIC, AND THAT COSTS THE FOREIGN KEY.** `platform` + `project_id` is a POLYMORPHIC reference, so Postgres cannot key it at one table and there is no FK on `project_id`. The cascade is therefore a TRIGGER, and a trigger is the only thing that works: `refm_projects` rows are also deleted by the `users` cascade, which runs **entirely inside Postgres where no application code can see it**. The applier proves the trigger fires on exactly that path. A new platform adds one trigger beside its `PROJECT_SOURCES` entry, and the verifier fails if a membership platform has none.
  - **THE REQUEST OUTLIVES THE REQUESTER**, `requested_by` ON DELETE SET NULL, the same rule as 230's `created_by` and 234's `version_id`. Losing MEMBERSHIP does not touch the row at all, and the admin queue SHOWS whether the requester is still a member rather than hiding the request. **ONE OPEN REQUEST PER PROJECT** by partial unique index, so two editors cannot queue two deletes for one thing; the second is told a request is already open rather than getting a conflict for doing something reasonable. **THE DECLINE SURVIVES A LATER APPROVAL** by design, which migration 218 had to bolt onto `trial_requests` after the fact.
  - **THE BUG THE STEP WAS DESIGNED AGAINST, AND HOW IT WAS ACTUALLY CAUGHT.** `softDeleteProject` filters `deleted_at IS NULL`, and a service-role write reports no rows-affected, so approving a request on a project somebody else had already deleted would have updated zero rows and returned no error: **a false record of a delete that never happened**. Approval now READS the project first and refuses with `already_deleted`, leaving the request PENDING so an admin can decline it and tell the requester what happened. **The first version of the check for this had no teeth**: replacing the guard with `if (false)` left the whole section green, because it grepped for the guard's PRESENCE while the invariant is BEHAVIOUR (TRAPS 3.19). It was replaced with a LIVE test that builds a real request, soft-deletes the project by another route, calls the engine, and asserts the refusal; re-running the same sabotage now fails it by name, printing the exact false success `{"ok":true,"action":"approved"}`.
  - **A DECLINE REQUIRES A REASON**, because there is no notification system and that sentence on the requester's own project card is the only way they find out. **The state rides on the project LIST**, one query for the whole page like the member ordering beside it, and a card with a request offers no delete button.
  - **THE QUEUE IS ON THE PROJECTS BROWSER**, not the Plans page: approving a delete and restoring one from the Deleted bin are the same job on the same objects, and splitting them would have an admin approving on one screen and undoing on another. Approving a request drops the project straight into the bin below it.
  - **THE DASHBOARD DELETE BUTTON IS NOW GATED PER CARD.** It rendered on every card with no role check, so a Reviewer saw a Delete link the server refused with a 404, the same class as the Edit button (2026-09-03) and the Team Access button. `can()` reads the OPEN project's role and falls back to Owner on the dashboard, which is right for project-agnostic actions and wrong for a per-project one; a `cardRole` lookup now feeds `roleCan`, the same matrix the server gates on, and the Projects screen uses it too.
  - **A CORRECTION TO MY OWN DIAGNOSIS, recorded because the build was scoped on it.** The step-9 diagnosis reported that the Projects Browser had no Deleted bin and that CLAUDE.md wrongly recorded it as shipped. **That was wrong.** The bin exists and works: a `deleted` filter tab with counts, a `restore()` call to `POST /api/admin/projects {action:'restore'}`, and `daysLeft` per row, all in `src/components/admin/ProjectsBrowser.tsx`. The diagnosis grepped `app/admin/projects/page.tsx`, a 33-line wrapper that only renders `<ProjectsBrowser />`. **CLAUDE.md was right and the diagnosis was wrong**, so no bin was built and no doc was corrected. Evidence taken from the wrong file, the same shape as TRAPS 3.17.
  - **NOT BUILT**: no email or notification of any kind, no approval for archive (it stays the user's own reversible action and nothing here touches the archived column), no Owner request path. Section G of the verifier fails if any appears. **Sabotage-tested**: removing the already-deleted guard, granting the owner a request path, and dropping the Dashboard's per-card gate each fail by name.

- **MODULE 10 STEP 8: SEATS ARE ENFORCED (2026-09-03, mig 237 APPLIED). NEW `verify-seats` 42; suite 154 verifiers.** A seat is a DISTINCT PERSON ON AN ACCOUNT, counted across every project it owns, on every platform. **The owner consumes one**, so Pro at 1 seat means a single-person account and collaboration is a Firm feature. Firm is 10. An admin raises a client with an override; extra seats are invoiced manually, outside the platform.
  - **THE COUNT LIVES IN THE REGISTRY LAYER, NOT IN REFM** (`src/shared/admin/seats.ts`), and iterates `PROJECT_SOURCES`. A counter reading `refm_project_members` directly would keep returning a plausible number and start UNDER-counting the day ERM or BVM shipped, which is the worst kind of wrong: quiet, believable, and in the customer's favour until someone audits it. A source with no membership declared is SKIPPED rather than counted as zero members, and a failed read THROWS rather than contributing nothing.
  - **THE OWNER IS COUNTED UNCONDITIONALLY**, whether or not a membership row happens to exist for them.
