Migration 231 seeded one per project, but a project created through a path that writes no membership would otherwise make the owner free, and "Pro is 1, which means no collaboration" only holds if the owner IS that one. **A soft-deleted project's members do not count** (`deleted_at` makes it unopenable, so charging for that access would bill for nothing); **archived projects DO count**, because archiving is visible, reversible and leaves the project openable.
  - **A ROLE CHANGE IS NOT A NEW SEAT.** The question asked is "does this person already hold a seat anywhere on this account", NOT "will a row be inserted". The write is an UPSERT, so a demotion travels the same path as an add, and a check counting rows-to-be-written would have refused to demote the tenth member of a full Firm account. Adding an existing collaborator to a SECOND project is free for the same reason, which is what "counted across all projects" means.
  - **ONE ENFORCEMENT POINT, and it is genuinely one.** The upsert in `POST /api/admin/project-members` is the ONLY insert into any membership table in the codebase: create and duplicate seed none, and the other writes update `priority` / `sort_order` on existing rows. The check sits AFTER the user lookup, so a seat is never refused for somebody who does not exist, and BEFORE the upsert, so it blocks rather than warns. A counting failure REFUSES: a seat handed out by an errored read is invisible until an audit.
  - **THE LIMIT BELONGS TO THE ACCOUNT, NOT TO WHOEVER IS TYPING.** Only an admin can reach this route, so testing the actor would mean the limit never fired at all; the project's OWNER is measured, because the plan is theirs. The one exemption is an account whose holder is a PLATFORM ADMIN, following the standing "admin is never blocked" rule `resolveUserGate` applies everywhere else.
  - **NO NEW TABLE AND NO NEW ADMIN UI.** The override is the existing `user_permissions` row, and the limit REUSES `resolveEffectiveFeatures` rather than restating "an active override with a value wins, an expired one does not", which is exactly the sentence this codebase keeps finding in two places. **PROVED LIVE, then cleaned up**: granting the trial account 5 seats moved its limit from 1 to 5 and let the add through; expiring the same override put it back to 1 and blocked it again; the table was left at zero rows.
  - **THE BLOCK IS OPERATOR-FACING** and names the account, its plan, seats used counting the owner, what the addition would need, and the exact route to raise it (`/admin/access`, grant Team Seats), plus that removing a member frees a seat immediately. **The end-user "contact the team" copy was deliberately NOT written**: adding members is admin-only in this step, so that sentence has no surface to appear on and writing it now would be writing it blind.
  - **MIGRATION 158 IS CORRECTED WITHOUT BEING EDITED.** It seeded `pro = 3`; 237 declares trial 1 / solo 1 / pro 1 / firm 10 so the migration log and production finally agree, and the applier proves every OTHER entitlement row is byte-identical across the change. The `seats` feature is promoted from `needs_build` + "Coming soon" to `live` with copy stating that the owner uses a seat and how to get more, because the old label would now be false. `CLAUDE-TODO.md`'s "pro 3" line is corrected in place with the reason.
  - **NOT BUILT**: no Paddle seat purchase, no Owner-adding path (members stay admin-only), no stored counter. Section G of the verifier fails if any appears. **Sabotage-tested four ways**: making the owner free, hardcoding the REFM table, counting soft-deleted projects, and moving the check off the write path each produce exactly one failure naming the rule. **Two of the verifier's own checks were wrong first and were re-aimed**: D5 read `/UPDATE .*seat/i` over the migration and failed on its own legitimate `UPDATE plan_permissions`, and E2 used `indexOf` over the whole file and found `checkSeatForMember` in the IMPORT line, so the ordering read as wrong. Both are TRAPS 3.17 again, evidence taken from the wrong place.

- **THE TEAM ACCESS BUTTON DID NOTHING, AND NOTHING COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT (2026-09-03, no migration). NEW `verify-dashboard-nav` 20; suite 153 verifiers.** Reported from live: the admin-only "Team access" item on the Modeling Hub dashboard sidebar was inert. Not an error, not a blank screen, not a failed request. The click was swallowed, and the item did not even take the active highlight.
  - **EVERYTHING BEHIND IT WAS FINISHED AND DEPLOYED.** `TeamAccessPanel.tsx` is a complete 237-line component, `DashView` already included `'team'`, the render branch `activeView === 'team' && isAdmin` already existed, and `/api/admin/project-members` answers 401 unauthenticated on production. **One `if` had never heard of it.**
  - **THE MECHANISM: A POSITIVE ALLOW-LIST.** The one sidebar click handler decided what to do by testing the id, `if (item.id === 'dashboard' || item.id === 'billing')`, and `NavItem` was an optional-field bag, so `{ id: 'team', icon, label }` was a legal declaration matching no branch. **Two commits, months apart, and neither was wrong on its own**: `7ade6b88` wrote the allow-list when those were the only two in-page views, and `b44cef95` (Module 10 step 2) added the item, the view, the render branch, the panel and the route without adding the third id to a condition elsewhere in the file. **An enumerated allow-list plus a new entry elsewhere compiles clean and fails silently**: no type error, no lint error, no failing test, no runtime error. The failure mode of "not in the list" is silence. Recorded as TRAPS 8.3.
  - **A THIRD COPY OF THE SAME FACT WAS HIDING BESIDE IT.** The hash written on click (`item.id === 'billing' ? '#billing' : ' '`) and the hash read on load (`hash === '#billing'`) were two more hand-maintained enumerations of "which views are deep-linkable".
  - **THE ALLOW-LIST IS DELETED, NOT EXTENDED.** `NavItem` is now a DISCRIMINATED UNION (`view` / `link` / `soon`), so a dead entry cannot be declared: a new item must pick a kind and every kind has a branch, enforced by the compiler rather than by a convention. For a `view` item **the `id` IS the `DashView`**, so the id and the view are one value that cannot drift, and the deep-link hash is a field on the item that both the writer and the restorer read through `VIEW_BY_HASH`, built from `NAV_ITEMS`. Three lists became one declaration.
  - **THE RESTORE STAYS STATIC-ONLY, deliberately**: the admin items are appended per session, so restoring one from a URL hash before the session resolved would set a view the user may not be allowed to see.
  - **PINNED WHERE THE COMPILER CANNOT REACH.** `verify-dashboard-nav` covers the two remaining holes: a `DashView` with no render branch (the click works, the page goes blank) and the allow-list pattern creeping back. **Sabotage-tested**: adding a fourth view with no render branch fails A5 BY NAME, and restoring an id-list condition in the handler fails B3. Lint parity was checked by stashing: the page carries the same 6 pre-existing errors before and after, and the one warning the change did introduce (an unused `DEFAULT_VIEW`) was resolved by using it for the initial state rather than by deleting the constant.

- **MODULE 10 STEP 7: COMMENTS (2026-09-03, mig 236 APPLIED). NEW `verify-comments` 50; suite 152 verifiers.** People's words about the model, beside the machine's record of it. **A THIRD TABLE, `refm_project_comments`, and both reasons are stated rather than assumed**: `refm_project_changes` (234) is what the SYSTEM observed, appended by the save path and immutable by trigger, whereas a comment is written, edited and withdrawn BY A PERSON; and `refm_project_versions.comment` (153) is the required note on ONE save, with one author, no thread and no resolve state.
  - **EVERYTHING IS NULLABLE EXCEPT THE PROJECT AND THE BODY.** A comment on the project as a whole is the expected common case, not an edge case, so `version_id` and `path` are both optional and neither implies the other: a comment can name a field without a version, or a version without a field. **`path` REUSES THE SNAPSHOT-DIFF GRAMMAR** (`assets[id=x].buaSqm`), the same vocabulary 234 stores and `applyOverrides.getByPath` resolves. A second path vocabulary would have disagreed with the first on its first day.
  - **A COMMENT OUTLIVES WHAT IT POINTS AT.** `version_id` is ON DELETE SET NULL like 234's, and nothing filters the read by version, so a comment written against v3 stays visible after v4 is saved and shows which version it was written against. NULL reads as "that version is gone", never as "written against the current one". `user_id` is SET NULL too, so a closed account leaves the words standing with an unknown author.
  - **REPLIES ARE ONE LEVEL AND THE DATABASE ENFORCES IT.** `trg_refm_comments_one_level` refuses a reply whose parent is itself a reply, and a comment replying to itself. A depth rule kept only in application code is one the next writer breaks, and a thread that has quietly become a tree cannot be flattened afterwards without deciding, for somebody else, what their reply was replying to. The applier PROVES it by attempting the insert and being refused, rather than reading the DDL back.
  - **SOFT DELETE, AND THE BODY NEVER LEAVES THE SERVER AGAIN.** `deleted_at` keeps the row so a thread holds its shape and no reply is orphaned; the body stays in the table (an admin undoing a mis-click has something to restore) and is STRIPPED in `toDto` before serialisation, so a client cannot render withdrawn text and a network tab does not hand it back. A deleted root with live replies renders as a tombstone; a deleted leaf disappears.
  - **AUTHORSHIP IS ENFORCED IN THE WHERE CLAUSE, NOT IN AN IF.** Edit and delete match `user_id = me` inside the statement, so a request naming someone else's comment updates zero rows and is reported as not found. **RESOLVE IS DELIBERATELY NOT AUTHORSHIP**: a reviewer raises a point and an editor closes it, so anyone with `canAddComments` may resolve or reopen, and requiring the author would leave threads open forever when somebody leaves. Resolve targets ROOTS ONLY (`parent_id IS NULL` in the WHERE), since a resolved reply would be a second, contradicting state inside one thread.
  - **`canAddComments` IS FINALLY ENFORCED.** It has been in the shared matrix since step 0 and was read by nothing: Owner, Editor and Reviewer may write, a Viewer may not. **ALL FOUR ROLES READ THE SAME THREAD**, with no role narrowing on the GET, the same rule the change log follows: a review a reviewer cannot read back is not a review. **THE EDIT LOCK IS NOT INVOLVED**, and that is a decision: `canAddComments` is absent from `LOCK_REQUIRED` because a comment is not a model edit, and requiring the lock would stop a reviewer leaving a note while the editor had the project open, which is precisely when a review happens.
  - **THE SURFACE IS A COMMENTS TAB beside Activity in the version manager**, on the same lazy-load-per-tab pattern and the same ONE-object-keyed-by-project state discipline, so a slow response that lands after a project switch is discarded rather than rendered under the wrong name, and loading is DERIVED rather than a second flag that can disagree. Every write re-reads from the server instead of patching locally, because several people are looking at the same thread.
  - **NOT BUILT, and pinned as not built**: no notifications, no email, no unread counts, no jump-to-field navigation (a path renders as text; nothing in this platform maps a snapshot path to a screen and this step did not build that), no approval workflow. Section F of the verifier fails if any of them appears. **That section caught itself first**: reading the raw files, it matched `comments.ts`'s own header sentence "no unread count" and failed on the PROSE DESCRIBING THE ABSENCE it was testing for, the same shape as TRAPS 3.17. It now reads stripped source.
  - **SABOTAGE-TESTED, three ways**: un-stripping a deleted body, dropping the author match from the edit statement, and letting resolve target a reply each produce exactly one failure naming the rule (44 passed / 1 failed in each case).

- **MODULE NAMES ARE DERIVED TOO: BOTH HAND-WRITTEN MAPS LOSE THEIR MODULE KEYS (2026-09-03, no migration). `verify-plan-builder-modules` 26 -> 31.** Follow-on from the numbering entry below, and the same disease one layer up. **TWO hand-written module maps existed and disagreed INSIDE A SINGLE MODAL**: the shared `UpgradePrompt`'s `FEATURE_LABELS` supplied the heading and the platform's `FEATURE_DISPLAY_LABELS` supplied the sentence under it. The shared one had **`module_4` crossed with `module_5` and `module_6` crossed with `module_7`** against the registry (4 is Financial Statements, 5 is Returns and Valuation, 6 is Scenario Analysis, 7 is IC Presentation), stale since migration 157 swapped reports and scenarios. A user clicking a locked Module 4 was offered a different module BY NAME, on a lock they can actually reach.
  - **THE FIX IS NOT A CORRECTED COPY.** A third hand-written map would go stale a fourth time, and `featureLabels.ts` proves it: its header already said the shared map was "marketing-oriented and partly stale" and it worked AROUND that rather than fixing it. **Neither map may now contain a module key at all.** The platform resolves a module's name and number from `dynamicSidebarModules`, the same nav list the sidebar renders, which `usePlatformModules` builds from the live `platform_modules` registry, and passes it to `UpgradePrompt` as a new `label` prop. One resolution, `lockedFeatureLabel`, feeds BOTH the heading and the sentence, so the modal cannot contradict itself again.
  - **THE BOUNDARY MAKES THIS THE ONLY CORRECT SHAPE, not a convenience.** `shared` may import only core / shared / integ (eslint `boundaries/dependencies`), and the registry lives under `src/hubs/modeling/platforms/refm`, so a shared component CANNOT read it. The platform already holds the derived list for its own sidebar; passing the answer in is the only direction available, and the reason is recorded where the map used to be so nobody "simplifies" it back.
  - **NEVER EMPTY, so the lookup always has an answer**: `usePlatformModules` seeds the list from the static `MODULES` before the fetch resolves and keeps it on failure. Non-module keys (sensitivity, versioning, branding, the export keys) are not in the nav list and fall through to the maps that still own them; `module_8_full` / `module_9_full` stay too, since they are separate entitlement keys and not module rows.
  - **PINNED AND SABOTAGE-TESTED.** Nine checks: neither map holds a module key, the passed label wins over the map, the platform resolves from the live nav list, the heading and the sentence share one resolution, the boundary reason is recorded, and the non-module keys still resolve. Typing a `module_4:` entry back into the shared map gives 30 passed / 1 failed, naming the row. TRAPS 8.2 extended.
  - **REVIEWER VISIBILITY IS NOW BROWSER-VERIFIED ON LIVE** (commit `b32106c8`, deployed in `4370d654`), by the user, against a throwaway project with their membership set to Reviewer: the **Reviewer badge shows**, the view-only banner reads the read-only wording, **Modules 1 through 5 open and are populated**, and **no Edit button appears in the topbar or in the banner**. FMP - MARINA GATE was the control in the same session and still shows Edit and Save as an Owner. The throwaway project has been deleted and both live projects re-probed: FMP RE HUB and FMP - MARINA GATE both read `role=owner`, 2 projects owned. That commit's message says NOT BROWSER-VERIFIED; this entry supersedes it.

- **THE MODULE NUMBER A USER SEES IS A POSITION, NOT THE MODULE'S IDENTITY, AND A THIRD COPY OF IT WAS FROZEN IN THE DATABASE (2026-09-03, migration 235 APPLIED). `verify-plan-builder-modules` 16 -> 26. No renumber: `module_10` is still `module_10`.** The live sidebar shows "Module 8 Collaborate" and "Module 9 API Access" and no Portfolio module at all, while every doc, verifier and entitlement key says Collaborate is 10. Both were right, about different things, and a third value was right about nothing.
  - **TWO LEGITIMATE NUMBERS.** IDENTITY is the slug: `collaborate` maps to component 10 (`SLUG_TO_COMPONENT_NUMBER`), which is why the entitlement key is `module_10` and why plan assignments survive a reorder. POSITION is what renders: `orderModulesForDisplay` drops hidden modules and numbers what remains 1..N, and `platform_modules` has `portfolio` (8) and `market-data` (9) both at `status: 'hidden'`, so Collaborate is the EIGHTH VISIBLE module. The sidebar was correct, the key was correct, and the gap between them is by design (it is documented in `orderModulesForDisplay`, which already recorded a previous instance of the same confusion on the marketing page).
  - **THE DEFECT WAS THE THIRD COPY.** Migration 158 seeded the rendered sentence `'Module 10: Collaborate'` into `features_registry.label` and nothing ever recomputed it. It agreed with the identity by luck at seed time and diverged the moment a module was hidden. **A derived value with a stored copy is a divergence waiting for the next edit, and this one had no owner:** no code wrote it, so no code could keep it true. Migration 235 takes the number OUT of storage (the label is the NAME only) rather than resyncing it, because resyncing would just restart the clock. The fallback path, used when the live registry is unreachable, now renders "Collaborate" with no number: no number is the honest answer when the registry that owns the numbering is unavailable.
  - **STEP 0's NUMBERING WAS RIGHT; ONE WORD OF ITS JUSTIFICATION WAS NOT.** It read "8 is Portfolio, already shipped and sold". Sold is true and load-bearing: `plan_permissions` grants `module_8` to solo, pro and firm, so numbering collaboration as 8 would have handed it to every paying subscriber through a permission they already hold, and `UNIQUE(platform_slug, number)` would have blocked the DB half anyway. **Shipped is false**: Portfolio is `status: 'hidden'`, `build_status: 'needs_build'`, and `lib/modules/module8-placeholder.ts` is a stub. What made it look shipped is that the free all-projects hub is called **Portfolio Dashboard**, a different and ungated surface (`STATIC_NAV` key `dashboard`, `featureKey: null`, fed by `/api/refm/portfolio` since 2026-08-30).
  - **AND THAT NAME COLLISION HAD REACHED A USER-FACING LOCK.** `UpgradePrompt` labelled `module_8` "Module 8 - Portfolio Dashboard", so anyone hitting that lock was told to buy a plan for the screen they were already looking at. It now reads "Module 8 - Portfolio (cross-project roll-up)", which is what `platform_modules` says it is ("Roll-up across multiple projects with consolidated returns and waterfall"). The identity / position split is written down beside the map, because the numbers in it are identities and will keep disagreeing with the sidebar.
  - **PINNED.** `verify-plan-builder-modules` gained ten checks: hiding a module moves the POSITION and never the key, unhiding moves it back, the lock is not named after the free hub, and a LIVE half fails if any stored module label carries a number again. The UpgradePrompt check was sabotage-tested by restoring the old string (25 passed, 1 failed, naming the row). The applier proved 11 labels carried a prefix before and 0 after with `plan_permissions` byte-identical across the change. Recorded as TRAPS 8.2.
  - **FOUND, NOT FIXED:** the rest of the `UpgradePrompt` map is stale in the same way. It calls `module_4` "Returns & Valuation" and `module_5` "Financial Statements" (the registry has 4 = Financial Statements, 5 = Returns and Valuation), and `module_6` "Reports & Export" against `module_7` "Scenario Analysis" (the registry has 6 = Scenario Analysis, 7 = IC Presentation). That is a pre-migration-157 snapshot: 157 swapped reports and scenarios and this hand-written map never followed. Four wrong labels on locks a user can hit, reported and deliberately left alone.

- **PRODUCTION OUTAGE: THE ADMIN USER LIST WENT BLANK, AND A SECOND ADMIN SCREEN WAS BROKEN THE SAME WAY WITHOUT ANYONE NOTICING (2026-09-03, no migration). `verify-admin-users-cleanup` 20 -> 23, `verify-admin-projects-browser` 19 -> 24, `verify-ai-admin` 68 -> 72, `verify-legacy-training-retirement` 21 -> 22, NEW `scripts/run-verifiers.ts`. Suite 151 pass / 0 fail, 8858 individual checks, MEASURED WITH CREDENTIALS FOR THE FIRST TIME.** `/admin/users` reported "0 total users" and "No users found" while all eight users sat untouched in the database. Data loss was ruled out first, by direct SQL that bypassed PostgREST entirely: 8 rows, 7 `user` and 1 `admin`.
  - **THE CAUSE WAS A TABLE, NOT A LINE OF CODE.** No route, page or query had been edited for months. Migration 231 (Module 10 step 2) added `refm_project_members` with `PRIMARY KEY (project_id, user_id)` and an FK to each side. A table whose primary key is two foreign keys is a textbook many-to-many junction, so PostgREST began offering it as a SECOND path between `users` and `refm_projects` and refused to guess between them: **HTTP 300, PGRST201, "Could not embed because more than one relationship was found"**. The bare embed `projects:refm_projects(count)` had been an unambiguous inference for months and silently stopped being one.
  - **ADDING A TABLE IS NOT THE SAFE MIGRATION IT LOOKS LIKE.** A bare embed is not a query, it is a REQUEST FOR AN INFERENCE computed over the whole schema, so a new table changes the answer to a question asked somewhere else entirely. Nothing in the diff of 231 points at `/admin/users`. Recorded as TRAPS 2.14.
  - **WHICH TABLES DO THIS, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** Migrations 230, 233 and 234 also added tables with FKs to BOTH `users` and `refm_projects` (`refm_project_versions`, `refm_project_locks`, `refm_project_changes`) and **none of them caused it**: each has its own single-column primary key, so PostgREST does not read it as a junction. Only the table whose PK is exactly the two FKs did, which is why the error message naming `refm_project_members` was the confirmation rather than the starting assumption.
  - **A SECOND BROKEN SCREEN, FOUND BY RUNNING THE LIVE CHECKS.** `/admin/projects` carried the mirror-image embed `users(email, name)` and was failing identically in production, unreported. Both embeds now NAME the relationship: `projects:refm_projects!refm_projects_user_id_fkey(count)`, and `` `users!${source.ownerColumn}(email, name)` `` for the Projects Browser, taking the hint from the registry's OWN declaration so ERM and BVM are disambiguated by their own config rather than by a constraint name that only fits REFM. A repo-wide sweep found no third case; `trial_requests` looked like one and was TESTED live rather than reasoned about, and is genuinely unambiguous.
  - **THE ERROR WAS SWALLOWED BY THE PAGE, NOT THE ROUTE.** The route was correct and answered HTTP 500 with the PGRST201 message. The page did `.then(r => r.json())` with no `res.ok` check, so `j.users` was `undefined`, `?? []` turned it into an empty array, and **a hard server error rendered as a perfectly ordinary empty table**. That is the absent-value trap (TRAPS 2.4) in its most expensive form: an ERROR collapsed into a REAL VALUE. `?? []` is not the bug; defaulting BEFORE establishing the response was a success is.
  - **THIRTEEN SITES ACROSS TEN ADMIN SCREENS HAD THE SAME DEFECT**, found with a detector rather than by eye, and all converted to a new `src/components/admin/adminFetch.ts` that checks `res.ok` and throws with the server's own message. The detector now reports zero. The users page states the failure, and its header reads **"User count unavailable"** rather than the false "0 total users", because a count is a CLAIM about the account and must not be made when the load failed. The error state is checked BEFORE the empty state, since a failure also leaves the list empty.
  - **THE SUITE HAD NO COMMITTED RUNNER, AND THAT IS WHY THIS SURVIVED.** Every session, including every one of mine, invented its own shell loop and invoked `npx tsx <file>` without `--env-file=.env.local`. Fourteen verifiers have a live half. **`verify-admin-users-cleanup` runs this exact embed and compares it against a direct count: it would have caught the outage instantly, and instead printed `SKIP A4/A5 (no DB creds)` and `20 passed, 0 failed`.** The suite reported 151/0 while the page was down. The check was correct, complete, and had never run.
  - **NEW `scripts/run-verifiers.ts` (`npm run verify:suite`) IS NOW THE RUNNER**: it loads `.env.local` by default, REFUSES to run without credentials unless `--allow-offline` is passed (and then labels the result PARTIAL so the number is never quoted as the suite), and treats an unexpected skip as a FAILURE. Known skips live in an `ACCEPTED_SKIPS` map with a stated reason each, so the runner can be meaningfully green while a new skip still fails it; a permanently red runner teaches everyone to ignore it, which is how the silent skip survived. Sabotage-tested by removing an entry: the run flips to NOT CLEAN.
  - **RUNNING THE LIVE HALVES FOR THE FIRST TIME FOUND TWO MORE REAL PROBLEMS.** `verify-admin-projects-browser` failed 3 live checks, which is how the second broken screen was found at all. And `verify-ai-admin` was simulating its "unreadable usage store" case by OMITTING the client, but `loadAiUsage` falls back to `getServerClient()`, which SUCCEEDS whenever credentials are present, so the check passed only in an environment without them and inverted the moment the suite gained them. **The premise was wrong, not the product.** It is now driven through a client whose reads fail, and reads identically with and without credentials. Of the fourteen, only those two were losing real coverage; the other twelve behave the same either way, measured one by one.
  - **CONFIRMED FIXED**: the select string read OUT OF the deployed route source and executed against production returns **total 8, rows 8, no error**, with the embedded `deleted_at` filter still applied and per-user project counts matching a direct count exactly. `/api/admin/users` answers 401, not 500. Health SHA `a6f3bb18` matches HEAD. The rendered page was NOT viewed by me: Chrome is blocked from that host in this environment and no credentials were entered.
  - **TWO OF MY OWN CHECKS WERE INERT, AND BOTH ARE WORTH RECORDING.** The suite number I quoted repeatedly on 2026-09-02 was true as stated (every script exited 0) and **thinner than it sounded**. Then the new runner's first skip detector matched `/\bskip/i` anywhere in a line and reported ten verifiers as skipping when two were: it was matching the PROSE inside PASSING checks, such as "[PASS] a recipient with no token is skipped, never emailed". That is TRAPS 3.17 committed into the file written to prevent this class of thing, roughly an hour after TRAPS 3.19 described its sibling. The rule is now structural: a skip is a line that BEGINS by announcing itself as one.
  - **ALSO REPAIRED, both caused by this work**: `verify-legacy-training-retirement` B2 pinned the literal string `` fetch(`/api/admin/training?courseId= `` and failed when that call moved to `adminFetchJson`, though the editor still requested exactly the same URL; it now matches either transport, because the ENDPOINT is the behaviour it exists to protect and the helper issuing the request is not.

## 2026-09-02

- **MODULE 10 COLLABORATION, STEP 6: THE APPEND-ONLY CHANGE LOG (2026-09-02f, mig 234 APPLIED). `verify-change-log` 57, four sabotages, one of which caught a check that was not doing its job. Suite 150 -> 151.** Who changed what, when, per project, in a record nothing rewrites.
  - **ITS OWN TABLE, AND BOTH ALTERNATIVES ARE REFUSED FOR STATED REASONS.** `refm_project_versions.change_log` answers a different question: it is a DIFF recomputed against `base_version_id` on every save, it has NO AUTHOR and NO TIMESTAMP, and it is REWRITTEN rather than accumulated, so an afternoon of editing autosaved every 1.5 seconds collapses into one diff with no sequence. It is kept exactly as it was, for the version-history view it already serves. `admin_audit_log` models ADMIN ACTIONS ON USERS and its `admin_id` is NOT NULL (TRAPS 2.5), so every model edit would have needed a fabricated actor.
  - **APPEND-ONLY IS ENFORCED BY THE DATABASE, NOT BY A CONVENTION.** A rule that lives only in application code is a rule the next writer breaks for a good reason, and an audit trail that can be edited is not one.
  - **A REAL DESIGN COLLISION, CAUGHT BY THE APPLIER DRY RUN BEFORE IT SHIPPED. `ON DELETE SET NULL` IS IMPLEMENTED AS AN UPDATE on the child row**, so the first version's blanket UPDATE ban did not merely protect the log: it made it impossible to delete a version or close an account, since both cascades would have hit the trigger and aborted. The probe reported `version-delete probe skipped: refm_project_changes is APPEND ONLY`, which is exactly the shape of failure that reports success if nobody attempts the thing that must work. **The rule is now about CONTENT, not about the operation**: id, project_id, action, path, before, after and created_at are immutable, an FK may be RELEASED to NULL, and re-pointing an FK at a different row is refused because that would silently re-attribute a change, which is the worst thing an audit row can do quietly. DELETE stays permitted, because it is how the project cascade removes a deleted project's history. A no-op UPDATE is now allowed, which costs nothing since it leaves no trace, and that consequence is written into the migration rather than left to be rediscovered.
  - **EACH SAVE LOGS ITS OWN DELTA, NOT THE SESSION'S.** The version PATCH already holds both the stored snapshot and the incoming one, so the appended entries are `diffSnapshots(existing.snapshot, body.snapshot)`: what THIS beat changed, normally one field. Diffing against `base_version_id`, which is what the version row's own `change_log` does, would re-log the entire session on every autosave beat, growing with the length of the session rather than with the number of changes.
  - **PAST 40 PATHS IN ONE SAVE IT SUMMARISES RATHER THAN TRUNCATING.** Some single saves legitimately move thousands of paths (a first save, a case switch, a phase length that cascades). One row is written that STATES the true count and keeps a sample. Truncating silently would leave a log that looks complete and is not.
  - **FAILING TO LOG NEVER FAILS THE SAVE**, a deliberate trade that would go the other way for a compliance ledger: a gap in a history is better than losing a user's work. It is not silent to the operator, which is the part that makes it defensible rather than merely convenient.
  - **WHERE IT SURFACES, AND WHO SEES WHAT.** A third tab, **Activity**, in the version manager, beside Save and History: rows grouped by day, each showing the time, the author, the action, the path and the before/after. **EVERY MEMBER SEES THE SAME ROWS.** Access is `getProject` and nothing else, with NO role narrowing, so a Viewer's log is an Owner's log; a history a Reviewer cannot read is one they cannot review against. **AN ADMIN SEES NO MORE THAN A MEMBER on any project they open.** An admin's extra reach is that `getProject` admits them to more PROJECTS, which is the existing platform-wide rule, not a wider view of any one log. There is no per-row redaction, no admin-only field and no client write path.
  - **"NOT RECORDED" AND "NOTHING HAPPENED" ARE DIFFERENT STATEMENTS**, and the UI makes both, because saying the second when the first is true is a false claim about the project. A pre-234 database degrades to "activity tracking is not enabled yet", never to a blank log and never to a blocked save.
  - **A SABOTAGE RUN CAUGHT MY OWN CHECK BEING INERT.** D1 asserted that the right assignment was PRESENT; a sabotage that ADDED a second assignment, overwriting the delta with the base diff, sailed straight through it. It is now structural: `saveDelta` must be assigned EXACTLY ONCE and that assignment must be the stored-snapshot diff. Same family as TRAPS 3.17. Three other sabotages (narrowing the read by role, unbounding the read, letting an append failure throw) were caught first time.
  - **AND ONE MORE DOUBLE-CONVERSION, TRAPS 3.18 AGAIN.** The patch helper joined its lines with the file's own EOL and then converted again, producing `\r\r\n`. It was invisible on the `.sql` file, which is LF, and only surfaced on a CRLF `.tsx`, where the anchor simply stopped matching. The helper now joins with a plain newline and converts exactly once; every touched file was measured at zero double-CR and zero LF-only lines afterwards.
  - **NOTHING CHANGES FOR A SINGLE-USER ACCOUNT.** The append is keyed on the SESSION user, not the project owner, so one person's own edits are logged against them; nothing in the append path branches on membership, role or admin status; and no existing surface moved.

- **MODULE 10 COLLABORATION, STEP 5: THE EDIT LOCK (2026-09-02e, mig 233 APPLIED). `verify-edit-lock` 41, eighteen sabotages all caught. Suite 149 -> 150.** One person edits at a time, and the owner-only narrowing that stood in for it since step 2 is gone.
  - **THE ROW IS THE LOCK.** `project_id` is the PRIMARY KEY of `refm_project_locks`, so two holders are impossible by construction rather than by convention, and there is no `is_locked` boolean to drift. No row means unlocked.
  - **THE ACQUIRE IS ONE STATEMENT**, an INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE whose WHERE decides mine-or-stale inside the statement. Two concurrent callers serialise on the primary key and the second evaluates against the first's committed heartbeat. **PROVED, NOT ARGUED: 25 simultaneous races on two real connections gave exactly one winner every time and both winners zero times.** A race is the thing reasoning gets wrong, so it was measured.
  - **A REAL BUG, CAUGHT BY THE PROBE BEFORE IT SHIPPED.** Declared `RETURNS refm_project_locks`, the function returns a NULL COMPOSITE when its query matches nothing, and `SELECT * FROM fn(...)` renders that as ONE ROW OF NULLS rather than zero rows. A refusal therefore looked exactly like a win to any caller counting rows, which would have let two people hold the lock: the precise failure the whole step exists to prevent. `RETURNS SETOF` makes "no rows" mean no rows. Pinned by check B3 so it cannot come back.
  - **`clock_timestamp()`, not `now()`.** `now()` is the TRANSACTION timestamp and is frozen for the life of a transaction, so a heartbeat written inside one would record when the transaction began. A heartbeat is a wall-clock fact. This also surfaced as a false "acquired_at did not move" in the first probe run.
  - **HEARTBEAT, NOT AN UNLOAD HANDLER**, and the reason is recorded in three places: `beforeunload` does not fire on a crash, a killed tab, a closed laptop or a dropped network, which are exactly the cases that would otherwise hold a lock forever on behalf of nobody. A release IS sent on unload as a courtesy and NOTHING depends on it. **That beacon needed its own fix**: `sendBeacon` can only POST, and the POST route defaults to ACQUIRE, so an unload would have EXTENDED the lock rather than releasing it, which is worse than having no beacon at all.
  - **Stale release is by the NEXT ACQUIRER, not a cron.** A cron makes the release window depend on its cadence and leaves a lock held by nobody until the next tick.
  - **WHICH WRITES NEED THE LOCK IS DATA** (`LOCK_REQUIRED`), so the answer is in one readable place. Model editing needs it; **project management deliberately does not**, because requiring the lock to rename or archive would mean an owner could not archive their own project while a colleague had it open, a worse outcome than the collision it would prevent. Export is exempt for the same reason it gates as a read: it writes nothing.
  - **`roleMayWrite` is RETIRED, not deleted**, and keeps its history: it was never a statement about what an Editor may do (the matrix has always said an Editor may save), but about what the platform could safely allow. **The matrix is untouched.**
  - **A lock refusal answers 409 naming the holder**, not the 404 a role refusal gives. On the autosave route especially: told "not found" mid-edit, a user's project would appear to vanish.
  - **Proved live end to end**: an Editor acquires and CAN NOW SAVE (step 4 denied exactly this); the owner is refused while they hold it, with reason LOCKED_BY_OTHER, but can still rename and archive; request, decline (keeps the lock, clears the request) and accept (which IS releasing) all behave; an aged-out lock is taken by the next acquirer.
  - **Nothing changes for a single-user account**: acquire, save, release, uncontended, and the banner renders NOTHING when you hold your own lock with nobody waiting. On a pre-233 database the platform degrades to "no locking", never to "no editing": `holdsLock` returns TRUE when the table is absent, so a lone user keeps saving exactly as before.
  - Two of my own checks were INERT on the first sabotage run, both the TRAPS 3.17 shape: one tested that the 409 VOCABULARY existed anywhere in the file (disabling the guard left every string in a dead block), and one matched `editMode && !graceReadOnly` anywhere in the platform (that expression also drives the Topbar prop, so pointing the lock hook at `true` changed nothing it could see). Re-aimed at the branch and at the call.


- **MODULE 10 COLLABORATION, STEP 4: THE ROLE IS ENFORCED (2026-09-02d). `verify-role-enforcement` 37, sixteen sabotages all caught. Suite 148 -> 149.** `can()` was a client lookup against a pinned role, so it returned true for everything; the server gated every mutation on one blanket "may write".
  - **EVERY GATED CALL SITE NOW NAMES THE PERMISSION IT NEEDS**, and the compiler enumerated all 15 by refusing a two-argument call. Making `need` REQUIRED rather than optional is what forced that: an optional parameter would have let every site keep a blanket gate silently. It immediately found a real miss, DELETE gated as `canEditProject` rather than `canDeleteProject`, which under the matrix is the difference between an Editor being allowed to delete a project and not.
  - **TWO GATES, DELIBERATELY KEPT SEPARATE.** The MATRIX (`roleCan`) is the permanent rule: a Viewer cannot save because a Viewer has never been able to save. The OWNER-ONLY NARROWING (`roleMayWrite`) is temporary and exists only because there is no edit lock yet. Merging them would mean that removing the temporary one in step 5 required editing the permanent one, and nobody could tell which restriction was which.
  - **EXPORT IS A READ.** `getProjectForAction` gates the two export routes on the matrix ALONE, with no owner-only narrowing: they render a file and write nothing (verified: zero insert / update / upsert / delete calls in either), so the missing lock has no bearing on them. A Reviewer may export, which the matrix has always said; a Viewer may not.
  - **PROVED LIVE, per role, against the real gate functions**: viewer denied everything; reviewer denied every mutation but ALLOWED export; editor the same, and the output states WHY (the matrix allows it, `roleCan(editor, canSave) = true`, and the temporary narrowing does not); owner allowed everything. A member can still READ the project throughout.
  - **`can()` reads the RESOLVED role through the SAME matrix the server gates on**, so a control the UI shows and a write the server accepts are decided by one table. The local `PERMISSIONS` import is gone. The UI is documented as a courtesy, not the boundary: every permission it checks is checked again server-side.
  - **THE EDITOR / RETURNS EXCLUSION WAS AN OMISSION, SETTLED FROM THE HISTORY, AND IS FIXED.** The original map (`js/settings.js`, March 2026) carried its own legend: "ANALYST -> ... MODULE 4 (RETURNS), Module 6 (Reports)", and its list granted the analyst a named `returns` screen outright. **The analyst was always meant to see Returns.** What happened is a collision between two numbering schemes: when the named slugs were dropped and the modules renumbered, Returns moved from 4 to 5, the list was carried across POSITIONALLY keeping `module1` to `module4`, and the exclusion of `module5` (then a different module) landed on Returns. It still reads like "modules 1 through 4", but what it excludes is precisely the screen the legend says the editor should have. Restored, with the history recorded so it cannot be "corrected" back.
  - **Nothing changes for a single-user account**: an owner holds every permission in the matrix and passes the narrowing, so every gate that now consults the matrix lets them through exactly as the blanket gate did. A pre-231 owner with a null role is ALLOWED, not denied, and with no project open `can()` falls back to owner (the only thing it gates there is creating a project in your own space).
  - One check was INERT on the first sabotage run and is worth recording: it tested that the role RESOLVER exists, which a sabotage satisfied by leaving the resolver in place and ignoring it. Split into "the resolver exists" and "`can()` actually reads it".


- **MODULE 10 COLLABORATION, STEP 3: ORDERING AND URGENCY BECOME PER USER (2026-09-02c, mig 232 APPLIED). `verify-member-ordering` 35, fourteen sabotages all caught. Suite 147 -> 148.** `priority` and `sort_order` were one value per PROJECT, correct while a project had one owner and wrong the moment it has members: the second person to drag would silently rearrange the first one's page.
  - **NOTHING TO MIGRATE, AND IT WAS MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN.** Probed on production first: 4 projects, none archived, none soft deleted, `sort_order IS NOT NULL` on ZERO rows and `priority IS TRUE` on ZERO rows. The migration then RE-CHECKS that at apply time rather than trusting the measurement, and REFUSES if anyone has ordered or flagged a project in the meantime, because moving would strand their arrangement on a column nothing reads. The applier proves that guard fires in both directions.
  - **STATUS DID NOT MOVE, deliberately.** It is a property of the PROJECT: a building under construction is under construction for everyone looking at it. Ordering and urgency are properties of the RELATIONSHIP between a person and a project, which is why they moved and status did not. Two checks fail if a later change "finishes the job" by moving status or deprecating it.
  - **THE OLD COLUMNS ARE DEPRECATED, NOT DROPPED.** Dropping a column is the one migration a later one cannot undo, and the schema-tolerant fallback still reads them on a database with 229 but not 232, which is exactly the deployment order this repo is careful about.
  - **THE ROW SHAPE IS UNCHANGED.** `RefmProjectRow` still carries `priority` and `sort_order`; only their SOURCE changed, from the project column to an overlay of this caller's membership. No client, report or export had to learn a new field, and a single-user account sees the same numbers through a different join.
  - **A dedicated `setProjectPriority` writer**, separate from `updateProject`. Folding priority into the project patch would have written the DEPRECATED column: the flag would appear to save, would be shared with every member, and nothing would read it back.
  - **Proved live, in a rolled-back transaction**: two members of ONE project hold different orders (owner priority=true sort=0, viewer priority=false sort=9) and neither overwrites the other, while the deprecated project columns stay at their defaults. And for the single-user case, every existing user still sees the same projects with every ordering value at its default, exactly as the project columns were.
  - **I REINTRODUCED A BUG I HAD ALREADY DIAGNOSED AND FIXED ONCE.** A patch script joined its lines with the detected EOL and then the swap helper converted them AGAIN, writing **83 double carriage returns** (`\r\r\n`) into `persistence/server.ts`. TypeScript ignored them, every verifier passed, and the file looked fine; the only thing that caught it was a SABOTAGE anchor failing to match. Collapsed, and proven to be whitespace-only by comparing both versions with all CRs stripped. The lesson from the dashboard patcher two steps ago was written down and not applied.


- **MODULE 10 COLLABORATION, STEP 2: MEMBERSHIP REPLACES OWNERSHIP (2026-09-02b, mig 231 APPLIED). `verify-project-membership` 29, fourteen sabotages all caught. Suite 146 -> 147.** The pivot. Everything after this step is additive; this is the one that changes who can reach what.
  - **NOTHING CHANGED FOR A SINGLE-USER ACCOUNT, and it was measured rather than reasoned.** A live query compares the OLD rule (projects where `user_id` is me) against the NEW rule (projects I hold a membership on), user by user: **4 pairs each, 0 lost, 0 gained, identical sets.** Every owner was seeded as an Owner member, so membership is a SUPERSET of ownership and a lone user's world is untouched.
  - **ONE CHOKE POINT, and it stayed one.** `getProject` matched `user_id = me`; it now matches "I hold a membership" and returns the caller's ROLE. Thirteen routes gate on it and the version helpers deliberately do not filter by user because they rely on it having run, which is why this was a small change and why a mistake in it would have been a large one.
  - **NON-OWNERS ARE READ-ONLY, and the gate is unforgettable by construction.** `getProjectForWrite` is a SEPARATE function rather than a flag on `getProject`, precisely so it can be ENUMERATED: the verifier walks every route file, splits it into handlers, and fails if any POST / PATCH / PUT / DELETE gates on the read resolver. A flag could be forgotten and the route would still compile, still run, and silently let a Viewer save. Six routes share one `requireOwnedProject` helper across their GET and their writes, so they gained a write VARIANT rather than having the shared one narrowed, which would have stopped a Reviewer reading a project they are legitimately a member of.
  - **Owner-only is deliberate, not provisional.** There is no server-side edit lock yet: `editMode` is React state, `editingVersionId` is a module variable, and the autosave PATCHes the same version row every 1.5 seconds. Two people editing one project would be last-write-wins, silently. So the window is never opened, the code says why at the gate, and **the admin screen says so on screen** rather than showing four roles and implying more than the platform does.
  - **Denial is the default in every direction**: an unrecognised role denies, a failed membership lookup denies, no membership means the project is not found, and a PRE-231 DATABASE FALLS BACK TO THE OWNER CHECK rather than to an open one.
  - **Registry driven**, all-or-nothing: `membersTable` / `membersProjectColumn` / `membersUserColumn` / `membersRoleColumn`. A partially wired membership is an access control that half works, so a platform answering "partly" is treated as having none and access falls back to the owner. ERM and BVM inherit the feature, the admin screen and the API by naming four columns.
  - **The admin screen is HUB LEVEL**, at `/modeling/dashboard` behind an admin-only "Team access" nav item, with `/api/admin/project-members` behind it. Not inside REFM, because assigning projects is something an admin does before entering a platform. Ownership cannot be granted or revoked there: it comes from the project row, and a membership disagreeing with it would be a second answer to "who owns this".
  - **Three of my own mistakes, all caught before shipping.** The migration's re-run probe demoted the ONLY owner, which legitimately leaves a project ownerless, so the guard fired and aborted the dry run: the guard was right and the probe was wrong. Check A4 matched `'owner'` anywhere in the migration file, including the comment explaining the seed, so a sabotage that neutered the seed left it green. And a patch script applied the line-ending conversion twice, producing `\r\r\n`. **A fourth was caught by the SUITE rather than by me**: `listProjectRowsByIds` reads projects by id with no visible bound, safe only because `id` is the primary key, and `verify-refm-version-reads` rejects exactly that shape because PostgREST silently truncates an unbounded read at its own cap (TRAPS 2.1). A read whose safety rests on an invariant the reader cannot see is not a bounded read; it now carries an explicit limit.


- **THE FLAKY RATE-LIMIT TEST IS FIXED BY INJECTING THE CLOCK (2026-09-02). `verify-public-pages-api` 40/3 (or 43/0 when lucky) -> 47 passed / 0 failed, confirmed on EIGHT consecutive runs.** It fired 61 live requests at a 60-per-60,000ms limiter on the real clock; each request runs the real handler with its database queries, so the loop took about as long as the window it was measuring and straddled the boundary. The limiter was correct throughout; the test timed it with a stopwatch the same length as the thing being timed.
  - **The seam is in `rateLimit.ts`, not the route.** `setRateLimitClock(fn | null)` joins `resetRateLimit` in the module that exists precisely to hold test seams (a Next route may only export a fixed set of names, which the file header already explained). **The route is unchanged.** With no injection the clock is `Date.now`, and a check asserts that nothing under `app/` calls the seam, so a route can never quietly acquire a fake clock.
  - **The limit was not widened and the loop is not retried.** Both would have hidden the race rather than removed it.
  - **The rewrite is STRONGER than what it replaced.** A frozen clock can assert what a wall clock cannot without sleeping for a minute: 1ms BEFORE the reset the caller is still refused, and AT the reset the same caller is allowed again. 43 checks became 47.
  - **Two mistakes of my own, both caught by sabotage rather than by reading.** (1) **The per-IP check went vacuous**: placed AFTER the window-roll steps, time had already moved past the reset, so a limiter keyed on one GLOBAL bucket looked correct too, and swapping the per-IP key for a constant failed NOTHING until the check moved back above the roll. (2) **The clock sabotage cannot name a single expected failure**: removing the injection breaks four section-5 checks but WHICH ones depends on machine speed, which is the flakiness itself reappearing, so that one case accepts any failure and says why.
  - Seven sabotages, all caught, including one that puts the seam into a route.

## 2026-09-01

- **THE MIGRATION LOG IS DE-DUPED, AND THE DIVERGENCE SCAN WAS RE-RUN FIRST (2026-09-01a).** The previous entry logged 47 migrations appearing on two rows each and deliberately stopped short of acting, because a row edited between the scan and the edit is exactly the case that makes a de-dup unsafe. **Re-scanned before touching anything: all 47 pairs were still byte-identical, in full row text and in status marker, so no pair had diverged and nothing had to be reconciled by hand.** De-duped by dropping one row of each identical pair. **Measured after: 216 table rows, 216 distinct migrations, ZERO duplicated.** The flag audit still reads 0 flagged PENDING and 0 flags to correct, and its own count is higher (237 rows) because it reads markers out of the head blockquotes as well as the table, which is the intended behaviour and not a leftover duplicate. Worth doing because a duplicated row is two places to update and two chances to disagree, which is precisely how eleven stale flags survived from 2026-08-16 to 2026-09-01 in the tool that mirrored them.

- **MODULE 10 COLLABORATION, STEP 0: THE ROLES, THE MODULE NUMBER, AND WHERE VISIBILITY LIVES (2026-09-01g). `verify-collab-roles` 24.** The groundwork, with nothing enforced yet and that fact stated rather than implied.
  - **IT IS MODULE 10, NOT 8**, settled from two independent live tables rather than from preference: `platform_modules` holds `{number: 10, slug: 'collaborate'}` and `features_registry` holds `module_10` = "Module 10: Collaborate", while **8 is Portfolio**, which is already shipped (the Portfolio Dashboard, 2026-08-30) and already sold (`module_8` is included for solo / pro / firm). Numbering collaboration as 8 would have collided with a live, paid feature.
  - **FOUR ROLES, DEFINED ONCE: Owner / Editor / Reviewer / Viewer**, in `src/core/collab/projectRoles.ts`. It sits in `core/` rather than `shared/` because the eslint boundaries rule lets core import only core, and two core files re-export it; putting it in shared would have made the rule unenforceable at exactly the layer that needs it.
  - **OWNER IS NOT ADMIN, and the distinction is load-bearing.** `users.role === 'admin'` is the PLATFORM administrator, compared as a bare string across the auth layer; owner is a role on ONE PROJECT. Conflating them would have made every project owner an administrator of the platform.
  - **MODULE VISIBILITY IS PER PLATFORM** (`refm/lib/moduleVisibility.ts`), never in the shared role table, because which modules exist is a property of the platform and REFM's list is not ERM's or BVM's.
  - **NOTHING IS ENFORCED YET, SAID PLAINLY.** `currentUserRole` is client state pinned to owner, so `can()` still returns true for everything; a role has nowhere to be read from until membership lands in step 2, and enforcement lands in step 4. The verifier pins that this is the CURRENT state rather than pinning it as correct, so it will fail when the pin stops matching reality.

- **MODULE 10 COLLABORATION, STEP 1: WHO SAVED THIS VERSION (2026-09-01i, mig 230 APPLIED). `verify-version-authorship` 23, thirteen sabotages all caught. Suite 145 pass / 1 fail of 146, and the 1 is NOT this change: `verify-public-pages-api` is FLAKY (fail / pass / fail on three identical runs, and it fails on a clean tree too). Diagnosed and logged in CLAUDE-TODO.md.** `refm_project_versions` carried no author column at all, so the platform could not answer "who saved this" even for a single user. The smallest useful step, and deliberately independent: no membership, no roles, no lock.
  - **NOT BACKFILLED, on purpose.** The obvious move is to fill 1,404 existing rows from `refm_projects.user_id`. That is who owns the project TODAY, a different claim from who saved a given version, and from step 2 a project has several people who can save. It would manufacture an audit trail, and an invented author reads as evidence. NULL means "unknown", it means the same thing three ways (predates the column / BASE-select fallback / author deleted their account), and no reader has to distinguish them.
  - **ON DELETE SET NULL, and PROVED rather than asserted.** CASCADE would delete a project's history because a former colleague closed their account. The applier builds a throwaway user -> project -> version chain inside a transaction, deletes the AUTHOR, confirms the version SURVIVES with `created_by` nulled, and rolls back. One word in a migration, and the difference between keeping and losing history.
  - **A DEFECT IN MY OWN CODE, CAUGHT BY ITS OWN VERIFIER BEFORE IT SHIPPED.** `resolveAuthorNames` documented "never throws" and did not deliver: it took `sb: Db = getServerClient()` as a DEFAULT PARAMETER, which is evaluated at call time and throws outright when `SUPABASE_URL` is absent. A missing env or a client-construction failure would have escaped the resolver and 500ed the entire versions GET, taking version history down over a cosmetic decoration. The client is now built inside the try. The check that should have caught it earlier was `check('never throws', true)`, a literal, which is exactly how an unchecked claim survives; it now actually calls the resolver with no env and asserts it does not throw.
  - **Three creation paths, all authored**: saving a version, creating a project, and duplicating one. Duplicating records the DUPLICATOR, not the source version's author, who did not make that save. A count guard fails if a fourth path is added without an author.
  - **Names resolve server-side, once per page**, because `created_by` is a uuid and a uuid is not something a reader can use. An unresolvable id is ABSENT from the map rather than echoed back, and the UI renders nothing at all for an unknown author instead of the word "Unknown", which would add a word to every pre-230 row without adding a fact.
  - **Live state after applying**: 1,404 versions, 0 authored, which is the correct and intended reading. A probe insert inside a rolled-back transaction confirmed a new version stores its author and joins to a real name.

- **Pro seats corrected to 1 (2026-09-01h).** The diagnosis flagged that Pro was sold 3 Team Seats while `rbac` and `module_10` (Collaborate) are both Firm-only: three seats with no roles and no Collaborate module. Set to 1 in the admin panel; confirmed live in `plan_permissions` and on the deployed pricing page, which reads that table directly with `revalidate = 0` and carries no hardcoded seat copy. The `seats` row also carries `build_status: needs_build` and a "Coming soon" description, so it is honestly labelled.


- **PROJECT CARD ORDERING: STATUS, AN URGENT FLAG, AND A MANUAL ORDER (2026-09-01f, mig 229 APPLIED). `verify-project-card-ordering` 54, seventeen sabotages all caught.** Cards sorted by last-modified, so any write reordered the page and restoring a deleted project moved it to the front.
  - **THE ORDERING RULE IS ONE PURE FUNCTION**, `compareProjectCards` in the new `src/shared/admin/projectStatus.ts`: status group, then priority WITHIN the group, then manual order within the group, then recency LAST. The server, the grid and the verifier all call it, so they cannot disagree about what the user is looking at.
  - **THE GROUP ORDER IS NOT THE LIFECYCLE ORDER, deliberately.** Construction, Operation, Funded, Draft, Completed, Closed, Dropped: live work, then pipeline, then not started, then finished, then abandoned. Priority sorts within a group and never across one, so a Draft-first order would bury a live site under twenty drafts with no flag able to lift it. The cost is that the order is not self-evident, which is why each group renders a heading and a one-line hint rather than an unexplained sequence.
  - **THE STATUS VOCABULARY WAS REPLACED, NOT EXTENDED, and that was measured before it was written.** 149's CHECK is an approval workflow (Draft / Active / IC Review / Approved / Archived); the new set is a lifecycle (Draft / Funded / Construction / Operation / Completed / Closed / Dropped). They overlap on 'Draft' ALONE. Every production row was 'Draft' (4 projects, 3 users, probed), so NO ROW WAS REWRITTEN. This is the one part of the change that is not additive, and it was confirmed before building rather than assumed.
  - **'Archived' IS GONE FROM THE DATABASE ENUM AND MUST NOT RETURN.** The application-side enum dropped it on 2026-08-30 but the CHECK still carried it, so the duplication was only half removed; 229 finishes it. **STATUS IS A LABEL**: it is read in exactly two presentational places and gates nothing, and the standing rule now sits at the top of `projectStatus.ts` and in a COMMENT on the column. `Closed` and `Dropped` are the obvious candidates to acquire behaviour and MUST NOT: archiving is `archived`, deletion is `deleted_at`, and a third and fourth word meaning the same thing is exactly the defect that was just removed.
  - **THE ABSENT-VALUE TRAP, TWICE.** `sort_order` is NULLABLE and decorates to NULL, never 0: NULL means "never dragged" and falls back to recency, while 0 is a real position at the top of a group. Decorating it to 0 would silently promote every project on a pre-229 database to first place, and would make the first drag appear to do nothing. Pinned by C4 and G3, both sabotage-tested.
  - **THREE COPIES OF THE VOCABULARY EXISTED**, and the compiler found two of them the moment the shared one landed: `StorageProject.status` was a spelled-out literal union, and a fake ERM entry in `verify-project-soft-delete` had to gain the new registry fields. Both now read the shared definition, and A3 fails on any file that restates the union.
  - **REGISTRY DRIVEN.** `PROJECT_SOURCES` gains `statusColumn` / `priorityColumn` / `sortOrderColumn`, and `hasCardOrdering` is all-or-nothing (status without a sort order groups but cannot be reordered; a sort order without a status has no group to be an order within). ERM and BVM inherit the whole feature by naming three columns; the vocabulary and the comparator are not restated per platform.
  - **REORDERING IS A WHOLE-GROUP OPERATION**, its own route (`POST /api/refm/projects/reorder`), not a field on the card PATCH. The client sends the dense 0..n-1 assignment it is displaying and the server stores it verbatim, so there is no second derivation to disagree with the first. Ownership is enforced PER ROW, so a payload naming another user's project reorders nothing, and the count actually updated is returned rather than discarded. A drag NEVER changes a status: status is set from the dropdown, one card at a time, so a mis-drop cannot reclassify a project.
  - **A LATENT BUG FOUND ON THE WAY**: `updateProject` selected the BASE column list and then decorated, so every write echoed back `archived:false` regardless of what had just been stored. Harmless while nothing read the echo, wrong the moment the urgent toggle did (it would have snapped straight back off). Now selects the full list with the same probe-and-fall-back.
  - **THE MIGRATION WAS PROVED BEFORE IT WAS APPLIED**, in the shape 227 and 228 established: run and rolled back, re-run for idempotence, both enforcement directions probed (retired 'Active' rejected 23514, new 'Construction' accepted), and **THE GUARD PROVED TO FIRE** by planting an unmappable status inside a savepoint, where it refused naming the count and the value rather than dropping a constraint live data would violate. A guard nobody has seen fire is a guard nobody knows works.
  - **Three of seventeen sabotages were INERT on the first run, all the same root cause**: a file-wide regex matching a DIFFERENT occurrence than the one sabotaged. The ownership check sliced to end-of-file and was satisfied by a later function; the archived-disabled check was satisfied by the urgent button while the select had lost it; the cross-group drag guard was satisfied by the drop-target highlight while the actual guard was deleted. All three re-scoped to the code they mean to test, and all seventeen now caught.
  - **No number moved: 0 of 8,435** on the reference project, full financials and returns snapshots at 17 significant figures. Nothing here touches the engine.


- **THE CENSUS MOVED TO THE REFERENCE PROJECT, AND WHAT IT FOUND THERE WAS MOSTLY ITSELF (2026-09-01e). Suite 142 pass / 1 fail -> 143 pass / 0 fail, the first fully green measurement since the 2026-08-31 audit opened at 123 / 19. `verify-module6-field-census` 16 passed / 1 failed -> 18 passed / 0 failed.** Two instructed changes, and then everything the second one uncovered.
  - **HIDDEN NOW MEANS ONE THING, the narrower of the two gate functions.** `nonEconomicLeverReason` FILTERS a field out of `fields` in `Module6Scenarios`, so it is unreachable: not in the picker, not in search, not under "Show all", never in the grid. `inactiveLeverReason` hides nothing: the field stays in the picker and renders in the grid with the amber "not used under current settings" badge carrying its reason as a tooltip, and is dropped only from the CURATED DEFAULTS. The check treated both as concealment, which is why the four land phasing fields, visible and annotated with a note that says outright that moving them shifts returns, read as hidden levers. Only the first is now "hidden". **Annotated movers are not waved through**: they are listed in the output and pinned by pattern (`ANNOTATED_MOVER`), so a NEW gate placed over a live lever still fails red.
  - **THE FIXTURE IS MARINA GATE**, not FMP RE HUB. `scripts/fetch-fmp-re-hub.ts` is superseded by `scripts/fetch-census-fixture.ts`, which fetches either and prints the facility-share sum on every run. RE HUB's two facilities summed to 83.33 rather than 100 and its `buaSqm` / `unitArea` sat at zero, so the candidate ladder reached 100,000x and reported movers off 0.0005% deltas; both produced findings that were facts about the fixture. Both are gone at source.
  - **THE PER-FIELD PRECONDITION IS STILL NEEDED, and the fixture move changed which clause carries it.** Asked and answered by removing it: the SUM clause is now quiet (one facility at 100, so the sum is clean) but the SINGLE-FACILITY clause fires, and without it `facilitySharePct` goes straight back into the failure with 11 KPIs "moved", because dialling the only facility to 150 or 50 measures a funding plan the platform reports as broken. Same false finding, different route. Both clauses stay and the one that fired is now PRINTED, so it is never an unstated assumption.
  - **THE MOVE THEN EXPOSED 71 UNGATED DEAD FIELDS AND 3 FAILING CHECKS, and the first thing it convicted was the census.** The gates had been written by walking RE HUB's dead list, which is the mirrored-list failure: a list whose correctness is defined by ANOTHER list drifts silently. **Thirty-one of the 71 were not dead at all.**
    - **A STRING FIELD WITH NO DECLARED DOMAIN WAS PROBED WITH ZERO CANDIDATES AND FILED AS A DEAD LEVER.** `candidatesFor` fell through to `return []` for any string matching no `PATH_ALTERNATES` entry, so the dead bucket said "inert, no gating reason" about fields it had never touched. Seven sat in that hole and five are live, measured: `costLines[].catalogId` -> 'commission' moves 13 KPIs, `costLines[].phasingSource` -> 'own' moves 11, `costLines[].stageOverride` -> 'land' moves 8, `assets[].capexPhasing.phasing` -> 'even' moves 11, `fundTerms.managementFeeFunding` -> 'deficit' moves 11. The domains are declared, and **an undeclared one is now a hard failure**: a check that never ran is not evidence of inertness.
    - **PERIOD FIELDS WERE PROBED UPWARD ONLY.** A phase-2 line seeded `endPeriod` 4 against a 3-period construction has its extra periods clamped away, so 5 and 7 changed nothing while 2 moves 11 KPIs. Candidates now go both ways.
    - **THE TERMINAL SPOT-PROOF NAMED ONE METHOD.** It pinned perpetuity growth; the reference project exits on a CAP RATE, so it asserted that an input the model correctly ignores should move the terminal value, and failed for being right. It now drives whichever method is active, and fails if it meets one it does not know. `cap_rate` was also missing from the `terminalMethod` domain.
  - **THE 40 THAT WERE GENUINELY INERT, each gated with a reason grounded in the code rather than in the fixture.**
    - **`windowFollowsConstruction` x14 is not an engine input at all.** It is read only by `updatePhase` in the store, which re-derives a flagged line's window when the phase construction length is edited. A value-only case override patches the snapshot the engine reads and runs no store action, so it can never do anything. REMOVED from the picker.
    - **The migration-208 legacy fund fields x4 are retired.** `fundTerms.ts` says so in its own comment; `FUND_FEE_SPECS` replaced the shared `feeBase` with a per-fee base, and `carryPct` is an alias the resolver never consults while `performanceFeePct` is present. Inert even in PAIRS (committedCapital + managementFeePct together move nothing). REMOVED.
    - **GATED, shown with a note**: a followed phasing source (land cash / sales collections) replaces the WINDOW, so the line's own start / end is not what the engine spends on (`resolvePhasingSource`, the engine's own resolver); a selling cost in a phase where no asset sells has nothing to be a percentage of (`deriveAssetScope` + `assetStrategySells`, the master-line twin of the existing override rule); a per-asset `customRate` on an asset drawing from a real parcel; HQ opex indexation with every HQ line off or zero; `fundSize` while `fundSizeOverride` is off (with it ON, it moves 8 KPIs); `facilityLimit`, because NO entry in `FUND_FEE_SPECS` is charged on it (the four rate fees run on fund size, total equity and the debt ACTUALLY RAISED); and `performanceFeePct` / `hurdleRatePct`, which move the waterfall's net-of-carry LP distributions but not the consolidated returns the comparison KPIs are struck on.
  - **A THIRD FALSE GATE REASON, and a fourth gate deleted for a premise its own neighbour refutes.**
    - **`land-cash[].value` read "Land is funded in-kind here; the cash-land cost line contributes nothing".** Marina Gate funds land BOTH ways (both lines at 100%), and overriding it moves 13 KPIs including Land Cost and Total Development Cost. True of the project it was written against, false of the reference one. It now MEASURES the split rather than asserting it: inert only when that half is actually zero.
    - **`otherRevenue.mode` is UNGATED.** It claimed "other revenue is not active for this Operate asset" whenever the ASSET-LEVEL `startingADR` was zero. Hospitality revenue runs off per-SUB-UNIT ADR, which is exactly why the asset-level scalar is gated a few lines above, so room revenue exists and a percent-of-rooms other-revenue mode has plenty to scale. Marina Hotel carries `startingADR` 0 with `otherRevenue percent_of_rooms` 0.08, and switching the mode moves 10 KPIs including Gross Development Value. The comment directly above it already made this argument for F&B mode and then drew the opposite conclusion for the identical field one line down. A live revenue lever, restored to the curated defaults.
  - **Thirteen sabotages, all caught, file restored green after each.** Every new gate withdrawn one at a time, the two new checks broken by moving a proven mover into each gate function, the string domain withdrawn, the period candidates reverted, and the terminal proof pointed at the inactive method.
  - **No number moved on Marina Gate: 0 of 8,435**, the full financials AND returns snapshots walked at 17 significant figures. `assumptionGrid.ts` is presentation; `applyOverrides` and the engine were not touched.


- **THE TWO REAL GATE REASONS CORRECTED, AND THE CENSUS STOPPED MEASURING BROKEN MODELS (2026-09-01d). Suite 142/1 both sides; `verify-module6-field-census` 14 passed / 3 failed -> 16 passed / 1 failed.** Re-measured first against **FMP MARINA GATE**, the reference project, after the correction that RE HUB is a test project and a finding that appears only there is not a finding. **Two of the four survived that re-measurement and two did not.**
  - **REWORDED, not ungated: the four land-in-kind phasing reasons.** They read "land cost is incurred upfront; the phasing is not used". On Marina Gate, moving `land-inkind__phase_1.startPeriod` from 0 to 3 moves **Equity IRR 21.87%**, Distributed-Equity IRR 10.83%, Project IRR 7.18%, NPV 2.61%. The reason collapsed two claims into one: land IS a lump so the window does not spread it into a curve, but the window is what `phaseLocalToProjectIndex` places the lump AGAINST, so it decides WHICH YEAR the charge lands. Shape is not used; placement is. The note now says exactly that, and names the magnitude.
  - **REWORDED, not ungated: `Marina Residences.buaSqm`.** It read "this Sell asset enters built area per sub-unit; the asset-level BUA is not used". Setting it 0 -> 100,000 moves **14 KPIs, equity IRR 90%, equity MOIC 71%**. The missing distinction: such an asset takes its SALEABLE area from the sub-units, so the field does not drive REVENUE, but any cost line allocated on BUA share still reads it, so it does drive COST. The note now separates the two.
  - **DROPPED, census fixed instead: `facilitySharePct`.** On Marina Gate there is ONE facility at 100% and any other value makes the platform report `reconciliation.ok = false`, "Facility shares sum 50 (expected 100)", the wrong-sum state it STATES with a one-click repair rather than rescaling. The census was measuring a model no user can save. **Three attempts were needed to guard it correctly, and the first two are recorded because each was wrong in an instructive way**: rejecting `reconciliation.ok === false` rejected the BASE (which already carries the deposit advisory) and computed nothing at all; rejecting any NEW issue lost two correctly-passing checks, because a higher construction rate legitimately outgrowing the funding is a real consequence of a real lever. **And no result filter can work here, because THIS SNAPSHOT'S BASE IS ALREADY MALFORMED for that field**: RE HUB's two facilities carry 33.33% and 50%, summing to 83.33, before any override. So the field is declared UNMEASURABLE on the project, reported as such, and excluded from BOTH halves of the contract.
  - **DROPPED, census fixed instead: the land-price check.** `parcels[].rate` is exactly proportional on Marina Gate (rate x2 -> land cost 123,000,000 -> 246,000,000, 13 KPIs move). It is inert on RE HUB for a documented reason: every asset there holds `landAllocation.parcelId: "__custom__"` with its own `customRate`, a supported branch of `computeAssetLandBreakdown` where land value is `split.sqm x customRate` and the parcel's rate feeds nothing (the only asset pointing at `parcel_1` has `sqm: 0`). Land still responded to `cashPct` / `inKindPct`, which is what proved the value never came from the parcel. **Not a defect and not an undocumented consequence of `landAllocationMode: 'sqm'`.** The check now tests the precondition and skips with a reason instead of asserting a project-invariant property.
  - **ONE CHECK REMAINS RED, and rewording a reason was never going to clear it.** "NO live lever is hidden" tests whether a mover IS GATED, not whether its reason is TRUE, so a truthful note on a still-gated field still fails. Six fields remain: the four land-in-kind phasing fields plus RE HUB's `buaSqm` and `unitArea` (the latter two are the fixture-noise pair, moving 0.0005% off a zero base on a 100,000x override, and do not reproduce on Marina Gate). Closing it needs a decision that was NOT authorised here, so it was not taken: either ungate the phasing fields, or teach the census that `inactiveLeverReason` means "shown with a note" while only `nonEconomicLeverReason` means "hidden from the picker", which is what the two functions actually do in `Module6Scenarios`.
  - **No number moved on Marina Gate**: all 14 KPIs byte-identical (Equity IRR 0.371716, Project IRR 0.261199, Equity MOIC 4.727423, NPV 211,654,548), cost of sales 254,301,653.83, the capex split still reconciles. Fixture fingerprint 0 of 4,491. The reasons are strings; changing them cannot move a number, and this proves it rather than asserting it.

- **THE WHOLE LOGGED BACKLOG, CLEARED (2026-09-01c). Suite 135 pass / 8 fail -> see below.** Six items, diagnosed together before any was touched, because two of them changed the plan.
  - **ITEM 5, TREATED AS THE PRIORITY: A LIVE WRONG NUMBER IN AN EXPORT.** Three of the workbook's four M4 emitters read "this row has a `totalOverride`" as "this row is a balance, print the LAST period". Right only while every override IS the last period, and on the Financing tab five are not (`priorBal`, a literal 0, `priorExisting`, `opening[0]`, `-minCash`). **Measured: SIX of sixteen override rows printed a Total the builder never asked for. Every "Opening" row showed a CLOSING figure**, so "Opening Cash" read 58,922,877.94 where the builder stated 0.00. **Correcting my own first measurement**: I reported 9 of 17, inflated by a label-keyed diagnostic aliasing rows ("Opening" and "Closing" each appear more than once); keyed on label PLUS values the truth is 6 of 16, and the verifier keys the same way so it cannot repeat the mistake. Also a correction to the brief: the two unfixed copies were in `addOpex` and `addFinancing`, NOT the statements, which already used the shared emitter. A BLANK override stays the "no total" sentinel (`Number('')` is 0 AND finite, so a naive parse prints 0.00 for a closing balance). Four pins in `verify-excel-export`, three sabotages.
  - **ITEM 1: eleven dead levers were four kinds.** `project.idcConfig.fundingMode` (once live: debt_drawdown / cash / conditional, retired 2026-08-18) and `cashPaymentProfile.profileMode` x3 (once live: absolute_with_catchup / relative_to_sale, superseded 2026-08-19) are REMOVED; `isCompanion` was never a lever and joins the non-economic set. **Matched on the FULL PATH, not the leaf**: `profileMode` is a leaf on TWO objects and `recognitionProfile.profileMode` is LIVE under over-time recognition, so a leaf-name entry would have killed a working lever. The six commission `costOverrides` are **GATED, not removed**: on a Sell asset that override is real, and it is inert only because these three sit on Lease / Operate assets where a commission has nothing to be a percentage of. The reason is computed with `deriveAssetScope` and `assetStrategySells`, the engine's own functions.
  - **ITEM 2: thirty checks across seven verifiers, all fixtures.** Sale-cohort 21, deck 3, Module 6 3 (see the dedicated entry). Every rewrite sabotage-tested AND each sabotage confirmed to land inside the intended function: the post-handover check turned out to cover `sell.ts`'s post-sales convention, not `cohortTerms`, and disabling all THREE cohort guards left it passing until the right function was sabotaged.
  - **ITEM 3: 47 duplicate migration rows removed.** Divergence re-scanned first as the precondition: all 47 pairs byte-identical, none diverged. 257 -> 210 rows, one per migration; the diff is 47 deletions, every one a migration row, zero insertions.
  - **ITEMS 4 and 6: migration 228** adds `branding_config.scope UNIQUE` (declared by 003, never created, table empty) and four `session_watch_history` NOT NULLs (declared by 107, no-opped by `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` on pre-existing columns, zero NULLs live, defaults intact).
  - **`training_pending_registrations.course` IS DROPPED FROM SCOPE, PERMANENTLY.** The drift report calls it declared-NOT-NULL-but-nullable, which is true per-file and wrong as a conclusion: **migration 131 dropped that NOT NULL on purpose** ("the new registration form no longer asks for course at signup time"), and **15 of 18 live rows are NULL**. Adding it would contradict a deliberate decision and fail on 15 rows. Recorded in the 228 header and in CLAUDE-DB so nobody tries again.
  - **STILL OPEN, and NOT fixture problems**: two `verify-module6-field-census` checks. "NO live lever is hidden" reports 7 fields that MOVE a KPI while being gated or excluded (`buaSqm`, `unitArea`, two `land-inkind` window fields, `facilitySharePct`), and "Land price moves at least one comparison KPI" reports none moved. My earlier report grouped these with the OpEx-fixture failures; that was wrong. They are genuine curation findings and each needs a modelling decision (is the gate's stated reason false, or is the field genuinely inert and the census's mover-detection too eager), not a fixture edit.

- **`211_refm_fund_size_override` marked APPLIED, and the marker rule is now forward-looking (2026-09-01b).** It was the one PROBED migration whose CLAUDE-DB.md row carried no status marker, which only became visible once `audit-migration-flags` started reading the document instead of a hardcoded copy of it. **Confirmed applied from the CATALOG, not from PostgREST**: `refm_fund_terms.fund_size_override` is `boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` AND carries the COLUMN COMMENT this migration writes. The comment is the part that matters: `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` no-ops on a pre-existing column and leaves no other trace, so column-exists alone cannot tell "211 ran" from "a column of that name exists". 2 rows in the table, 0 with the override on. **BOTH duplicate rows were updated**, not one: editing a single row would have created the first divergent pair in the file, which is the exact failure the duplicate-row TODO is about and which the audit reports as DIVERGED.
