Re-scanned after: all 47 pairs still byte-identical. **The other 153 unmarked rows are deliberately LEFT**: they predate the discipline and a working production is evidence enough. The rule is forward-looking instead, recorded in CLAUDE.md beside the placement rule: **every NEW migration row must carry uppercase `APPLIED` or `PENDING`**, cleared in the session it is applied, with what was probed. Sabotage-tested in both directions: downgrade the marker to lowercase prose and the audit reports "NO MARKER" again; restore it and the row reads `flag correct`. **Audit after: Flags to correct 0, Flagged PENDING 0, Recorded APPLIED 30, Doc states no usable flag 0.**

- **THE FLAG AUDIT READS THE DOCUMENT NOW, AND CANNOT CONTRADICT IT (2026-09-01).** `scripts/audit-migration-flags.ts` held its `doc:` flag HARDCODED beside each probe, mirroring what CLAUDE-DB.md said the day it was written. The 2026-08-16 sweep cleared all eleven PENDING markers from the document and nobody updated the copy, so for two weeks the script reported **"Flags to correct: 11"** on every run, naming eleven migrations the document already recorded APPLIED. The mirrored-list failure again, and worse than merely wrong: a REAL stale flag would have arrived in a pile of eleven false ones nobody reads. All 30 hardcoded flags are DELETED and the script now PARSES CLAUDE-DB.md. **Flags to correct: 11 -> 0.** CASE IS THE DISCRIMINATOR, deliberately: the document uses uppercase APPLIED / PENDING as markers and lowercase freely in prose ("applied at the effective date", "pending/approved/declined" as enum values), and 13 rows carry a lowercase "pending" with no marker, so a case-insensitive match would invent 13 PENDING flags out of description text. **It refuses to guess**: a row with no marker, or a duplicated migration whose rows DISAGREE, is reported as "doc states no usable flag" and counted separately, never collapsed into either status, because picking one is how a contradiction gets laundered into a fact. It also states COVERAGE (180 of 210 logged migrations are unprobed), so the summary cannot read as a clean bill of health for the whole log. Sabotage-tested three ways: flip a doc row to PENDING and it reports the discrepancy; make a duplicate pair disagree and it reports DIVERGED; restore and it returns to 0. One real finding surfaced that the hardcoding hid: `211_refm_fund_size_override` carries NO status marker in the document.
- **CLAUDE-DB.md's migration log has 47 DUPLICATED migrations (2026-09-01, logged; DE-DUPED the same day, see 2026-09-01a above).** 257 rows across 210 migrations: 47 appear on two rows each. Present long before this session. **THE DIVERGENCE QUESTION IS ANSWERED, and the answer is the good one: all 47 pairs are BYTE-IDENTICAL.** Zero pairs differ in text, zero differ in status flag. So a de-dup is mechanically safe (drop one row of each identical pair) and cannot lose content. Measured with a scan over every `| \`NNN_name.sql\` |` row, comparing full row text and the parsed status marker; re-run it before de-duping rather than trusting this line, because a row edited in the meantime is exactly the case that makes it unsafe. **Why it is worth doing anyway**: a duplicated row is two places to update and two chances to disagree, which is how eleven stale flags survived from 2026-08-16 to 2026-09-01 in the tool that mirrored them. **Also surfaced by the same scan**: 153 of 210 migration rows carry NO status marker at all (57 carry APPLIED, 0 carry PENDING), so the log's flag discipline covers about a quarter of it; and `211_refm_fund_size_override` is the one PROBED migration with no marker, which `audit-migration-flags` now reports as "doc states no usable flag" rather than guessing.


## 2026-08-31

- **MIGRATION 227 APPLIED AND VERIFIED ON THE LIVE DATABASE (2026-08-31h).** Flag cleared in [CLAUDE-DB.md](CLAUDE-DB.md). **The verification had to be indirect, and that is the interesting part**: 227 declares a shape production ALREADY had, so the FK definition and NOT NULL cannot distinguish applied from not-applied. The two COMMENTs it writes are the only fingerprint, and the transaction test the day it was written had already proven they revert to ABSENT on rollback. Live now: constraint COMMENT **PRESENT**, column COMMENT **PRESENT**, `admin_id` `NOT NULL` with `confdeltype = 'a'` (NO ACTION), 71 rows, 0 NULL actors. Both FKs on the table report `convalidated = true`, so the drop-and-re-add VALIDATED every existing row rather than landing `NOT VALID`, which is the failure mode that would have left a constraint present but unenforced. `npx tsx scripts/audit-constraints-live.ts` against the applied database: **0 ON DELETE differences**, 55 of 59 effective FK columns matching after 12 superseding ALTERs.
- **TWO THINGS FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, NEITHER FIXED, BOTH PRE-EXISTING.** (1) **`scripts/audit-migration-flags.ts` reports "Flags to correct: 11" permanently and cannot do otherwise.** Its `doc:` column is HARDCODED in the script, not read from CLAUDE-DB.md, so it still carries the eleven PENDING markers that the 2026-08-16 sweep cleared from the document. The script's own header describes those eleven as the thing it exists to check. It is the mirrored-list trap (TRAPS 3.9 family): a list whose correctness is defined by ANOTHER list, kept by hand, drifted. Consequence: the house tool for "is this migration applied" always reports the same 11 false positives, so a REAL stale flag would arrive in a pile of noise nobody reads. (2) **CLAUDE-DB.md's migration table has 43 DUPLICATED rows** (`170_payment_paddle_client_token.sql` appears at two line numbers, `189_article_hero_position.sql` likewise, and 41 more). Present at HEAD and before this session, so not introduced here. A duplicated row is two places to update and two chances to disagree, which is exactly how the eleven stale flags survived.

- **MIGRATION 227: the admin_id decision is written into the schema, not just into a comment (2026-08-31g, PENDING APPLY, no-op against production by design).** `admin_audit_log.admin_id` was the one REAL divergence the constraint audit found: `007` declares `ON DELETE SET NULL` and nullable, live is `NO ACTION` and `NOT NULL` (read from `pg_constraint`, 71 rows, 0 NULLs), and 007's clause never took effect because it opens `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` on a table that already existed. `221` MADE the decision and wrote it in prose, *"admin_id is DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE (NOT NULL, NO ACTION): it names the ACTING admin"*, but ALTERed nothing, because live already matched. **A decision recorded only in a comment is not reproducible: a database rebuilt from this repo did not match production for this column.** 227 declares it. `ON DELETE NO ACTION` is spelled out even though it is the Postgres default and `pg_get_constraintdef` will not print it back, so the next reader sees a choice rather than an omission. **007 is NOT edited**: a migration records what RAN, and rewriting it would claim history that did not happen and destroy the only evidence of the drift. **TESTED AGAINST PRODUCTION INSIDE A TRANSACTION AND ROLLED BACK**: runs clean, a second run is a no-op, the 71 rows are untouched, and the NULL-actor guard fires with its explicit message (probed by dropping NOT NULL and inserting a null-actor row in a savepoint) rather than deleting or rewriting an audit row whose actor is unknown, which is a question for a human. Only observable change on production: two COMMENTs.
- **The constraint audit now reads the migration CHAIN, not one file (2026-08-31g).** It parsed `CREATE TABLE` only, so a later `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT` was invisible and the audit reported on whichever file happened to mention a column FIRST. That gave the OPPOSITE answer twice: 158 declares `user_permissions.created_by` NO ACTION and 221 alters it to SET NULL; 007 declares `admin_audit_log.admin_id` SET NULL and 227 alters it to NO ACTION. Read per-file, both look like live drift; read as a chain, both CONVERGE on live and there is nothing to report. **Applying 227 would not have cleared its own finding.** FK declarations are now collected in file order with the LAST one winning, which is how migrations actually compose. **ON DELETE differences: 2 -> 0.** Sabotage-tested in both directions: remove 227 and the admin_id finding returns; remove 221 and the created_by finding returns. The remaining 6 findings are unchanged and all recorded in [CLAUDE-DB.md](CLAUDE-DB.md).

- **CONSTRAINTS ARE NO LONGER ASSUMED: 157 DECLARED-UNVERIFIABLE, NOW READ (2026-08-31f, `scripts/audit-constraints-live.ts` NEW, read-only, nothing fixed).** Direct Postgres access is back, so the half of the schema audit that could never run has run. `audit-schema-drift.ts` reads the live schema through PostgREST's OpenAPI output, which exposes tables, columns, types, NOT NULL, defaults and FK TARGETS and nothing that lives in `pg_constraint`; it reported 157 declarations as unknown rather than assumed, which was the right call and is exactly how the 007 drift hid for months. **186 declarations parsed, 176 checkable, 8 findings: 57 of 65 FK `ON DELETE` clauses match exactly, all 51 column CHECKs present, 40 of 45 table-level constraints present.** Capability confirmed before trusting any of it: connection, `pg_constraint` + `pg_get_constraintdef` reads, and transactional DDL (`CREATE TABLE` + `ADD CONSTRAINT` inside a transaction, rolled back, confirmed absent; not superuser). Full record in [CLAUDE-DB.md](CLAUDE-DB.md) "Constraint drift, READ from pg_constraint".
  - **`newsletter_subscribers.email`: the LIVE composite is RIGHT and the migration is the stale side.** 078 declares `email` UNIQUE alone; live is `UNIQUE (email, hub)`, which lets one person subscribe to both hubs. **The data already relies on it: 248 rows, 246 distinct emails, two addresses appearing once per hub, so adding the declared constraint would FAIL today.** Recorded loudly in CLAUDE-DB so nobody "restores" it later.
  - **`branding_config.scope`: declared UNIQUE, nothing enforces it.** No unique constraint and no unique index, alone or composite. The table is EMPTY, so nothing violates it and nothing is at risk today, but the guarantee 003 claims does not exist. Logged, not fixed.
  - **The two `ON DELETE` differences: one needs nothing, one is a real divergence left open ON PURPOSE.** `user_permissions.created_by` reads as drift only in isolation: 158 declares NO ACTION, live is SET NULL, and **221 explicitly ALTERs it to SET NULL**, so the chain 006 -> 158 -> 221 CONVERGES and a rebuild reproduces production. Nothing to correct. `admin_audit_log.admin_id` IS a divergence: 007 declares `SET NULL` + nullable, live is `NO ACTION` + `NOT NULL`, because 007 opens `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` on a table that already existed and its whole column list was a no-op. 221 states the decision in prose ("admin_id is DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE") without ALTERing it, because live already was that. **So a database rebuilt from the migrations does NOT match production for this column.** The migration files are NOT edited: a migration records what RAN, and rewriting 007 would claim history that did not happen and destroy the only evidence of the drift. The remedy is a NEW migration (`ALTER ... ON DELETE NO ACTION` plus `SET NOT NULL`); not done, this pass was report-only.
  - **Two false positives in my own first draft, fixed before reporting.** `assessment_attempts_in_progress.chk_one_identifier` was reported absent while sitting in the catalog under exactly that name: the matcher took a "column list" from the first parenthesis group of `CONSTRAINT <name> CHECK ((tab_key IS NOT NULL AND ...))`, which is an expression. Named constraints are now matched by NAME, and CHECKs by the columns they MENTION. A composite UNIQUE that includes the declared column is now reported as `UNIQUE NARROWER LIVE` rather than lumped in with `UNIQUE MISSING`, because "the guarantee is different" and "there is no guarantee" are not the same finding.
  - **What it still cannot decide, and says so:** whether two CHECK expressions are EQUIVALENT. Postgres stores a normalised form, so a declared `CHECK (status IN ('a','b'))` returns as `CHECK ((status = ANY (ARRAY[...])))`. Presence is asserted, the live definition is printed, and judging the text is left to a reader.
- **THREE THINGS BROKE THE DB CONNECTION, EACH LOOKING LIKE A BAD PASSWORD (2026-08-31f, TRAPS 2.11).** (1) `db.<ref>.supabase.co` NO LONGER RESOLVES: Supabase retired IPv4 direct connections, so it fails ENOTFOUND, and against a standing "the credential is stale" assumption that read as more of the same. The credential was fine throughout. (2) The session pooler is `aws-<n>-<region>.pooler.supabase.com:5432` with user `postgres.<ref>`, and the WRONG region answers "tenant/user not found", which reads like a bad username; `aws-0` and `aws-1` are different tenants. (3) A STRAY SPACE after the colon in the refreshed value produced `28P01 password authentication failed for user "postgres"`, naming a role that appears nowhere in the string, because the pooler resolves the tenant from the username and then reports the underlying role. One character removed and everything worked. `connectionConfig` now REFUSES a `db.` host and a password with leading or trailing whitespace, each with a message naming the actual problem, so the next reader is not told "authentication failed" about a space.

- **A PROMO NEVER LINKS TO THE PAGE YOU ARE ON (2026-08-31e, `verify-promo-self-link` 27 NEW).** On `/modeling/real-estate` the "Real Estate Financial Modeling Platform is live" popup announced the platform to a visitor already standing on it, with a button back to the same page. **Where the target is configured**, in resolution order: `training_settings.modeling_hub_launched_cta_href` (an ADMIN OVERRIDE, currently set to `/modeling/real-estate`, edited on /admin/modules through `LaunchBannerCard`); if blank, the DERIVED default `platformHref(slug)` in `LaunchCountdownBanner.tsx` = `${APP_URL}/modeling/${slug}`, whose slug comes from `training_settings.modeling_hub_launch_platform` (`real-estate`). Where it is ALLOWED to appear is a separate list, `LAUNCH_BANNER_PATHS` in `launchCountdown.ts`, five exact paths including `/modeling/real-estate`. **The two were set independently and nothing compared them.** **FIXED AS A RULE**: new pure `src/shared/promo/samePageTarget.ts` answers "is this promo's destination the page being viewed", and BOTH popups ask it. The pricing `PromoPopup` had the identical collision and escaped it only by coincidence, because `/pricing` sits in its hide-list for an unrelated reason (the plan cards already show the offer) and nothing there names the destination; pointing that promo elsewhere would have re-opened it silently. The rule compares ORIGIN as well as path (the apex and the app subdomain both serve a real-estate path), normalises trailing slash, query and hash, treats the app subdomain's `/` -> `/modeling` rewrite as one page through an ORIGIN-SCOPED equivalence (unscoped, it would have wrongly suppressed on the apex home), and **refuses to guess**: no href, no path, a mailto, a bare fragment or an unparseable URL all mean SHOW, because a missed suppression is visible and a wrong one is silent. Verifier pins the live shape (a relative admin override of `/modeling/real-estate`), that the popup still shows on the other four allowed paths, and that the pricing promo would suppress itself even if `/pricing` left its hide-list.
- **CAPEX SPLIT RECONCILIATION, both live projects, MEASURED AND CLEAN (2026-08-31e, no code changed).** New read-only `scripts/probe-capex-split-reconciliation.ts`. **The identity is cost-of-sales base + fixed-asset base = capex incl. all land PLUS capitalised IDC**, and the "plus IDC" term is not slack: IDC is capitalised into the asset that financed it, so it sits inside whichever of the two bases that asset belongs to, while the capex aggregate itself does not carry it. **MARINA GATE**: 254,301,653.83 + 240,149,132.11 = 494,450,785.94 against 438,943,005.32 + 55,507,780.62 = 494,450,785.94, **residue exactly 0**. **RE HUB**: 4,473,786,097.14 + 527,035,387.27 = 5,000,821,484.41 against 4,912,201,581.86 + 88,619,902.55, **residue -9.54e-7**, which is IEEE-754 accumulation noise at 1.9e-16 relative on a 5.0bn base. **The split rule**: `isDepreciableAsset` (`fixed-assets-resolvers.ts`) is `Operate` or `Lease` plus companions; cost of sales is driven by `revenue.bySellAsset`, which covers `Sell` AND `Sell + Manage`. **A Sell + Manage asset is in exactly one base**: RE HUB's "Residential Tower" (Sell + Manage, 649,082,592.70) is in cost of sales and not in fixed assets, and its Operate COMPANION is a separate asset that would go to fixed assets and carries zero capex of its own. **Zero assets in both bases, zero in neither while carrying capex** on either project. **IDC is in exactly one base per asset**: Marina Gate 26,762,898.51 cost of sales + 28,744,882.11 fixed assets = 55,507,780.62 with 0.00 unaccounted; RE HUB 80,425,096.87 + 8,194,805.69 = 88,619,902.55 with -1.2e-8 unaccounted. One trap worth recording: an Operate or Lease asset's IDC is NOT inside `fixedAssets.projectTotals.depreciable.additionsPerPeriod`; it is a second capitalised stream with its own roll-forward (`idc.idcNbvPerPeriod`), so a reconciliation that reads only the depreciable additions understates the fixed-asset base by exactly the non-Sell IDC and looks like a real break.

- **`verify-tab3-default-seed` CLOSED (2026-08-31d, 24/6 -> 32/0), and the 2026-08-31b diagnosis of it was WRONG.** The instruction was to remove the RETT entry from `STANDARD_COST_LINE_IDS`. **That entry is not stale and removing it would have caused three defects**, so it was not removed and no Module 1 code was touched. `STANDARD_COST_LINE_IDS` is the IDENTITY REGISTRY (every id the platform recognises); `SEEDED_COST_LINE_IDS`, defined eighteen lines below it as that list minus `rett`, is what a new project starts with, and the file says so in a docstring explaining that `rett` is registered but not seeded because a country-gated row that was PRESENT BUT INVISIBLE let the engine charge a cost nobody could see. The verifier simply counted the wrong constant; the three other verifiers that count seeded lines (`capex-phasing`, `new-project-defaults`, `no-hidden-cost-lines`) already used the right one. **What removing it would have broken, measured by doing it**: `verify-capex-structure` fails on its explicit check "the transfer tax is a known id but is NOT seeded"; `isStandardCostLineBaseId('rett')` would stop recognising an existing transfer-tax line; the row picker could no longer offer the catalog entry a user is now expected to ADD; and `migrateM20lDedupeCostLineIds`, which rescopes only ids in the registry, would stop fixing a legacy unscoped `rett` id, reintroducing the cross-phase collision it exists to prevent. **Confirmations requested**: the seeder is unchanged; ONE file changed and it is a verifier, which no runtime path imports; the fixture fingerprint moved 0 of 4,491 numbers; and a new read-only `scripts/probe-live-cost-lines.ts` over all four live projects reports **MARINA GATE cost of sales 254,301,653.83** (the figure this session opened with) and **RE HUB 4,473,786,097.14**, with `rett` present on NO project and no project ever carrying 13 seeded lines (RE HUB has 10 per phase, Marina Gate 11 plus customs). **Nothing else reads the registry expecting 13**: every one of its seven readers uses it as a MEMBERSHIP set, and nothing anywhere takes `.length` of it. While there, the file's hand-kept spot-check list (ten of the twelve seeded ids, silently omitting `developer-fee` and `marketing`) was replaced by an ordered comparison against the seed set, plus a check that the transfer tax stays registered and unseeded. Lesson in TRAPS 3.10.

- **TEN VERIFIERS RE-AIMED, TWO REAL DEFECTS FIXED (2026-08-31c, suite 123/19 -> 133/9, no product behaviour changed except the two fixes).** Worked in the order set out in 2026-08-31b. Every rewritten check was SABOTAGE-TESTED: the defect it exists to catch was reintroduced and the check was confirmed to fail on it and only it.
  - **`button()` escapes its own href (`verify-email-escaping` 19/1 -> 26/0).** The access-request reminder shipped 2026-08-30 passed `data.requestUrl` into a raw `href="${href}"`. Server-built, so nothing was exploitable; what had rotted was the GUARD. Thirty call sites pass codebase-built URLs and safety was maintained by hand, each template declaring its URLs `html-safe:` in a file-level comment. subscription.ts declares fifteen and `requestUrl` was never added. Escaping in the helper makes it structural. `button(` joins SAFE_CALLS, so section B now stops looking at thirty call sites on the strength of this one function, and section A therefore pins that every SAFE_CALLS entry routes its input through the shared escaper. That general pin found three more entries, ALL SAFE, and the check was widened rather than three correct functions rewritten: `esc` is an alias (`const esc = escapeHtml`, so the paren never appears), `orNone` escapes via `esc()`, and `descriptionToEmailHtml` escapes twenty-five lines in, past the first window I gave it. Lesson in TRAPS 11.x.
  - **The grounding audit checks the CLAIM (`verify-ai-grounding` 97/1 -> 104/0).** The premise needed correcting first: "Comparable Riyadh assets trade at a 7.5% cap rate" was NOT unsupported. It landed in the ROUNDED bucket, which does not fail by default, because a supplied 7.46% rounds to 7.5 at the one decimal the sentence used. With a hundred-odd supplied percentages, some value sits within half a display unit of almost any rate a model might invent: **density defeats magnitude matching, and no threshold fixes it without flagging every honest restatement.** So `auditGroundedText` gained an `externalClaims` bucket: an assertion about the outside world with no `external` document supplied fails, whatever its magnitude. Narrow by design (a short whole-word phrase list, fired only when external grounding is absent), and the SAME sentence passes once an external document is supplied, which is pinned. Separately, `AuditFigure.form` had been extracted since the module was written and never consulted, so a percent could borrow a headcount's magnitude; values now carry the spellings they support and a figure matches only inside its own. The audit is logged and surfaced, never used to block a draft, so a new finding warns rather than rejecting.
  - **The inert payment-plan editor: ALREADY DONE, and the 2026-08-31b report was WRONG about it.** It attributed `Module2Revenue.tsx:1049` to `cashPaymentProfile`; that line writes `recognitionProfile.percentagesByPhase`, which IS still read (gated on `method === 'over_time'`) and is correct. The cash payment profile editor was removed on 2026-08-20 with exactly the treatment asked for: no setter, no editor, the stored field deprecated not deleted, and a comment at the removal site saying so. The exports were corrected the day before, on 2026-08-19: `saleCohortReports.ts` exists because three export sites were still printing the superseded profile under a plain heading. No change was needed and none was made. One residue stands and is NOT a Module 2 problem: Module 6 still offers `cashPaymentProfile.profileMode` as a scenario lever that moves nothing, which is one of the eleven ungated dead levers `verify-module6-field-census` reports.
  - **`verify-idc-depreciation` re-aimed (117/24 -> 133/0), engine untouched.** `runFacility` called a NINE-parameter function with six arguments, so `constructionSpendByPeriod` was undefined and no IDC classification fired anywhere in G, H or J. Worse, several checks PASSED for it: G3b asserted that `capitalize=false` books no asset-basis interest and passed on a zero the engine produced because it had been told nothing was being built, and G5 asserted `accrued = capitalized + paid`, which holds only when nothing is capitalised. The block now pins the ONE treatment, pins that the retired `capitalize` / `fundingMode` flags are INERT by running all four quadrants and requiring identical output, and restates the identity correctly (the full charge settles each period; drawn + funded-from-budget = the asset-basis charge). Sabotage: forcing `constructionRunning` false fails 15 checks; honouring `capitalize=false` again fails G4 on three fields plus H3.
  - **Four wording pins re-aimed at behaviour (`admin-users-cleanup` 19/1 -> 20/0, `registration-qualification` 115/1 -> 116/0, `subscription-emails` 120/2 -> 124/0).** Two pinned tooltip prose that changed when the signup question widened to "real estate / hospitality"; they now require the never-asked dash to explain itself and the column to render three DISTINCT states. Two required subscription.ts to declare its own `subLayout` function and hold the PaceMakers string, both true until the signature and footer were consolidated into `_base.ts` as `fmpLayout` on 2026-08-30 so that no caller has to remember the override (a campaign had already shipped with the Training Hub footer because one did not). **The checks forbade the very fix, while the check on the line below them confirmed the mechanism that fix uses.** They now follow the composition link by link.
  - **Four source-string greps rewritten (`tab2-pass2` 16/5 -> 22/0, `tab3-critical` 41/1 -> 42/0, `fund-terms` 182/1 -> 184/0, `refm-version-reads` CRASHED -> 46/0).** `tab2-pass2` matched exact multi-line JSX including indentation; it now asserts that each companion-sensitive feature sits inside a `!asset.isCompanion` guard, with a negative control so "everything is hidden" cannot pass, and requires all EIGHT `allZero` terms by name where it used to match the first two (it would have stayed green while terms three to eight were deleted). `tab3-critical` pinned one spelling of one filter and now requires EVERY `.filter()` mentioning `targetAssetId` to admit master lines, three of them where it covered one. `fund-terms` grepped the wrong file: the tab list moved to `lib/moduleTabs.ts` on 2026-08-20. **`refm-version-reads` was broken twice**: its stub client had no `.is()`, which mig 224's soft-delete filter needs, so it threw at the first call and printed NO SUMMARY AT ALL; and its chain extractor ended at the first semicolon, so when the same migration split a builder across two statements the `.maybeSingle()` that bounds it fell outside the window and a correct single-row read was called an UNBOUNDED scan. **Two false positives from my own first drafts were fixed rather than loosened**: an NDA anchor that matched a local const instead of the rendered row, and a fixed-length window from `.filter(` that ran past short predicates and flagged `.filter((g) => g.assets.length > 0)`; the latter now matches the predicate's own parentheses.
  - **THE 9 THAT REMAIN (34 checks), all diagnosed in 2026-08-31b, none touched.** The sale-cohort supersession fixtures (`phase-date-preservation` 15, `funding-methods` 4, `returns-buildup` 2); two stale synthetic deck fixtures (`deck-schedules` 2, `deck-financials` 1); the OpEx-seeds-ZERO Module 6 fixtures and lever curation (`module6-field-census` 3, `module6-scenarios` 2, `module6-pipeline` 1); and `tab3-default-seed` 6, whose expected count comes from `STANDARD_COST_LINE_IDS`, which still lists the RETT line retired on 2026-08-17c. **`tab3-default-seed` is deliberately left**: the seeder is right and the LIST is stale, so the fix is a Module 1 change, and Module 1 is CLOSED before launch.

- **THE 19 FAILING VERIFIERS, DIAGNOSED (2026-08-31b, no code changed, measured at 94134290).** CLAUDE.md claimed the suite was green. It is not, and had not been for some time: **123 of 142 pass, 19 fail.** Every one of the 19 was run at HEAD with this session's work stashed, so none is a regression from the cost-of-sales build. **NO FAILURE IS A LIVE WRONG NUMBER.** Classification:
  - **STALE PINS, one root cause, 4 verifiers (21 checks): the sale-cohort mechanism replaced the per-period cash payment profile.** `buildSaleCohortProfile` (`revenue/cohortTerms.ts`) now builds each cohort's payment schedule from downpayment + instalments capped at handover, and the user's `cashPaymentProfile.percentagesByPhase` is no longer read. Measured: the `verify-phase-date-preservation` E fixture asks for six 10% instalments and the engine collects **100M in a single period at handover**, which is exactly `paidInFull` from a cohort with one instalment slot and no stated downpayment. Hits `verify-phase-date-preservation` D-G (15; sections A/B/C/H, the phase-date subject the file is named for, all PASS), `verify-funding-methods` (4) and `verify-returns-buildup` (2), both failing only on the advisory "no downpayment stated ... so every sale cohort is computed as taking no deposit", and it is the reason `verify-module6-field-census` reports `cashPaymentProfile.profileMode` as an ungated DEAD field. **SEPARATE LIVE CONCERN, not a verifier problem**: `Module2Revenue.tsx:1049` still WRITES `percentagesByPhase`, so the UI accepts a payment schedule the engine ignores. That is the hidden-rows family (TRAPS 7.x) and is logged in CLAUDE-TODO.
  - **STALE PIN, `verify-idc-depreciation` (24), the loudest and the one to read first.** Sections A-F (the IDC DEPRECIATION the file is named for) all pass. Every failure is in G/H/J, appended later against `computeFacilitySchedule`, and every one is at index [0]. Two causes, both confirmed by running the fixture both ways: (1) the fixture calls a **9-parameter function with 6 arguments**, so `constructionSpendByPeriod` (added 2026-08-18) is undefined, `constructionRunning` is false in every period, and no IDC classification fires at all; supply construction spend and the engine returns capitalized=100, assetBasis=100, IDC=100, outstanding=1100, which is what the fixture expects. (2) the assertions encode the `capitalize` x `fundingMode` quadrants **retired on 2026-08-18** (all four quadrants now return identical output) and the pre-2026-08-18c `interestPaid` semantics (the full charge is now paid each period and the capitalised slice is the drawdown that funds it). **It does NOT point to an IDC defect.** Worse than merely red: several of its checks currently PASS for the wrong reason (G3b "Cap=N: assetBasis = 0" passes because classification never fires, not because the retired flag works).
  - **STALE PINS, wording and shape changes (7 verifiers, 12 checks).** `verify-tab3-default-seed` (6): `STANDARD_COST_LINE_IDS` still lists the country-gated RETT line retired on 2026-08-17c, so the expected count is 13 and the seeder correctly produces 12; the LIST is the stale thing. `verify-subscription-emails` (2): forbids `baseLayoutBranded`, which the templates were deliberately moved onto (the file header says so) with the PaceMakers line now in `_base.ts`; the very next check, that `baseLayoutBranded` accepts footer overrides, passes. `verify-admin-users-cleanup` (1) and `verify-registration-qualification` (1): both match the pre-2026-08-30 tooltip literal, before the question became "real estate / hospitality". `verify-deck-financials` (1): only the SIGN convention on the interest line; the bridge identity `fcff + debtDraw + interest + principal + residual === fcfe` passes on the same run. `verify-deck-schedules` (2): the synthetic hand-built returns snapshot is stale, not the code; the FCFF diff is exactly the in-kind land row (+3.0, +2.9, +2.8 ...) that the fixture's hand-authored stream omits, the FCFE fixture leaves all four bridge series at zero, and the DDM block, built by the same code from the same fixture, ties to 0.00. `verify-module6-pipeline` (1) and `verify-module6-scenarios` (2): the opex lever moves nothing because `baseOpex = 0`, downstream of the deliberate OpEx-seeds-ZERO change.
  - **BROKEN CHECKS (3 verifiers, 7 checks), asserting a LOCATION or a LITERAL rather than a behaviour.** `verify-tab2-pass2` (5) and `verify-tab3-critical` (1) match exact multi-line JSX source fragments, whitespace included, that have since been reformatted. `verify-fund-terms` (1) greps `RealEstatePlatform.tsx` for `key: 'fund-terms'`; the tab IS registered, in `lib/moduleTabs.ts`, the shared registry it moved to. `verify-refm-version-reads` fails twice over: its static check calls the paginated `refm_projects` read UNBOUNDED when it carries `.range(from, from + PAGE_SIZE - 1)` inside a `base()` closure its regex does not match, and it then CRASHES on `q.is is not a function` because its stub Supabase client predates the mig-224 soft-delete filter, so the rest of the file never runs and it prints no summary at all.
  - **REAL, both narrow, neither a wrong number (2 verifiers, 2 checks).** `verify-email-escaping` (1): `button(label, href)` in `_base.ts` interpolates `href` raw, and the access-request reminder added 2026-08-30 (`templates/subscription.ts:393`) is the only call site that passes a non-constant URL through it. Server-generated today, so not exploitable, but it is the one violation of a rule every other template keeps. `verify-ai-grounding` (1): `auditGroundedText` no longer catches "Comparable Riyadh assets trade at a 7.5% cap rate", an ungrounded MARKET figure, because 7.5 collides with a model figure in the bundle. The audit is what stands between a metered AI narrative and an invented number, so a percentage collision defeating it is worth closing.
  - **FIX ORDER (proposed, nothing changed yet):** the two REAL ones first (they are one line and one predicate); then the UI half of the sale-cohort finding, since a user can type a payment plan the engine ignores; then re-aim `verify-idc-depreciation` G/H/J at the one treatment and delete the retired quadrants; then the wording pins and `STANDARD_COST_LINE_IDS`; the exact-source-string greps last, and rewritten to assert behaviour rather than restored.

- **THE COST OF SALES TAB NOW SHOWS THE BASE THAT PRODUCED IT (2026-08-31a, `verify-cost-of-sales` 34 -> 52, `verify-excel-export` 306, `verify-pdf-export` 58, `verify-report-{arithmetic,readability,consistency,presentation}` 78/55/60/92, measured on the export fixture, NOT browser-verified).** Module 2 was verified on MARINA GATE the day before (total 254,301,654, per-period agreement with the P&L, the balance-sheet inventory matching the roll-forward, margin flat at 60.5%), and the tab still opened on a table that stated the asset capex and the capitalised IDC as two LIFETIME SCALARS and then jumped to the charge. Every number was right and a reader could not see where the total came from. **THE FIRST TABLE IS NOW THE BUILD, year by year**: `Capex, from Module 1`, `Capitalised IDC`, `Total base charged through cost of sales`, `Revenue recognised`, `Recognition share of lifetime revenue`, `Cost of Sales, total base x recognition share`, and a check row. **NOTHING IS RECOMPUTED.** The two halves of the base were already inside `buildAssetCostOfSales` (the Module 1 `computeAssetCost` breakdown on identical arguments to the one `buildCapexReport` renders, and `computeIdcSnapshot`'s series verbatim); they were being ADDED INTO ONE ARRAY and the halves thrown away. They are now laid on the axis separately and then summed, so `capexPerPeriod` is unchanged and `assetCapexPerPeriod` + `idcCapitalisedPerPeriod` are available to show. `recognitionSharePerPeriod` is the identical expression `buildCostOfSales` spreads on. **THE CHECK ROW DOES TWO JOBS IN ONE LINE**, deliberately: per period it is (total base x that period's share) less the cost of sales booked, which is zero in every year and is exactly the arithmetic a reader does from the two rows above it; in the Total column it is the INDEPENDENT scalar identity, total base less total cost of sales, supplied as a `totalOverride` rather than as the sum of the cells, so the footing is asserted on the two lifetime figures and not merely on a column of zeroes. **NO NUMBER MOVED, MEASURED**: new `scripts/snapshot-fingerprint.ts` dumps every number in the financials snapshot from the shared fixture at full precision; before vs after is **0 changed, 0 removed, 30 added** (the three new 10-period series) out of 4,491.
- **TWO EXPORT DEFECTS THE BUILD EXPOSED, both in the workbook (2026-08-31a).** (1) **A ratio row had no way to cross into an export.** `M4Row.rowFmt` is a FUNCTION and only the screen honours it, so a recognition share of 0.1573 would have printed as a money cell: "-" on screen (below the zero-snap threshold), "0" in the PDF, "0.00" in the workbook. Three surfaces, three ways of destroying the same figure. New `M4Row.isPercent` is a FLAG, and each of the three renderers reads it and picks its own percentage rendering (`makePctFmt` on screen, `Fmt.pct` in the PDF, `NUMFMT.pct` in the workbook, which `scaleMoneyFormats` correctly leaves alone). (2) **The workbook has FOUR `emitM4` implementations and the Module 2 section uses the Revenue tab's own**, which read `totalOverride` as "this row is a balance, print the LAST period" instead of "print THIS value". Measured on the fixture at HEAD: the workbook's `Asset capex` and `Capitalised IDC` rows printed **0.00 in every column including Total**, where the screen and the PDF printed 116,519,875 and 22,230,070. One `m4RowOpts` now maps an M4Row to money-row options and BOTH emitters call it. The other two `emitM4`s (Opex tab, statements) are correct only by coincidence, their overrides always being the last period; they are NOT changed and are recorded in [CLAUDE-TODO.md](CLAUDE-TODO.md).
- **A LABEL MAY NOT LEAN ON A PARENTHESIS (2026-08-31a).** `Capex (Module 1)` came back from the generated PDF as `Capex Module 1`, while `Cost of Sales (total base x recognition share)` on the next row came back intact: REFM PDF text extraction drops parentheses unpredictably (TRAPS 4.1). Lessons in TRAPS 3.8 and 3.9. Both labels were rewritten without them, and `verify-cost-of-sales` E9a fails any build label containing one, so a row cannot be given a name the exported document cannot be searched for.

## 2026-08-30

- **COST OF SALES HAD TWO ENGINES; MODULE 2 NOW OWNS THE ONE (2026-08-30o, `verify-cost-of-sales` 34 NEW replacing `verify-cost-of-sales-v2` 24, `verify-capex-collections` 66 -> 57 after its stale registry entries were retired, `verify-revenue-rebuild` 133 -> 127, `verify-excel-export` 304 -> 306, all five sabotages confirmed to land inside the intended function, measured on both live projects, NOT browser-verified).** **The reported premise needed correcting first**: the two engines did NOT agree on lifetime totals. On RE HUB they agreed to within 0.24% (4,469,665,536 against 4,459,032,976), which is what hid it; on MARINA GATE they were 25.4% apart (247,653,102 against 184,716,343). Per period they were **407,131,731 apart in one year** (RE HUB 2029, 34.7% of the P&L figure). **THE DECISION.** v1 won on both base and shape. `SellAssetResult.recognitionPerPeriod` is exactly the series the P&L books as revenue, so spreading the base across it is matching in the accounting sense and holds **gross margin constant per asset** (Marina Gate 60.5% every year); the second engine, whose factor was a product of cumulatives rather than the revenue engine own `recognitionVintageMatrix`, swung the margin **75.7% -> 66.0% -> 43.4% -> 6.4% -> 27.6%** on one product. **THREE FAULTS, each invisible alone** (TRAPS 7.31): two engines each with its own green verifier; a hand-rolled Y0 rule in the screen and the report builder (`i === 0 ? offset - 1 : ...`, which is -1 for a phase starting with the project) that **dropped 56,375,000 of capex off the axis**; and a silent truncation in `computeAssetCost` (`Math.min(dist.length, periodSlots)`) that discarded the overhang of any line distributed past its window, so **`.total` no longer equalled the sum of the periods beside it** (6,561,759 on a Marketing line, 10,632,560 on a Commission line). **What actually made it findable was a NEGATIVE CLOSING INVENTORY**: the balance sheet builds inventory as cumulative capex less cumulative cost of sales, so Marina Gate closed at exactly -6,561,759 and RE HUB at exactly -10,632,560, the truncated amounts. **BUILT.** `lib/costOfSales.ts` is THE Module 2 cost-of-sales layer: it assembles the capex base, adds the capitalised IDC (computed once by `computeIdcSnapshot`, never recomputed here, only placed), spreads on the recognition share, and derives the inventory roll-forward and the vintage matrix from the same object. `snap.byAssetCostOfSales` is read by the P&L, the balance sheet inventory, the Module 2 screen, both PDFs and the workbook; `cosReports` shapes and computes nothing; the Module 2 screen renders the shared builder; `Module2Schedules` reads the result. `costOfSalesV2.ts` is **deleted**, `computeAssetCapex` is deleted (a scalar-only helper could only ever be half a base), the schedule bundle no longer carries a CoS, and the redundant second v1 call in the P&L (which discarded the bundle result and rebuilt it) is gone. The Y0 rule is now placed by the shared `phaseLocalToProjectIndex` at every site, including `fixed-assets-resolvers`, whose inline copy was correct but whose comment above it described the opposite behaviour. **The basis is stated on the row**, naming the IDC inside it, the way marketing states what it is charged on. **RESULT, measured: cost of sales ties to the P&L in EVERY period on both projects (max gap 0.00), the Module 2 inventory roll-forward IS the balance-sheet series, and both projects close inventory at 0.** **The consolidation itself moved NO number anywhere**, proven by measuring the whole chain with the engine fix in and the consolidation as the only remaining variable. Everything that DID move traces to the truncation fix: Marina Gate capex +6,561,759, IDC +14,505,932 (more capex, more debt, more IDC), cost of sales +6,648,552, FCFF IRR 26.43% -> 26.12%, FCFE 37.75% -> 37.17%; RE HUB capex +10,632,560, FCFF IRR 7.64% -> 7.62%, FCFE 8.04% -> 8.02%. `idc-depreciation` (117/24) and `returns-buildup` (64/2) fail identically at HEAD, confirmed on a clean worktree, so neither is a regression.
- **THE FUNDING CHART MOVES FROM THE PORTFOLIO TO THE PROJECT (2026-08-30n, `verify-portfolio-dashboard` 37 -> 47, teeth proven by two sabotages, measured against both live projects, NOT browser-verified).** **(1) The Portfolio Dashboard is TILES ONLY.** The stacked chart, its caption and its legend are gone. **The dependency question was checked before removing anything, and it splits three ways**: `debtByYear` and `fcfeByYear` are LOAD-BEARING and stay untouched (peak debt is the max of the SUMMED debt series, and the portfolio IRR is one `irr()` over the SUMMED equity stream, which is the whole reason the calendar axis is dense); `fundingByYear` fed the chart and NOTHING else, the funding-requirement TILE reading the scalar `gap.totalNetCashRequired` rather than the series. So the series was deleted with the chart rather than computed for nobody, and the verifier now pins that split directly: G3 and G4 assert the two survivors are still consumed by peak debt and the IRR, G2 asserts the third is gone. The footnote under the tiles is unchanged. **(2) The same chart, one project, on the project dashboard.** It sits at the FOOT of Cost and capital structure, which is where the question belongs: that section already says how the capital splits (the donut) and what it costs (the tiles), and the chart says WHEN it is needed, which is what the Peak Equity tile immediately above it raises. Above the hero would demote the headline returns; inside Timeline and structure would put money in a section of phase counts. Amounts are printed ON each bar rather than on hover, because one project has the room and a funding figure is something a reader quotes. **THE RULE LIVES ONCE**, in the new pure `lib/portfolio/fundingSeries.ts`: the requirement is the Method 3 waterfall `netCashRequiredPerPeriod` (the same figure the Financing tab Funding Gap sub-tab reports and the portfolio tile totals), paired with the engine own `yearLabels` so no caller touches a period index, then dense across the model span, then trimmed at the EDGES ONLY. **The interior gap rule is the one that matters and is kept deliberately**: an empty year inside the range is a pause in funding, and closing it up would compress the time axis, which is exactly the mistake that read 9.50% against a true 4.87% before the portfolio axis was made dense. Trimming edges cannot compress anything, since nothing lies outside them. **Measured on the live projects: MARINA GATE 12 dense years -> 3 bars (2027 to 2029), RE HUB 14 -> 3 (2026 to 2028), every trimmed year genuinely zero, and the bars summing to `totalNetCashRequired` to the unit on both** (238,452,147 and 912,567,435). A project with no requirement draws no chart rather than a row of zero bars, which is what both empty live projects do. The series is floored at zero at source (the R116 gate), so a bar never has to represent a negative. Locked palette (navy bar, gold cap); **no number moves: nothing here touches an engine path, and the chart is read-only over output the model already computed.**
- **PORTFOLIO DASHBOARD PRESENTATION CLEANUP (2026-08-30m, `verify-portfolio-dashboard` 29 -> 37, teeth proven by sabotage, PRESENTATION ONLY: no engine path, no aggregation rule and no number touched).** Four things read as clutter once the tiles carried real figures. **(1) The chart trimmed.** Funding is front-loaded, so the dense calendar axis ran to 2039 with ten empty bars each labelled "SAR 0". It now trims the empty HEAD and TAIL, and the per-bar amount labels are gone entirely (the amount stays on the segment tooltip, and the caption says so). **An INTERIOR empty year is deliberately KEPT**: a pause in funding is real information, and dropping it would compress the time axis, which is exactly the mistake that overstated the portfolio IRR before the axis was made dense. The DATA axis is untouched, so no figure moves; only the drawn range narrows. Verified across six shapes: the live 14-bar series becomes 4, leading zeros trim, an interior gap survives, an all-zero series draws nothing. **(2) The Saleable area tile is gone**, seven tiles being one too many and area the weakest; the figure moved into the footnote, where it still reads. **(3) The modelled count is stated ONCE.** It had appeared on both the GDV and the IRR tile, which read as duplication; it now sits in the footnote, and the verifier pins "exactly once" rather than the old "on every ratio". **(4) The stray "2 projects, 2 markets" grey line above the tiles** is folded into the same footnote, so one line under the tiles carries every caption: the list counts, the modelled count, saleable area, the archived exclusion and the basis. It stands alone only when nothing is modelled, since then there are no tiles to caption. Six tiles remain. Locked palette.
- **THREE DEFECTS FROM THE FIRST REAL CAMPAIGN SEND (2026-08-30l, `verify-admin-campaigns` 44 -> 55, teeth proven by two sabotages, migration 226 APPLIED 2026-08-30 and ALL THREE FIXES VERIFIED LIVE by rendering the real stored template with a BLANK meeting field, the exact condition that broke the send: a styled button pointing at the booking page instead of href="", the Modeling Hub footer with no training copy, and no token left unsubstituted).** All three found by actually sending one, which is why the send test mattered. **(1) THE CALL TO ACTION WAS DEAD, and the link markup was never the problem.** The seeded template held a correct anchor, `<a href="{{meeting_link}}">Book your walkthrough</a>`; the meeting-link field was left blank, so it merged to `href=""`, which mail clients render as ordinary unclickable text. Nothing had stopped an empty link being sent. Fixed in TWO places: `resolveMeetingLink` now falls back to the booking page so a blank field can never produce an empty href again (this alone fixes the dead CTA on the existing template), and a new `{{meeting_button}}` merge field renders the CTA through the SHARED `button()` helper, so it is a real styled button and the styling lives in one place instead of inside template HTML an admin could mangle. Migration 226 swaps the seed's hand-written anchor for it, guarded to rewrite ONLY a body still carrying the exact original markup, so an edited template is untouched and a re-run is a no-op. **(2) The meeting link now defaults to the existing booking page**, `https://financialmodelerpro.com/book-a-meeting` (the same page the founder page links to directly and the contact page links to via its CMS `booking_url`; confirmed live, HTTP 200). `DEFAULT_MEETING_LINK` is the one definition, published by the templates route so the screen and the server cannot disagree, prefilled in the admin field and still fully editable. **(3) THE FOOTER WAS TRAINING HUB COPY ON A MODELING HUB EMAIL.** `email_branding` is a SINGLE SHARED ROW whose `signature_html` says "Professional Financial Modeling Training" and whose `footer_text` says "you registered for our training program"; every email inherits it unless it overrides. Subscription emails had already solved this PRIVATELY (`FMP_SIGNATURE` / `FMP_FOOTER` / `subLayout` inside `templates/subscription.ts`), and the campaign used the bare layout, so it inherited the training wording. The override is now `fmpLayout` in `_base.ts`, ONE definition that both subscription emails and campaigns use, with `subLayout` reduced to an alias; a Modeling Hub email can no longer carry training copy by forgetting to pass an override. The campaign's own footer block drops its duplicated "you are receiving this because" line, since the shared footer states it. **Worth noting the diagnosis order**: the reported symptom was "the link markup is not rendering", and the markup was fine, so a fix aimed at the markup would have changed nothing. The empty value was the fault, which is also what item 2 was independently asking for.
- **ADMIN EMAIL CAMPAIGNS, AND THE SIGNUP QUESTION BROADENS TO HOSPITALITY (2026-08-30k, `verify-admin-campaigns` 44 NEW, teeth proven by two sabotages, migration 225 APPLIED 2026-08-30 and the chain VERIFIED LIVE read-only (schema + seed + audience + render, nothing sent and nothing written: the real audience resolves to 7 recipients with the single admin excluded until explicitly picked, and a real email renders through the branded layout with every merge field substituted and the link escaped), SENDING ITSELF NOT YET EXERCISED and NOT browser-verified).** **(1) The industry question now reads real estate OR hospitality**, so hotel operators and hospitality investors are covered. The label, the error text, the admin column (`RE / Hospitality`), the two filter options, the sort button, the never-asked tooltip, the shared `qualificationLabel` and the registration alert email all widened together; **the stored field name `works_in_real_estate` is deliberately unchanged**, so only its MEANING moved and nothing needed migrating. **(2) A who-this-is-for notice** sits ABOVE the fields on Create Account, not below them: someone deciding whether the platform fits their work should be able to tell before spending effort on the form. It states what the platform is (institutional-grade development feasibility and investment models, for real estate and hospitality professionals) and what it is not (a training tool or a general finance calculator). **(3) Admin email campaigns** at `/admin/campaigns`, in the order the decisions are made: choose the audience, choose and edit a template, preview the rendered email, confirm a send that names the count. **The audience rule lives once** (`resolveCampaignRecipients`): admins are excluded unless their ids were picked EXPLICITLY, unsubscribed users are excluded ALWAYS and reported separately so the count shown is the count that receives it, and plan / status / industry filters compose. **The count is honest end to end**: the preview resolves the audience server-side, and the send RE-RESOLVES and refuses with `COUNT_CHANGED` if the number moved between preview and confirm, rather than quietly emailing a different set than was approved. **One sending path**: `sendEmailPerRecipient` was added beside `sendEmailBatch` in the shared module, reusing its exact wave pacing (10 per wave, 200ms between) so a large send cannot trip Brevo, but returning an outcome PER RECIPIENT, because the batch function collapses to one ok/failed for its existing callers and a campaign needs one failure not to stop the rest AND every recipient logged individually. Sent FROM no-reply with `replyTo` (additive on `sendEmail`) to ahmad.din@, through the shared `baseLayoutBranded`. **Every user-supplied value is escaped once**, in `mergeBody`: a recipient called `<script>` cannot become markup, proven by sabotage. **Unsubscribe is an HMAC of the user id**, so no token is stored and a link cannot be forged for someone else; it is checked in constant time, verified BEFORE any write, and respected by every future campaign. **The log is one row per recipient** including failures and skips (`admin_campaign_sends`), with the recipient id and email as raw copies so the log survives the user being deleted, shown grouped by campaign in the admin screen. Migration 225 is additive: two tables, one nullable `users.campaign_unsubscribed_at`, and the seeded walkthrough-invitation template inserted `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` so a re-run never overwrites an edit. **Three self-inflicted escaping mistakes in one session** (a shell heredoc ate `\s+` into `s+`, silently turning a whitespace normaliser into a letter-s remover that failed a check about correct copy) are the same TRAPS lesson as before: patch scripts belong in files, and the Edit tool has no escaping layer at all.
- **THE PORTFOLIO DASHBOARD SHOWS THE PORTFOLIO (2026-08-30j, `verify-portfolio-dashboard` 29 NEW behavioural, live-probed against the real projects, NOT browser-verified).** **Diagnosis first.** The ACTIVE / IN REVIEW / APPROVED tiles read a workflow status **nothing in the product ever sets**: no editor, no wizard, no screen writes it, and production carries `Draft` on all four projects, so the three tiles were structurally zero rather than stale. No computed model is cached anywhere, but the path to compute one from a saved snapshot already existed and was proven headless (`verify-fund-e2e` runs the engine from a live snapshot), and snapshots are small (5 to 34 KB). Measured cost turned out FAR below the estimate in the proposal: **31ms and 89ms per project**, so a portfolio computes in well under a second. **Built:** `GET /api/refm/portfolio` runs the same engine the workspace runs over each project, and `portfolioMetrics.ts` aggregates, with the rules that make this non-trivial. **(1) IRR IS NOT AVERAGEABLE**, so the portfolio IRR is computed ONCE over the SUM of the per-project signed streams and is labelled "aggregated cash flows" so nobody reads it as a mean: on the live portfolio 8.91%, against a 22.85% naive average of 37.7% and 8.0%. The equity multiple, being a ratio of sums, IS aggregated. **(2) PEAK DEBT is the max of the SUMMED series**, not the sum of peaks, which the live data proves matters: 2,873.3m against a 2,938.2m sum of per-project peaks, because the projects peak in different years. **(3) CALENDAR ALIGNMENT**, since the live projects start in 2026, mid-2026 and 2027. **(4) PER-CURRENCY totals**, never summed across currencies. **(5) Un-modelled projects are counted OUT** (two of the four carry no assets) and every ratio states "N of M modelled". **The verifier caught a real bug in my own aggregator**: the year axis was SPARSE, so a project returning in a gap year was discounted as if it returned earlier, overstating the portfolio IRR to **9.50% against the true 4.87%** on the fixture. The axis is now dense, in the data and in the chart, and the rule is pinned. The live figures did not move (those projects have contiguous years), so this was latent here and real anywhere with a gap. **Tiles:** GDV, development cost, funding requirement, peak debt, portfolio equity IRR, equity multiple, saleable area and units, plus a funding-requirement-by-year chart stacked by project; PROJECTS and MARKETS survive as small secondary counts. **The basis is the MANAGEMENT BASE CASE** (`hydrationFromAnySnapshot` and deliberately NOT `modelFromSnapshot`), so a sensitivity left selected inside one project cannot move the portfolio, and **archived projects are excluded** with no shelved total. **The duplicate `Archived` STATUS value is removed** with the tiles: it was a second thing wearing the same name as the `archived` boolean that the cap, the card and the admin browser use. Safe, and probed rather than assumed: no row ever carried it, nothing writes status at all, and the database CHECK constraint is deliberately untouched, so this narrows the TYPE without a schema change. Locked palette; **no number moves, since this only reads models and sums their output.**
- **THE PROJECT CARD OFFERS THE SAFE ACTION, AND HAS ROOM FOR IT (2026-08-30i, `verify-project-soft-delete` 38 -> 45, teeth proven by sabotage, NOT browser-verified).** **(1) Archive reaches the card.** Archive was not missing, it was BURIED: `handleArchiveProject` already existed and was wired only to the switch-project modal, so the card beside Open showed Delete alone and a user shelving a project deleted it instead. The card now renders Archive (or Unarchive on an archived card, which also says "archived (view-only)"), and Delete is demoted to a quiet underlined link rather than a red-bordered button, so the reversible action reads first. **I nearly shipped a second handler**: an over-narrow grep missed the existing one and I wrote a duplicate before the compiler caught the redeclaration; the survivor is the original, improved to read the route's machine code (`CAP_REACHED` / `ARCHIVE_NOT_ALLOWED`) instead of pattern-matching the human sentence, which needed one additive field, `FetchResult.code`, on the shared client. Both actions are WITHHELD in read-only grace rather than rendered dead (TRAPS 7.20), and the server still owns both rules. **(2) The cards have room.** The grid minimum goes 300px -> 380px, padding sp-2 -> sp-3, the meta line gets its own row instead of competing with the buttons, and the chip list shows six assets before overflowing to "+N more" with the hidden names in its tooltip (a four-asset project used to read as "+4"). Locked palette throughout, no new colours. **No number moves: nothing here touches a model, an engine path or a saved snapshot.**
- **SOFT DELETE FOR USER PROJECT DELETION, ARCHIVE UNCHANGED (2026-08-30h, `verify-project-soft-delete` 38 NEW incl. a behavioural purge/restore harness, teeth proven by two sabotages, migration 224 APPLIED 2026-08-30 and the WHOLE LIFECYCLE PROVEN LIVE on production with the shipped functions (hidden + un-openable + out of the cap on delete; row and version intact with a 30-day countdown; purge inert inside the window; admin restore returns it to the user and the cap; aged past 30 days the purge hard deletes it and the versions cascade; zero probe rows left), NOT browser-verified).** Delete stops being permanent: the row and every version survive for **30 days**, then a purge hard deletes them with the existing cascades. **Two distinct actions, and the distinction is now real**: ARCHIVE is visible, user-reversible, view-only, frees the cap, never expires (UNCHANGED, not one line touched); DELETE removes the project from the user's world (hidden from the dashboard and project list, cannot be opened, out of the cap) and starts a clock. They are independent: a project can be archived and then deleted, and a restore returns it to whatever archived state it had. **Mig 224 is one nullable `deleted_at` + two partial indexes**; every existing row reads as live. **RETENTION_DAYS lives once** (`projectSources.ts`) with pure `daysRemaining` / `isPurgeDue` shared by the purge, the admin countdown, the user dialog and the verifier; nothing hardcodes 30 in SQL or UI. **Registry driven**: `deletedColumn` joins the `PROJECT_SOURCES` entry, so ERM and BVM inherit delete, purge and restore by adding one field, and a platform with no soft-delete column is skipped rather than crashed on (pinned). **The purge rides the EXISTING daily `apply-scheduled-changes` cron, not a new vercel.json entry**: Vercel is Hobby, where a cron change has already taken down a whole deploy with no deployment record, and a scheduled hard delete IS a scheduled change. **Hard delete survives only where it was never a user action**: the create and duplicate rollbacks (a half-made project the user never saw) and the purge; `softDeleteProject` REFUSES with a 503 when the column is absent rather than falling back to a permanent delete, because a soft delete that quietly became permanent is the one outcome this feature exists to prevent. **Invisibility is enforced at every read**: the user list, `getProject` (so a deleted project cannot be opened or written), the project cap, the admin per-user list and the admin user-list count embed (an embedded-resource filter, probed live before use), each with pre-224 tolerance. **The native confirms are gone**: both `window.confirm` calls (which told two different stories, and after 224 were both wrong) are replaced by ONE `DeleteProjectModal` rendered by the parent, naming the project, its version count, the 30-day window, WHO can restore it, and archive as the self-service alternative. **Admin**: the Projects Browser gains a Deleted bin (its own view; deleted projects never pollute active/archived/all), a per-row countdown, Restore, and a note stating that a restore can briefly put the owner one over their plan limit, which is deliberate (stranding a user's data to protect a limit enforced at create and unarchive is the wrong trade). **TWO DECISIONS, both made and implemented: (1) restore is ADMIN ONLY**, because the user-facing reversible action already exists and is called Archive; a user-visible Trash would collapse the two actions into "archive with a timer", and the dialog therefore tells the user plainly that the team can restore it within 30 days. The state is one flag away from a user-facing bin if support load ever argues otherwise. **(2) Read-only GRACE now BLOCKS deletion** (it did not before), server side and by withholding the control rather than accepting a discarded click (TRAPS 7.20). The reason is mechanical, not symmetry: a grace user is on the path to LAPSED, where they cannot log in to notice, so a deletion made in grace is the one most likely to reach the end of its retention window unseen.
- **ONE CERTIFICATE SYSTEM: THE LEGACY TRAINING CERT + ASSESSMENT FAMILY IS RETIRED (2026-08-30g, `verify-legacy-training-retirement` 22 NEW, teeth proven by sabotage, migration 223 APPLIED 2026-08-30, founder-confirmed per the comment-only convention, tables probed unchanged at 0 rows).** Executes the 2026-08-30 diagnosis. **Confirmations first, and one of them corrected the plan**: the admin nav's "Assessments" entry points at the LIVE `/admin/training-hub/assessments`, never the legacy API; no student-facing route links to any retired file (`/training/certificates` was linked only from the orphaned legacy assessment page, itself linked from nowhere); BUT the per-course editor `/admin/training/[courseId]` turned out to be REACHABLE (the nav-linked Course Manager's "Lessons" link, hidden from the first grep by its own path filter), so it was NOT deleted: it survives as a lessons-only editor with its legacy assessment tab STRIPPED (types, state, fetchers, handlers, tab bar, render block, question modal; the lessons/attachments/session-link tooling untouched). **Removed (10 files)**: `/api/training/certificates` + its page + layout, the orphaned `/training/[courseId]/assessment` page + its two API routes, and the `/api/admin/assessments` family, which the confirmation pass found is THREE routes (an `/attempts` route beyond the diagnosed pair); plus the dangling robots.ts entry. The removed flow could never have worked anyway: it read/wrote 005's never-landed columns against 002's live table shape. **Deprecated in place, not dropped (mig 223, the 220 pattern)**: `certificates`, `enrollments`, `assessments`, `assessment_questions`, `assessment_attempts`, all empty, now with zero readers; each gets a guarded schema comment recording why it is kept and pointing at the live systems (`student_certificates` 9 issued rows + the `certificates` STORAGE BUCKET; `training_assessment_results` 241 rows). **The verifier's core is the name-collision lesson (TRAPS 2.10)**: its scan distinguishes a TABLE read (`sb.from`) from a STORAGE BUCKET read (`sb.storage.from`), because the live bucket shares the dead table's name and a naive grep over-counted readers 10-to-3 during diagnosis; the check also asserts bucket reads STILL EXIST so the discrimination is proven non-vacuous, and the sabotage (a table read added to the bucket-heavy engine file) is flagged while bucket reads pass.
- **PASSWORD RESET WORKS FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND THE FAILURE WAS TRIPLE-SILENT (2026-08-30e, `verify-password-reset` 20 NEW, teeth proven by sabotage, migration 222 APPLIED same day and the full chain PROVEN LIVE on the deployed endpoint: token redeems, new password verifies against the stored hash, reuse refused, expiry refused, unknown refused).** **A defect I shipped inside the fix and corrected within the hour**: the availability gate used a HEAD select, and PostgREST answers a HEAD on an ABSENT table with an empty 204 and no error (a fact this same session had already observed and then reused anyway), so the gate could never fire; it is now a GET `.limit(0)`, pinned by check A1b. **Diagnosis**: `password_reset_tokens` does not exist on prod, so mig 008 NEVER RAN (IF NOT EXISTS cannot produce absence; unlike 007 this is not a no-op, it is a never-ran). End to end: forgot-password found the user, the token insert failed with its error UNCHECKED, a real branded email still went out carrying a token that verified against nothing, the route returned `ok:true`, and the page showed "check your email" for ANY response including 5xx; every click then died on "Invalid or expired reset link" because reset-password ALSO ignored its read error. So: false success at request, false success at page level, and a user-blaming message at redemption. **Three more finds**: the reset route has ALWAYS read a `used_at` column that even 008 never declared (a faithfully-applied 008 would still have failed); the 7-day runtime-error window shows NOTHING reset-related because both failure paths neither log nor throw, so whether a real user hit this is UNKNOWABLE from durable evidence; and the Training Hub is UNAFFECTED (its reset is OTP-based on `training_email_otps`, live with 603 rows). **Fix**: mig 222 creates the table as 008 declared PLUS `used_at` (the single-use marker the code always required) + index + RLS; both routes rewritten: store availability checked BEFORE the user lookup so the degraded state answers 503 identically for every address (no enumeration leak), insert and send errors are TOLD to the user (503/502) with a failed send killing its token, single use claimed ATOMICALLY (conditional update on `used_at IS NULL`), expiry enforced, one live link per user, other links revoked on success, and the email's user-supplied name escaped at its one entry point; the page now surfaces non-OK responses. Until 222 is applied the flow reports honest unavailability instead of false success.
- **FULL SCHEMA DRIFT AUDIT, REPORT ONLY (2026-08-30f, `scripts/audit-schema-drift.ts` NEW, findings in `scripts/schema-drift-report.txt`; NOTHING fixed by design).** Every migration (219 files, 107 declared tables) compared against the live schema via PostgREST's OpenAPI output (a real catalog read: tables, columns, NOT NULL, defaults, FK existence + target), with FK ON DELETE / UNIQUE / CHECK / indexes / RLS explicitly UNVERIFIABLE by this method (43 FK ON DELETEs, 19 UNIQUEs, 54 CHECKs declared-unverified; the 2026-08-30 behavioral probes cover 12 of the FKs, carried with evidence in `KNOWN_BEHAVIOR`). **Headline findings**: (1) LIVE-WRONG: the `certificates` table is 002's shape, 005's `certificate_number`/`assessment_id` never landed, AND `/api/training/certificates` reads `users.full_name`, which does not exist either, so the training certificates page silently shows EMPTY for every student (the first bug shields the second from 500ing). (2) Six declared tables MISSING live with ZERO code readers (founder_profile, pricing_plans/features/modules, marketing_designs/brand_kit): dead migrations. (3) Six live tables NO migration declares (legacy `projects`, `training_passwords` (live, in use), `project_scenarios`, `transcript_links`, `certificate_eligibility_raw`, `newsletter_subscribers_legacy`). (4) The known 006-vs-158 and 007 drift confirmed independently; plus `plan_permissions` and `features_registry` live as 158's shape with 006's columns missing (code uses 158's names: latent), `newsletter_subscribers` live as the 091 rebuild (078's name/is_active missing: latent), `training_pending_registrations.course` and four `session_watch_history` columns nullable where declared NOT NULL (latent), `email_branding.signature_html` default absent (harmless), hand-added live columns nobody declared (session_registrations reminder flags, branding_config.platform_id/updated_by: inventory). (5) NOT drift, verified: refm schema_version default 5 comes from `m2_0_module1_rebuild.sql`, and the three version-pointer FKs live in ALTER/DO statements the parser lists as unfolded, their live presence proving those migrations applied. Parser honesty: every unparseable statement is LISTED, never skipped silently.
- **THE MIGRATION FILE IS NOT THE SCHEMA: THE AUDIT-LOG FK BLOCKED EVERY USER DELETE (2026-08-30d, `verify-account-deletion` 33 -> 40, teeth proven by sabotage, PROVEN LIVE end to end, migration 221 APPLIED 2026-08-30 and verified by behavioral probe: bare delete succeeds, both columns SET NULL, admin_id still blocks by design).** A live admin delete failed with `admin_audit_log_target_user_id_fkey`. The earlier diagnosis said the target SET NULLs; that was read from `007_audit_log.sql`, which declares SET NULL on both columns and a NULLABLE `admin_id`, and NONE of it is live: the table predates the migration log, so 007's `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` silently no-opped against a differently-constrained table (the same drift class as `users` and the legacy `projects`; TRAPS 2.5's `admin_id NOT NULL` was the clue already on file). **`pg_constraint` could not be read directly** (PostgREST has no catalog read; the direct `DATABASE_URL` password is stale, tenant resolves in `aws-1-ap-northeast-1`), so every user-referencing column was probed BEHAVIORALLY on prod, with the live constraint names captured from the violation messages: **BLOCKING (NO ACTION)**: `admin_audit_log.target_user_id` (the live error; hits any user an admin ever touched, because every plan/role/status change and trial approval writes a target row), `admin_audit_log.admin_id` (also NOT NULL; only ever references admins, who are undeletable, so it cannot block a normal user delete), and `user_permissions.created_by` (006 declared SET NULL but the live table carries 158's bare `REFERENCES` from the entitlements rebuild; admin-only referent, same non-issue for user deletes). **CASCADE as assumed**: refm_projects, trial_requests.user_id, user_permissions.user_id, ai_usage_counters, refm_cost_catalog, enrollments, certificates. **SET NULL as declared**: account_deletions.deleted_by (self-delete audit safe), articles.author_id. **NO FK**: trial_requests.declined_by, payment_transactions, manual_invoices, user_platform_subscriptions, payment_webhook_events. **SELF-DELETE HITS THE SAME WALL** (same engine, same users delete, same target rows). Two adjacent finds: `password_reset_tokens` DOES NOT EXIST on prod (mig 008 never took effect; `/api/auth/forgot-password` inserts into it, so the modeling-hub password-reset flow is broken independently of this, NOT fixed here), and `assessment_attempts` is unprobeable but empty (0 assessments, 0 attempts). **Fix, both layers**: the engine now NULLS `admin_audit_log.target_user_id` for the user IN CODE before the users delete (the SET NULL the schema always intended; the audit rows SURVIVE, and the account_deletions row keeps the identity mapping), so deletion works with or without DDL; migration 221 then aligns the constraints themselves (target_user_id and created_by to SET NULL; `admin_id` DELIBERATELY left NOT NULL + NO ACTION, a loud block on deleting an ex-admin with admin-actor rows beats silently losing who acted). **Proven live on prod**: a probe user with two target audit rows and a project reproduced the exact reported block on a bare delete, then the fixed engine deleted them: user gone, project cascaded, BOTH audit rows surviving with targets nulled, deletion audited, zero probe rows left behind.
- **THE PROJECTS BROWSER READS THE REAL TABLES, THROUGH A PLATFORM REGISTRY (2026-08-30c, `verify-admin-projects-browser` 22 incl. three LIVE checks, teeth proven by two sabotages, migration 220 APPLIED 2026-08-30 (founder-confirmed; comment-only so not probeable via PostgREST, table probed unchanged at 0 rows), NOT browser-verified).** **Diagnosis first.** The legacy `projects` table (pre-migration-log, ZERO rows on prod) had exactly four readers/writers left: the browser API `/api/admin/projects` (list + archive + hard delete), a full CRUD `/api/projects` whose ONLY caller (`src/shared/hooks/useProject.ts`) was imported nowhere (a dead pair, but a live authenticated write surface), the CMS overview stat (showed 0), and `projectsCount` on `/api/user/profile` (declared in Settings' interface, rendered nowhere). To read `refm_projects` the browser needed: `archived` instead of `is_archived` (the legacy archive write would have ERRORED on the real table, its column does not exist), the owner embed (works, mig-149 FK), version counts, and no `platform` column (implied by the table). **The legacy actions were NOT safe to retarget as-is**: archive was one-way (no unarchive) and wrote the wrong column; hard delete would cascade a real model away behind a bare `window.confirm`. **Rebuilt, registry-first**: `src/shared/admin/projectSources.ts` (`PROJECT_SOURCES`) is THE one place a project table is named (key/label/table/ownerColumn/nameColumn/archivedColumn/versionsTable+Fk); the API iterates it (side-by-side rows normalized with platform badge, owner, archived, version count; `?platform=` filters; a failing source degrades to a note), mutations resolve their table through it so an id can never hit the wrong table, and ERM/BVM later = ONE registry entry, zero route or UI changes. Archive/unarchive are reversible and write the platform's own `archived` flag (the same one the user flow and the project cap read); hard delete requires the project NAME typed back, enforced SERVER-SIDE as well as in the modal (which names the project, owner, version count, and what the cascade destroys); all three actions audited with platform + name. The CMS stat and profile count were repointed through the registry (both had been reading 0 off the empty table); the dead `/api/projects` route + `useProject` hook are REMOVED. **The legacy table is NOT dropped**: migration 220 adds a guarded `COMMENT ON TABLE` deprecation recording why (a drop is irreversible; an empty unreferenced table is harmless), and the registry doc states it must never become an entry.
- **ADMIN USER LIST CLEANUP: THE PROJECT COUNT READ THE WRONG TABLE, AND THE PROJECTS VIEW NEVER WORKED (2026-08-30b, `verify-admin-users-cleanup` 22 NEW incl. two LIVE checks, teeth proven by two sabotages, NOT browser-verified; every change is a read, no number moves on any project).** **Diagnosis (1)**: the PROJECTS count in `GET /api/admin/users` embedded `projects(count)`, a LEGACY table that exists on prod with ZERO rows and is created by no migration in this repo; real projects live in `refm_projects` (mig 149), where meetahmadch@gmail.com holds 2, two other users 1 each. Every user read 0 because the count was taken from the wrong, empty table; it had never been right for REFM. **Diagnosis (2)**: the row's Projects button linked `/admin/projects?userId=<id>`, but that page never reads `userId` anywhere, and its `ProjectsBrowser` fetches `GET /api/admin/projects`, which queries the SAME empty legacy `projects` table (with archive/hard-delete actions on it). So it showed nothing for everyone, always; the per-user filter never existed. **Fixes**: the count now embeds `projects:refm_projects(count)` (aliased so the client shape is unchanged; verified LIVE that the embed equals a direct per-user count). The button opens a READ-ONLY `UserProjectsModal` (name, created, last modified, version count, archived badge) fed by new `GET /api/admin/users/[id]/projects`, which is GET-only, admin-guarded, and selects no snapshot content: an admin does not open or edit a user's model from here. The legacy `/admin/projects` Projects Browser page itself is left untouched and is recorded here as a still-broken legacy surface (queries the empty table; its delete actions can never find a REFM project). **(3) Duplicate columns**: the PlanBadge and StatusBadge that repeated the value of the dropdown beside them are REMOVED (the dropdowns are the single statement); the ACCESS column now renders ONLY when a row genuinely diverges from STATUS, which it can: ACCESS is the date-driven lapse state (shared gate helpers), so it differs when a trial/manual/canceled-Paddle expiry date has passed while the stored status still reads active (the gate enforces by date; the stored value only moves via cron or an admin), during the one-month read-only grace window (which STATUS has no vocabulary for), and when the durable cancel marker (mig 183) says canceling/canceled. On a page where no row diverges the column disappears entirely. **(4) Stale banner**: the static "Modeling Hub is in pre-launch lockdown" banner claimed only admins and whitelisted emails could register or sign in; both mig-136 flags are `false` on prod (the hub is live), so it was flatly wrong. It now reads the SAME two flag routes the auth pages enforce and renders only while a surface is actually closed, worded for whichever one is. **(5)** The never-asked real estate dash already carried the tooltip; it was undiscoverable (bare hyphen), so it now has a help cursor + dotted underline and names the state plainly.
- **REGISTERED USERS WHO NEVER REQUEST ACCESS NOW GET A CARD AND ONE REMINDER (2026-08-30, `verify-access-request` 36 NEW, teeth proven by two sabotages, NOT browser-verified).** Diagnosis on the live DB: 13 users, 11 confirmed, and **4 confirmed non-admin users sat on plan `none` having never filed a trial request** (no `trial_requests` row of any status, no subscription row), 3 of them for more than two days; the approval toggle is ON, so requests queue for review. The request button lived only on `/choose-plan` and the pricing cards, behind a slim dashboard banner, and NO user-facing surface read `trial_requests`, so after the one-time toast a pending request was invisible. Now: **(1) the dashboard leads with an access card** for `planKey === 'none'` non-admins ("Your access is not active yet", request button INSIDE the card posting the same `/api/refm/trial` action; instant grant refreshes the gate, a queued request flips the card to "Your access request is with the team", which also survives reload via a new `trialRequestPending` on `GET /api/refm/entitlements`, queried only for no-plan non-admins so entitled users pay nothing).
