A LAPSED user keeps a renew banner instead: their path is a plan, not a trial request. **(2) One reminder email, ever**: `runAccessReminderScan` joins the existing daily cron; the pure `isAccessReminderEligible` rule (confirmed 2+ days by `confirmed_at ?? created_at`, no plan, no trial request of ANY status, no subscription row, never admins) is exported and pinned; once-only is structural, the `subscription_email_log` key is (`access_reminder`, `once`, confirm-day) and the anchor never moves. The email links straight to `/choose-plan`. No migration needed.
- **ACCOUNT DELETION, BOTH SIDES, WITH THE SEMANTICS STATED (2026-08-30, `verify-account-deletion` 33 NEW behavioral against a fake client, teeth proven by two sabotages, migration 219 APPLIED 2026-08-30 and verified live by probe, NOT browser-verified).** Diagnosis: self-delete existed in Settings as a bare `users` delete relying on FK cascades; there was NO admin delete; a live Paddle subscription was never cancelled (Paddle would keep billing an account that no longer resolves); and the no-FK tables (`user_platform_subscriptions`, `subscription_email_log`, `manual_invoices`, `payment_transactions`, `trusted_devices`) silently orphaned. Cascades already covered the whole project tree, permissions, trial requests, AI counters and the 002/005 training tables; `admin_audit_log` was believed to SET NULL its target (WRONG, corrected 2026-08-30d: that was read from migration 007, which never took effect; the live FK is NO ACTION and BLOCKED deletes, see the 2026-08-30d entry above), which is in any case why a deletion audit cannot live there. Now ONE shared engine (`src/shared/account/deleteUserAccount.ts`) serves both routes. **REMOVES**: the users row + cascades, plus explicit cleanup of subscriptions rows, email log, trusted devices and the avatar file. **RETAINS deliberately**: `payment_transactions` and `manual_invoices` (deleting them would falsify recorded revenue and issued invoices), audit-log rows, authored articles, Training Hub roster records (separate identity), and a new `account_deletions` row (mig 219: raw-copy identity, deleted_by, timestamp, the optional message, `removed` jsonb). **REFUSES**: admin accounts (demote first); a live Paddle subscription without explicit opt-in, and when opted in it is cancelled at Paddle IMMEDIATELY first, with a cancel failure ABORTING the whole delete; and an admin deletion that cannot be audited (503 until 219 is applied; self-delete instead proceeds with a loud log so a user's right to delete does not wait on migration lag). Admin UI: Delete on each `/admin/users` row and a danger zone on the user record, both opening ONE `DeleteUserModal` that names the user, lists removed vs retained, takes the optional message (emailed via `accountDeletedEmail`), and arms only when the user's email is typed. Self UI: Settings now previews what is lost (project/version counts) and a live paid sub adds a required "cancelled immediately" checkbox feeding `acknowledgeSubscriptionCancel`. **Migration 219 was applied by the founder via the dashboard the same day and verified live by probe** (all 11 columns selectable, 0 rows), so admin deletions are fully unblocked; the session itself could not run DDL because `.env.local`'s `DATABASE_URL` is stale (tenant resolves in `aws-1-ap-northeast-1`, password fails auth), which still needs refreshing for future sessions.

## 2026-08-20

- **Platform infra**: project-switch state-leak fixed; session-based versioning + per-version change_log. **View/edit lock**: a project opens read-only (no version churn) until you click Edit, which presents an **Edit choice** (edit this version in place / edit a different version / create a new version) plus a mid-session **Save as new version** (`startEditInPlace` / `saveAsNewVersion` in `lib/persistence/module1-sync.ts`); schema-tolerant reads; paginated version history. **PDF full-project report** (`lib/pdf/generateProjectPdf.ts` on pdf-lib) mirrors every module tab via shared `lib/reports/` builders (m4/opex/capex/financing/cos), with per-module part picker + number-scale + **version picker** in `ExportModal` (file named after the chosen version). **PDF EXPORT REVIEW, three passes (2026-08-12, commits `2f546659` / `3cea19dd` / `76ed9e50`):** the equivalent of the workbook review, over BOTH PDFs at all three display scales. Pass 1 (arithmetic + signs): the shared cash-sweep waterfall ADDED interest and principal instead of subtracting them (both series are stored already-negative and the builder negated each twice, overstating every row below "Cash Available" by 2x interest + 2x principal, and breaking the tie to closing cash that the last row claims); the PDF FCFE build-up started from FCFF and re-added the terminal value; "new debt" had three figures under one name; per-asset economics reported ZERO cost and 100% margin on every asset (a sign-convention mismatch in the returns engine, ships on screen too); the summary P&L printed deductions positive beside a negative fund fee row so the column could not be added; and stock rows were summed across periods. Pass 2 (readability + scale): column geometry is now RESOLVED PER DOCUMENT from the widest cell it will draw, because a full-unit export ellipsised **3,867 numeric cells** and thousands 43 (millions stays 104pp; thousands and full go 104 -> 140pp, so wider numbers cost pages, never digits); three label-truncation bugs, two of them the SAME off-by-padding mistake (measure the text, then hand that width to a `drawCell` that subtracts 4pt a side); per-unit rates stay unscaled but now name their unit; and a value that rounds to zero at the displayed precision prints the en-dash instead of "(0.0)" (126 + 13 cells, fixed in the shared `formatAccounting` so screen and PDF move together). Pass 3 (consistency + missing content): one name per metric with its basis stated, gross-vs-net named on a fund project, a Model Integrity Checks table in BOTH documents, DSCR flagged as a covenant reading with a Lender Covenants table, the model version on the cover and every footer, derived Module 5 tab numbers, and Timeline / Land & Area / Fund Inputs / Sensitivity (entitlement-gated) added. **Two shared builders came out of it**, `lib/reports/streamReports.ts` (the FCFF/FCFE/dividend row lists, previously FOUR hand-maintained copies, which is why one drifted) and `lib/reports/checksReport.ts` (the three integrity identities + a RELATIVE, PEAK-anchored tolerance, now shared by both PDFs AND the workbook Checks tab). New verifiers `verify-report-arithmetic` **78**, `verify-report-readability` **55**, `verify-report-consistency` **59**, each with teeth proven by sabotage; toggle-off byte-identical at all three scales. **PASS 4, PRESENTATION (2026-08-13, six items over BOTH PDFs AND the workbook, `verify-report-presentation` 92):** every item was a figure the model computes correctly and a reader cannot interpret. (1) The fund waterfall printed Hurdle Paid's LIFETIME total (5,668.5m) directly under a Total Hurdle Owed row carrying no total and closing at 4,834.0m, so the column read as more paid than was ever owed; a shared `fundWaterfallTotalsNote` now names which rows are flows and which are balances and quotes both figures FROM THE MODEL. (2) **"Total" meant two things in one document**: the P&L used it for a lifetime sum while the balance sheet used it for the CLOSING balance (TOTAL ASSETS printed 2,512.1m, the at-exit figure) and the cash flow statement used it for both at once. The heading is now DERIVED from the rows via a new `M4Row.totalIsBalance` + `resolveTotalColumnKind`: `Closing` on the balance sheet, `Total / Closing` on cash flow and schedules, `Total` unchanged on the P&L. The classifier is shared with the summary PDF's hand-built rows (which had a parallel copy), proven by a sabotage that now breaks all three surfaces where it used to break only two. NOTE `drawPeriodTable` had hardcoded the literal `'Total'` and ignored `table.columns[1]`, so the builders alone changed nothing; and `resolveMetrics` now measures the HEADING as column content, since it is no longer a fixed 5-character word. (3) **A structural zero now says so**: two live assets reported 0 sqm BUA and zero cost with a 100% margin, both correct (an existing operational asset with 3,682.1m of historical pre-capex and no new build, and a companion whose area and cost sit on its parent). New shared `lib/reports/assetNotes.ts` marks the cell `- [a]` and footnotes the reason, on the composition table, the assets table, per-asset economics (cost, margin AND yield) and the workbook's Returns and Land & Area tabs. Detection is on the asset's DECLARED shape (`isCompanion`, `status === 'operational'` + historical spend), never on the zero itself, or a genuinely empty asset would be explained away; a real figure is never suppressed, and `takeFootnotes()` prints only the markers a table actually raised. (4) The three capital bases sat INSIDE the fee basis table with Timing / Rate (and in Excel, Base) empty and their amount in the column that holds a fee charged, so a quantity of capital read as a fee; they are now their own captioned block above it, tagged `capital base`. **Worth recording: the reported symptom was exact for the WORKBOOK only.** Both PDFs already placed the amount under `Basis charged on` (grid columns are per-table and content-sized, so nothing could shift it); the workbook has one `Total` column serving both the basis and the charge rows, which is where the ambiguity actually lived. (5) Distributed Equity IRR and MOIC appeared three times in a ten-page summary, twice as a BYTE-IDENTICAL card set (`headlineReturnCards` called on both the exec summary and the returns page); the returns page now points at them instead, and a shared `fundHeadlineRestatementNote` captions the fund page as the gross-vs-net split of the same metric rather than a third statement of it. (6) All of it confirmed in the FULL report and the workbook, not only the summary. Teeth proven by nine sabotages (8 / 5 / 19 / 2 / 2 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 4 failures); toggle-off re-proved, and the non-fund fixes are asserted to still render with the fund OFF so half a fix cannot pass. **NOT browser-verified.** **Excel MODEL export** (`lib/excel/buildModelWorkbook.ts`): a HARDCODED platform snapshot (every computed cell is the platform value written as a constant, editing does NOT recalculate, re-export after changing inputs), now a FULL module-for-module mirror of every module (Modules 1-6). 16 tabs in MODULE ORDER (Cover, **Summary**, Inputs, Timeline, Land & Area, Capex, Financing, Revenue, Opex, Schedules, P&L, Cash Flow, Balance Sheet, Returns, **Scenarios**, Checks); each output tab is a section-for-section mirror built from the SAME shared `lib/reports/` builders + snapshots the on-screen tabs use. **Cover + Summary + print setup (2026-07-14 cosmetic pass):** the Cover is a clean, module-grouped **Table of Contents** (numbered, hyperlinked, Module 1-6 + Reference bands + legend; shared front-matter primitives `frontMatterCanvas`/`Banner`/`Band`); a new **Summary** tab (2nd, `always`-part) is a one-page executive summary (key facts, a headline-metric tile wall, two side-by-side highlight tables Development economics / Returns & leverage, all in millions, computed from the same returns engine as the Returns tab). `applyPrintSetup` gives every sheet a print-ready A4 layout (fit-to-width, landscape wide tables repeating header rows 1:4, centred portrait one-page Cover/Summary, page-numbered footer). Inputs holds EVERY input grouped by domain band in module order (Project / Phases / Land / Assets / Sub-units / Returns / Capex, then FINANCING / REVENUE / OPEX INPUTS). **Scenarios tab = Module 6 mirror (2026-07-14):** built from the SAME shared `caseComparisonReport` + `caseYoYReport` that feed the on-screen Module 6 and the PDF: 1. Cases & Assumptions (every case + differing-lever grid), 2. Scenario Comparison (headline KPI matrix, delta vs base), 3. Year-on-Year Impact (each changed input + the per-period outputs it drives, base + each scenario delta). `ExportModal` threads the `caseComparison` bundle into `generateModelWorkbookBuffer` and shows the case picker for Excel too, **defaulting the statement tabs to the Management/base case while the Scenarios tab always compares ALL cases** (the user can pick another case for the statements). Degrades to a "no scenarios" note when no cases. Scenarios is an `outputs`-part sheet (hidden under an outputs-off filter). ONE standard navy palette (navy tabs + totals, deep-navy section bands, pale-navy subtotals, navy-pale inputs, red only for genuine check failures); the verifier locks the palette + tab order. **Export improvements (2026-07-28):** (1) **per-tab sub-TOC**, a nav strip at the top of every data tab listing that tab`s sections as internal hyperlinks plus a link straight to the basis-of-calc block and a "Back to Cover" link (reuses the `setSectionSink`/`sectionReg` + `{text,hyperlink}` mechanism the Cover ToC uses). Placement is structure-aware: rows are RESERVED below the frozen header on gridded tabs via a new `insertRowsAt` helper in `styles.ts` (ExcelJS `spliceRows` shifts values/styles but NOT merged ranges, which corrupts the file, so it unmerges, splices, re-merges shifted and rewrites same-sheet hyperlink targets), and appended at the bottom on the designed one-page canvases (Summary / Checks) whose merges span the insert point. ORDER is load-bearing: `applyTabGuides` -> `applyTabSubToc` (both re-base `sectionReg`) -> Cover/Guide last, so every deep link still lands on its section. (2) **palette LOCKED** in `ARGB` + a new exported `ALLOWED_FILLS`: the off-palette `#0C2340` and the `#1B3A6B` FILLS are gone (`#1B3A6B` survives as a text tone only, by decision), the sanctioned darker band is `#0D2E5A`, the sub-TOC strip is `#DDE7F3`, pass green is `#2E7D52`, and the FAST input fill `#E2EAF4` stays deliberately distinct; the verifier now asserts every fill is in `ALLOWED_FILLS`, the three forbidden fills are absent, and every section band is navy or sectionDark. (3) **basis-of-calc DEEPENED** (it already existed on all 15 data tabs since 2026-07-26; the gap was depth + burial): `TAB_GUIDES` is now `{kind:inputs|logic|feeds}` rendered as a labelled Inputs / Calculation / Feeds mini-table describing the real mechanics per tab, shared with the Guide tab. (4) **Timeline rebuilt** from 2 rows into a dated phase schedule (construction / operations windows + project envelope) plus a cell-based Gantt over the period grid, driven by the canonical `computePhaseTimeline` / `computeProjectTimeline`; ExcelJS 4.4 exposes NO chart API (`worksheet.addChart` is undefined) so a native chart is not possible and coloured cells are the fallback. Timeline columns B/C/D reclaimed from 3-char spacers to readable widths. Verifier `verify-excel-export` 187 -> **304**. Verifier `verify-excel-export` 159/159; two-way Sensitivity grid (Returns) is the one pending unit. **Platform guide REBUILT + guided tour (2026-08-20, detail in CLAUDE-REFM.md 2026-08-20g/h):** full-screen overlay with side nav + search, ONE content source (`lib/guide/guideContent.ts`) rewritten current that the 34-step guided tour renders VERBATIM (string-identity pinned), structure from the live registries via the pure `lib/moduleTabs.ts` (the frozen-copy verifier trap is closed; a new tab with no guide content fails the suite). Tour: auto-runs once per user (mig 217 blob + localStorage fallback, MERGING writes), module-only walk when no project is open, per-tab DOM emptiness skip, first-run guide prompt before the tour offer. **Trial exports (2026-08-20):** every PDF a watermark-listed plan exports (full, summary, IC deck) carries a server-resolved diagonal stamp + footer (admin control on /admin/plans, default ON for trial only, `white_label_pdf` untouched, paid exports byte-identical); the deck route now enforces Module 7 per plan server-side (403 FEATURE_NOT_INCLUDED); Excel gates on `excel_snapshot` (was `excel_formula`, false on every plan, which denied pro/firm an export they are sold; trial stays denied). **OpEx seeds ZERO (2026-08-20):** all 26 seeded builder values are 0, line items intact, stored-but-disabled seed values cleared on hydrate with user-typed values preserved, no number moved on any live project.

## 2026-08-19

- **SELLING COSTS BELONG TO REVENUE, DEPRECIATION STARTS AT OPERATIONS, AND A LOCKED BUTTON LOOKS LOCKED (2026-08-19e to 19h, four shipped blocks, BROWSER-VERIFIED on production).** Detail per block in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-19e/f/g/h. **(Pass B, 19e) THE VIEW LOCK NOW COVERS BUTTONS, BY DECLARATION.** It covered `input`/`select`/`textarea` only, so the fund fee toggles were clickable in view mode, `setProject` no-opped and the click was discarded in silence (TRAPS 7.20, reintroduced). The rule for buttons is INVERTED from the rule for inputs and has to be, since nothing in the DOM distinguishes a mutating button from a collapsible: a button declares `data-view-mutates="true"` and gets `pointer-events:none`, a grey dashed treatment AND `disabled` (CSS cannot stop a keyboard Enter), while a check asserts the once-reverted blanket lock STAYS reverted. The split brain is gone: `saveFundTerms` is a network call that does not no-op, so a view-mode click had been writing the DURABLE row while the snapshot stayed put, measured live on MARINA GATE (durable `enabled=false, deficit` against a snapshot `enabled=true, equity`); the durable row was aligned TO THE SNAPSHOT, no chosen setting changed, and the snapshot is never written because it is versioned history. **(Pass C, 19f) `computeAssetCost` TAKES THE REVENUE SNAPSHOT** and resolves the bases internally; `collectionsTotal` / `saleRevenueTotal` / `totalRevenueTotal` are DELETED with their three helpers. Pass A wired six sites by hand, which fixed the numbers and left a twelfth free to forget; with ONE input a site either has revenue in scope or it does not. `core/calculations/revenue/sellingCosts.ts` (ZERO imports, so `src/core` consumes the snapshot structurally) owns basis resolution AND the multiplication, the engine does no arithmetic of its own, and Module 2 Revenue renders a **Selling Costs** section with the basis NAMED and held assets showing zero with the reason. `computeAllSellResults` keeps its inputs and reads no cost input, which is load-bearing because `computeAssetCapex` calls BACK into `computeAssetCost`. **(19g) DEPRECIATION STARTS WHEN THE ASSET IS AVAILABLE FOR USE**, not in the last construction year: `offset + cp - 1` is the M2 PIT revenue-recognition handover (verifier-pinned A2-1..A2-5) reused for a different question. **IT TOOK TWO PASSES because there are TWO streams**, the asset's own capex and the IDC capitalised into it, and `AssetPL.daPerPeriod` is their sum; fixing one left the other charging during construction and **my measurement script read only the first and printed "none" while the defect was live**. One shared `operationsStartIndex`, in PERIODS not dates (`computePhaseTimeline` defaults to MONTHLY without `modelType`, which collapsed the index to zero on the first attempt, caught by the fixture). **Measured: 5.791m + 0.654m and 14.294m + 0.076m charged early -> 0; P&L D&A 58.008m -> 51.562m and 1,614.267m -> 1,599.897m; closing NBV rises by the same amount to the cent, so it is conservation, not value lost; BS 0.00 throughout.** **(19h) SELLING COSTS YEAR ON YEAR** in Module 2 (Total column first, reading the engine's own `perLinePerPeriod`), and an **EXIT CAP RATE** terminal method derived as `r - g` unless typed, with a forward-income toggle for the `(1 + g)` step that had been buried inside the perpetuity branch. Terminal EV and both IRRs byte-identical on both projects. **A defect I shipped between the two and fixed the same day: the year-on-year table omitted `collectionsPerPeriod`**, so a selling cost degraded to an even spread and disagreed with Capex while the LIFETIME TOTALS matched (Marina Residences 2027: 1,253,606 against 114,380), and the registry entry I had written for that site said it did not need collections, which is TRAPS 7.26 committed into the registry built to prevent it. Verifiers: `verify-view-lock` 37 -> 57, `verify-selling-cost-scope` 44 -> 91, `verify-fixed-assets` 82 -> 99, `verify-returns-engine` 107 -> 123, `verify-capex-collections` 52 -> 66, every new section proven by sabotages that break the CODE.

- **THE RECONCILIATION KNOWS ABOUT DEDICATED EQUITY, AND EVERY REACHABLE CALL SITE VALUES A COST LINE THE SAME WAY (2026-08-19d, Pass A, `verify-capex-collections` 52 -> 63 and `verify-returns-buildup` 62 -> 66, teeth proven by reverting each fix, DEPLOYED at `2771650e` with health SHA matching HEAD).** Two defects, both introduced the same day by the two commits below it and both LIVE on production when found, fixed as a narrow pass on their own. **(1) A FALSE IMBALANCE.** `reconcile` compared `EquityMovement.totalCash` against `sum(split.equity)`, but cash equity now has TWO halves, the base development draw in `split.equity` and the management fee in `split.dedicatedEquity` drawn outside the ratio, so the check ran short by exactly the fee whenever the fee is equity funded: measured **61,153,555.75 against 52,485,540.76, a gap of 8,668,014.99**, the fee to the cent. It now compares against both halves, plus a new PER-PERIOD check that the halves sum to `cashPerPeriod`, because a lifetime total can agree while the timing does not and the halves are what schedule 9 renders. **Invisible because both live projects are set to `deficit`**, where the dedicated series is all zeros and the two forms of the check agree: the identity was broken and the suite stayed green. **(2) THE MODEL DISAGREED WITH ITSELF ABOUT CAPEX.** 19c wired FIVE of `computeAssetCost`'s ELEVEN call sites; the rest fell back to the old sub-unit product, so one cost line had two values depending on which surface asked. **Measured: financing aggregate vs per-asset Cash Flow, 432,082,531.35 vs 431,585,397.11 on MARINA GATE (497,134.24) and 1,625.87 on RE HUB; both now agree to 0.00.** Five sites wired (`financials-resolvers` per-asset CF, `fixed-assets-resolvers`, `capexReports`, `cosReports`, and `revenue-resolvers.computeAssetCapex` via an optional `revenue` argument); `financing-hooks.ts` deliberately NOT wired because `createFinancingHooks` is imported nowhere in src, app or scripts, and wiring unreachable code would be pretending. RE HUB keeps two PRE-EXISTING unrelated reconciliation issues (facility shares 33.33, a period-0 drawdown mismatch), unchanged. **The guard's shape is the lesson (TRAPS 7.26):** the call-site registry now carries TWO dimensions, `wired` for collections (which drive PHASING) and `bases` for the revenue bases (which drive the AMOUNT), because the single flag had `computeAssetCapex` recorded as `wired: false` with the reason "phasing cannot move a total", which is TRUE and was read as permission to omit the bases as well, which move it by over a million. **STILL OPEN, diagnosed in full and NOT built, preserved in [CLAUDE-TODO.md](CLAUDE-TODO.md): Pass B** (the fee toggles are plain buttons that the view lock does not cover, so a click is silently discarded, TRAPS 7.20 again; and the Financing toggle writes the durable row even when the store write no-ops, a split brain) **and Pass C** (marketing and commission computed and displayed in Module 2 Revenue, with `computeAssetCost` taking the revenue snapshot so no call site can forget a base). **Also logged, out of scope: depreciation starts in the last CONSTRUCTION year**, 5.791m early on MARINA GATE and 14.294m on RE HUB.

- **A SELLING COST APPLIES ONLY TO AN ASSET THAT SELLS, AND A SHARED RATE SAYS SO (2026-08-19, `verify-selling-cost-scope` 44 NEW, teeth proven by two sabotages, NOT browser-verified). NO NUMBER MOVES on either live project, measured to the byte.** Three symptoms reported from the Marketing line. **(1) "A rate typed on one asset appeared on another, and clearing it on the second removed it from the first."** That is what happens: a cost line belongs to a PHASE, and with no per-asset override an edit writes the LINE, which every asset in the phase reads. The design is not the defect; saying nothing about it was. The row now NAMES the other assets a typed value reaches and the Override button says so in its tooltip. **(2) Marketing was charged to Operate and Lease assets.** A commission and a marketing budget are percentages of a SALE, and a held asset carries its own operating expenses with no sale for them to be a percentage of. New `CostLine.assetScopeOverride` + `deriveAssetScope` scope the two selling lines to `Sell` / `Sell + Manage`, resolved from the BASE ID when nothing is stamped so every saved line gains it with no migration (the `stageOverride` precedence, for the same reason). Enforced in ONE place, `assetVisibleLines`, which the Costs tab, the engine and the copy planner all call, so an asset that cannot see the row is not charged for it and the tab STATES the absence; a row-level "Applies to" picker is the escape hatch. This is the reference structure (Schedules R40: its Marketing charges on Sale Revenue / GDV and phases on the sale-revenue curve). **Measured: total capex identical on both live projects** (426,406,950.00 and 4,912,199,955.98 before and after), because the one project with a marketing line carries a zero rate and the other had already been zeroed BY HAND on every operated and leased asset, which is the manual workaround this replaces. **(3) THE AMOUNT WAS WRONG AND IS NOW FIXED (second stage, same day).** `computeAssetRevenue` was the basis every percent-of-revenue method charged on and it sums `metricValue x unitPrice` over Sellable AND Operable AND Leasable; that product is a sale value only for Sellable, is ONE YEAR of rent for Leasable and ONE NIGHT at full occupancy for Operable. **Measured against the revenue engine: a leased asset understated 5x to 11x, a hotel 2,255x to 4,739x** (Hotel Phase 1, a 1.158m basis against 5,488.913m of revenue the model itself computes). **THE BASE NOW COMES FROM THE REVENUE MODULE and the RATE stays an input on the Capex tab**, which is the split asked for: `percent_of_revenue_sale` reads the sale value (GDV), `percent_of_total_revenue` the whole-hold revenue, `percent_of_revenue_cash` the collections it already read (new `saleRevenueTotalForAsset` / `totalRevenueTotalForAsset` beside the collections helper, wired at all five sites that already pass collections; revenue depends on sub-units and phases and never on cost, so there is no circularity). **ZERO IS NOT ABSENT and that distinction IS the fix**: a held asset has no sale value so its base is 0, where `undefined` would fall back to the broken product, which is why a sale-basis line charges zero on a hotel even with the scope forced OPEN and the two fixes are independent defences. The caption names the figure the engine used and, on a held asset, says "no sale value: this asset is held, not sold". **MEASURED: FMP - MARINA GATE total capex 437,844,950.00 -> 438,943,005.32** (+1,098,055.32, all of it Marina Residences' marketing moving from a 571.900m product to a 626.803m sale value, the gap being sale price indexation), **FMP RE HUB +1,625.87 on 4.9bn** (rounding scale: both its selling assets are Sell, so product and sale value already agreed). **A DEFECT I SHIPPED AND THE USER FOUND (same day, TRAPS 7.25): the scope missed the live marketing line.** `deriveAssetScope` resolved identity from the BASE ID alone, a SECOND identity rule that disagrees with the shared `resolveCatalogId` on the shape a real project produces: a line added from the catalog picker mints `custom-<timestamp>__<phase>` and carries its identity in `catalogId`, so its base id matches no selling id. The live row is `custom-1787123860417__phase_1`, catalogId "marketing", and it kept charging Podium Retail **119,700**, while the seeded `commission__phase_2` on the same project was correctly scoped: half the rule worked and the half on screen did not. **The verifier could not see it because its fixture used seeded ids exclusively, so both resolvers agreed on every line in it.** Fixed by having ONE implementation (moved into `selectedBase.ts`, calls `resolveCatalogId`, re-exported from `index.ts`), and pinned by a permanent section H carrying the minted shape, whose teeth were proven by REVERTING the fix (6 checks red, a held asset charged 9,600,000) rather than by mutating the fixture. **Also found: the Costs tab's row list was a FOURTH inline copy of the visibility filter**, which would have shown a marketing row the engine no longer charged (the mirror of the country-gate defect). Three guards earned their keep: `verify-cost-catalog` caught `assetScopeOverride` missing from the migration literal, which would have made it the FOURTH field dropped on every hydrate, and two source-shape checks pinned the old three-argument call and now demand the strategy. `verify-selling-cost-scope` **44 -> 64**, three sabotages (a fourth was deleted for proving nothing). Detail in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.23, 7.24 and 7.25.

- **THE FEE IS DRAWN FROM EQUITY, THE STATEMENTS FOOT, AND THE DDM HAS TWO HALVES (2026-08-19, `verify-returns-buildup` 51 -> 62 and `verify-cf-capex-foot` 63 -> 72, teeth proven by seven and four sabotages, NOT browser-verified).** Six items, read from the reference formulas. **(1) The fee funding toggle is on the FINANCING tab**, beside the funding method, which is where it decides whether the fee sits inside the deficit that method sizes; it was only on Fund Terms, which is why it could not be found. One field, both screens, written to the snapshot AND the durable row. **(2) The fee equity draw is CAPPED.** It was the whole fee in every period, so it drew throughout the project life; it is now drawn only while construction is SPENDING (the R116 gate) and only as far as keeps cash at the minimum after the base funding, so after construction the fee is paid from cash. Measured on the fixture: 5.079m drawn of a 17.478m fee. **(3) It is its own row, not part of the ratio.** `DebtEquitySplit.dedicatedEquity` and `EquityMovement.developmentPerPeriod` / `managementFeePerPeriod`; it renders as the THIRD row of schedule 9 Total Equity Required and in Equity Movement, and base equity is now exactly the ratio share of base debt with nothing folded in. **(4) The deficit schedule IS the reference schedule** (Schedules R105 to R124): cash capex, operating inflows BY ASSET CLASS (residential cash collection / hospitality EBITDA / retail NOI, with the fee as a line when the deficit funds it), pre-financing net cash, minimum, opening, the gated funding need split at the ratio, then the debt drawdowns (capex, IDC) and the equity drawdowns (development, management fee). **Every "NOT in sizing" memo line is gone**: each row is now in the arithmetic of the row beneath it. **(5) The in-kind pair is INSIDE the totals**, Land In-Kind inside Total Capex and In-Kind Equity inside Cash Flow from Financing, so both sections foot on the FULL cost, the two net to zero and Net Cash Flow is unchanged; they were memos outside the totals. **Interest Paid is ONE line per origin again** (Returns R65), the IDC / operating split being a classification that belongs on the Financing schedules. **(6) TWO DDMs, in the reference order** (Returns R114 to R139): the DDM before the performance fee with its IRR / MOIC, then the hurdle waterfall, then the DDM after the fee against the same equity investment with the post-fee IRR / MOIC (new shared `buildDdmPostFeeRows`). **THE FEE BASES NOW FOLLOW THE REFERENCE**: fund size is the SELECTED METHOD'S FUNDING REQUIREMENT split at its base ratio, not every draw the model ever made, so IDC drawdowns, the fee's own equity draw, in-kind land and pre-existing capital are all outside it; still resolved on the fee-free pass and FROZEN before the solver, so `fund_size_solved` stays forbidden. The 18c pro rata equity attribution (four memo lines ESTIMATING what a draw funded) is retired: the engine now supplies the real split.

- **THREE CORRECTIONS AGAINST THE REFERENCE (2026-08-18f, `verify-returns-buildup` 47, six sabotages, migration 215 APPLIED 2026-08-19 and verified live by probe).** **(1) The in-kind credit is REMOVED from FCFE**: built in 18e, never asked for. FCFF charges full land incl. in-kind and FCFE inherits it, as the reference does, so FCFE is the return on TOTAL equity. Equity IRR 109.63% -> **37.96%** (MARINA GATE), 14.37% -> **7.44%** (RE HUB); project IRR unchanged. **(2) The management fee funding toggle, at the ENGINE, both paths.** The reference has NO such toggle (its fee feeds the deficit at the project ratio); ours did neither path cleanly (18b added the equity draw AFTER the engine ran, so the fee was funded twice, and there was no UI). Now `fundTerms.managementFeeFunding` = `deficit` (default, reference, every project unchanged) or `equity`, which removes the fee from the deficit sizing and hands it to the engine as `dedicatedEquityByPeriod`, drawn as equity on top of the ratio split. Toggle on the Fund Terms tab, four mappers, fifth server tier (215). RE HUB equity path: net cash required 420,532k -> 354,839k, equity drawn exactly the 347,832k fee. **(3) Cash deficit funding, reported LINE BY LINE against Schedules R102-R124, NO change made.** Three differences, two of them the same thing: the reference keeps ALL finance cost out of the sizing (R112 has none, R123 measures IDC headroom from pre-interest R115 + R116) where ours subtracts cash finance cost from cash available AND feeds it into the IDC budget; and the reference gates the need on `cash capex > 0`. MARINA GATE identical either way; RE HUB 420,532k ours vs 263,780k reference rule, the gap being the existing hotel loan interest, which is 18d Item D, still HELD pending the operating-shortfall decision. Detail in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-18f. **Not browser-verified.**

## 2026-08-18

- **Module 1 (Setup / Costs / Financing): CLOSED 2026-08-17** after **28 defects found by entering a real project end to end** (seven batches over one day, every one reported from the SCREEN rather than by a check), plus two more the new verifiers found themselves. Every fix is browser-verified against the live project and a throwaway, each is pinned by a verifier proven by sabotage, and every one that could move a saved number was measured before and after. **Three structural outcomes are load-bearing and are detailed in the bullets below: the country-gated RETT mechanism is REMOVED (no cost line can be present but invisible, and RETT is no longer seeded), VIEW MODE IS VISIBLY READ-ONLY (an input that silently discarded an edit is the same defect as a screen showing a number the model does not use), and the legacy migration no longer enumerates the fields it keeps.** The recurring shape across the 28: **one rule written in more than one place, with more than one answer.** Read [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) section 7 before touching Module 1: 7.12 (a fix applied to one copy leaves the others wrong), 7.15 (silent normalisation), 7.16 (a whitelist is the wrong shape for a migration), 7.19 (a gate that hides a row is a defect generator) and 7.21 (a list that mirrors another list diverges) are all from this block. **No further Module 1 changes before launch.** Base lock unchanged at M2.0 Pass 58. **Asset strategy changes are a MODEL operation (2026-08-11), not a field write** (`lib/state/strategySwitch.ts`, pure): the Strategy dropdown used to write one field, and since the engine gates on `asset.strategy` in every resolver, the old strategy's assumptions stopped feeding the model while the new strategy's never started. Measured: EVERY change dropped the asset's revenue to zero (a Sell asset switched to Operate took project revenue 14,055.0m -> 11,233.0m with no warning) and Sell <-> Sell + Manage was a model no-op. Two causes: `asset.revenue` is partitioned (.sell/.operate/.lease) but an asset carries only the one it was built with, and SUB-UNITS are not partitioned at all (one `category` each), so an Operate resolver counted ZERO keys and even a seeded config would compute nothing. Leaving Sell + Manage additionally HARD DELETED the companion asset with its sub-units, cost lines and cost overrides. Now: the outgoing strategy's sub-units, opex and companion are PARKED in `Asset.retainedByStrategy` and the incoming set is restored, or seeded from the outgoing one (names, counts and areas carry; the RATE does not, because a sale price is not an ADR); Support sub-units belong to no strategy and never move. Retention sits OFF TO ONE SIDE deliberately: `state.subUnits` / `costLines` / `costOverrides` still hold ONLY the active strategy's rows, so none of the ~40 engine and UI readers had to learn to filter. The dropdown now previews via a DRY RUN of the same pure function the store commits (so the dialog cannot promise something else), then a `strategyReview` banner persists across navigation until dismissed, because the empty assumptions live on other tabs. **Both the dialog and the banner are GATED on `assetHasStrategyAssumptions` (2026-08-15)**: an asset is created as `Sell`, so the user's first pick from the dropdown was technically a change and fired a four-item review on an asset with nothing on it. `needsReview` cannot answer this itself (it means "active but empty", which on a new asset is everything), so the gate is a separate pure predicate read from the state BEFORE the switch, shared by the store and the dropdown so they cannot disagree. The switch still RUNS when suppressed (Sell + Manage still seeds its companion). Detail in CLAUDE-REFM.md 2026-08-15. `verify-strategy-switch` **84 passed, 0 failed** (CORRECTED 2026-08-18. The recorded "54 passed + 1 PRE-EXISTING FAILURE" was an artefact of running it WITHOUT `--env-file=.env.local`, which the script documented but did not do for itself: it fell back to a fixture with no Sell + Manage asset, and EIGHT companion-retention checks silently did not run while the output read "56 passed, 1 failed". It now loads the env itself and seeds a Sell + Manage asset when the loaded model has none, so both paths exercise the branch; sabotage confirms deleting the companion instead of parking it now fails three checks that previously could not fire. See docs/TRAPS.md 10.10). Teeth proven by three sabotages (4, 12 and 7 failures). **Not browser-verified.** Funding Methods 2/3 gap-sized; conditional IDC + iterative fixed-point solver. Financing sub-tabs: Inputs / Schedules / Funding Gap / Cash Sweep. Dividends are ONE project-level after-debt policy (`Project.dividendPolicy` + `dividendStartYear`) with a terminal 100% payout in the engine.

- **THE DEFICIT SIZING RULE, APPLIED FROM THE REFERENCE FORMULAS (2026-08-18g, `verify-returns-buildup` 51, `verify-m4-bs-reconciliation` section Q rewritten).** Three rules from Schedules R112-R116 and R123, on instruction: **(a) no finance cost of any kind enters the sizing** (nor before-sweep dividends; interest is paid from the cash the project has); **(b) the `capex > 0` gate**, so once construction spend stops no development funding is raised and the forward walk carries the true cash; **(c) IDC headroom measured PRE-interest after the development draw**, where before the cash interest fed back into its own headroom. MARINA GATE unchanged in returns (26.30% / 37.96%), requirement 172,152k -> 182,642k. **RE HUB requirement 420,532k -> 263,244k and closing cash -230,355k -> -288,172k: the honest number**, the difference being construction funding that was servicing the existing hotel loan interest, which construction capital does not do; RE HUB was already negative before any of this. Supersedes 18d Item D (HELD, now applied and stronger) and makes the 2026-08-04 known issue BY DESIGN for RE HUB. Detail in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-18g. **Not browser-verified.**

- **THE REFERENCE FORMULAS, READ DIRECTLY (2026-08-18e, `verify-returns-buildup` REWRITTEN to 39, teeth proven by six sabotages). REVERTS 18d ITEM A.** The reference model was placed in the repo and its formulas read (Schedules R100-165, Returns R93-109). Its closing cash ADDS THE TOTAL DRAWDOWN (capex + IDC) AND SUBTRACTS THE FULL FINANCE COST, so 18d's "interest is cash-only, IDC drawdown is a memo" was the opposite of the reference and is reverted: the whole charge is paid, the whole drawdown comes in, nothing is a memo. **The Finance Cost ledger was the thing at fault**: it deducted BOTH Paid and Capitalized from a payable, and once the charge is paid in full the capitalised figure is a DRAWDOWN, so it removed the same money twice (stuck at -21,525k). It is now Opening + Charge - Paid = Closing, footing at 0.00. **Four items:** (1) the reference line structure, ONE drawdown line, one repayment, and the finance cost as TWO genuine charges (IDC / operating), not the same movement three ways; (2) a construction period is where construction COST is incurred, verified on MARINA GATE 2030 (79,254k of cost, zero drawdown, 11,299k IDC), no change needed; (3) in-kind land CHARGED in FCFF and CREDITED in FCFE, both changed together, so FCFE is the return on CASH equity (equity IRR 109.63% / 14.37%, chosen with that number in view; the reference does NOT credit it, recorded as a deliberate departure); (4) the equity draw attributed across what it funded, ONE line whose total never changes plus a MEMO pro rata across capex / fund fees / operating shortfall / finance cost, summing exactly. **FCFE is FOUR STEPS** from the PRE-TERMINAL FCFF, no removal rows. Project IRR 26.30% / 7.15%. **The reference file was one `git add -A` from being committed** (root `.xlsx` was not ignored); root `.xlsx` is now ignored by default with the two tracked ones re-included by name. Detail in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-18e. **Not browser-verified.**

- **THE FINANCE-COST CHAIN, SIX ITEMS ON THE REFERENCE STRUCTURE (2026-08-18d, `verify-returns-buildup` 37, teeth proven by five sabotages). CORRECTS THE BULLET BELOW, SAME DAY.** **(A) The Finance Cost ledger was removing the capitalised slice TWICE.** `interestPaid` had been set to the WHOLE accrued charge with the capitalised part treated as a matching drawdown: cash-neutral, and wrong exactly as M4 Pass 2P was wrong about capex and in-kind equity, both gross lines overstated by the same amount so the error cancelled in closing cash. The ledger's own invariant `Opening + Accrued - Capitalised - Paid = Closing` walked to a stuck **-21,525k** on MARINA GATE, and two definitions of cash interest disagreed by exactly that (waterfall 21,305k right, `interestPaid` 42,829k wrong). **`interestPaid` is CASH ONLY again**; the capitalised slice grows the balance and leaves as PRINCIPAL; the roll closes at **0.00** on both projects; and the cash statement stops presenting the IDC drawdown as a financing inflow. **(B) FCFF is UNLEVERED**: the IDC line added hours earlier comes OUT, matching both reference models. In-kind land STAYS (a real resource, not a financing charge). **Project IRR 25.25% -> 28.64% (3.113x -> 3.487x) on MARINA GATE, 7.04% -> 7.15% on RE HUB.** **(C) The whole finance cost flows through FCFE**, gross, with the IDC drawdown beside it netting to the cash actually paid. **FCFE does not move (41.75% / 6.453x, 7.44% / 2.129x)**, which is the guard: the gross presentation is only right if the capitalised slice returns later as principal. **(D) HELD, NOT APPLIED.** Sizing the deficit on the IDC half only would drop RE HUB's requirement 420,532k -> 263,244k and take closing cash to -276,696k / -288,172k. **RE HUB is ALREADY negative** (-230,371k measured at `0ce10096`), so the shortfall is pre-existing; item D would deepen it, not create it, and auto-funding an operating shortfall with construction capital is a separate decision. The split series are exposed; only the sizing is held, one line away. **This reopens the 2026-08-04 known issue for RE HUB**, whose earlier closure was correct for the fixture only. **(E)** Revenue and opex stay visible per asset, with a per-class subtotal struck from them (Residential Cash Collection / Hospitality EBITDA / Retail NOI), additive so the existing subtotals keep their meaning. **(F)** `fundTerms.managementFeeFunding`, absent means `debt` so nothing moves; `equity` draws the fee as its own equity line AND credits it as cash available, without which the fee is funded twice. Measured on RE HUB: equity drawn 0 -> 347,832k, net cash required 420,532k -> 354,839k. **Guards added: UNLEVERED PURITY** (no interest / IDC / finance-cost row in FCFF, on the row list and the arithmetic) **and THE FINANCE COST ROLL FOOTS** (closes at zero, cash-paid equals accrued less capitalised, capitalised non-zero so neither is vacuous). Detail in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-18d. **Not browser-verified.**

- **FCFF IS FULL COST, FCFE CHAINS FROM IT, AND IDC HAS ONE TREATMENT (2026-08-18, `verify-returns-buildup` 30 NEW, teeth proven by five sabotages). MOVES PROJECT IRR ON BOTH LIVE PROJECTS, deliberately.** Three corrections matching the reference. **(1) FCFF deducts FULL-COST capex.** It was `CFO + CFI` with CFI on the CASH basis, so the unlevered return was measured against a cost base excluding the land contributed in kind and the interest capitalised while building; the card even claimed "less in-kind land contributed", which described the FCFE line. **Measured: project IRR 41.21% -> 25.25% (MOIC 4.909x -> 3.113x) on FMP - MARINA GATE, 11.31% -> 7.04% (2.401x -> 1.763x) on FMP RE HUB.** **FIGURES CORRECTED 2026-08-18: the first MARINA GATE measurement was taken against a snapshot the user then edited IN PLACE (version v2 is rewritten in place, so its content changes without a new version number). Re-measured before and after at ONE instant against the current snapshot; the deltas and the FCFE-unchanged invariant are unaffected. See TRAPS 5.1, which already records this for FMP RE HUB.** **(2) FCFE now builds VISIBLY from FCFF** (it was built in parallel, deliberately, because an earlier PDF chained from FCFF and forgot to back out the enterprise terminal), with the terminal swap as TWO VISIBLE ROWS. **The consistency trap is why this had to be one change:** FCFE carried its own in-kind row, so making CFI full-cost while leaving it would charge the sponsor TWICE for land contributed once, silently, and the same applies to the finance cost (FCFF absorbs the IDC half, so FCFE deducts only the OPERATING half). The guard is an algebraic identity, not a row count: FCFE must equal the equity holder's actual net cash rebuilt from the cash statement, and sabotaging either double-charge trips it by exactly the amount. **FCFE does not move on either project** (41.75% and 7.44% before and after), which is the proof the pair stayed consistent. CFO was ALREADY pre-interest and is now labelled so. **(3) IDC, one treatment.** It never reached `capex.perPeriod` (capex = cash + in-kind exactly) though it did reach CWIP; under `debt_drawdown` the balance rolled up with `interestPaid` left at ZERO, so the interest appeared in the cash statement NOWHERE. **The reported "no IDC paid in 2030": construction WAS still running** (79,254k of spend, Phase 1 cp=4 to 2030), and that year's interest WAS classified as IDC internally, and the defect was that **every row labelled IDC carried only the CAPITALISED slice** while 2030 was the one year the whole IDC was paid from surplus cash, so every IDC line read zero. Now the split follows construction SPEND rather than a phase-date window (RE HUB's 2030 gains 9,971k it was missing), IDC is PAID in the period it arises, debt is drawn only for what cash cannot cover, and the two drawdowns are separate lines. **Cash-neutral and proven: closing cash and the balance sheet unchanged, max |Assets - L&E| 0.00 on both.** Total drawn 142,031k against 142,031k repaid on MARINA GATE, the reference invariant that base plus IDC is all repaid. `idcConfig.capitalize` and `fundingMode` are RETIRED (a saved project carrying `capitalize:false, fundingMode:cash` is proven to compute identically); `allocationBasis` stays because it changes no project-level total. A found defect went with them: the Excel Inputs tab printed the funding mode defaulting to `debt_drawdown` while the engine defaulted to `conditional`. **The IAS 23 question is RESOLVED 2026-08-18, with NO engine change: the STRICT qualifying-asset test applies, so interest is IDC only to the extent it funds an asset still UNDER CONSTRUCTION, and a specific borrowing against a complete, operating asset stays an operating finance cost however much is being built elsewhere. Both live projects were already correct under it. The capex-share allocation was designed, measured and REJECTED (it swept 604,800k of a pre-existing hotel loan into IDC on RE HUB and peeled 9,971k onto the operating phase); `tranche.phaseId` is logged as an unusable attribution key and deliberately not fixed. Pinned by three new `verify-returns-buildup` checks proven by sabotage.** Detail in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-18c. **Not browser-verified.**

- **A CASH FLOW SECTION MUST FOOT, AND ONE QUANTITY MUST HAVE ONE RECOGNITION RULE (2026-08-18, `verify-cf-capex-foot` 57 NEW, teeth proven by four sabotages plus a permanent counterfactual section). FIXES A REAL BALANCE SHEET IMBALANCE ON BOTH LIVE PROJECTS.** Reported from the screen: the Cash Flow's Investing section did not add up, `Total Capex` showing 366,407.0k under asset rows summing to 426,407.0k. **The 60,000.0k gap was the LAND CONTRIBUTED IN KIND**, and chasing it found a much larger defect underneath. **(1) The section now foots.** The subtotal has been the CASH basis since M4 Pass 2P (in-kind land never leaves the bank; it is recognised as Land and Share Capital at once) while the asset rows were the FULL cost, so two bases sat under one heading; the phase filter made it worse by using the full cost when filtered and the cash basis when not, so changing the FILTER changed the BASIS. `AssetCF` gained `landInKindPerPeriod`, the rows and both subtotals are the cash slice, and the in-kind land is stated on its own `(memo) Land In-Kind` row rather than dropped. `AssetCF.capexPerPeriod` is deliberately UNCHANGED and still the full cost, because inventory, per-asset returns and the IC report are the asset's CARRYING value, not its cash. **(2) THE BALANCE SHEET WAS OUT BY 25,000,000.** In-kind LAND is capitalised by the capex engine in the CONSUMING asset's window; in-kind EQUITY was stamped by a separate walk over the parcels at the OWNING parcel's phase. Those are the same quantity described twice, and they agreed only for as long as a parcel belonged to its phase: **since 2026-08-17 a parcel is PROJECT-WIDE**, so a Phase 2 asset drawing on Phase 1 land splits them. Measured live: equity recognised the whole 60,000,000 at t=0 while the land arrived 35,000,000 at t=0 and 25,000,000 at t=1, and `bsDifferencePerPeriod` was -25,000,000 in the construction year and zero after. `computeDebtEquitySplit` now reads `capex.perPeriod.landInKind` verbatim, with **no fallback** to the parcel valuation (land no asset capitalises is not on the Balance Sheet, so crediting equity for it is exactly the imbalance being removed), and its now-dead `phases` / `project` parameters are gone. **Measured after: max |Assets - L&E| is 0.00 on BOTH live projects** (FMP - MARINA GATE 25,000,000.00 -> 0.00; FMP RE HUB 360.79 -> 0.00). **That 360.79 was recorded in this file and in the fund guideline as a pre-existing solver-convergence residue and pinned as a non-defect; it was this, and both records are corrected in place.** The only figure that moves besides the imbalance is FMP RE HUB's total in-kind equity, by 400 currency units on 1.35bn, because it now equals the in-kind land actually capitalised. Also pointed the per-asset capex loop at the shared `phaseLocalToProjectIndex` instead of spelling the rule out again (TRAPS 7.12). **Why the suite stayed green:** every pre-existing fixture puts the parcel in the SAME phase as the asset consuming it, and the defect is unreachable in that shape; it also needs the later phase's land line off phase-local index 0, since index 0 clamps to `offset - 1` and makes the two rules agree by accident. Detail in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.22. **Not browser-verified**, but verified through the real shared row builders (`buildDirectCFRows` / `buildIndirectCFRows`, which the screen, both PDFs and Excel all render from) against both live saved snapshots. Note `verify-fund-fees` is **133 passed + 3 PRE-EXISTING FAILURES**, confirmed by stashing these changes and re-running; the long-recorded 136 was stale.

Admin never blocked; unknown plan = access-preserving safety net. **Admin (one consolidated Users tab + Plan Builder)**: `/admin/users` (+ `/admin/users/[id]` = the merged former "User Access" panel: resolved entitlements, grant/revoke overrides, plan assign, trial) and `/admin/plans` (features + limits + price + Most-Popular/badge + non-module visibility + Trial **length (days)** + coupons). **ONE shared plan-setting path** `setUserPlan`. **Plans reconciled** to trial/solo/pro/firm (mig 160). **Pricing single source of truth = entitlement_plans** (prices mig 162, popular/badge mig 163, feature visibility mig 164, trial_days mig 165). **UNIFIED + path-based (2026-06-25):** ONE shared `app/pricing/PricingPageBody.tsx` (server-loads via `loadPricingCatalog`) renders the polished `PricingExplorer` (platform picker -> per-platform `LivePlanCards` + comparison) in BOTH contexts. The `PricingExplorer` island is SESSION-AWARE: logged-out keeps the `/register` handoff, logged-in runs in-app checkout/trial + resume (no separate marketing-vs-app design or URL). **Clean per-platform URL `/pricing/<segment>`** (`app/pricing/[platform]/page.tsx`): segment SOURCE-DERIVED via `platformPricingSegment`/`platformSlugForSegment` in `src/hubs/modeling/config/platforms.ts` (shortName lowercased, REFM->refm, slug fallback; unknown -> picker). Bare `/pricing` = picker.
