Verifiers: `verify-entitlement-gate` 92, `verify-payments-structure` 79, `verify-subscription-management` 204, `verify-trial-days` 7, `verify-pricing-display` 23, `verify-user-overrides` 13, `verify-footer-legal-links` 12, `verify-plan-builder-modules` 16, `verify-pricing-application-ready` **49** (DIAGNOSED and fixed 2026-08-18: the markup and the behaviour were always correct; the check demanded the literal `onClick={() => setSelected(p.slug)}` and the handler had been extracted to `selectPlatform(p.slug)`, so it was a refactor, not a regression. The originally recorded 49 was right all along), `verify-feature-description` 41. See [CLAUDE-ROUTES.md](CLAUDE-ROUTES.md) + [CLAUDE-DB.md](CLAUDE-DB.md) for the per-route/migration detail.

- [docs/FUND_LAYER_GUIDELINE.md](docs/FUND_LAYER_GUIDELINE.md), Fund layer (fund fees, hurdle, carry, gross vs net returns): scope, the linear-fee-base decision, the 7-step sequence, standing rules and known gaps. **Load it when touching anything fund; it is the source of truth and is kept current.** State as of 2026-08-11 (see section 8 of the guideline for the presentation corrections and the toggle-now-ON state): **Steps 1 to 7 LIVE, the fund layer is FEATURE COMPLETE for v1**, migs 208/209/210/211 APPLIED (Steps 4, 6, 6b and 7 needed none). **The ONLY remaining work is browser verification**, which Step 7 explicitly did not close. **Step 7 (end-to-end verify, 2026-08-10):** `scripts/verify-fund-e2e.ts` **86 checks, 0 failures**, run against the REAL project **FMP RE HUB** (3 phases, 8 assets, 14 periods) read from its saved version snapshot and NEVER written back; falls back to the fixture without DB creds and says so. **CORRECTED 2026-08-10:** an earlier version of this bullet said `refm_fund_terms` had ZERO rows. Wrong, and the error is worth remembering: the probe selected a column named `enabled`, the column is `fund_enabled`, the query errored and the empty fallback was reported without checking the error. **FMP RE HUB is CONFIGURED but DISABLED**, carrying a fully populated row written 2026-08-05 (structure fee 0.50%, management 0.25%, custody 0.50%, debt arranging 0.50%, other expenses 3m pa, 8% hurdle, 20% carry, named Fund Manager, 3-row fee matrix) with `fund_enabled` **false**. An empty export is therefore the CORRECT tested behaviour (it is exactly Step 1's fully-populated-but-disabled case), not a defect; flipping only that flag yields 403.844m of fees and 57 fund rows (P&L 17, Cash Flow 4, Returns 36). **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-11: the toggle IS now on.** `refm_fund_terms.fund_enabled` is true and the latest saved version (labelled 1.0, 2026-08-10) carries `project.fundTerms.enabled: true`, so FMP RE HUB exports a full fund workbook: 359.941m of fees, 5,651.850m distributed, ZERO performance fee (the 8% hurdle is never cleared, 1,477.007m unpaid at exit). Both earlier sentences in this paragraph, that the toggle had never been on and that every saved version predates fund terms, are now false and are kept only so the correction is legible. NOTE the project snapshot is edited in place by the user, so a fingerprint captured against it is NOT a stable baseline: use `buildExcelSampleState` for any before/after identity proof. Proves: toggle-off byte-identical across engine + screen builders + Excel + BOTH PDFs (terms fully populated but disabled, and separately at zero rates); the cash chain as an EXACT identity `CFO_on = CFO_off - fee + taxShield` (worst residue 2.4e-7 on 2,040m; **taxPaid is stored NEGATIVE so the shield ADDS**); **the freeze proven decisively**, the structure fee charging on the FEE-FREE fund size 5,466.799280m to 0.000e+0 while the with-fees figure is 5,626.859262m; waterfall conservation and hurdle roll-forward every period; post-fee IRR/MOIC recomputed independently to 1e-9; changing the hurdle leaving FCFF/FCFE/gross dividends byte-identical; and row order + totals agreeing across all four surfaces. **`ebitdaBeforeFundFeesPerPeriod` is NOT the standalone EBITDA** (5,021.992m vs 5,053.947m fund-off, a 31.956m gap): the fee raises funding, which capitalises +35.212m of IDC into Sell-asset inventory, which releases +31.956m to cost of sales. Not a circularity breach (fees are exactly 1,047.144m either way); compare two RUNS, not two fields. One non-defect pinned so it is not re-reported: waterfall `unallocated` is -9.5e-7 on 4,437m (2.2e-16, one machine epsilon). **A SECOND was pinned here and was WRONG: the 360.8-unit BS residue, recorded as pre-existing solver convergence because it was small and identical fund off vs on, was a REAL defect, fixed 2026-08-18 (see the Cash Flow bullet below) and now 0.00 on both live projects.** **The real project does not clear an 8% hurdle** (gross DDM IRR 4.38%), so the carry path only runs in the verifier's second cleared-hurdle case; keep such a case in any future edit or the section passes vacuously. **Step 6b (PDF exports + shared presentation builder, 2026-08-10):** the full PDF already had the fee lines / total / EBITDA-after / cash flow row free from the shared `m4Reports` builders but NO waterfall, gross-vs-net, fee income or basis block (the word "hurdle" appeared nowhere in the document); the **summary PDF had nothing and was actively misleading**, printing an `ebitdaPerPeriod` already net of fees with no fee line, so revenue less CoS less opex missed the printed EBITDA by exactly the fee (-35.98m vs -56.76m, 20.78m unexplained). Fixed by extracting `lib/reports/fundReports.ts` as the ONE definition of fund PRESENTATION (row order, labels, which rows are balances, gross-vs-net, earner grid, headline cards); the M5 screen, Excel, the full PDF and the summary PDF ALL render from it and the Step 6 inline Excel rows were DELETED rather than left as a copy. Formatting stays per-surface (each passes its own money/pct/mult); STRUCTURE is what must not drift. **Measurement note worth reusing:** pdf-lib embeds Inter as a CID font, so PDF text is hex GLYPH IDS and a naive grep silently returns nothing (a first diagnosis pass did exactly that and was worthless); `scripts/pdfTextExtract.ts` maps glyph ids back through the embedded font. The check that earns its place is `verify-fund-pdf` section 2: every surface must IMPORT the builder and re-declare NO row label, so two hand-maintained copies cannot pass. Toggle-off proven across all three exports at once by an 8,660-line fingerprint (Excel cells + fill + numFmt + `pageSetup`, plus both PDFs' decoded text), identical at HEAD and after. `verify-fund-pdf` **49**, teeth proven by three sabotages. Also repointed two `verify-fund-fee-income` checks from grepping the M5 component source to asserting the builder output (107 -> 108) and widened a hole in the `verify-fund-excel` toggle-off regex that did not match `Fund Management and Other Expenses`.   **Fee bases corrected to THREE DISTINCT bases (2026-08-10):** total equity 2,632.733m (cash + in kind + existing), debt facility 2,834.066m (existing + draws + capitalised IDC), fund size 5,466.799m = the sum, so a reader can add two and get the third. Management + custody charge on TOTAL EQUITY, structure on FUND SIZE, arranging on the DEBT FACILITY. **The arranging fee is NOT on the facility limit**: that resolves from an LTV cap to 4,273.822m vs 2,834.066m raised, a 1,439.756m gap, so it charged a ceiling never drawn; pinned that facilityLimitOverride and a stated principal do NOT move the fee. `totalEquity`/`debtFacility` resolve INDEPENDENTLY of fundSize (a fundSizeOverride zeroes its components) and the reconciliation display suppresses itself under that override. Interim step the same day, superseded: the two annual fees charged on OPENING NAV; the engine was correct (`fee_t == openingNAV_t x rate`, max single-period NAV 3,953.2m = 0.72x fund size, so NAV never exceeded capital) but the base did not match the model (which sizes a fund by TOTAL period debt+equity funding) and the DISPLAYED basis summed a balance over 14 years (41,752.1m vs 5,466.8m fund size, ~7x, reading as a fault). Both now charge on `fund_size`, the same frozen constant the structure fee uses: all three capital fees share ONE base, the annual fees are now charged in period 0 too (fund size is known at the start where opening NAV was zero), and the basis shows the per-period constant + period count ("0.50% of 5,466.8m, 14 periods"). `opening_nav` stays a legal linear base kind, unused. **Export fix (same day, found by a user export not a verifier):** `Project.fundTerms` only reached the store when the Fund Terms tab MOUNTED, so opening a project and exporting produced a file with no fund content and no warning; `attachToProject` now loads it before hydrate (and before the autosave subscriber, or it causes version churn), filling only an ABSENT value. ExportModal now checks the SELECTED version and warns plainly when it carries no fund terms. The stale "terms do not yet flow into the model" notice and module header (true at Step 2, false since Step 3) are rewritten. Known: the full PDF truncates the inline fee labels in its narrow label column (hence the columnar basis block), and the Excel Summary tab can no longer be one page on a fund project (accepted). **Step 6 (Excel export rows, 2026-08-10):** the diagnosis found MORE THAN HALF of it already shipped, because the Excel P&L and Cash Flow tabs render from the same shared builders as the screen and the PDF, so the five fee lines with rate + base inline, `Total Fund Management Fee`, the single EBITDA struck after it, the Step 5b Fund Fee Basis block and the `Fund Management and Other Expenses` row in operating cash flow all arrived free at Steps 3 and 5b. What was genuinely missing was the M5 fund surface: the Returns tab read `rs.waterfall` / `rs.resultNetDividends` / `rs.feeEarners` nowhere. Step 6 adds ONE section, `3. Fund Layer`, APPENDED rather than interleaved (interleaving would open a section band inside `1. Returns` and renumber RE Metrics on a fund project only), carrying gross-vs-net KPI tiles, the terms applied, a gross-vs-net table, the ten waterfall rows in the reference's exact order (the three BALANCE rows carry no lifetime total), fee income by earner with the unallocated remainder SHOWN, the shared basis table, and per-period fee income. `fundRow` is a SEPARATE emitter on purpose: the existing local `moneyRow` is shared with the FCFF/FCFE stream rows that already pass `style:'subtotal'`, so widening it would have moved rows on a standalone project. Rows stay CONSTANTS like their neighbours, so `verify-excel-export`'s pinned "zero formula cells" holds. **One real defect fixed:** the Excel P&L passes `fmt: String(v)` so a `totalOverride` round-trips to a number, and `fundFeeLineLabel` used that same fmt, so the workbook had been reading `1.00% of Fund size 154519446.40625` since Step 5b (Excel-only; the PDF passes `fmt.money`). `M4ReportCtx` gained an optional `labelFmt` defaulting to `fmt`. **Toggle-off PROVEN not asserted:** a full cell-level fingerprint (8,615 entries: value + fill + numFmt, all 17 sheets) captured before any Step 6 code is byte-identical after. `verify-fund-excel` **69**, teeth proven by four sabotages each caught by the right check. Known gaps: fund terms were absent from the Inputs tab (CLOSED 2026-08-11, see below), and the phase-filtered Direct CF shows the full project fee in every phase (pre-existing, so do Total Revenue Received / Tax Paid / Total Capex there). **Step 5b (fee basis display + MODEL-DERIVED fund size, mig 211)** reversed a decision this guideline defends, so read it before touching fee bases: fund size is no longer a typed input, it is resolved from the model (equity + debt, LIFETIME total) by `resolveFundSize` in the FEE-FREE pass and FROZEN before the solver, exactly as opening NAV has been since Step 3. The invariant is narrow: the base KIND `fund_size` is linear and allowed, the VALUE is resolved once and frozen, and `fund_size_solved` (a live solved figure read INSIDE the loop) stays in `CIRCULAR_FEE_BASES` and stays forbidden. `fundSizeOverride` pins a typed target instead, and the typed figure is still the fallback when the model raises no capital. Five invariants worth knowing before you read anything else: (1) toggle OFF is byte-identical to today, pinned by `verify-fund-layer-guard` against a fully populated but DISABLED block; (2) every fee base is linear and the rule is a DATA STRUCTURE (`FUND_FEE_SPECS` + `LINEAR_FEE_BASES` / `CIRCULAR_FEE_BASES`), so the verifier fails on the registry, not on a comment; (3) the fee schedule is FROZEN before the iterative solver, which is what stops funding raising the fees; (4) the Step 4 WATERFALL (`src/core/calculations/returns/waterfall.ts`, pure + primitives-only) runs over the M5 Distributed-Equity stream on the REFERENCE structure, rebuilt 2026-08-05 from an earlier three-tier shape: `accrued = (unpaidBoP + equityDrawn) x rate`, `owed = equityDrawn + unpaidBoP + accrued`, `paid = MIN(dist, owed)`, `excess = dist - paid`, `fee = excess x pct`, so there is NO return-of-capital tier (equity drawn folds into ONE hurdle balance), NO catch-up and NO residual split, and the fee is FLAT on the excess. The accrual compounds AND charges the same-period draw, collapsing to `owed = (BoP + drawn) x (1+r)`, so a draw held n periods owes `C(1+r)^(n+1)` and an investor paid exactly the hurdle earns MORE than the hurdle rate as an IRR (a stated accrual convention, not an IRR the payment reproduces). It is NOT in the funding solve (the fee splits cash that already left the project), which `verify-fund-waterfall` proves by holding the full financials snapshot identical across three hurdle/fee settings; gross streams and `result` are untouched, net rides alongside as `netDividendStreamPerPeriod` + `resultNetDividends`; (5) **Step 5 (2026-08-05) made it VISIBLE**: two toggle-gated sections on the M5 Returns tab (Fund Distribution Waterfall with gross-vs-net IRR/MOIC and the reference row order, balances carrying no lifetime total; Fund Fee Income per earner), plus `feeEarners` on the snapshot from a SEPARATE pure engine (`src/core/calculations/returns/feeEarners.ts`). Fee earners sit BESIDE the equity partners: `PartnersSection` and `computePartnerReturns` were not edited at all, the Fund Manager takes 100% of the five management fees plus its matrix share of the performance fee, and matrix shares are NEVER normalised (an 80% matrix allocates 80% and reports the remainder). `verify-fund-fee-income` pins that changing the matrix leaves the whole partners block and every gross stream byte-identical; note that all 556 pre-existing checks PASS when the Fund Manager is leaked into the partner roster (a zero-equity partner keeps Sigma partners == consolidated true), so only this verifier sees it. UI is asserted at SOURCE level only, a real render under tsx is blocked by the CSS-module import; (6) the ENGINE reads `Project.fundTerms` in the version snapshot, `refm_fund_terms` is the tab's durable store, and the server three-tier probes 210 -> 209 -> 208. **Ten-item export review (2026-08-11), items 1 to 9, all measured on the real project before any code changed:** (1) **Checks tab reported CHECK on a healthy model.** The tolerance was `maxBsDiff < 1`, an ABSOLUTE one-currency-unit band on a seven-billion balance sheet (1.4e-10 relative), so a residue of 360.792 at 5.1e-8 failed. It is now RELATIVE (1e-6 of the PEAK magnitude, not the worst period's value, because net cash flow crosses zero and produced "5.0e+0 of 0.0 m"), the status reads OK with the residue and its scale stated, and the other two rows, which were the string `'OK'` with an unrelated magnitude in the note column (one printed total capex, -3,561,517,930, beside a green OK), are now real comparisons: closing cash vs BS cash, and Direct CF vs Indirect CF. All three read the PLATFORM snapshot; `addChecks` no longer destructures `lm` at all. (2) **The workbook shipped TWO models.** Summary and the Returns tab's own "Returns Metrics" block read `lm.*` from `lib/excel/liveModel.ts`, the simplified twin left over from when this workbook emitted live formulas, whose IRR streams carry no inception outflow and none of the historical development investment: Summary said Project IRR **177.3%** / Equity IRR 38.3% / multiple 7.31x while the grid ten rows above said **10.9%** / 7.0% / 2.33x, and the Checks tab hyperlinked to the wrong one. Summary, the Returns metrics block and the Checks headline now all read `rs` (the platform returns engine) and every label names its basis. The third figure the review flagged, Distributed Equity 6.8%, was NOT an error: it is `rs.result.dividends`, a genuinely different basis. **liveModel is no longer a reporting source anywhere**; it still backs the formula-cached cells. (3) **Min DSCR 0.43x is REAL**, not a period artefact: per-period 0.56 / 2.17 / 3.19 / 0.56 / 0.43 / 1.52, three of six debt-service years below 1.00x, while ICR over the same years runs 0.56 to 25.63, so interest is covered and the amortisation is not. Flagged in check-red on the Summary highlight (with a sentence naming the count), on the Returns coverage tile, and per year in the DSCR row. (4) **A base is a STOCK.** The Total column summed the per-period bases, so an annual fee on total equity printed 36,858.3m against a 5,466.8m fund size on the Excel P&L, the Excel Returns tab AND both PDFs. `FundFeeBasisRow` gained `basisDisplay` + `basisIsPerPeriod` so the rule is encoded ONCE; the text surfaces render "2,632.7 x 14" via the shared `fundFeeBasisText`, Excel keeps a plain NUMBER in the cell (the display scale cannot reach text) and puts the period count in the row label. (5) **Raw-vs-millions was in the LABELS, not the values.** Every fee-basis cell was measured at `full` and `millions` scale in Excel and both PDFs and carries the same money format as its neighbours; no string cell anywhere holds a 7+ digit run. The real defect was `labelMoney`, hardcoded to millions regardless of export scale, so a full-unit export read "0.50% of Total equity 2,632.7 m" beside a cell of 13,163,667. It now follows `displayScale` (threaded through `EmitCtx.labelMoney`). (6) **Hurdle Accrued lost its lifetime total** (4,496.1m), joining the three balance rows. It is an accrual on a compounding balance printed between two balance rows. Noted for reversibility: the sum DOES reconcile (accrued 4,496.1 + drawn 2,632.7 == paid 5,651.8 + unpaid 1,477.0), so this is presentation judgement, not an identity. `FUND_WATERFALL_BALANCE_ROWS` is now `FUND_WATERFALL_NO_TOTAL_ROWS` (the old name is a deprecated alias). (7) **Gross == net is explained**, from a shared `fundGrossNetNote` that renders on the M5 screen, Excel Returns, Excel Summary and both PDFs and returns '' the moment a fee arises. (8) **FUND INPUTS band added to the Inputs tab**: the toggle, five rates, other expenses, hurdle, performance fee, Fund Manager, both override flags, the three resolved capital bases (marked computed, not input) and the un-normalised distribution matrix. (9) **The Cover claim was partly WRONG and is corrected here:** the Cover already listed "Fund Fee Basis" and "3. Fund Layer" as hyperlinked sub-entries; the first scan reported zero only because hyperlink cells hold `{text,hyperlink}` objects that stringify to `[object Object]`. What was genuinely missing was a top-level signal, now a "Fund layer active" marker on the identity strip, plus an exemption so fund sections can never be dropped by the 14-section-per-tab cap (they sit at the END of both P&L and Returns). Registering the Summary tab's front-matter bands as sections was TRIED AND REVERTED: every section link is an `!A<row>` anchor whose column A must hold the title, and a front-matter canvas keeps column A as a margin, so it broke the anchor invariant workbook-wide to gain three entries. **Toggle-off PROVEN on the FIXTURE** (the real project is edited in place and is not a stable baseline): 17,230 cells across 17 sheets at two display scales, both PDFs at 8,316 decoded lines, byte-identical except Checks / Returns / Summary, which are exactly items 1 to 3, and zero fund labels anywhere. **Basis block readability follow-up (2026-08-11b):** (a) the labels were being TRUNCATED by the 34-character label column ("Custody and admin fee: basis charged on (per period, 14 periods)" is 64 chars and rendered as "...basis charged on (p", hiding the very count it was added to show). Labels are now `<fee>: basis` / `<fee>: charged` (longest 34 of 34) and the period count moved to the **Base** column, which is 30 wide and held only "Total equity", so it reads "Total equity x 14". (b) The **Rate column was 2 characters** and could not overflow (the Total cell beside it is never empty), so "0.50%" was a sliver; widened to 10, and only when the fund block renders so a standalone project keeps its geometry. **ExcelJS trap worth remembering: `isCustomWidth` is `width !== DEFAULT_COLUMN_WIDTH` and that constant is 9, so a column set to exactly 9 is dropped from `<cols>` entirely and the width silently does not apply.** First attempt used 9 and did nothing; the verifier now asserts `>= 8 && !== 9`. (c) A **flat-amount fee is now ONE row**: its basis and its charge are the same quantity (3.0m per period charged on a basis of 3.0m per period), so the pair said the same thing twice. `FundFeeBasisRow.hasRate` carries that semantically rather than inferring it from `rate === '-'`; rate-based fees keep both rows. **A reported defect that was NOT one:** "Other expenses prints raw 3000000 while neighbours are in millions" was investigated to the byte (file round-trip, raw sheet XML, styles.xml numFmtId resolution, shared-strings scan) and the cells carry style `s=105` -> `numFmtId=166` -> the millions format, IDENTICAL to every neighbouring fee row; the reporter was reading stored values programmatically rather than the rendered cell. Recorded so it is not re-investigated. `verify-fund-excel` 70 -> **75**, teeth proven by four sabotages (no collapse, collapse everything, long labels, width back to 9). `verify-excel-export` 301 -> **304**, `verify-fund-fee-income` 107 -> **109**, `verify-tab2-fixes` 47. Verifiers `verify-fund-layer-guard` 75, `verify-fund-terms` 182, `verify-fund-fees` 138, `verify-fund-waterfall` 382, `verify-fund-pdf` 49, `verify-fund-e2e` 86

## 2026-08-17

- **THE CAPEX TABLE IS AN ORDERED, USER-CLASSIFIED LIST, and a parcel is PROJECT-WIDE (2026-08-17, `verify-capex-structure` 115, teeth proven by four sabotages, BROWSER-VERIFIED on the live project and on a throwaway one).** Thirteen defects found by entering a real project end to end, treated as one piece because the last five change the same structure. **(1) A parcel is project-wide.** The explicit parcel lookup was scoped to the asset's own phase in the UI AND in three engine sites, so a Phase 2 asset drawing on the Phase 1 land resolved `undefined` -> rate 0 -> land value 0, silently, and it was reachable by doing nothing: `handleAddAssetToPhase` seeds `parcelId` from `phaseParcels[0] ?? parcels[0]`, and that fallback crosses phases, so the asset was BORN holding a reference the lookup could not resolve. Only the EXPLICIT lookup widened; the implicit rules (autoByBua / percent / equal share) keep their phase scoping, so nothing that was resolving moves. **(2) Weighted average has two scopes**, `__weighted__` (this phase, unchanged) and the new `__weighted_all__`, and every option in the dropdown now prints the rate it resolves to, which is what "shows no rate" actually meant. **(3) No silent zero:** `computeAssetLandBreakdown` returns `rateIssue` (`parcel_missing` / `zero_parcel_rate` / `no_parcels_in_scope` / `zero_custom_rate`) and the card prints the reason beside the number. Third of this family after the allocation-share zero and `Number(null)`. **(4) The period window follows the phase.** `deriveCostWindow(baseId, cp)` is the ONE definition, used by the seed and by the store's re-derive on a phase-length change; seeded lines carry `windowFollowsConstruction` and typing a Start or End clears it. The 1-to-25 the user saw was ONE caller: `buildWizardSnapshot` called `makeBlankCostLines(p.id)` without the phase length, so the parameter default of 24 applied to every wizard-created project. `repairStaleWizardCostWindows` repairs existing ones, running on the RAW snapshot BEFORE the chain (see TRAPS 7.8) and recognising the clamped shape; measured on the live project, 8 lines repaired and the hand-set phase untouched. The default now ends AT `cp`, not `cp + 1` (that buffer made every seeded line render a permanent "extends into operations" warning). **(5) A followed source owns the window:** `AssetCostBreakdown.resolvedWindowByLineId` reports what the engine spent on, and the row renders THAT instead of two editable boxes, so marketing shows "from collections" or "no collections yet" rather than a construction window it does not use. **(6) Stage is a per-line choice** via a new `stageOverride` (NOT a write to `stage`: the id map outranks `stage`, and that precedence is how the marketing reclassification reached saved models with no migration). `byStage` and the `percent_of_construction` base now read `deriveCostStage`, which also settles `pre-operating`, seeded soft and mapped operating since the map was written (see TRAPS 7.10). **(7) Order is editable:** `insertCostLineNear` + `moveCostLine` (within the phase, swapping with the row the user can SEE, since a stage filter can hide the array neighbour), and a line whose selection has fallen below it says so on the row. Case overrides are id-keyed so a reorder is safe, and `snapshotsEqual` is `JSON.stringify` so a reorder is detected and saved. **(8) Catalog order** is land -> hard -> soft -> **developer fee** (new, zero rate, charging on the hard and soft lines above it) -> contingency (its base now includes the fee) -> **marketing LAST**, which by the positional rule puts marketing OUTSIDE the fee and contingency bases: a deliberate departure from the reference, chosen because a visible positional rule beats a hidden exception. NEW SEEDS ONLY; reordering saved projects would change their numbers unasked. **(9) `% of selected lines` shows its base** (`selectedBaseByLineId`), which the caption had always been able to print and had always been handed `undefined`. **(10) The `InputLabel` help icon leaves the tab order** (`tabIndex={-1}` + `title`): the label renders before its input, so every second Tab landed on an icon and popped a tooltip over the next field. Measured after: 54 icons on the page, 0 tab stops. **(11) A sub-unit area typed before a unit size is HELD, not discarded** (the Pass 57 no-op protected a hand-typed count and silently threw away the area), and converts to keys the moment a size arrives.

- **NO COST LINE IS PRESENT BUT INVISIBLE, AND A LOCKED INPUT LOOKS LOCKED (2026-08-17c, `verify-no-hidden-cost-lines` 53 NEW (replaces `verify-country-gate`), `verify-view-lock` 37 NEW, teeth proven by four sabotages, BROWSER-VERIFIED). NO NUMBER MOVES on either live project.** **(1) THE COUNTRY GATE IS RETIRED.** `CostLine.requiresCountry` let a line exist in the model while being absent from the screen, and that one property produced two silent money defects in a week, in opposite directions: the engine CHARGED a hidden transfer-tax row (937,500 on a Phase 2 hotel, on top of the user's own line), and once the gate was honoured, SELECTING A COUNTRY made that row appear and charge, doubling a cost already entered by hand. The fix for a defect on both sides of a gate is to remove the gate. RETT is **no longer seeded** (the seed is 12 lines, not 13); it is a catalog entry the user adds when the project needs it, and `stampFromEntry` puts `phasingSource: 'land_cash'` on the line so it still lands with the land cash. `assetVisibleLines` no longer takes a country: visibility depends on phase and target asset and nothing else, so **shown is charged**. `retireCountryGatedLines` (raw-snapshot repair, runs on every hydrate) clears saved rows with TWO cases and neither moves a number: not chargeable today -> REMOVED with its overrides (it was invisible and contributing zero), chargeable today -> KEPT with the dead flag stripped (it is already charged, so removing it would delete a live cost). **Measured on both live projects: total capex identical to the byte** (FMP - MARINA GATE 426,406,950 before and after, lines 23 -> 21; FMP RE HUB unchanged, it never had a gated row). `requiresCountry` stays on the type, DEPRECATED, so the repair can find it. The country select stays (it drives statement terminology and records where the project is) but creates and activates nothing. **(2) VIEW MODE NOW LOOKS READ-ONLY.** A project opens read-only until Edit, and the lock lived only at the store's model mutators, which no-op SILENTLY: an input accepted a change, showed it, and discarded it on the next render. That is how the phasing curve appeared to "revert". The panel-level `pointer-events` lock was tried once before and removed because it killed collapsibles, selectors and view toggles, so the lock is on the CONTROLS: `input` / `select` / `textarea` get `pointer-events: none` plus a muted dashed not-allowed treatment (globals.css, `!important` because these carry inline styles), buttons and rows are untouched, and capture-phase handlers swallow every key except Tab, Escape and the copy shortcuts (CSS cannot stop typing into a Tab-focused field). `data-view-editable` opts out the four controls that filter a view rather than change the model (Capex stage filter, the two sensitivity axes, the Module 6 field finder). **The store lock and the screen lock are now ONE derivation** (`modelLocked` -> `viewLocked`), so a field cannot look editable while the store refuses the write. Module 7 is exempt: the deck carries its own state and its own save. Browser-verified: typing into the project name while locked leaves the value untouched, the stage filter stays live, tabs and expand-all still work, and after Edit the same field takes the text. Detail in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.19 (a gate that hides a row is a defect generator) and 7.20 (a control that accepts a change and discards it is a lying screen).

- **A COUNTRY IS A SELECTED VALUE, AND A SAVED FIELD MUST SURVIVE BEING LOADED (2026-08-17b, `verify-country-gate` 15 -> 51, `verify-snapshot-field-survival` 27 NEW, `verify-capex-phasing` 84 -> 91, `verify-capex-structure` 126 -> 133, teeth proven by six sabotages, BROWSER-VERIFIED on the live project and a throwaway).** Four screen-found defects. **(1) `Project.country` had NO EDITOR ANYWHERE**, on any screen or wizard step, while two behaviours read it: the `requiresCountry` cost-line gate and the default statement terminology (Zakat). The visible field is `location` ("free-text city / country / region, display only"), which is where the user had typed "Jeddah, Saudi Arabia" while `country` sat at its default `''` on every project this app has created. So the gate could never match and the Capex tab correctly said the country was not set. **The earlier-flagged risk (a gate matching free text against the literal 'Saudi Arabia') was NOT the cause**, and fixing that would have left the field unreachable. Now a `<select>` on Project & Phases writes an ISO code from `src/core/countries.ts` (249 entries) and ONE comparison, `countryMatches`, resolves BOTH sides, so a line saved as 'Saudi Arabia' matches a project storing 'SA' with nothing migrated either side and an unrecognised string still comparing as text. The location can offer its country as a ONE-CLICK suggestion (`guessCountryFromLocation`, matched on comma-separated parts so a substring cannot resolve); it never infers. **Closing a gate opens its mirror:** users added their own copy of the line they could not see, so setting a country now DOUBLES that cost. Measured on the live project: a user RETT line at 5% charges 1,375,000 (phase 1) and 937,500 (phase 2), and the seeded `rett__phase_2` carries a stale 5% that goes live the moment a country is set. So the country field names what it switched on, and the Capex tab carries a `costs-duplicate-charge-notice` keyed on CATALOG IDENTITY plus phase (the two rows are named differently), stating the duplication and deleting nothing. **(2) RETT was not following the land cash.** The shape a `land_cash` follower follows was built by scanning for `method === 'percent_of_cash_land'`, which is ALSO RETT's own method (a transfer tax is a percentage of the cash land value), so RETT was half of the shape it was following. Measured: phase 2 land cash sits entirely in period 1, and RETT resolved across 1..3 as 625,000 / 156,250 / 156,250; it now resolves to 1..1 as 937,500. `isParcelDrivenLandLine` is the one definition of which lines are the land value and its own docstring warns that method is the wrong discriminator; the site now calls it. **(3) THE ASSET PHASING CURVE WAS SAVED AND THEN DESTROYED ON READ.** `migrateLegacyToV8` rebuilt every phase, parcel, asset and cost line from a literal naming a FIXED set of fields, so `asset.capexPhasing` was dropped on every open; the database row really does carry it. **Third field lost this way** (after `windowFollowsConstruction` and `phasingSource`), and the guard added last round covers `CostLine` only. Fixed by SHAPE, not by a fourth name: every record spreads the raw object and normalises on top, which also makes the loose path agree with the v8 path (which has always spread and runs the identical chain). Note every app-saved snapshot takes this path, because `extractPersistSnapshot` writes no version marker. **A second live defect fell out of the new verifier:** the v6 rename map turned `marketing` into `commission` and `project-management` into `professional-fee`, and both are CURRENT catalog ids (2026-08-15 and 2026-08-17), so a marketing line saved by a new project was renamed and then deduped away on the next open. Both keys removed and the invariant checked: no rename key may be a catalog id. **(4) Area-mode sub-units had no unit size**, so the area x unit size = count identity was only enforceable in Units mode (three of the four sub-units on the live project are Area-metric). Area mode now takes a Unit Size and derives a read-only count; the stored quantity is unchanged (`metric` stays 'area', `metricValue` stays the total sqm) and the whole cost breakdown is proven byte-identical with a unit size set, because `computeSubUnitArea` and `makeSubUnitMaterial` both branch on `metric` and never read `unitArea` there. Also noted, not fixed: in VIEW mode every input is live-looking and silently discards the edit (`MODEL_MUTATORS` no-op on `viewLocked`). Detail in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.16 (a whitelist is the wrong shape for a migration), 7.17 (a legacy rename must never target a line that exists today) and 7.18 (a field with no editor is not a default, it is unreachable).

- **A FACILITY SHARE IS USED EXACTLY AS TYPED (2026-08-17, `verify-facility-shares` 29 -> 55, teeth proven by restoring the rescale (12 failures reproducing the reported 66.67 / 33.33), BROWSER-VERIFIED). MOVES LIVE NUMBERS, deliberately.** `normaliseFacilityShares` RESCALED: any share set whose sum was not 100 was divided through by that sum, so typing 100 into the first of two facilities (the second still at 50) produced 66.67 and 33.33 on every recompute, one facility could never carry the whole requirement, and the card printed "Normalised: 66.67%" under the typed 100, presenting the divergence as a fait accompli. A second defect in the same function: ONE missing share triggered an equal split across ALL of them, so a deliberate 70 became 50 because a neighbour had no value. And `reconcile` already carried `Facility shares sum N (expected 100)`, a check that **could never fire** because the function it guarded guaranteed 100 by construction. Now `resolveFacilityShares` is verbatim (floored at zero); the equal split survives ONLY when no share is typed anywhere (nothing to override); an absent share beside a typed one resolves to zero, which is what makes "set one to 100 and the others go to zero" work; and a wrong sum is STATED, by the reconciliation and by a banner naming the direction ("raises more debt than the project needs" / "leaves part of the requirement unfunded") with a one-click explicit repair. **Consequence to expect on any project whose shares do not sum to 100: the drawdown changes.** Measured live, a project carrying 100 and 50 drew 214,852 under the rescale and draws 322,279 (150%) as typed, with the banner explaining it and the button fixing it in one click. Detail in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.15, whose general form is the point: **normalising an input behind the user's back is worse than accepting it or rejecting it.**

- **TWO IDENTICAL SENIOR DEBT TABLES ARE NOT A DOUBLE COUNT (2026-08-17, `verify-facility-shares` 29, teeth proven by two sabotages, BROWSER-VERIFIED). NO NUMBER MOVED, and that is the finding.** The Financing tab rendered the Senior debt schedule twice with the same opening, drawdown, IDC and closing, and Combined Debt Service showed 214,852 against 107,426 in each, which reads exactly like the RETT defect. **It is not.** There are genuinely two tranches (the wizard seeded one PER PHASE) and a facility's schedule is PROJECT-WIDE: since Pass 28 (2026-05-14) the engine deliberately stopped windowing a tranche to `tranche.phaseId`, because a bank funds drawdowns in every phase and the interest on all of them is IDC. So a facility draws the project debt requirement TIMES ITS SHARE, and `normaliseFacilityShares` splits equally when no share is set. **Measured: 107,426,203 x 2 = 214,852,407 = the debt requirement exactly, and collapsing the two facilities into one leaves capex drawdown, IDC, interest expensed, principal repaid and the requirement byte-identical.** The real defect was seeding one facility per phase for an engine that finances the project as a whole, so a per-phase facility was an unlabelled 50% share. Now: the wizard seeds ONE facility (new projects only, the schema is unchanged and `phaseId` stays because `removePhase` cascades on it); every facility table names its share when there is more than one; and a note states that identical schedules are shares of one requirement, that the combined is their SUM, and that deleting all but one is number-neutral. The note keys on the SYMPTOM (more than one new facility with identical draw schedules), not on "no share is set", because the live project carries explicit 50 and 50. Detail in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.14, whose lesson is the point: **the fix for a genuine double count and the fix for a split presented badly are opposite actions.** **Alongside it: two project-total tiles on Development Costs**, Total Project Cost Incl. Land and Total Project Cost Excl. In-Kind Land, derived from `COST_STAGES` (so a future stage cannot be silently left out of the headline) with the in-kind slice read from the engine's own `perPeriodLandInKind`. They are the same two measures the Capex summary tables report as "Incl All Land" and "Excl Land In-Kind", so the tile bar and the schedule cannot disagree: verified live at 429,932 and 369,932 against a 60,000 in-kind land slice.

- **A COUNTRY GATE MUST STOP THE MONEY, NOT JUST THE ROW (2026-08-17, `verify-country-gate` 15, teeth proven by one sabotage, BROWSER-VERIFIED). A WRONG NUMBER, now corrected.** `CostLine.requiresCountry` gates a country-specific line (the catalog's RETT). Three input-side filters in the Costs tab honoured it; `computeAssetCost` built its own list filtering on phase and target asset only, so a gated line was **CHARGED while invisible, uneditable and undeletable**, and the same function respected the gate for the percent-of-selected base thirty lines below. Reaching the broken state takes nothing unusual: type a rate while the country matches, then change the country. **Measured live: the Phase 2 hotel was charged 937,500 for a RETT row it did not show, on top of the user's own RETT at the same rate, so the transfer tax was double-charged into the financing schedule, the statements, the returns and both exports; the asset subtotal falls from 146,889 to 145,952 exactly.** Now the engine builds its list from the shared `assetVisibleLines`. Because closing a silent overcharge opens the mirror image, the tab now states any gated line that carries a rate it cannot use. Detail in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.13. **Alongside it: (1) COPY IS A PURE PLAN** (`lib/state/costCopyPlan.ts`), extracted from the JSX click handler that made it untestable (which is how "copy reproduces the line set" passed as a source-string grep while the live project still did not do it), and **both sides now use the same visibility rule**: the source list dropped country-gated lines while the target list counted them, so a hidden `rett__phase_2` matched the user's own RETT, nothing was created and the override landed on a row they cannot see. Verified by running the planner against the live line sets: all 10 source lines map to real counterparts, including "Permits & Approvals" to "Permits and Approvals" by catalog id despite the different names. **(2) AREA x UNIT SIZE = COUNT, so only two of the three are inputs.** All three were editable and each edit rewrote another, so typing a count silently rewrote the area. Area and Unit Size are authoritative, Count is DERIVED and read-only, and the one exception is the same rule: with no unit size there is nothing to divide by, so the count becomes the input (the existing-operations case Pass 57 added). A count is a whole number, so an area that does not divide evenly SNAPS, and the row now says "you entered 13,300; 160 x 83 = 13,280" instead of silently changing a number the user typed.

- **THE Y0 UPFRONT LUMP HAS ONE PLACEMENT RULE (2026-08-17, `verify-y0-lump-placement` 23, teeth proven by two sabotages, BROWSER-VERIFIED).** A cost line's phase-local index 0 is the upfront lump (land cash, land in-kind, RETT) and belongs in the period BEFORE the phase starts, clamped to the first project period for a phase starting with the project. That rule was written out in FIVE places with TWO answers: M4 Pass 2W (2026-05-24) clamped it in the financing aggregate and the equity engine, and the Costs tab's own schedule kept `if (offset > 0)`, which added a PHASE 1 lump to **no column at all**. Reported from the screen as "Phase 1 land shows a total but no period allocation, while Phase 2 phases correctly". **Measured before the fix: 70,000,000 of phase 1 land, present in the model, in the financing schedule and in the capex table's Total column, and absent from every period column of that table**, so the row did not foot; and one of the three tables accumulated its row total INSIDE the dropped branch, so it under-reported rather than merely misplacing. Now `phaseLocalToProjectIndex` in `capexPhasing.ts` is the one definition and all five sites call it. The financing docstring, which described the pre-Pass-2W behaviour and claimed the two surfaces matched, was false on both counts and is rewritten. Detail in [docs/TRAPS.md](docs/TRAPS.md) 7.12.

- **A COST LINE IS A CATALOG ENTRY PLUS A LABEL (2026-08-17, migration 214, `verify-cost-catalog` 194, teeth proven by four sabotages, BROWSER-VERIFIED on the live project and a throwaway).** The defect class: a row's behaviour lived in its `id` (the seeded method, `STANDARD_STAGE_BY_ID`, the seeded `phasingSource`) while its label was free text, so a live project carried a row labelled "Permits and approvals" that was the seeded Commission line, following sales collections, with nothing on screen saying so. **Now `CostLine.catalogId` names the entry**, selecting one STAMPS method + stage + `stageOverride` + phasing source + basis onto the LINE, and renaming changes the label only. **The line owns its behaviour, not the catalog**: the entry is written onto the line at selection time and is never read by the engine, the reports or the exports, so an unreachable catalog can never change a number (at worst a caption reads "Custom line"). Identity RESOLVES from the base id when `catalogId` is absent, so every existing line declares itself with no migration and no data change. **Built-ins live in code** (`lib/state/costCatalog.ts`, 17 entries covering a real development; the four new ones, engineering supervision / design consultancy / permits and approvals / project management, are SELECTABLE BUT NOT SEEDED, so the 13-line seed is unchanged and a new project gains no extra zero rows). **User additions are shared per user** in `refm_cost_catalog` (migration 214), added in place from the row that needs them, and whoever adds one sets its method and stage because a name with no behaviour is the problem being solved; the API fails SOFT (absent table returns `available:false` and the built-ins carry on). A reassignment states what it will change before doing it, and the amber "renamed" flag fires only when a label is ANOTHER entry's name (comparing to its own entry's label marked every seeded synonym, measured: "Construction (BUA)" against the entry "Superstructure"). **Four defects fixed alongside it. (1) ONE money strip.** Two strips were guarded off two different variables (`displayPhasing` vs `effPhasing`), so an inherited manual curve rendered the real amounts and an even spread beneath; the row now renders `perLinePerPeriod` and calls no distribution function at all (TRAPS 7.11). **(2) `phasingSource` was being DESTROYED** by the loose-path field whitelist, measured on the live project (v1 `collections` / `land_cash`, working version ABSENT), which is why marketing phased on the build. Restored where absent, never where the user chose, and a new verifier section fails on ANY `CostLine` field the whitelist does not name so a third field cannot go the same way (TRAPS 7.9). **(3) Copy between assets reproduces the LINE SET**, matched by catalog identity plus occurrence rather than by display name (a renamed or added line had no name twin and was silently skipped, so a target phase kept the seeded catalog while the panel reported success); missing lines are created with selections remapped into the target phase, removal is opt-in, the counts are reported, and the dialog says that cost lines belong to a PHASE so the change reaches every asset in it. **(4) Deleting a row is undoable**, restoring at its index with its overrides, and the confirm dialogs are gone.

## 2026-08-16

- **`marketing` is a COST STAGE (2026-08-16)**: `CostStage` is `land | hard | soft | marketing | operating`. A selling cost is not a construction cost, so **construction cost excluding land also excludes marketing**, while the developer fee and contingency still charge ON marketing because bases are SELECTIONS of named lines and never consult a stage. A stage rather than an exclude-from-totals flag, deliberately: the stage states something true, a flag would be a general mechanism for omitting any line from any total. **Traps when adding a stage:** only the sites typed `Record<CostStage, number>` are compiler-guarded, and two INFERRED literals in `Module1Costs.tsx` would silently drop the new stage from the Capex-by-Stage table and its Total; and `deriveCostType` defaults to **`'hard'`**, so an unhandled stage reads as a hard cost. The `Total Capex Excl. Land` tile is now `Construction Cost Excl. Land`. Detail in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-16b.

- **`% of Selected Lines` is POSITIONAL (2026-08-16)**: a cost line may charge on any line **ABOVE it** on that asset, in display order (order is array order; the Capex table filters `costLines` and never sorts), and on nothing else. One shared rule in `src/core/calculations/selectedBase.ts` used by the picker AND enforced in the engine's Pass 3, so the list offered and the base computed cannot diverge. This **replaced a blanket ban on the `percent_of_selected` method**, which had blocked the ordinary cascade a budget is built from (a developers fee on hard plus softs, then a contingency including that fee) and even stopped the standard catalog putting professional fee inside contingency's base. Upward-only references make a cycle unrepresentable, so no method ban is needed. **Position was already load-bearing in the engine but unguarded**: a downward reference to a DIRECT-method line read a real number and produced a wrong base (measured 150 vs 100), while one to another percent line silently read zero. No saved model moves (audited all 1400 versions). Detail in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-16.

- **Public read-only page API (2026-08-10)**: `GET /api/public/pages/[slug]` serves selected CMS pages to **PaceMakers Business Consultants**, so the partner site renders FMP page content without a database connection or a shared deploy. Returns `{version:1, page:{slug,title,meta_title,meta_description,og_image_url,status,updated_at}, sections:[{section_type,content,styles,display_order}]}`, sections filtered to `visible=true` and sorted by `display_order`; no internal columns (row id, is_system, the visible flag, created_at) are in the response. **The public slug is NOT the internal slug**: `SLUG_WHITELIST` in the route file maps `modeling-hub`->`modeling`, `refm`->`modeling-real-estate`, `training-hub`->`training`, because the three names in the spec matched no live `cms_pages` row and a literal whitelist would have 404ed on all of them; the mapping also means a CMS rename cannot break the partner. Anything not in that constant 404s, and so does a draft, so unpublished work cannot be probed. Auth is `x-api-key`, constant-time, resolved by the shared `src/shared/api/publicApiKeys.ts` and **fails CLOSED** (no usable key refuses every request). Since 2026-08-16 the key is ROTATABLE and `FMP_PUBLIC_API_KEY` is only the PRE-ROTATION fallback: see the rotation paragraph at the end of this bullet before assuming the env var is what the endpoint accepts. Rate limit 60/min/IP, in-memory and therefore PER INSTANCE on serverless, so the effective ceiling is a multiple of that. Success carries `Cache-Control: public, max-age=60, s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300`; errors are `no-store`. Rejections write `public_api_unauthorized` to **`public_api_audit` (migration 212)**, a NEW table because `admin_audit_log.admin_id` is NOT NULL (proven by a live insert probe) and an unauthenticated caller has no admin to attribute; the audit write is best effort and never turns a 401 into a 500, so the route works before the migration is applied. Note the section `content` jsonb is passed through VERBATIM, including any nested `visible:false` items the FMP renderer would hide, so a consumer must respect those itself. Verifier `scripts/verify-public-pages-api.ts` **43** (calls the real handler against the live DB). **Admin > API Keys (2026-08-11):** `/admin/api-keys` (System group) shows the key masked with a reveal toggle and a copy button, the endpoint URL, the whitelisted slugs and what the key grants, so the value never has to be dug out of an env file. `SLUG_WHITELIST` moved to `src/shared/api/publicPagesConfig.ts` so the screen and the endpoint read ONE constant. The rule that makes it an API keys screen rather than an arbitrary environment reader: **the client never names an environment variable**, it sends a registry id that the route resolves against a literal constant, so `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` and friends are unreachable. GET carries a character count and no part of the value; POST returns the value and writes `admin_audit_log` action `api_key_revealed`, and COPY goes through that same POST so it cannot bypass the record. `requireAdmin` try/catches `getServerSession`, which THROWS without a request scope and would otherwise turn a missing session into a 500. **KEY ROTATION (2026-08-16, migration 213, `public_api_keys` + `rotate_public_api_key`):** the screen can now regenerate the key, which required moving the live value out of the environment: a button cannot write a Vercel environment variable, and could not reach a running deployment if it could, so a rotate button that only generated a value could never make the old key stop working. **THE RESOLUTION RULE, read this before touching either route:** the environment variable is consulted only when the table holds **NO ROW AT ALL** for the key id, not "no active row". An active row supersedes the environment permanently; a **retired-only history closes the endpoint** rather than resurrecting the key a rotation retired; an **unreadable key store REFUSES** rather than being read as "no rows"; and a table that is absent (a deploy landing before the migration) falls back to the environment so the partner keeps working. Rotation is ONE transaction (`rotate_public_api_key` retires the active row and inserts the new one, so there is never a moment with two valid keys or none) and the database enforces at most one active key per id with a **partial unique index**, so "the old key stopped working" is a schema guarantee rather than a careful caller. **Resolution is NOT cached**: an in-memory TTL would let a retired key keep working on a warm serverless instance, which is exactly what rotation must prevent, so the public route pays one indexed read per request. **Only a SHA-256 and a 14-character prefix are stored**, so the value exists for exactly one HTTP response, Reveal is REFUSED for a rotated key (`409 hashed_not_revealable`) rather than returning an empty string that reads as a broken button, and the one-time panel on the screen must be dismissed by hand rather than sharing the 60-second auto-hide a revealed key uses (a revealed key can be revealed again; a rotated one cannot). Rotation requires `confirm:true` in the body as well as the dialog, and writes `admin_audit_log` action `api_key_rotated` carrying PREFIXES only, reporting `audited:false` to the screen if that write fails rather than pretending. **Cutover is immediate with no overlap window** and the dialog says so: the consumer gets 401s until they are sent the new value. `REGISTRY` and `requireAdmin` moved to `src/shared/api/apiKeyRegistry.ts`, since a `route.ts` may export only its handlers and there are now two routes needing both. Verifiers `verify-admin-api-keys` 55 -> **80** and NEW `verify-api-key-rotation` **85** (behavioural, against the live database under a PROBE key id, never the real one, because a verifier must not be able to hand the partner a 401 and a rotation cannot be undone; the probe rows are removed and the real key id's rows are proven byte identical to before the run). Teeth proven by six sabotages (3 / 7 / 3 / 1 / 2 / 1 failures). `verify-public-pages-api` stays **43** but now SKIPS its 200-path checks when a rotated key is live, since no plaintext exists for it to present: rotation working must not read as the partner feed being broken. **Not browser-verified.**

## 2026-08-15

- **COST ALLOCATION, SILENT MONEY LOSS, FIXED 2026-08-15 (predates all of the above; read this before touching `resolveAllocationFactor` / `resolveDriverFactor` in `src/core/calculations/index.ts`)**: every share basis was written `return total > 0 ? mine / total : 0`, which returns 0 for **EVERY** asset when the denominator is zero. The shares then sum to **0 instead of 1**, so a project-level lump sum on that basis was allocated to NOBODY and **left the model with no warning anywhere**, no banner, no check failure, no zero-looking cell to notice. **Measured**: one asset, a phase with no land, a `fixed` cost line of 1,000,000 moved total capex by **0.000m** on `land_share`, against the full 1,000,000 on `bua_share` or `per_asset`. Reachable on any phase whose basis quantity is genuinely zero (no parcels entered yet, an operational asset with no new build, areas not filled in), so it had been losing money quietly for as long as those bases have existed. Now every share path falls back to a shared `equalPhaseShare` equal split (SIX call sites: bua / gfa / land on the basis path, bua / land / value_share on the driver path), which is the convention `computeAssetLandSqm` and the `category` basis already used; companions are excluded and the factors are proven to sum to exactly 1. Only the `fixed` method consults it (`FIXED_METHODS_NEEDING_ALLOC`), since every `rate_per_X` method already returns an asset-scoped contribution. **The fix can move existing models, upward only, wherever a cost was being lost.** Pinned by `verify-new-project-defaults` section E; full narrative in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-15b.

- **Capex phasing inheritance + hard/soft (2026-08-15)**: ONE curve per asset (`Asset.capexPhasing`) that every cost line inherits, with per-line break-out and two DERIVED sources: **RETT follows the land cash outflow** (a transfer tax is due when the land cash is paid, so it must never take the construction curve) and **marketing + commission follow sales collections** (paid out of cash received, so they arise when collections arrive). Built on a SHARED generic primitive `lib/state/inheritance.ts` (own -> derived -> group -> fallback), deliberately free of capex vocabulary because the consolidated input matrices reuse it. Resolution lives INSIDE `computeAssetCost` (`capexPhasing.ts`) because that function has TEN call sites and resolving at them would let the screen and the export disagree; it is skipped when `capexPhasingIsInert`, so an existing project is byte-identical. **A followed source replaces the WINDOW, not just the shape.** `rett` + `marketing` added to the standard catalog at zero rate (RETT country gated). Hard/soft: the row marker deleted on 2026-05-11 is restored, and the shared report layer gained stage SUBTOTALS (it had no hard/soft aggregation at all, which is why no export carried the split). Additive schema, no migration. `verify-capex-phasing` 69. Detail in [CLAUDE-REFM.md](CLAUDE-REFM.md) 2026-08-15d.

## 2026-08-13

- **Module 7 (IC Presentation Builder)**: LIVE. **Free-form AI drafting + fund sections (2026-08-13, `verify-ic-freeform` 72 / `verify-ic-fund-sections` 84, teeth proven by eight sabotages, NOT browser-verified).** (A) The AI panel no longer offers only six fixed narrative fields: select ANY block and give a free instruction ("write this up", "explain this IRR"). Targeting resolves from the SELECTED OBJECT, deliberately not from `findNarrativeTargets`, which is template-driven and returns nothing for a blank slide. **Grounding is unchanged**: same `collectGrounding`, same whitelisted model facts, no new source, and the verifier MEASURES that by capturing the real request rather than reading the source. `buildGroundedRequest` puts `GROUNDING_RULES` in the system prompt and repeats the figure reminder after the task, so a user instruction sits INSIDE rules it cannot reach around, which is why a user-authored task is safe to send at all. **The refusal contract is stricter than the standing rules**: rule 4 permits a partial answer, which is wrong for an arbitrary instruction because a half-grounded paragraph hides which half was real, so free-form is ALL OR NOTHING via a machine-detectable sentinel (`CANNOT_ANSWER_FROM_MODEL:`) and the review offers NO Apply on a refusal. A refusal returns 200 with an empty draft and KEEPS its counted generation (the call was made; refunding it would make refusing free and answering expensive). **An empty block on a blank slide is described as empty in the prompt**, with its own branch, because a model handed a silent empty string invents a subject. Metered through the SAME `ic_narrative` feature, cap and toggle: a free instruction is a different way to ask, not a second allowance. Draft identity in the review moved from the field key to an explicit `id` (the field key for a fixed draft, the object id for a free-form one), since a block-targeted draft has no field. (B) The fund layer reaches the deck: three toggle-gated slides (Fund Terms and Fees, Fund Distribution Waterfall, Fund Returns and Fee Income) covering all five fund sections, fed by a new `fund` block on `ICReportModel` that reads ENTIRELY from the shared `fundReports.ts` / `m4Reports.ts` builders, following the `schedules.fcff` precedent. Toggle off is byte-identical, proven by seeding absent-vs-populated-but-disabled decks and comparing in full. Detail in [docs/FUND_LAYER_GUIDELINE.md](docs/FUND_LAYER_GUIDELINE.md) section 8b and [CLAUDE-AI.md](CLAUDE-AI.md). A PowerPoint-style slide editor (navigator / 16:9 canvas / properties) that replaced the old form-plus-fixed-report UI. **Slides hold BINDING KEYS, never copied numbers**, so the deck follows Modules 1-6 with no sync step and an unresolved binding renders a visible amber UNLINKED state rather than a stale figure. Direct manipulation (drag / resize / snap / inline edit / undo), 18 institutional templates that auto-omit on `available(model)`, full year-by-year schedules paginated across slides, a live ToC, and native PPTX + PDF export from ONE shared resolved-export contract so a file figure cannot drift from the screen. AI drafting on the narrative blocks (see CLAUDE-AI.md). Free text boxes, presentation versions (migration 207) and a save choice on EVERY save (update the version on screen in place, or save as a new version) rather than a silent overwrite. **Report versions are AUTO-NAMED and the last one opens with the project (2026-08-03):** nothing is typed (`{Project}_Presentation_v1.3_08032026`, named server-side, reusing the model version rollover helper as a PURE function), every save keeps a version (an unlinked save creates the first one), and `getDeck` returns the last saved version plus which version it is, so the editor names what is open and the versions modal is for browsing and opening the others. Report versioning stays entirely separate from model/project versioning: its own table, routes, client and UI. ## 2026-08-09

- **Discount normalization fix, an unlimited discount is not a used-up one (2026-08-09)**: a live 25% pre-launch promo showed in the admin discount list but never reached the public pricing page and was never passed to Paddle at checkout, so customers were charged full price with nothing rejected and nothing logged. **Cause was ONE normalization line**: `shapeDiscount` (`paddleApi.ts`) read Paddle's nullable numerics as `Number.isFinite(Number(x)) ? Number(x) : null`, and `Number(null)` is `0`, which is finite. So `usage_limit: null`, meaning UNLIMITED, normalized to `usageLimit: 0`, and `isLiveDiscount` then read `timesUsed >= usageLimit` (0 >= 0) as fully redeemed and filtered the discount out. **The two surfaces disagreed silently because only one of them filters**: `loadAdminDiscountView` returns the Paddle list RAW (so admin kept showing it), while the pricing page and checkout both go through `loadActivePublicPromo`, which requires `isLiveDiscount`; a typed code via `resolveCouponForCheckout` would have reported it expired. **Fix**: a `nullableNumber` helper preserves absence as null instead of collapsing it to zero, applied to `usage_limit` and `times_used`. `isLiveDiscount` needed NO change (it already guarded `!= null`), so the bug was never in the predicate: normalization destroyed the null before the predicate saw it. `shapeDiscount` + `isLiveDiscount` are now EXPORTED so the behaviour is pinned rather than trusted from a comment. **Verified live after the fix**: the featured promo resolves (25% off), `restrict_to` is empty so it covers every plan price id, and Paddle applies it by discount id **despite `enabled_for_checkout: false`** (a `pricing-preview` call returned 1,500 -> 1,125 on pro monthly), which confirms the standing assumption that the flag only governs the customer-typed code field. Confirmed end to end by a real checkout. `verify-payments-structure` 65 -> **79** (14 checks: the pin "no usage limit + zero redemptions is LIVE", a code-less checkout-disabled discount still live, and the exhaustion clause still biting so the fix is not just a deleted check; **proven to have teeth, 7 fail against the previous normalization**). Resolver/gate/enforcement UNCHANGED. No migration.

- **Launch countdown banner (2026-08-09)**: a wide (~800px, scaling down), centred, dismissible launch banner announcing the Modeling Hub launch, shown to visitors on the marketing home, both Modeling Hub pages and both Real Estate platform pages. Reads `training_settings.modeling_hub_launch_date`, the SAME key `/api/cron/auto-launch-check` reads, so the countdown a visitor sees and the moment the hub flips are one date rather than two that can drift; nothing is hardcoded. That key had **no admin editor** (the `/api/admin/modeling-coming-soon` route existed but had ZERO callers), so this wires that existing route to a new "Modeling Hub - Public Launch Date" card on `/admin/modules`; the per-surface signin/register dates are a separate thing and are untouched, so the public announcement can be staged apart from when the auth pages open. **Setting the date does NOT schedule an automatic launch**: the cron also requires `modeling_hub_auto_launch`, which is `'false'` and has no UI writing it. **THREE STATES (2026-08-09c)**: `countdown` while the date is in the future, `launched` once it passes (its own editable headline/supporting line + a call to action, and it STAYS until retired rather than vanishing), `hidden` when the admin switches it off or there is no usable date. Only the OFF choice is stored (`modeling_hub_launch_banner_enabled`, **absent means ON** so existing installs are unaffected, checked FIRST so it overrides both date-derived states); countdown vs launched is derived from the date so the two can never disagree with the clock. **The off switch exists to prevent collateral damage**: the only previous way to retire the banner was clearing the launch date, which is the same key the auto-launch cron reads. At zero the banner **switches in place** to the launched message (both copies are sent to the client) instead of disappearing under a visitor. The CTA destination defaults to the platform's own page derived from the config slug (`${APP_URL}/modeling/<slug>`, absolute because the banner also renders on the apex) and is admin-overridable; no destination + no label means no button rather than a dead link. **Session dismissal is keyed on date AND mode**, so dismissing the countdown does not swallow the launch announcement later the same session. **Sizing**: ~800px wide (`min(800px, 100vw - 32px)`), height follows content capped to the viewport with internal scroll; it was square at 400px until 2026-08-09c. `CountdownTimer` caps its own digit grid at `min(440px, 100%)`, so the digits centre in the wide card rather than stretching. Placement is an **ALLOWLIST of five EXACT paths**, not the blocklist `PromoPopup` uses, because this banner covers content: exact matching keeps it off deeper routes, and `'/'` serves both the apex home and the app subdomain root (which rewrites to `/modeling` with the browser path unchanged). Dismissal is **sessionStorage keyed on the launch date**: gone for the session, back next session, and a rescheduled launch is a new announcement. Square on every screen via `min(400px, 100vw - 32px, 100vh - 32px)`. Brand navy gradient + gold accent. Mounted ONCE in `app/layout.tsx` beside `PromoBanner`; pure rules in `launchCountdown.ts`, server read in `LaunchCountdownBanner.tsx`, path/dismissal/tick in `LaunchCountdownPopup.tsx`. **No migration** (`training_settings` is a free-form key/value store, `key text primary key` with no constraint on the key set, so every launch setting is just another row). **Editable copy + platform-aware (2026-08-09b)**: headline and supporting line are admin-editable (`modeling_hub_launch_headline` / `_subline`), and `modeling_hub_launch_platform` stores the platform SLUG only, with the NAME resolved from the platform config at render (`getPlatform(slug).name`, else the first LIVE platform), so no product name is hardcoded in the banner and a rename carries through. Either field may contain a `{platform}` token, mirroring the `{trialDays}` precedent; blank restores the default; an unresolved platform never leaks a literal token to a visitor. **Admin is now ONE dedicated `LaunchBannerCard`** (date + copy + platform + live preview) instead of a second `LaunchStatusCard`, and it carries a **BANNER SHOWING / NOT SHOWING status computed by the same pure resolver the banner uses**, from the SAVED (not draft) date. That status is the fix for the reported "banner does not appear": the date had never been written (`reason: not_set`), and with three cards on the page carrying a launch date and none stating which drove the banner, a save on the wrong card (where "Save" is disabled when the value is unchanged) looked identical to success. `LaunchStatusCard` is back to its pre-banner shape (the two props added earlier were reverted rather than left dead). `verify-launch-banner` **141**. **Not browser-verified.** Note `/modeling-hub` and `/modeling-hub/real-estate` are `revalidate = 60`, so a date change can lag up to a minute there; the other three are `revalidate = 0`.

## 2026-07-06

- **Discounts / coupons (Model 1: Paddle owns the discount, 2026-07-06)**: the platform REFERENCES, displays, and passes Paddle discounts; it does not compute them (a discount affects PRICE only, never entitlements/gate). **Schema (mig 184)**: `coupon_codes` gains `paddle_discount_id` (the `dsc_...` the code references; NULL = display-only, will not reduce the charge), `kind` (`public` auto-apply / `private` code), `display_label`, `starts_at`. **Resolver** `src/shared/payments/coupons.ts`: `resolveCouponForCheckout` (code -> Paddle discount id, validity + platform scoped, clear rejection reason on every failure) + `loadActivePublicPromo` (newest active public promo, ONLY when it has a `paddle_discount_id`, so a displayed promo always really applies). **Checkout** (`POST /api/payments/checkout`): accepts `coupon_code`; a typed code resolves server-side (invalid -> `{status:'error'}` rejection, no overlay), else the active public promo auto-applies; the resolved `discountId` flows through `CheckoutRequest`/`CheckoutResult` -> the Paddle adapter's `open_overlay` -> `paddleBrowser.openPaddleCheckout` -> `Paddle.Checkout.open({ discountId })`, where Paddle validates + applies it (a rejected discount surfaces via the existing `checkout.error` handler). A discount id is client-safe; the API key stays server-side. A 100%-off Paddle discount yields a zero charge while Paddle still creates the subscription and the webhook grants the plan (entitlements unaffected). **Checkout coupon field** (`app/pricing/CouponInput.tsx`, controlled): logged-in only (logged-out hands off to `/register`); "Apply" gives immediate feedback via `POST /api/payments/coupon/resolve` (no charge/state); the code is held in `PricingExplorer` and passed to checkout. **Marketing/pricing display**: `loadPricingCatalog` returns the active public `promo`; `LivePlanCards` shows a promo banner + the discounted (struck-through) price for percentage promos; `PricingPageBody` threads it through (the Paddle discount id stays server-side, only label/percent reach the client). **Admin** (`/admin/plans` CouponManager): each entry has the Paddle discount id (with a clear "must exist in Paddle, else it only displays" warning), kind, display label, active toggle, start/expiry; a **Not wired** badge flags rows with no `paddle_discount_id`. **Manual plans**: unchanged, the discount is just the amount the admin enters and the manual receipt shows it (no Paddle). Resolver/gate/enforcement UNCHANGED (`verify-entitlement-gate` 92, `verify-pricing-display` 23, `verify-pricing-application-ready` 48 + 1 pre-existing failure, see the Entitlements bullet). Mig 184 APPLIED 2026-07-06.

- **Subscription-email gap closure (2026-07-06)**: fixes the six gaps from the email-system audit; resolver/gate/enforcement UNCHANGED. **P1 (1) Period-end convert-to-manual is no longer silent**: `applyScheduledManualConversion` (webhook `subscription.canceled` + the cron backstop) now sends the manual welcome + issues the branded receipt (when an amount is owed), matching the immediate path; startedAt is anchored to `conv.effectiveAt` so the welcome/receipt dedupe token is STABLE across the webhook/cron race (one email, one receipt). **(2) Welcome/receipt self-heal**: new `selfHealWelcomePaddleEmail` sends the welcome+invoice for a live paid Paddle sub whose `activated` webhook never delivered. It first checks for an existing `welcome_paddle` marker on the STABLE `evt:{subId}` threshold (IGNORING anchor_day), so already-welcomed subs (incl. all existing prod rows) are skipped, no mass re-send. Wired at the billing-panel live read (`GET /api/payments/subscription`, active non-canceled only) AND daily in the reminder cron Pass B (stored row, no extra live call). **P2 (3) Dunning**: adapter now maps `subscription.past_due`/`transaction.payment_failed` -> new neutral `payment_failed`; webhook sends `paymentFailedEmail` (update-card), NO plan change (Paddle retries, access continues), deduped PER BILLING PERIOD (`evt:{subId}` + period-end anchor so the several retry events collapse to one). Retain coexistence: safe alongside Paddle Retain; to hand dunning fully to Retain, drop the `sendPaymentFailedEmail` call in the webhook. **(4) Renewal receipt**: adapter splits `transaction.completed` by `origin` (`subscription_recurring` -> new neutral `renewed`, else `activated`), so a renewal sends the new `renewalReceiptEmail` (not the welcome copy); the `renewed` webhook branch records the ledger txn + sends the receipt with the invoice attached, and does NOT call setUserPlan (plan unchanged). **P3 (5) Double-submit guard**: admin plan route's fallback `started_at` is now DATE-ONLY (`slice(0,10)`), so a rapid double-submit resolves to one dedupe token -> one welcome + one receipt. **(6) Paddle-dashboard cancel**: the webhook `updated` branch now sends the cancellation confirmation when it carries a scheduled cancel-at-period-end, deduped on the SAME `evt:{accessUntil}` key the in-app cancel route uses (no double for in-app cancels). New parsed-event fields `billingPeriodEnd` (+ `renewed`/`payment_failed` types); webhook also resolves the user by stored `paddle_subscription_id` as a fallback for thin renewal/dunning events. All sends go through the existing `[sub-email]` dedupe dispatch (logged sent/skipped/FAILED). Two new templates (`renewalReceiptEmail`, `paymentFailedEmail`), FMP/PaceMakers branding + per-platform `/pricing` links. Verifiers `verify-subscription-emails` 113, `verify-subscription-management` 210, `verify-entitlement-gate` 92. No migration.

(2) Full invoice history survives source flips: Paddle history reads from the DURABLE `payment_transactions` ledger (`listPaddleLedgerInvoices`) not the live API alone; the invoices route merges manual + ledger Paddle + live enrichment (deduped by txn id); the Paddle PDF route authorizes from the ledger (`userOwnsPaddleTransaction`) + builds cfg without a live sub, 404s gracefully when a PDF is unavailable. The webhook baseline-drop now CLEARS the dead Paddle ids (`config.clearPaddleSubscriptionIds`) so a canceled row can't masquerade as live (which mis-rendered billing and blocked manual-assign recovery). They were on `threshold:'once'` + `anchor_day: today`, so a second purchase/trial the SAME day was swallowed as a duplicate (the "was receiving, now not" on repeated testing).
